Design complete for `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`; the audit gate is **closed** by `06.deferred-multiroot-audit.md` (§0.4). **Four** items are escalated to the user and are the only things blocking (§0.7) — X-01 was closed by charter C3 and its apparatus travelled into phase 2 with the scope cut, X-03 was decided by `08` §2, and X-06 is new in this revision. |

This is the design that will be built. It supersedes the four independent proposals. Everything
below is decided; an implementer should need no further design decisions, only SRS authoring and
test-first execution — with the four exceptions in §0.7, which are user rulings, not design
decisions, and which this document must not make on the user's behalf.

---

## Scope — what ships in phase 1, and where the rest lives

**Two normative rulings landed after revision 3 and are applied throughout this revision.**

**1. `08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md` cut concurrent lane execution to a second target** — 5-0,
on a measured finding distribution (`08:12-42`). The target this document designs is
`2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`; the deferred half is `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`.

| | Phase 1 — `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` | Phase 2 — `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Intake, design, freeze | §1, §2.1 Phase 1, §3.3, §7.2 | — |
| **Routing by scale and scope** | §2.1a, `09` — new in this revision | — |
| Wave decomposition + per-wave design | §2.1 Phase 2–3.a, §7.1 loop W | — |
| Handoff documents, schema, five-layer validator, loop H, untested-AC cap | §6 | — |
| **Per-task parallelisability *analysis*, in full** | §5.1, §5.2, §5.3, §7.9 (a), the postmortem | — |
| **Execution** | **serial, at the host, on the integration branch** (§5.14) | concurrent lanes (§P2 5.4–5.9, 5.13) |
| Isolation interface, profiles, probe, worktrees | — | §P2 5.4–5.8 |
| Lane manifest, lane bookkeeping, claim audit, loop L, per-lane merge | — | §P2 3.4, 3.5, 7.3–7.5 |
| Wave-boundary issues, compaction survival, post-merge verification, promotion | §8, §11, §7.6, §7.10 | — |
| The `--auto` contract change | §12, §15.4 | — |
| The harvest (H1–H3) and the missing `docs/next/` entry | §0.4, §0.5, §10.5 | H4 (§10.5 step 4′, §15.5) |

**Phase 1 delivers no wall-clock speedup** (`08:118-119`). What the user asked for and does **not**
get in phase 1 is the last three of the four parallelism steps: split into worktrees, verify per
worktree, merge per worktree. The **first** step — deciding correctly which tasks may run in
parallel — ships in full, frozen, published and reviewed, because `08:112-115` records that as the
user's own stated priority. `08` §4 states the reduction plainly and this document does not soften it.

Everything deferred is **moved, not deleted**: **Part P2**, near the end of this document, carries
every deferred section verbatim under its original section number, so `§5.5` still means §5.5.

**2. `09.routing-design.md` is normative on routing** and is integrated here rather than restated:
§2.1a (position and classifier summary), §2.1 Phase 1.c′ (flow), §3.1 (artifacts), §4.1 / §4.4
(ledger and verbs), §10.1 / §10.6 (executable split and surface), §13 (gates) and §15 (requirements).
Read `09` for the disqualifier table, the route table and the rejected alternatives; this document
does not duplicate them.

## 0. Precondition — the deferred branch, resolved

Charter §7 opened an audit gate. **`06.deferred-multiroot-audit.md` now exists and has closed it**
(`06:5-6`, `06:235-236`). Its verdict is binding on this document and overrides three of the four
gated rulings the first revision of §0.4 recorded. §0.1–§0.3 below were this design's own read-only
reconnaissance; they are retained because `06` **confirmed** every load-bearing measurement in them,
and they are re-labelled as confirmed rather than as this design's own basis for a ruling. §0.4 is
now `06`'s rulings, not this design's recommendation.

### 0.1 Reconnaissance — performed for this design, confirmed by `06`

Read-only. No branch was checked out, merged, or modified.

| Measurement | Command | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Merge base with `main` | `git merge-base main work/kiwi-wave-master-multiroot-g0` | `21e0f5e` — *"chore: 패키지 버전을 2.3.1로 올린다"*, 2026-07-13 |
| Branch tip | `git log -1 work/kiwi-wave-master-multiroot-g0` | `a12fe15`, 2026-07-16 |
| Commits `main` has since the merge base | `git rev-list --count 21e0f5e..main` | **10** |
| Total delta vs `main` | `git diff --stat main work/...-g0` | 251 files, **+31,427 / −10,172** |
| `src/` delta | `git diff --stat main work/...-g0 -- src/` | 53 files, +15,996 / −1,214 |

**The 10,172 deletions are not deletions.** They are `main`-only additions post-dating the merge
base. Verified for three samples — `src/core/mutation/scaffold-step.ts`,
`src/core/query/vibe-gate.ts`, `test/skills/kiwi-tdd-content.test.ts`: absent at `21e0f5e`, absent
on the branch, present on `main`. So the branch is **not a superset of `main`**; it is an 18-day
fork that predates the whole of `v2.4.0` (tdd work-mode, `kiwi-tdd`, `kiwi-step` tooling),
`v2.4.1`, `v2.5.0` and `v2.5.1`. Adopting it as a foundation would revert verified requirements.

`06` re-measured the divergence independently and reports **26 behind, 2 ahead** with the same merge
base (`06:245-248`), and 25 conflicted files against 72 clean auto-merges from `git merge-tree`
(`06:250-252`). Nothing in §0.1 is contradicted.

### 0.2 Module roles — the charter's name-based guesses corrected, and confirmed by `06`

The charter's §7 table inferred roles from file names and flagged the inference as unverified. Four
of those inferences are wrong or too generous, which materially changes the duplication risk:

| Module | Charter's guess | What the source actually shows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `wave-file-allocation.ts` (438) | "file allocation — parallel write-conflict avoidance" | Spawns a **pinned PowerShell** (`spawn`, `PinnedPowerShell {path,dev,ino,size,digest}`, `comparableWindowsPath`), exports `WaveSparseProofSession`, halts through `WaveSafetyHaltError("trust", "WAVE_FILE_ALLOCATION_SAFETY", …)`. It is a Windows **sparse-checkout allocation proof** for multi-root hosting. It does **not** read a planner sidecar, `depends_on_task`, or `files[]`. It is not a task scheduler. **No standalone test file exists** — only `test/support/wave-file-allocation-test-support.ts` and a release-policy shim. |
| `wave-agent-instructions.ts` (568) | "candidate handoff generator" | Does not **author** anything. It **pins** already-written documents: `WaveAgentInstructionDocument {path, scope, gitBlobOid, sha256, byteLength, text}`, `WAVE_AGENT_INSTRUCTION_LIMITS {maxDocuments:32, maxDocumentBytes:256KB, maxTotalBytes:2MB, maxPathBytes:1024}`, strict UTF-8 decode, `trustedGitInvocation`. Genuinely useful as a **pinning primitive**; it is not a handoff author. Has a test. |
| `wave-product-committer.ts` (836) | "merge" | `commitValidatedWaveProductDelta({root, specRoot, expectedParent, …})` — a trusted-git commit of a validated delta with an expected-parent guard, `MAX_CHANGED_PATHS 1024`, byte caps. A real commit primitive, not a merge/conflict resolver. **No standalone test file.** |
| `wave-spec-client.ts` (824) | "candidate answer to C5 split-brain" | Spawns a **separate `speckiwi mcp` process per root** with `cwd: workspaceRoot` over `StdioClientTransport` (`docs/research/12` §6, `wave-spec-client.ts:791-794`). This is the multi-root thesis. Has a test. |

This design previously listed four modules as "genuinely overlapping, with tests, and correctly
named": `event-schema.ts`, `waves.ts`, `lease-lock.ts`, `trusted-git.ts`. **`06` refuted three of
those four**, and the refutations are load-bearing:

| Module | This design's earlier read | `06`'s finding |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `event-schema.ts` (257) | "the strongest harvest candidate" | `validateWaveEvent` hard-rejects any `schema_version !== "1.0.0"` (`event-schema.ts:199`) while mainline shipped `waves-event.md` **v1.3.0**, and requires `manifest_hash` on every event (`:207-209`) against a manifest model this design does not have. Porting it is a **contract regression** (`06:197-216`). |
| `waves.ts` (818) | co-candidate with `event-schema.ts` | Bound to the immutable run-manifest model and **not separable** (`06:113`, `06:229`). Out entirely. |
| `lease-lock.ts` (226) | "not gated, different mechanism" | Correct for the *lane claim*, but the module is the right answer to a need this design did not have: **one orchestrator run per repository** (`06:161`). Harvested — see §0.4. |
| `trusted-git.ts` (484) | harvest candidate for A3 | **Excluded** pending root-cause of Defect B: a guard misjudges on the caller's cwd, reproduced outside vitest against `dist`, with `dynamicCommandOverrides` spawning git with no `cwd` (`trusted-git.ts:402-406`, `06:318-331`). Every harvested component must drop its `runTrustedGit` dependency. |

### 0.3 Why "adopt as foundation" is off the table — confirmed, and strengthened

Three facts, all from committed content, none of which the audit can change:

1. **Security HOLD.** `docs/research/12:205`: production `manifest-child` execution fail-closes
   synchronously on `MANIFEST_CHILD_TRUST_SAFETY` (`manifest-child-runner.ts:93`) because a
   non-privileged Windows sandbox cannot enforce deny-read on the user's `auth.json`, and
   non-Windows has no approved credential boundary. **`--auto` cannot bypass it.** The branch's
   largest module (4,647 lines) cannot enter production as written.
2. **Four Requirement-ID collisions** against `main` requirements that shipped **verified** in
   `v2.4.0` — `FR-NODE-071`, `FR-FLOW-036`, `IR-CLI-071`, `FR-MCP-052` (`docs/research/12:214-221`).
   A merge produces four `SRS-E002`s and needs the full `repair requirement-id-collisions` workflow.
3. **The branch's thesis is superseded.** Its answer to the host-pinned MCP root is *per-root MCP
   server processes*. Report `02` §9.1 established a cheaper answer that needs no runtime work at
   all: **no parallel worker performs an SRS mutation** (charter C1). Once SRS mutation is hoisted
   to the host, the split-brain is irrelevant, and `wave-spec-client` / `wave-root-connector` /
   `manifest-child-runner` solve a problem this design does not have.

`06:226-236` closes A1–A3 and adds two components this design did not have. The table below is that
ruling, with the consequence for this document named on each row. **`deferred-branch-audit-pending`
is closed and has been deleted from the phase flow and from `critical_gates[]`** — see §0.6.

| `06` gate | Ruling | Effect in this document |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A1** — handoff pinning vs `wave-agent-instructions.ts` | **Harvest (H1).** | `freeze.ts` gains a **real** `git_blob_oid`. The earlier "records the blob-OID field as `null`" sentence is deleted. Take the pinning half only — `readBlob` / topology hash / `assertPinned*` (`wave-agent-instructions.ts:409-410`, `:517`, `:548`); **drop** the `AGENTS.md` discovery half and **drop** the `runTrustedGit` dependency in favour of plain `git cat-file` / `ls-tree` with an explicit `cwd` (`06:160`, `06:330`). |
| **A2** — `waves-journal.ts` vs `event-schema.ts` + `waves.ts` | **Do not port. Write fresh** against mainline `waves-event.md` v1.3.0, carrying the transition table only. | The earlier "default action is **port**" ruling is **reversed** (`06:200`: *"That default is wrong and is hereby overridden"*). What crosses is ~20 lines: the transition table `pending → in_progress → {complete, failed}` with both terminal states closed (`event-schema.ts:232-237`) and the cross-transition invariants at `:245-255`. `waves.ts` is out entirely. |
| **A3** — `integrate()` vs `wave-product-committer.ts` / `trusted-git.ts` | **Harvest the CAS technique (H4); reject both modules.** | `integrate()` stays `git merge --no-ff` at the host root **plus** an expected-parent CAS guard on the ref update — `update-ref <branch> <new> <expectedParent>` (`wave-product-committer.ts:759`) with post-CAS verification of HEAD / parent / tree / path-set (`:771-791`). Both module names are removed from the candidate list. §5.9 is authored **inside** that shape (RV-15). |

As written every lock path derives from a `ProjectRoot`, so N worktrees over one shared `.git` get N locks and **no mutual exclusion** (`06:161`). See §5.2 and §10.1. |

The transport around H3 is **not** harvested: `wave-spec-client.ts` answers C5 by spawning a per-root
`speckiwi mcp` process from Node (`:800-805`), which is unavailable to a skill, made moot by charter
C1, and already recorded as prohibited at `02:402` (`06:166-195`).

### 0.5 Pre-trust verification — a precondition on the harvest, not a wish

`06:373-384` lists what must be proven before **any** harvested line is trusted. This is carried into
the work order as step 4′ (§10.5) and into `§15.1` as its own executable requirement rows, because it
is per-harvest rather than per-module:

1. **H1** — reproduce the pinning suite with `runTrustedGit` replaced by plain `git` with an explicit
   `cwd`; re-run all 15 attack cases (`wave-agent-instructions.test.ts:161-293`); **add** a case where
   the pinned document is the orchestrator's own handoff rather than `AGENTS.md`.
2. **H2** — a **real cross-process** test (two Node processes, not two promises — the branch's 64-way
   race is 64 promises in one process, `06:357-359`); plus a test proving the common-dir-keyed lock
   excludes two worktrees over one `.git`.
3. **H3** — re-run all 15 readiness cases against a snapshot obtained from `speckiwi list --json`
   rather than MCP, to prove transport independence (`06:162`, `06:378`).
4. **H4** — a test where a second writer moves the branch between build and publish, asserting the
   CAS rejects rather than overwrites.
5. **All** — run under a **non-trivial global gitconfig** (filters + `autocrlf` set) and from **at
   least three different process working directories** (`06:383-384`, and §5.1's Defect B is exactly
   why).

And the TDD rule the audit states explicitly (`06:386-388`): *no harvested code enters the design
without its own test, authored test-first, under a `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` requirement. Porting a passing test
alongside ported code does not satisfy the TDD rule when the port changes the code's dependencies,
which H1 and H2 both do.*

### 0.6 Out of scope, recorded so it is neither folded in nor lost

- **H5 — the `safe-path.ts` TOCTOU fix — is not part of this design.** `06:164` and `06:479-480`
  rule that it lands as a **standalone security fix on `feat/2.3.0.1`, under its own requirement**,
  independent of the orchestrator, *"and should not wait for it"*. It is deliberately absent from
  §15. A later session must not fold it into a `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` wave.
- **`deferred-branch-audit-pending` is deleted, not satisfied.** It was a *build-time* precondition
  on this repository's implementation order, wrongly shipped as a *runtime* preflight gate in the
  product's `critical_gates[]`. In any consumer repository it would be either permanently
  unsatisfiable (halting every run) or permanently vacuous — dead prose inside a closed gate enum
  that the FR-FLOW-061 parity tests then assert across three variants. The A1–A3 sequencing now lives
  in §10.5's work order as an ordinary build precondition, which is where a build precondition
  belongs, and it is already satisfied.
- **`06`'s branch-disposition items 4 and 5** (`06:481-485`) are explicitly *"user decisions … not
  executed by this audit"*: tag the branch tip `archive/kiwi-wave-master-multiroot-g0` at `a12fe15`
  and delete the working branch **after H1–H5 land**, and write the missing `docs/next/` entry. They
  are sequenced in §10.5 as post-harvest user decisions, not as design work — the ordering constraint
  (branch deletion gated on harvest completion) is real.

### 0.7 Charter escalations — four open user rulings, and two now closed

Six findings showed the **charter or a binding decision**, not this design, was the problem. **Two
of the six are closed by rulings that landed after revision 3**, and their entries below are marked
rather than deleted, because a later session must be able to see what was decided and on what basis:

| Ruling | Status after `08` | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **X-01** — charter C3 forbids the multi-stage lane model | **CLOSED by charter C3 (`00.charter.md:145-160`).** The charter now carries the narrowed form this entry proposed, and `:155-160` records the over-stated wording as withdrawn. The *lane apparatus* it governed is still deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` by `08:128-129`, but no user ruling remains outstanding. Phase 1 uses no worktree, so C3 binds nothing it does. Entry retained below, marked | §0.7 X-01 (retained, **closed**), §P2 5.5 |
| **X-03** — `07`'s tie rung reinstates a ladder the charter deleted | **DECIDED, option (b): drop the rung.** `08` §2, 5-0: a gate with no majority escalates to `critical` and halts for the user; `07` Q2 was **re-decided** and stays `leave-all-three`; `07` §3a and §4.1's two conditions travel verbatim into the requirement text. Consequences: A7 is **DECLINED**, `AC-E20-4` is the shipped tie criterion, and `decideAutoGate` keeps its `tieRung: boolean` parameter so a reversal stays additive | §0.7 X-03 (marked), §12, §15.4 A7, §15.1 E20 |
| **X-02**, **X-04**, **X-05**, **X-06** | **Open.** Each states the narrowest change, what this document does meanwhile, and what dies on a refusal | below |

#### X-01 — Charter C3 as written forbids the multi-stage lane model `[CRITICAL]` — **CLOSED by charter C3 (`00.charter.md:145-160`); apparatus still phase 2**

> **Not a phase-1 blocker.** `08:128-129` retires this ruling from `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`
> along with the lane apparatus it governs. Phase 1 creates no worktree and dispatches no lane, so
> nothing in it depends on whether a worktree agent can be moved to a later committed sha. The whole
> entry is retained below because it is the record of how the constraint came to be narrowed; the
> charter has since adopted the narrowed form verbatim (`00.charter.md:145-149`, correction recorded
> at `:155-160`), so nothing here awaits a user ruling. Every reference below to
> §5.5, §5.6, §5.10's C3 row and probe assertion 2 now points into **Part P2**.

**The conflict.** C3 **as it stood when this entry was written** stated flatly: *"Worktree agents
cannot be chained. … Any design that feeds one worktree agent's output to another worktree agent is
broken."* The charter no longer contains that sentence except as a retraction — current C3 is
`00.charter.md:145-149`, and `:155-160` records the correction. (`00.charter.md:121-123` is now the
§5 research table.) `02:189`
concludes the same in the same words. But this design's stage-2 lanes start from an integration head
that already contains stage-1's merges (§2 Phase 3.g, §5.3's stage layering, §6.1 `base_sha`), and
§6.3's worked example instructs a lane *"Lane 2 … is extending `normalizeTasks`; that change is
already merged into your base commit"*. Either the design is illegal under a binding constraint, or
C3 is over-stated. §5.5 previously narrowed C3 to *"does not mean the lane is stuck there"* without
recording an amendment — that was a silent redefinition and is withdrawn. **Resolved:** the charter
adopted the narrowed reading, so this design's narrowed reading is no longer a divergence.

**Narrowest amendment proposed to the user.** Replace C3 with:

> **C3 — A worktree is created at session-start HEAD, and its *default* HEAD sees neither
> uncommitted work nor later session commits. A worktree agent MAY be moved to a later **committed**
> sha by an explicit `git checkout`, because worktrees share the object database. A design that
> relies on the *default* HEAD to carry a predecessor's output is broken.**

**Evidence the narrowed form is true.** `02:185` — not `:186`, which is blank — computed
`git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD 031c4ec` **from inside** the worktree and got
`HEAD_IS_ANCESTOR_OF_031c4ec`. That comparison requires `031c4ec` to *resolve* inside the worktree,
which it can only do because the worktree shares the repository's object database. That measurement
is the same one C3 is written from; on the object-database axis it establishes reachability, not
unreachability.

**The counter-evidence, quoted rather than omitted.** The two sentences `02` draws from that same
measurement are the strongest recorded statements against the amendment, and the user must see them:

> `02:187` — *"A worktree agent does not see uncommitted work **and does not see work committed
> during the session either**."*
>
> `02:189` — *"Practical consequence for an orchestrator: **you cannot chain worktree agents.** Task
> B spawned after Task A merged will not see A's work. Any sequential dependency must be handled by
> the orchestrator passing content explicitly, or by not using worktree isolation for dependent
> tasks."*

**How this design reads them, stated so the disagreement is visible rather than buried.** Both
sentences are about the worktree's **default HEAD** — what the agent lands on when it does nothing.
Neither measures whether an explicit `git checkout <sha>` inside the worktree succeeds; `02` never
ran that command. The amendment turns entirely on that untested gap, which is why probe assertion 2
(§5.5) is rewritten to run exactly it, and why the honest statement is *"this design believes `02:189`
over-generalises a default-HEAD measurement, and has built the experiment that decides it."* If
probe assertion 2 fails, `02:189` is right as written and multi-stage Profile A is dead regardless of
how the user rules. **Note also that `02:189`'s own escape hatch — *"the orchestrator passing content
explicitly"* — is what Phase 3.f′ does:** the predecessor's output is passed as a **commit**, not as a
worktree default HEAD.

**What this document does meanwhile.** It proceeds on the narrowed reading and says so at every point
that depends on it (§5.5, §5.6, §5.10 C3 row, probe assertion 2). Probe assertion 2 is rewritten
(RV-23) so that it **actually exercises the disputed case** — a sha created *after* the worktree
existed — rather than a sha that necessarily predates the probe.

**If the user declines the amendment — and the consequence is larger than revision 2 stated.**
Revision 2 wrote *"multi-stage Profile A is dead … Profile A's parallelism is confined to a single
stage per wave."* That understated it in the user's favour, and the understatement is corrected here
because the user rules from this paragraph. Under C3 as written, a worktree agent can reach **no**
commit made after session start — not merely a predecessor lane's. Every artifact a lane must read
is authored and committed **during** the run: the wave design and plan at 3.d, and the lane's **own
handoff** at 3.f′ (§2.1, §5.5). So under a refusal a **stage-1** lane cannot read its own handoff
either; its first tool call is an ENOENT and its second act (`git rev-parse HEAD == base_sha`) aborts
with `status=dead`. **The honest consequence of a refusal is that Profile A is dead entirely, not
halved**, and every stage of every wave falls back to Profile B — which is what §5.10's C3 row has
said all along (*"Profile B … is the fallback if the ruling goes the other way"*). §5.5's *"what
assertion 2 decides"* is restated on the same basis. The execution path is safe either way, because
§5.8 rule 3 requires all eight probe assertions to pass before Profile A is selected; what was wrong
was the escalation text, which is the artifact the user acts on.

#### X-02 — the commit subjects this design mandates carry markers the charter forbids `[HIGH]`

**The conflict.** `00.charter.md:302`: *"Commit messages carry no signature of any kind, and no phase
or step markers in the title."* This design previously mandated `orch({run_id}): integrate wave-{n}
lane-{k}` and `orch({run_id}): wave-{n} dispatch base`. `wave-{n}` / `lane-{k}` are step markers in a
title. The violation is load-bearing rather than cosmetic, because §4.4's `commit-dispatch-base`
recovery rule *inspects `git log` for that subject key* — deleting the marker would delete the
recovery mechanism.

**Resolution adopted here, which does not weaken `:302`.** Move the run coordinates **out of the
subject and into git trailers**, and re-point every inspection command at the trailer key. This is
already applied throughout §4.4, §4.5, §5.9 and §13:

```
Subject:  integrate a lane's verified changes          <- no run id, no wave, no lane, no phase
Trailers: Orch-Run: {run_id}
          Orch-Wave: {n}
          Orch-Stage: {s}
          Orch-Lane: {k}
          Orch-Verb: integrate-lane | commit-dispatch-base
```

Inspection becomes `git log --format='%H%x00%(trailers:key=Orch-Verb,valueonly)%x00%(trailers:key=Orch-Run,valueonly)'`
filtered on the pair, which is a strictly stronger key than a subject substring: trailers are
structured, and a subject is free text a human may reword.

**Still a user ruling.** If the user rules that machine-generated run markers are exempt from `:302`,
the subject form may return and the trailers become redundant. This document does not assume that
exemption, and does not resolve the conflict by weakening `:302`.

#### X-03 — `07`'s tie rung reinstates a ladder the charter deleted, and `A1` states both halves as one requirement `[HIGH]` — **DECIDED by `08` §2**

> **Ruling: option (b), drop the rung — 5-0** (`08:46-77`). A gate whose committee produces no
> majority escalates to `critical` and **halts for the user**. No charter amendment is proposed; the
> charter's *"the unanimity requirement and the 3 → 5 escalation ladder are removed"* is honoured
> literally.
>
> **`07` Q2 was re-decided, not merely carried forward.** `07` §3b conditioned Q2 on the rung
> existing, and that condition fails on its literal wording. The committee re-decided it anyway and
> Q2 **stays `leave-all-three`**: halting for the user removes §3b's named unacceptable outcome —
> one-member-alone settlement — *more* completely than the rung did, while promoting the three gates
> to `critical_gates[]` would halt on **every** invocation including a unanimous 3-0, which is a
> larger deviation from the charter than the rung was (`08:54-64`).
>
> **Two conditions travel with the decision and must be carried verbatim into the SRS** — they, not
> `critical_gates[]` membership, are the real boundary on the affected population (`08:66-73`,
> citing `07:88-92`): (i) `07` §3a — under a split vote *"the confidence"* is the **minimum within the
> winning bloc**, after the §4.1.1 adjustments; (ii) `07` §4.1 — §4.1.2's confidence-spread rung folds
> into §4.1.1's threshold mechanism, with **no re-vote on spread alone**.
>
> **Consequences, applied throughout this revision** (`08:75-77`): §15.4's `A7` becomes **DECLINED**
> rather than PENDING; `AC-E20-4` is the shipped tie criterion and there is no `AC-A7-1`; and
> `decideAutoGate` still takes a `tieRung: boolean` parameter so that a later reversal stays additive
> rather than structural.
>
> **Why the two `08` decisions reinforce each other**, recorded because it is the load-bearing part of
> the reasoning: the tie rung's only strong practical argument was that halting mid-run strands live
> background lanes. Under a serial scope there are no live lanes at a gate — a halt costs one prompt
> in a session the user is already attending, and the working tree is intact (`08:132-141`).
>
> The original escalation is retained below, unedited, so the ruling can be read against what was put
> to the user.

**The conflict.** `00.charter.md:35`: *"The unanimity requirement and the 3 → 5 escalation ladder are
**removed**."* `07:34` (Q3, decided **3-2 plurality** — the only non-unanimous decision in that
document) and this design's §12 reinstate *"add two members **once** and re-vote"* — a 3→5 rung on a
different trigger. The old §15.4 `A1` then wrote both halves into one requirement sentence
(*"the 3→5 / 5→7 ladder are removed; a tie escalates once by two members"*), which is
self-contradictory on its face and cannot be gated per-AC.

**They are a package and cannot be decided independently.** `07:77-78` (§3b) makes Q2's unanimous
*"leave all three gates to the committee"* **conditional on the tie rung existing**, and one member
stated their vote flips to `promote-all-three` without it. So dropping the rung re-opens Q2.

**The two options, stated for the user.**

- **(a) Amend charter §2** to name the tie rung as an explicit exception: *"a single two-member
  escalation on absence of a majority is not the removed ladder, because its trigger is
  irresolvability rather than non-unanimity."* Q2 stands as decided. Nothing else moves.
- **(b) Drop the rung.** With 3 members and a binary option set a tie is impossible; the rung only
  bites for ≥3 options and for the degraded-quorum case (`07:173-179`), both of which can escalate to
  `critical` instead. This is a charter-conformant answer that costs no amendment — but `07` Q2 must
  then be re-opened, because its stated condition fails.

**What this document does meanwhile.** §12 and §15.4 are written so the two halves are **separable**:
`A1` now states only the majority rule and the ladder removal, and the tie rung is a **separate
requirement `A7`, marked `PENDING X-03`** and not authored until the user rules. No requirement in
§15.4 states a contradiction, whichever way the ruling goes.

#### X-04 — the charter's no-duplication requirement has no computable *preventive* half `[HIGH]`

**The conflict, and it is a genuine impossibility rather than an oversight.** `00.charter.md:100`
requires *"Do not introduce code duplication or architectural technical debt."* Revision 1 discharged
the preventive half with a `shared-substrate` conflict edge quantified over *"a symbol appearing in
≥2 lanes' `read_set`"* (old §5.2). Three of that predicate's inputs do not exist where it ran:

- `analyzeConflicts` runs at Phase 3.e and its output is consumed to **compute** lanes (§5.3), so
  lanes do not exist when a lane-quantified edge is evaluated — the rule was circular;
- `read_set` is a **handoff** field authored at 3.f, one phase later, and the planner sidecar's
  `Task` interface carries no read set at all (verified: `id`, `phase_id`, `title`, `type`,
  `req_ids`, `files`, `action`, `acceptance_tests`, `verification_cmd`, `dod`, `rollback`,
  `trace_links`, `tdd`, `test_files`, `depends_on_task`, `covers_ac` —
  `skills/claude/kiwi-planner/SKILL.md:741-759`);
- **nothing in the sidecar carries symbols.** `files[]` is `Array<{path, line_range?}>`
  (`kiwi-planner:744`). A symbol-level predicate has no input on any surface this design reads.

So the preventive half as revision 1 wrote it could never fire, and E8's revision-1 strengthening
(*"every `conflict_reason` value is **reachable**"*) was unsatisfiable as authored.

**The two options, stated for the user.**

- **(a) Extend the planner sidecar with an additive `reads[]: Array<{path}>` field**, authored by
  `/kiwi-planner` alongside `files[]`. That makes a *path-level* shared-substrate predicate
  computable at 3.e, before lanes exist, and restores a genuine pre-lane hoist. Cost: a schema change
  to a skill this design does not own, on top of the seven flag changes it already imposes across both
  targets — five of them in phase 1 (§10.5 step 9; registered at §15.3
  S11–S13, S16), and it is a **new authoring obligation on every plan**, not just orchestrator runs.
- **(b) Accept that there is no *preventive* half for duplication at all, and ship the *coupling*
  check instead.** This document implements (b) meanwhile — but revision 3 had to correct what (b)
  actually delivers, because revision 2's version of it could not fire.

**Revision 2's (b) was itself vacuous, and saying so is the point.** Revision 2 replaced the
symbol-level edge with a 3.f″ *shared-substrate hoist* whose predicate was *"a **path** in the
`read_set` of ≥ 2 lanes of this stage, in the `write_set` of **no** lane, and absent from
`existing_paths`."* Two independent reasons it can never yield a hit:

1. §6.2 layer 4 (`handoff-unresolvable-reference`, C, evaluated at **3.f** — one step before 3.f″)
   requires every referenced path to *"exist at the dispatch base or be a `write_set` member."* A
   `read_set` entry that is absent from `existing_paths` and in no `write_set` is **exactly** what
   layer 4 refuses. Every handoff pair that would trigger the hoist is rejected first, under a gate
   that names the wrong defect.
2. Even setting layer 4 aside, the case it claimed to catch — *"a shared helper that two lanes each
   intend to create"* — cannot present in that shape. A lane that intends to create a file carries
   it in `write_set`; §6.2 layer 2 requires `write_set` to **equal** the lane's computed write set;
   so two lanes declaring the same new file intersect and `write-set-overlap` (§5.2) has **already**
   forced them into one lane at 3.e. The path-level preventive half is therefore not merely weak — it
   is **wholly subsumed by `write-set-overlap`** and adds nothing.

**What ships as (b), stated as what it is.** The 3.f″ step survives with the **coupling** half only,
which is computable and is genuinely not covered elsewhere: *a path in one lane's `write_set` and
another lane's `read_set`, within one stage.* That is the semantic-coupling predictor §7.5 needs, it
uses only fields that exist at 3.f″, and `write-set-overlap` does not see it (it is write∩write).
The *shared-substrate hoist* and its gate `shared-substrate-unhoisted` are **withdrawn** (§7.9 (a),
§16 R39). Nothing implements code at the host outside a plan Task any more, which was a second defect
the hoist carried (§16 R40).

**What dies if neither is granted, restated honestly.** The *preventive* half of `00.charter.md:100`
is **absent**, not weak: at file level `write-set-overlap` already covers everything a declared
surface can express, and at symbol level nothing on any surface this design reads carries a symbol.
What remains is detection: the 3.f″ coupling check forces the coupled pair same-lane for the rest of
the wave and the *next* wave (§5.3's `learned-coupling`), and the post-merge duplication audit
(§7.9 (b)) still runs, still produces an artifact and still fails a gate. Two lanes that each invent
the same helper the plan never named are detected **after** both have written it. That is a real
regression against *"do not introduce"*, and revision 3's statement of it is narrower than revision
2's.

#### X-05 — under `--auto`, the charter's only named user interaction is answered by a committee `[HIGH]`

**The conflict.** `00.charter.md:54` names the user exactly once in the intake section: *"ask the
user questions where the design is still weak."* §7.2 lets `--auto` route those questions to a
3-member committee instead. Revision 2 stopped calling this a *"user-accepted degradation"* (nobody
consented) and paid for it with three audit records — but it recorded the result in §17.17's
divergence register rather than escalating it, while four conflicts of equal or lesser weight became
X-01…X-04. `00.charter.md:10` makes the charter win where this document disagrees with it, so a
register entry is a *record*, not a *disposition*. The asymmetry is sharper than it looks: §8
requires user consent **even under `--auto`** for the strictly smaller act of deferring one
wave-boundary issue (`kiwi-wave-master:205`), so the design applied a consent rule to deferring an
issue while self-granting an exemption for skipping the charter's only named user interaction.

**The two options, stated for the user.**

- **(a) Amend charter §3's intake sentence** to read *"…ask the user questions where the design is
  still weak; under `--auto` these may be answered by the committee, recorded per decision."* One
  clause, and the design ships unchanged.
- **(b) Make intake QnA a `critical_gates[]` member**, so `--auto` halts on every `needs-decision`
  row. Charter-conformant with no amendment, and it costs the mode: a thin intent produces many
  `needs-decision` rows by construction, so `--auto` never survives Phase 1 for the class of work the
  charter is about (§16 R28).

**What this document does meanwhile.** (a)'s behaviour, with (b)'s evidence: §7.2's three audit
records — the Phase 0 notice plus the Phase 1.c line, `00.run-contract.md`'s `intake_autonomy` block,
and one journal `decision` line per committee-decided row — stay mandatory whichever way the user
rules, because under (a) they are the record the amendment relies on and under (b) they are dead
code that costs nothing.

**If the user declines both.** There is no third position: either the charter sentence admits a
committee or it does not. Declining (a) *is* choosing (b), and §7.2's paragraph plus R28 are
re-decided together. Revision 3 wrote that this ruling **also moves with X-03**, because `07:77-78`
conditioned Q2 on the tie rung and §7.2's degradation is the largest consumer of the committee path
(§12). **That coupling is discharged, not live:** `08` §2 dropped the rung *and* re-decided Q2 in the
same ruling, and Q2 stays `leave-all-three` (`08:54-64`), so the condition that would have moved this
ruling was discharged rather than triggered. **X-05 is unaffected by `08` §2 and stays open** (§7.2,
§21.3 RA-08, §21.4).

#### X-06 — the charter's front-loaded-SRS compaction defence is not implemented as written `[HIGH]`

**The conflict.** `00.charter.md:96-98` says *"The defence is front-loaded: **author the SRS carefully
at the start** and write enough guardrails that a resumed session cannot wander off course."* §2
Phase 3.b (`:704`) makes SRS registration per wave and `:786-787` states *"Targets are not registered
up front"*; `:549-550` and `:956` make 3.b the run's first SRS mutation. `00.charter.md:10` makes the
charter win where this document disagrees with it, so §17.17's entry is a record, not a disposition.

**The two options.** **(a)** Amend the charter sentence to *"The defence is front-loaded: front-load
the frozen design document and the run contract, author each wave's SRS at 3.b before that wave's
plan and before any implementation, and write enough guardrails that a resumed session cannot wander
off course."* One sentence; the design ships unchanged. **(b)** Register the target and author a
run-level SRS before Phase 2. Charter-conformant with no amendment, and it costs `09` §7.4's only
legal route downgrade at 2.e, whose whole justification is that nothing in `docs/spec/` has been
mutated yet (`09:129`, `09:732-733`, `09:1087`, `05:695-698`), plus the 3.b allocation model that
charter C2 and §5.11 are built on.

**What this document does meanwhile.** (a)'s behaviour: SRS stays at 3.b, and the compaction defence
is `08:100-102`'s package (journal, resume card with invariant digest, run contract, write-ahead /
write-behind, validate-on-every-append, refuse-on-resume) plus the frozen design document — all of
which ship whichever way the user rules.

**If the user declines both.** There is no third position: either the SRS is front-loaded or it is
not. Declining (a) *is* choosing (b), and §2 Phase 2.e, §5.11 and `09` §7.4 are re-decided together.

---

## 1. Scope, and the one rule that decides where everything goes

### 1.1 The boundary rule

> **If a claim can be checked against bytes on disk, it is a function in `src/` with a test.
> If it requires reading intent, it stays in the skill prose and the tool checks only that the
> judgment was recorded.**

This is the organising rule of the whole design. It is why the executable surface (§10) is exactly
what it is, and it is the honest answer to "a SKILL.md is not executable code" — the response is not
to test prose harder, it is to move the checkable half out of prose.

### 1.2 What `kiwi-orchestrator` owns

Seven verbs and nothing else: **intake** (QnA / document / issue → an implementable design), **route**
(work → one of three execution shells, `09` and §2.1a), **decompose** (design → waves), **schedule**
(wave → the frozen partition), **handoff** (unit of the partition → an English document), **execute**
(handoff → committed on the integration branch), **verify** (per wave post-merge, per run).

That is one verb more than revision 3 named, and the change is worth stating rather than hiding.
**`route` is new in revision 4** and is the charter's *"routing by scale and scope"*
(`00.charter.md:69-73`), which `09` designs. **`dispatch + integrate` is gone from phase 1**: with no
lane to dispatch and no lane branch to merge, `execute` is one verb that runs the work at the host on
the integration branch (§5.14). Both halves return in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` (§P2 5.4–5.9).

**`schedule` still ships, and it is not a smaller verb than it was.** It produces the frozen,
published, reviewed partition artifact — lanes, stages, conflict edges, coupling and the postmortem
(§5.1–§5.3). What changed is what consumes its output: in phase 1 the executor walks it serially.

Everything else is delegated by name, per D2: SRS authoring `/kiwi-srs`; deep research
`/kiwi-srs-research`; feasibility `/kiwi-srs-feasibility`; planning `/kiwi-planner`; task execution
`/kiwi-pm` → `/kiwi-coder`; remediation `/kiwi-review-fix-loop`; commit/PR `kiwi-commit-auto-pr`.
The orchestrator introduces **no fixer of its own**, reusing wave-master's stated rule
(`skills/claude/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md:364`).

### 1.3 Relationship to `kiwi-wave-master`

Per D1 they coexist over a shared engine. `kiwi-wave-master` stays the **serial, document-driven,
no-merge** orchestrator. In `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`, `kiwi-orchestrator` adds design
authoring, routing, the published parallelisability analysis, English handoffs, execution on its own
integration branch, and post-merge verification. **Task-level parallelism inside a wave is what
`2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` adds** (`08` §3).

**The overlap is therefore larger in phase 1 than revision 3 stated, and D1 is what keeps it from
being duplication.** Both skills now execute serially over a wave decomposition. What still separates
them: the orchestrator authors and freezes a design before decomposing, routes by scale and scope,
publishes a partition artifact, writes English handoffs, owns an integration branch, and verifies
post-merge against a frozen denominator. Under D1 the parts they share are one implementation in
`_shared/kiwi/` with the artifact root as a parameter (§9.1), not two.

**`kiwi-orchestrator` never invokes `kiwi-wave-master`** — `09` §6 settles this on four grounds
(input contract, run-scoped pins, journal ownership, and the fact that after D1 invoking the sibling
is the duplicated path). `R-ORCH` drives the shared engine directly. A user who names
`kiwi-wave-master` is not intercepted (§2.1a).

**`wt-delegation-refused` is not inherited, and the skill must say why in its own §0.**
`kiwi-wave-master:144` refuses `--wt` because *per-wave* worktrees break wave accumulation — each
wave founds the next. **In phase 1 there is no per-wave worktree to break accumulation.** §5.14
executes every unit of the wave at the host root on the integration branch — no worktree, no lane
branch, no merge — and each stage's units are executed and verified in stage and lane-id order before
that wave's `complete` line is written (§2.1: every stage's 3.f–3.g completes, then 3.k–3.n, and only
then 3.o). **Superseded by `08`'s scope cut:** the per-lane form of this argument — isolation at task
granularity, with every lane merged and validated inside the wave before the `complete` line — is the
phase-2 form and re-enters with the lanes (§P2 5.5/5.9; §2.1's relocation table, 3.i–3.j′).
The orchestrator likewise never
passes `--wt` to a delegated `kiwi-pipeline` — a cycle-scoped worktree nested inside a lane-scoped
one is not a topology this design supports, and that refusal *is* inherited.

### 1.4 The refusal floor

Evaluated at the **end of Phase 2**, not at Phase 3.e, so it can still save the expensive part.

**In phase 1 the refusal floor is no longer about concurrency, because there is none to disable.**
Revision 3's floor degraded a small run to `--serial`, which was *"Profile B with one lane per
stage"*; under `08` every run is serial and that degradation is the default path. What survives is
the *routing* question the floor was really asking — *is this work worth the wave apparatus?* — and
`09` §7.4 attaches it here:

- **Gate `route-downgrade-available`** (`business-decision`, **not** critical) fires at the end of
  Phase 2, alongside this evaluation, when all four hold: the decomposition returned `N == 1`; the
  wave's `design_items` count is at or below the step threshold recorded in the route probe; no
  disqualifier removed `R-STEP` at 1.c′; and no SRS mutation on this run has moved a requirement's
  `status` or `stability` (§7.5's second disjunct, not its first — §2.3 puts a commit on the
  integration branch on every run). Options: `continue-orchestrated` (**recommended**) and
  `downgrade-to-step`. It is legal at exactly this point and nowhere later: on a run that reached
  `R-ORCH` at 1.c′, Phase 3.b is the run's first SRS mutation — nothing has been registered, so
  nothing must be retracted — while a run that arrived by `09` §7.3 E2 (or §7.2 E1(b)) may already
  carry `status` mutations from its `R-PLAN` child, which the fourth condition excludes. After 3.b,
  `route-deescalation-refused` (C) applies. The marker sits on `continue-orchestrated` so `--auto`
  never silently downgrades an unattended run; the cheap path stays a deliberate human choice.
- If the run continues orchestrated, it keeps intake, design authoring, the wave design, the
  published partition artifact, the handoff documents, serial execution and post-merge verification.
  Nothing is dropped for being small.
- The orchestrator refuses outright only when there is no design and no ingestible input:
  `decomposition-input-missing`, inherited verbatim (`kiwi-wave-master:69`).

**What the floor bought in revision 3 and no longer needs to buy.** Its argument was that lane
isolation must survive the degradation, because the claim audit is the only mechanical enforcement
charter C1 and C2 have at execution time. Under a serial phase 1 that argument is moot in one
direction and open in the other: there is no parallel task for C1 to constrain, and C2's
host-allocation invariant is enforced at 3.c′ by `unallocated-req-id` (§5.11) rather than by an audit
after the fact. The audit returns with the lanes (§P2 7.4).

---

## 2. Phase flow, with every gate named

Severity legend: **C** = declared in `critical_gates[]`, halts regardless of `--auto`;
**H** = halt, but `--auto` may resolve it through the committee; **W** = warning, recorded, run
continues.

**One gate is loop-scoped rather than step-scoped and so is named here rather than in the flow:**
`verification-oscillation` (C) fires in **any** loop — D, W, H, P, F — when the same `finding_id`
opens and closes across ≥2 rounds, or the same hunk is reverted and re-applied (§7.8).

**A gate with a stated recovery is *raised* only when that recovery is exhausted or unavailable —
the recovery is never an alternative to the gate.** This sentence exists because revision 1 left five
gates reading as both, and `critical_gates[]` membership is a shipped contract that FR-FLOW-061
parity tests assert across three variants, so the ambiguity changed what ships. The seven, restated at
their own sections and here as one rule:

| Gate | Recovery that runs first | The gate fires when | Phase |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `handoff-verify-failed` | re-author the handoff for the failing unit; loop H re-runs (§6.4) | the loop-H cap is exhausted with the executability probe still mismatched. **Revision 3's recovery — *"demote the lane to the serial epilogue"* — is gone with the lanes**: in phase 1 every unit already runs serially, so there is nowhere to demote to, and the gate is reached one recovery earlier than it was | 1 |
| `lane-design-refuted` | **none — this gate is raised first.** §8.1's mid-wave amendment (bounded at 2 per wave) is the sanctioned route *out of* the halt, not a recovery that runs before it; and the wave's third refutation is `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` (§8.1), not this gate | the executing unit reports the refutation and stops — phase 1 at **3.g** and **3.k activity (0)**, phase 2 at 3.h (§8.1, §13) | 1 |
| `serial-unit-failed` | re-run the unit once against the same handoff, **for the `verification_cmd` disjunct only** (§5.14) | the retried `verification_cmd` still exits non-zero; or the unit produced no commit and declared no `intentionally_empty` reason in its `/kiwi-pm` run's `docs/analysis/` bundle (no retry); or the unit's child returns `NEEDS_USER` / `FAILED` (no retry) | 1 |
| `partition-review-unrecorded` | **none — this gate is raised first, and it is raised on every wave.** The review is the user's, at the gate; there is no unattended discharge, so `--auto` halts here once per wave by design (§2.1 3.e′, §17.20). A `revise` verdict is not a recovery *before* the gate — the gate fires, the user answers, and a re-planned partition is re-frozen and re-reviewed | no `review-partition` result line for this wave whose `partition_review.lane_plan_digest` equals the `lane_plan.digest` frozen at **3.e′**, or whose `verdict` is not `pass` (§7.9 a) | 1 |
| `lane-verify-failed` | demote the lane to the serial epilogue (§P2 7.3) | the loop-L cap is exhausted with CRITICAL/HIGH open **and** demotion is illegal (§P2 7.3) — the conjunct §13's `handoff-verify-failed` row defers to this gate in phase 2 | **2** |
| `merge-coupling-detected` | reset, replay, re-plan the coupled pair into the epilogue (§P2 7.5) | the same stage has already collapsed twice, or demotion is impossible | **2** |
| `partition-quality-insufficient` | run the stage serially in-tree (§P2 7.5) | a third collapse in one stage — this gate **is** the terminal, and the serial re-run is what it triggers *after* the user is told | **2** |

The legend's *"halts regardless of `--auto`"* is unchanged for every one of them: when the gate is
raised, the run stops for the user. What changed is that the document now says, per gate, what runs
before it is raised — so an implementer building `critical_gates[]` and an implementer building the
recovery path build the same thing.

### 2.0 Two words, two meanings — fixed here and used everywhere below

The first revision of this document used **stage** for two incompatible things, and named gates after
both. That is repaired by fixing the vocabulary once:

| Word | Meaning, fixed | Why this one wins |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **stage** | a **topological layer of lanes inside one wave** — the §5.3 meaning | It is the meaning the executable `computeLanePlan` emits, so it is the one a test can pin. |
| **wave** | the unit of Phase 3, registered in `waves.lock.json`, one `complete` line each | It is the unit `waves.jsonl` and `kiwi-wave-master` already use. |

Consequences, applied throughout: §8's gates are `wave-issues-open` and
`design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary`; the CLI verb is `orchestrate wave close --wave N`; the
resume card's `stage` field is the **structured** `{"wave": n, "stage": k}`, not a free string;
`lanes.lock.json` carries a `stage` coordinate per lane; and §4.6's reconciliation "agree" predicate
is defined **per stage** (§4.6).

**A third word, and it is the one the scope cut changed.** `lane` keeps its §5.3 meaning — a
connected component of the same-lane edges, a **unit of the frozen partition**. In phase 1 a lane is
an **execution unit and an ordering**, not a concurrency claim: units are executed one after another,
in lane-id order within a stage and in stage order within a wave (§5.14). The word is kept rather
than renamed to "unit", for two reasons stated once: `lanes.lock.json`, `computeLanePlan` and the
`conflict_reason` enum are the shipping analysis artifact and renaming them would churn the one part
`08` kept intact; and phase 2 attaches concurrency to exactly this object.

**The charter's "handoff per stage" is satisfied by "handoff per lane", and this is stated rather
than assumed.** `00.charter.md:58` says *"For each stage, write a handoff document"*; this design
writes one per **lane**. A stage's lanes are authored, frozen and committed **together** (Phase 3.f
runs once per stage; 3.e freezes the whole wave's lane plan once, outside the stage loop — see §2.1),
so the set of handoffs for a stage is written as one act and is frozen under one lock. Per-lane
granularity is strictly finer than what the charter asks for — a stage with one lane produces exactly
one handoff. In phase 1 that finer granularity buys the execution order, the commit pathspec and loop
H's denominator; in phase 2 it additionally buys the lease, the claim audit and loop L's denominator
(§P2 7.3, §P2 7.4). Nothing the charter asks for is dropped.

### 2.1 The flow

```
Preflight
  P.0  run-id + {work} resolution    (§14.1; no gate here — an invalid explicit value is

       waves/wave-{n}/partition.md naming, per lane, its tasks, its stage, its write set
       and the conflict edges that formed it, and, PER STAGE, why that stage boundary
       exists — EDGE-DERIVED (a lane-level dependency or phase edge, or a barrier task —
       §5.3's layering) or CAP-DERIVED (§5.3's post-layering split at `lane_cap`) — plus
       the `lane_cap` value in force, since a cap-derived boundary is formed by no
       conflict edge and the reviewer's remedy for it is `--lanes`. The provenance is
       recomputed deterministically from the frozen lock plus the sidecar it pins, so the
       3.f′ re-author after a 3.f″ merge (§7.9 a) reproduces it. 08:96 requires the
       analysis to be "frozen, published, reviewed"; freezing alone is not publishing.
       WHO REVIEWS: the user, at the gate. The orchestrator raises the review as a
       critical gate, so it halts even under --auto — there is NO unattended discharge,
       and that is the decision, not an omission (§17.20: a human judging a partition is
       exactly the judgment §1.1 says a tool cannot check). VERDICT VOCABULARY, closed:

       `--lanes`, `--constraint` or a sidecar correction — and `freeze-lane-plan` runs
       again under the append-new-artifact rule (§4.7), producing a new lane_plan digest
       that the next review must match; on `abort` the run halts. The verdict is recorded
       by the `review-partition` verb (§4.4) as a `partition_review` result object (§4.2),
       which is the record the gate reads — §1.1's "the tool checks only that the
       judgment was recorded", discharged the way P-HANDOFF-VERIFIED discharges it.
```

Phase 3.a–3.o repeat per wave. **Targets are not registered up front**, matching
`kiwi-wave-master:109`.

#### What Phase 3 looked like before the scope cut, and where each step went

Six steps left the flow with `08`'s scope cut. They are named here rather than only in Part P2, so a
reader of the flow above can see what is missing and a phase-2 session knows what to re-insert and
where.

| Step (revision 3) | What it did | Where it lives now | Re-enters at |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **3.g** dispatch | spawn every lane of stage s, non-blocking, staggered | §P2 5.5.3 | the same position, replacing §5.14's serial walk for the lanes it can dispatch |
| **3.g′** join | poll until every lane of the stage is terminal; `lane-timeout` | §P2 5.5.3 | after 3.g |
| **3.h** collect + claim audit | eleven audit rows against a lane's self-report | §P2 5.4 `collect()`, §P2 7.4 | after 3.g′ |
| **3.i** loop L | per-lane verification and remediation **before** merge | §P2 7.3 | after 3.h |
| **3.j** re-audit + integrate | per-lane merge, restore, CAS publish, merge gate | §P2 5.9, §P2 7.5 | after 3.i; charter C4's `validate` → `sync-index` moves back here from 3.k |
| **3.j′** harvest then reap | copy lane state to the host, then release workspaces | §P2 3.5, §P2 5.5.1 | after 3.j |

`08` §3's ships list is what remains: 3.a–3.f″ unchanged, 3.f′ reduced to a commit, 3.g replaced by
§5.14's serial execution, 3.k reduced to the epilogue set's execution plus close-out, and 3.l–3.o
unchanged.

**Six orderings were load-bearing in revision 3. One survives into phase 1; five are phase-2
orderings and are retained below, marked, because phase 2 is built from them.**

1. **3.d and 3.f′ are two commits, not one, and `dispatch-base-dirty` is evaluated at 3.f′.**
   At 3.d neither the lane plan nor any handoff exists, so a gate whose predicate is *"uncommitted
   content on a path the handoff references"* has no input there.

   **Phase 1 keeps both the split and the gate, for a weaker but still real reason.** Revision 3's
   justification was charter C3: a worktree lane sees no uncommitted work, so everything it reads
   must be committed first. Phase 1's executor runs at the host and *can* read uncommitted content,
   so C3 no longer forces the commit. What still does: §4.8 policy 5 commits the run's own artifacts
   on a stated schedule so `git clean -fd` cannot destroy them, and the gate's real content — *the
   paths this unit is about to write are not already dirty from something else* — is exactly what
   keeps `serial-unit-failed`'s "this unit produced no commit" reading honest. The gate is therefore
   retained with an unchanged predicate and a restated reason, rather than kept on a rationale that
   no longer holds.

   **The gate's path set, stated once — revision 2 gave two incompatible definitions one sentence
   apart and §13 adopted the first.** The predicate is **path-scoped**, not tree-wide, and the path
   set is:

   > `git status --porcelain --` over the union of each of this stage's handoffs' **front-matter**
   > `plan_path`, `sidecar_path`, `write_set[]` and `read_set[]`, plus the wave's `design.md` and the
   > handoff files themselves — **minus every path that matches an `orchestrator-only` glob under
   > §5.2's precedence order.**

   Three exclusions, each for a stated reason. Prose in `## Context` may name any path in the
   repository and is deliberately excluded. `manifest_path`, `heartbeat_path`, `decisions_path` and
   `deferred_mutations_path` are workspace-relative, and `regression_baseline_ref` points into the
   git-ignored journal. And the **orchestrator-only subtraction is the fix for a defect that fired on
   every multi-stage wave**: `plan_path` is `docs/plans/{plan_run_id}.plan.md`, which CP-07 classes
   `orchestrator-only`, and §5.4.2 consequence 4 has the orchestrator itself edit it at the **host**
   at 3.j through `workflow_task_check` (`src/core/workflow/mutation.ts:359`) and *"simply never
   commit"* it. So stage 1's own integration left `plan_path` modified, and stage 2's 3.f′ then
   found `git status --porcelain -- docs/plans/…` non-empty and halted the run. Subtracting the
   orchestrator-only class is exactly right rather than merely convenient: those paths are outside
   every lane's lease by construction (§7.4 R4), so their cleanliness tells a lane nothing.
   **The clause *"over the same pathspec that commit staged"* is deleted** — 3.f′ stages only the
   handoffs and the lane lock, under which the gate would never inspect `plan_path` or the sidecar
   at all and would be close to vacuous. §13's row states the surviving definition.

   **It is evaluated immediately after the 3.f′ commit**, so "clean" means "everything a lane will
   read is in `base_sha`". A tree-wide predicate can never pass here, because `kiwi/orchestrator/`
   is untracked by design (§4.8) and under Profile A `.claude/worktrees/` is untracked on any
   checkout lacking the local exclude (§5.5.2).

> **Orderings 2–6 below are `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` orderings.** Every one of them constrains a
> step Part P2 now owns — `base_sha` and dispatch (2), post-merge verification against per-lane merges
> (3), workspace release across a stage boundary (4), harvest before reap (5), and the re-audit at the
> tip actually merged (6). They are retained here, unedited and in place, because each was derived
> from a reproduced failure and phase 2 must not re-derive them. **In phase 1 none of them has a
> subject**: there is no `base_sha` a lane is moved to — the stage's 3.f′ commit still exists (rule
> 1's `dispatch-base-dirty` guards it) and survives as the card's `open[].base_sha` (§4.1) and the
> journal's `isolation.base_sha` (§4.2), but no handoff, spawn prompt or checkout carries it — and
> there is no workspace, no per-lane merge and no lane manifest. Phase 1's
> single execution ordering is stated in §5.14: stages in topological order, lanes in lane-id order
> within a stage, one unit at a time, and `validate` → `sync-index` at 3.k.

2. **`base_sha` is per stage, not per wave, and it is *not* a handoff field.** The value is the sha
   of the 3.f′ commit for **that stage**, so a stage-2 lane's base contains stage 1's merges.
   Pinning every stage to the wave's first dispatch base would silently discard earlier stages'
   merges from later lanes' view — and the resulting merge would still be clean and would still
   yield a valid `git-ancestor` proof, so nothing downstream would detect it. That is the failure
   class probe assertion 2 exists to prevent (§5.5), and it is why `lanes.lock.json` and the resume
   card both carry a `stage` coordinate.

   **`base_sha` is deliberately absent from the handoff's front matter, and this is a fixed point,
   not an ordering problem.** Revision 2 made `base_sha` a required front-matter field defined as
   *"the sha of this stage's 3.f′ commit"* while the same commit is the one that stages the handoff.
   A commit's sha is a hash over its own tree, so **no value written into a file inside that commit
   can ever equal it**; no `--amend` sequence converges, and `lane-{k}.lock.json`'s
   `front_matter_digest` (§3.3a) is committed alongside and pinned by drift digest 4, so a
   post-commit fill is `handoff-unresolvable-reference` plus a forced re-verify. The natural
   implementer workaround — write the last known sha, 3.d's — puts the handoff **outside** its own
   `base_sha` and reopens exactly the ENOENT-on-first-read failure RW-04 closed.

   The value is carried where it can be, all of it untracked or ephemeral: the **spawn prompt**
   (§5.5), the journal's `isolation.base_sha` (§4.2), and the resume card's `open[]` (§4.1). The
   handoff's `## Setup` says *"your base commit is already checked out"* and never names the sha.
   `dod` clauses that compare against it (§6.3) refer to the value the spawn prompt supplied.
   §6.2 layer 4's resolution point is therefore named explicitly rather than as *"at `base_sha`"*:
   **HEAD at 3.f, plus the set of paths 3.f′ is about to stage** — which is the same tree, one step
   earlier, and is available when layer 4 actually runs.
3. **3.j before 3.l.** Post-merge verification is genuinely post-merge. This closes the row-13 gap
   in `03:363` by re-ordering, not by new machinery: wave-master's §5.6 runs pre-merge only because
   merge is outside its flow.
4. **3.j′ before the next stage.** A stage's workspaces are released before the next stage's are
   acquired, so the lane cap bounds concurrent workspaces rather than cumulative ones, and so a
   still-running lane can never be silently carried across a stage boundary (§5.5.1).
5. **Harvest before reap, inside 3.j′ — and, under Profile B, harvest after loop L.** Under Profile
   A `release()` is `git worktree remove`, which deletes the directory and everything untracked in
   it — and §3.5 puts the lane manifest, the deferred-mutation queue and `decisions.jsonl` **inside
   the workspace**. Revision 1 reaped at 3.j′ and then read those files at 3.k (replay) and 3.l
   (loop P's bundle), which for every stage but the last is a read of a deleted file. `collect()`'s
   step 4 therefore copies all of them to the **host** run directory
   `{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/`, and 3.j′ asserts the copies exist
   before it reaps (`lane-state-harvest-failed`, C). Every downstream reader — 3.k replay, 3.l's
   bundle, `abort-run`'s report — reads the **host** copy and never the workspace. This is also what
   makes `abort-run` (§5.12) able to report a lane it reaped.

   **Under Profile B the harvest is the last step of `collect()`, not a middle one, because loop L
   creates evidence after the earlier steps run.** Revision 2's Profile B sequence was
   *harvest → loop L → re-audit → residue revert → switch*, and loop L's remediation runs
   `/kiwi-review-fix-loop`, which creates `docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-{run-id}/` by contract
   (`kiwi-review-fix-loop:200`, `:634`) — i.e. **after** the harvest that was supposed to copy it and
   **before** the residue revert, which is `git clean -fd` over the untracked residue globs. Loop L's
   entire evidence bundle was created and then deleted, unharvested, on the `--serial` path §1.4
   routes small runs into by default. The corrected Profile B order is `collect()` **1–3** → loop L
   → re-read `head_sha` → §7.4 re-audit → `collect()` **step 4 (harvest)** → residue revert → switch
   (§5.6). Under Profile A the question does not arise: the workspace survives to 3.j′ and the
   harvest there sees everything, including each remediation workspace's own manifest and analysis
   directory.
6. **3.j re-audits before it merges, against a *merged* evidence record.** Loop L at 3.i may apply
   fixes, and a lane's remediation commits on its own branch (§7.3), so the tip audited at 3.h is
   neither the tip nor the *manifest* merged at 3.j. The claim audit's artifact-derived rows are
   re-run at 3.j against the tip `integrate()` is about to merge, and `integrate()`'s post-CAS
   assertion pins `second-parent(new)` to **that** sha (§5.9 step 6). Without this, revision 1's
   headline claim — *"a lane's own success report is worth nothing … it re-derives every claim from
   artifacts"* — held for a sha that was never integrated.

   **Which manifest the re-audit reads is stated in §7.4, because leaving it open made R2 and R9
   fail on every remediated lane.** The remediation agent writes its own manifest; the lane wrote
   one earlier; revision 2 said neither which one 3.j reads nor whether they combine. Reading the
   lane's alone fails **R2** (`git diff --name-only base..head` now contains the fixer's paths, which
   the lane's `written_paths` never listed) → `lane-lease-breach` (C). Reading the fixer's alone fails
   **R9** (it carries no `red_evidence` for the original ACs). §7.4 defines the **effective lane
   record** — the lane's manifest unioned with every remediation manifest in round order — and every
   3.j row evaluates against that.

### 2.1a Routing by scale and scope — Phase 1.c′

`09.routing-design.md` is normative here and is **not** restated. This section states only what the
rest of *this* document needs: where the decision sits, what it produces, and what each rung does to
the flow above.

**The charter's requirement.** *"The orchestrator must judge the scale and scope of the work and
decide which execution route to take… This is the orchestrator's own judgment about the work, not a
mode read from configuration"* (`00.charter.md:69-73`), in scope for
`2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` because it is intake and dispatch rather than concurrent execution.

**Three rungs, one of them this document's own flow.**

| Rung | Shell | What happens to the flow above |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `R-STEP` | `Skill(kiwi-tdd)` | Phase 1 ends at 1.c′. Phases 1.d–4 do not run; charter C4's `validate` → `sync-index`, then Phase 5's emit and Phase 6's disposition (§5.12) close the run — the integration branch is named in the run report on this rung too |
| `R-PLAN` | `Skill(kiwi-pm)` → `Skill(kiwi-review-fix-loop --close-reqs)` | same, plus a plan-coverage close-out and charter C4's `validate` → `sync-index` |
| `R-ORCH` | this document | 1.d onward, unchanged |

**Where it sits, and why nowhere else.** 1.c′ is after the three investigators (1.b) and the QnA
(1.c), which produce the classifier's whole input set, and **before** 1.d, the first rung-specific
expense — a body-scope design document is worthless to `R-STEP`, which authors its own SDS, and
unnecessary for `R-PLAN`, whose requirements and plan already exist. Nothing irreversible has
happened: the run's first SRS mutation is Phase 3.b (`09` §2.2, §2.3).

**One thing *has* happened, and it is stated rather than glossed.** Phase 0.b has already created or
adopted `kiwi/orch/{run_id}/integration`, and P.5 holds the run lock. `kiwi-tdd` commits its red
tests onto whatever branch is checked out, which is that branch. That is intended: the branch is
never merged to base by this skill and is named in the run report (§5.12) on **every** rung,
including the two delegated ones.

**The classifier is computed, never voted.** `computeRoute(probe, opts)` is pure and total, in
`src/core/orchestrator/route.ts` (§10.1). It is disqualifier-first — D1–D8 each *remove* rungs and
none selects one — and the first survivor in the fixed order `R-PLAN → R-STEP → R-ORCH` wins;
`R-ORCH` is removed by no predicate, so the ladder always terminates. The property this buys is the
one the design is built around: **a wrong route traces to one named predicate and one recorded
value.** Only the *presentation* is a gate.

**The wave count is not a routing input.** It is a Phase 2 measurement made from the frozen design,
because the only component that could produce it has an output range of 3–8 sub-goals and
structurally cannot answer "one" (`09` §1). §1.4's `route-downgrade-available` is where a one-wave
measurement gets its second look.

**Work-mode does not remove the step rung.** P.3 reads it; a disagreement between the classifier and
the mode raises `route-step-requires-mode-switch` with `stay-and-orchestrate` recommended, and the
orchestrator never calls `set_work_mode` on its own authority. Under `--auto`, selecting `R-STEP`
raises `tdd-route-unattended` with `orchestrate-instead` recommended — `kiwi-tdd` declares no
`critical_gates[]`, so `--auto` is inactive for it and the run can stall at gates `--auto` is unable to clear
(`09` §4).

**Escalation is one-way.** `R-STEP → R-ORCH` and `R-PLAN → R-ORCH`, never downward after Phase 3.b —
`route-deescalation-refused` (C) fires on any attempt to move down a rung after 3.b, the one legal
downgrade being `route-downgrade-available` at 2.e and nowhere later (§1.4, `09` §7.4);
`route-escalation-after-landed-state` (C) fires when the escalation follows a landed commit **or** an
SRS `status` **or** `stability` mutation, and an escalation caught before the first red test is free
and ungated (`09` §7).

**Interaction with the scope cut, stated because both landed in the same revision.** Routing is
intake, so `08` leaves it entirely in phase 1. Two couplings are worth naming: `R-ORCH`'s cost is now
lower than `09` assumed in one respect (no worktrees are created on the expensive rung) and its
benefit is lower in another (no wall-clock speedup), which strengthens rather than weakens `09`
§7.4's downgrade gate — that gate is the design's only mitigation for over-routing, and it survives
unchanged.

---

## 3. Artifact inventory

Every file the run writes. "Reader" names the agent or tool that consumes it.

### 3.1 Authored and generated documents (D3 — one directory per unit of work)

Root: `docs/research/{work}/`, where `{work}` comes from `--work <name>` or defaults to the target
name. Prose is authored; `.json` and `.lock.json` are generated by `speckiwi orchestrate`.

| Path | Format | Language | Written by | Read by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `00.run-contract.md` | Markdown | **English** | orchestrator, Phase 0; amended at end of Phase 1 and at 2.d | **every resumed session, first** |
| `00.charter.md` | Markdown | user's language | user / intake | resumed session, verifiers |
| `01.intake.md` | Markdown | user's language | Phase 1.b/1.c | loop D verifiers |
| `0N.*.md` | Markdown | any | `/kiwi-srs-research`, investigators | loop D verifiers |
| `routing/probe.json` | JSON | — | `orchestrate route probe`, **before** the 1.c′ gate | `computeRoute` — its sole input; the reviewer who re-runs it |
| `routing/00.routing.md` | Markdown | **English** | Phase 1.c′, **before** the gate | **the user at the gate**: the proposal, the evidence table, the rejected rung and the predicate that rejected it, and the escalation contract |
| `routing/route-gate.json` | JSON | — | the orchestrator **at** the 1.c′ gate, before `orchestrate route freeze` | `freezeRoute`'s third argument — the gate-resolution record (`09` §8.3, §9.1); re-read on an interrupted redo so the override survives |
| `routing/route.lock.json` | JSON | — | `orchestrate route freeze`, **after** the gate | resume tool (`frozen.route`), every later rung-conditional step |
| `routing/misroute-{n}.json` | JSON | — | the orchestrator, inside `escalate-route` (§4.4), on every escalation (`09` §7) | the only instrument that ever measures D1's and D7's recall |
| `design/00.design.md` | Markdown, marked structure (§3.3) | **English** | Phase 1.d (**`R-ORCH` only**) | loop D verifiers, `freeze`, wave splitter |
| `design/00.design.lock.json` | JSON | — | `orchestrate freeze design` | resume tool, all verifier denominators |
| `design/00.design.{seq}.md` | Markdown, marked structure (§3.3) | **English** | `amend-design` (§8.1), mid-wave | loop D/W verifiers, `freeze`, wave splitter |
| `design/constraints.json` | JSON, **always written even when empty** | — | Phase 2.b | constraint-layer denominator |
| `design/convergence-registry.json` | JSON | — | Phase 2.c | `lane-plan`, `integrate`, claim audit |
| `waves/waves.lock.json` | JSON | — | `orchestrate freeze waves` | resume tool, coverage gate, final verify |
| `waves/wave-{n}/design.md` | Markdown | **English** | Phase 3.a | loop W verifiers, `/kiwi-srs` as `--research-doc` |
| `waves/wave-{n}/excerpt.md` | Markdown | source language | Phase 2.b | `/kiwi-srs` as `--research-doc` |
| `waves/wave-{n}/lanes.lock.json` | JSON, carries a `stage` per lane (§2.0) | — | `orchestrate freeze lanes` | §5.14's serial walk, drift check, loop P; **phase 2**: dispatch and claim audit |
| `waves/wave-{n}/partition.md` | Markdown | **English** | Phase 3.e′ — **new in revision 4** | **the user, at the 3.e′ gate** (§2.1 3.e′; there is no other reviewer in phase 1). `08:96` requires the analysis to be *published and reviewed*, not only frozen. The verdict does **not** live in this document: it is the `review-partition` verb's `partition_review` result object in the journal (§4.2, §4.4), which is what `partition-review-unrecorded` evaluates |
| `waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md` | Markdown + YAML front-matter (§6), `handoff_kind: lane` | **English, enforced** | Phase 3.f | **the unit's coding agent** (`/kiwi-pm` at the host in phase 1; the lane's agent in phase 2), loop H verifiers |
| `waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.fix-{r}.md` | same schema, `handoff_kind: remediation` | **English, enforced** | **deferred to phase 2** — it is loop L's artifact (§P2 7.3) | the remediation agent |
| `waves/wave-{n}/epilogue.md` | same schema, `handoff_kind: epilogue` | **English, enforced** | **Phase 3.k** — phase 1's carrier for `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned` (§5.14 difference 6, §2.1 3.k activity 0); **deferred to phase 2 (§P2 5.13)**: only its distinct executor, its `--lane epilogue` audit row set and `epilogue-task-failed` | the host-run `/kiwi-pm` |
| `waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.lock.json`, `…fix-{r}.lock.json`, `waves/wave-{n}/epilogue.lock.json` | JSON | — | `orchestrate freeze handoff` | drift check |
| `waves/wave-{n}/design-item-map.json` | JSON | — | Phase 3.b, from the `register-wave-srs` result | `computeLanePlan`'s sixth input (§5.3), 3.c′ gate |
| `waves/wave-{n}/duplication-audit.md` | Markdown | any | Phase 3.k (§7.9 (b)) | wave gate, run report |
| `waves/wave-{n}/issues.md` | Markdown | user's language | Phase **3.k** (`orchestrate issue open`, duplicate-sourced `local-defect` rows only, §7.9 (b)); Phase **3.m** (`wave-issue-triage`, the full union) | wave gate, next wave's inputs |
| `waves/wave-{n}/issues.lock.json` | JSON | — | `orchestrate issue …` | `wave close` gate |
| `partition-postmortem.wave-{n}.md` | Markdown | English | after each wave (§5.3) | **next wave's lane planner** |
| `partition-postmortem.lock.json` | JSON | — | `orchestrate freeze postmortem` | resolution pointer, drift check |
| `verify/{scope}.round-{r}.md` | Markdown | any | each verifier | cross-refutation step, journal pointer |
| `00.run-report.md` | Markdown | user's language | `abort-run`, and the normal end of run (§5.12) | the user |

**`{scope}` is a closed vocabulary**, because six loops are variously run-, wave-, stage- and
lane-scoped and revision 2 left the token undefined: `design` (loop D), `wave-{n}` (loop W),
`wave-{n}-lane-{k}` (loop L), `wave-{n}-post` (loop P), `wave-{n}-lane-{k}-handoff` (loop H) and
`run` (loop F). Nothing else is legal, and `freeze`/`round record` reject an out-of-vocabulary scope.

**Every prose artifact in this table is committed by the orchestrator**, on the schedule §4.8 policy
5 states. Revision 2 committed only the wave inputs (3.d) and the lane artifacts (3.f′), leaving the
run contract, the design and its lock, the waves lock, the issues doc and lock, the postmortem, the
verification rounds, the duplication audit and the run report **untracked** — while §4.8 names
`git clean -fd` as this repository's own documented recovery, which would delete the frozen design
every `invariant_digest` points at and halt the run on `run-invariant-drift` with the design gone.

**The lane manifest, the deferred-mutation queue and the lane decision log are deliberately absent
from this table.** They are **workspace-local and uncommitted** — see §3.5. This is the one place the
"profile-blind" claim of §5.4 genuinely breaks, and it is stated rather than papered over. All three
are **harvested to the host run directory by `collect()`** and read there by everything downstream
(§2.1 ordering rule 5); the host copies live under `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/` and are untracked by
policy (§4.8), which is why they are in §3.2 rather than here. **All three are phase-2 artifacts**:
phase 1's executor runs at the host, so its record is the commit range plus the child skill's own
`docs/analysis/` bundle, and there is nothing to harvest (§5.14).

**Six rows in §3.2 are likewise phase-2 only**, and are left in place rather than deleted so the
phase-2 layout is not re-invented: the lane manifest, `remediation-{r}.manifest.json`,
`deferred-mutations.jsonl`, `decisions.jsonl`, the `audit.3h.json` / `audit.3j.json` pair and
`replay-index.json` (§10.1 marks its only writer, `replay.ts`, phase 2). In phase 1 the run
directory `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/` holds the resume card and nothing else.

**The postmortem is wave-qualified, locked and journalled.** In the first revision it was a single
unqualified path at the work root, edited in place after every wave — which contradicts §4.7's own
append-new-artifact rule and made it the only artifact in this table with no lock, no digest and no
journal pointer. Wave 3's lane planner would read a file wave 3's own resumed session had partially
overwritten, or read wave 1's content believing it to be wave 2's. Since §5.3 calls the postmortem
*"the only mechanism in the design by which partition quality improves"*, a silently wrong one is
worse than none. Resolution reads the **latest journalled pointer**, and the planner's input is the
union of every `partition-postmortem.wave-{m}.md` for `m < n`.

### 3.2 Journals and machine state
