| Path | Format | Tracked? | Written by | Read by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `{git_root}/kiwi/waves.jsonl` | JSONL, schema **1.4.0** | **untracked, git-ignored (§4.8)** | `orchestrate journal append` (tool) | resume tool, validator, verifiers |
| `{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/resume-card.json` | JSON, hard cap **8 KB** | **untracked, git-ignored (§4.8)** | `orchestrate card write` (tool) | resumed session, first tool call |
| `{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/manifest.json` | JSON | **untracked, git-ignored (§4.8)** | `collect(lane)`, by copy from the workspace (§3.5) | claim audit, loop P bundle, `abort-run` report |
| `…/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/remediation-{r}.manifest.json` | JSON | **untracked, git-ignored** | `collect(lane)`, by copy from remediation round `r`'s workspace | the 3.j re-audit's effective lane record (§7.4) |
| `{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/deferred-mutations.jsonl` | JSONL | **untracked, git-ignored (§4.8)** | `collect(lane)`, by copy | 3.k replay (§5.9) |
| `{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/decisions.jsonl` | JSONL | **untracked, git-ignored (§4.8)** | `collect(lane)`, by copy | replayed into `docs/analysis/kiwi-orchestrator-{run_id}/decisions/` at 3.j (§5.4.1) |
| `…/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/audit.3h.json` and `audit.3j.json` | JSON | **untracked, git-ignored (§4.8)** | `orchestrate lane audit` at 3.h and, separately, at 3.j | loop P's lane layer reads **`audit.3j.json`** (§7.6) |
| `{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/replay-index.json` | JSON | **untracked, git-ignored (§4.8)** | `replay.ts`, after each successful call | `replay-deferred-mutations` idempotency (§5.9) |
| `{git_root}/kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` | JSONL | **tracked** (the only tracked file under `kiwi/`) | MCP `workflow_pipeline_emit`, **host root only** (§3.5) | `kiwi-pipeline` |
| `docs/plans/{plan_run_id}.plan.md` + `.sidecar.json` | Markdown + JSON | tracked | `/kiwi-planner` | lane planner, handoff author, lanes |
| `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/…` | JSON / JSONL | ignored (`.gitignore:20`) | `/kiwi-pm --session-suffix` | lane resume |
| `docs/analysis/kiwi-{skill}-{run_id}/` | Markdown | tracked | delegated skills | verifier evidence bundle |

Tracked status is measured, not assumed: `git ls-files kiwi/` returns `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` and
nothing else, and `.kiwi/` is ignored at `.gitignore:20`.

**`audit.json` is split into `audit.3h.json` and `audit.3j.json` in this revision.** Revision 2 gave
`orchestrate lane audit` one output path written *"at 3.h and again at 3.j"*, so the 3.h result was
destroyed by the 3.j re-run — while §7.6 requires *"a passing **3.j** claim audit … read from the
harvested `audit.json`"* and §8's issue intake wants the 3.h findings. Two paths cost nothing and
make both readable. Note also that `orchestrate lane audit` writes at the **host**, so neither file
is something `collect()` harvests out of a workspace; §7.6's word *"harvested"* is corrected to
*"host-written"* there.

`waves.jsonl` is **reused, not forked**. Its location resolution and run-root pin
(`waves-event.md:11-21`), run-scoped resume predicate (`:147-158`), `wave="all"` exclusion (`:139`),
`complete`-requires-a-passing-**latest**-wave-verify gate (`:135`), unreachable-PASS arithmetic
(`:160`) and round-counter accumulation (`:164`) are exactly the properties this design needs, and
they exist. A second journal file would fork the resume predicate and duplicate the completion gate.
The extension is an **additive minor**, which `waves-event.md:3` explicitly sanctions.

### 3.3 The design document's marked structure

`freeze` must not be asked to distinguish normative prose from illustration —
`kiwi-wave-master:177` states that examples and rationale sentences are explicitly *not* design
items, and that classification is a semantic judgment. So the **agent marks the items** and the
**tool validates the marking**. This is the boundary rule applied.

```markdown
## 3. Lane scheduling

### 3.1 Conflict edges

- [D-031] The scheduler MUST treat two tasks whose `files[]` sets intersect as same-lane.
- [D-032] The scheduler MUST refuse to parallelise a task whose `files[]` is empty.

> Rationale: file overlap is the actual merge-conflict predictor.   <-- not an item
```

Rules, all mechanically checked by `orchestrate freeze design`:

1. A design item is a top-level list row beginning `[D-nnn]` under a **lowest-level heading**,
   defined mechanically as *a heading with no deeper heading between it and the next heading of the
   same or shallower level*. This covers `####` and beyond and does not privilege `###`; revision 2
   wrote *"a lowest-level `###` heading"*, which is two different rules in one phrase and left
   `####` undefined while `unmarked-normative-prose` (C) keyed on it.
2. Ids are unique, contiguous within the document, and never reused across a run.
3. Every item's text contains **exactly one occurrence of a normative token** — `MUST`, `MUST NOT`
   or `SHALL`, counted as occurrences, with `MUST NOT` counted once and not twice. An item with none
   is `SRS`-style prose and is rejected; an item with two is split. **Token counting replaces the
   revision-2 phrase *"exactly one normative sentence"***, which needed a sentence splitter the
   document never specified — and a sentence splitter is a judgment call over abbreviations, code
   spans and list punctuation, feeding a critical gate and every downstream denominator.
   **The token set is English-only, and it is scoped to `design/00.design.md` and
   `waves/wave-{n}/design.md` — a constraint on those two inputs, not an oversight and not a rule
   about every artifact.** Both are marked **English** in §3.1 for exactly this reason. **The Layer-3
   skill-body vocabulary is a separate, bilingual set declared in §10.3**, because SKILL.md bodies in
   this repository are Korean-canonical and carry zero `MUST` tokens; the two sets are named
   separately so neither is read as the other, and no design-document denominator moves. The rule is restated here, where it lives, because the rest of §3.1 marks four other
   artifacts *"user's language"* and the existing harness uses bilingual regexes throughout — an
   implementer who generalises from those would build a bilingual detector and change the meaning of
   every downstream denominator.
4. Any paragraph under a lowest heading that contains a normative token but is **not** an item row
   is a **gate**, `unmarked-normative-prose` (C), not a warning. It was a warning in the first
   revision; that was wrong, because the miss is silent and the count is load-bearing: an
   under-counted `design_items[]` shrinks every frozen denominator in loops D, W, P and F while
   `invariant_digest` reports no drift, so nothing else in the design can catch it. The agent marks
   the line or rewrites it; there is no third option. The gate names the exact lines.
5. `integration_items` are marked `[I-nnn]` and follow the same rules.
6. Blockquote and fenced-code content is excluded from both the item scan and the gate scan.

`freeze` then emits `design_items[] = {id, heading_path, line_start, line_end, statement}` plus
`integration_items[]`, `out_of_scope[]` with a closed `exclusion_class`
(`waves-event.md:120`), and a sha256 over the whole lock. This preserves wave-master's granularity
rule while removing the *re-litigation after every compaction* problem.

#### 3.3a The other five lock schemas

**`orchestrate freeze` has six targets, and revision 2 stated three different sets of five.** §3.3a's
narrative covered waves / lanes / lane-`{k}` / issues / postmortem; §10.6's verb line read
`freeze design|waves|lanes|handoff|postmortem` with no `issues`; E38 named
`waves, lanes, handoff, issues, postmortem` while §3.1 said `issues.lock.json` is written by
`orchestrate issue …`, which carried no determinism contract. Fixed here: the six targets are
**`design | waves | lanes | handoff | issues | postmortem`**, `orchestrate issue …` mutates
`issues.md` and then calls `freeze issues` to regenerate the lock, and E38 asserts all six.

The five below are the substrate
of §4.7's drift digests and §4.6's `lock_lanes` source, so leaving them unstated left five JSON
contracts to be invented by whoever implemented the gates that read them. Every lock carries the same
envelope — `{schema_version: "1.0.0", run_id, kind, inputs_digest, git_blob_oid, sha256, written_at}`
where `sha256` covers everything above it and `inputs_digest` covers the exact inputs the lock was
computed from — plus a `body` whose shape is per-kind:

| Lock | `body` |
| --- | --- |
| `waves.lock.json` | `{waves: [{n, slug, excerpt_path, design_path, design_items: string[], integration_items: string[], order}], wave_count}` — `order` is the immutable registration order (`kiwi-wave-master:22`) |
| `lanes.lock.json` | `{plan_run_id, sidecar_path, sidecar_digest, registry_digest, existing_paths_digest, design_item_map_digest, prior_postmortem_digests: string[], lane_cap, code_roots: string[], test_roots: string[], lane_count, stage_count, lanes: [{lane_id, stage, task_ids: string[], write_set: string[], read_set: string[], req_ids: string[], design_items: string[]}], serial_epilogue: string[], unassigned: string[], serialized: string[], conflicts: ConflictEdge[]}` — the **five** digest fields plus the recorded `lane_cap`, `code_roots` and `test_roots` (literal arrays, no digest of their own) are what make §4.7 digest 3 recomputable (§5.3) |
| `lane-{k}.lock.json` (and `lane-{k}.fix-{r}.lock.json`, `epilogue.lock.json`) | `{handoff_kind, lane_id, stage, handoff_path, handoff_git_blob_oid, handoff_sha256, front_matter_digest, body_heading_digests: {heading: sha256}, task_field_count, acceptance_row_count, untested_row_count}` — digest 4 compares the handoff prose against these, so a hand-edit after verification is loud. One schema serves all three `handoff_kind` values; `task_field_count` is `0` for a `remediation` handoff (§6.2) and `stage` is `null` for an `epilogue` lock, carrying the handoff's marked `null` (§6.1) |
| `issues.lock.json` | `{wave, issues: [{issue_id, class, source, resolution_kind, resolution_ref, user_decision_ref}], counts: {open, planned, resolved, deferred}}` — `orchestrate wave close` evaluates `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` over exactly these rows (§8) |
| `partition-postmortem.lock.json` | `{waves: [{n, doc_path, digest, couplings: [{from_task, to_task, path, detected_at, resolution}]}]}` — one entry appended per wave; `couplings` is the machine-readable projection of the prose document, and it is the form `computeLanePlan` consumes (§5.3), so the pure function is never handed Markdown |

**The coupling row's shape is fixed here, and `symbol` is deleted.** Revision 2 declared
`{from, to, symbol, detected_at, resolution}` while E36a declared `{from, to, path}` and §5.3's
fallback read `from`/`to` as **paths** where the primary rule read them as **task ids** — three
shapes for one concept, feeding a pure deterministic function. Fixed: `from_task` and `to_task` are
always **task ids**, and `to_task` is **nullable on a 3.f″-sourced row** and non-null on a §7.5
merge-gate row, because no per-task read declaration exists at 3.f″ (X-04, R37 — §7.9 (a)); `path` is always the coupled **path** and is what the fallback matches on when a
task id no longer exists in a later wave's sidecar; and `symbol` is removed outright, because X-04
establishes that **no surface this design reads carries a symbol** (`kiwi-planner:744` — `files[]` is
`Array<{path, line_range?}>`), so the field could only ever have been null. `resolution` is a closed
three-value vocabulary — **`merge-into-one-lane` | `re-planned` | `accepted`** — and §7.5's coupling
recovery writes `merge-into-one-lane`, which is the only value §5.3's `learned-coupling` edge acts on.

`design/00.design.lock.json` keeps the shape already stated above. All five are byte-deterministic
over their declared inputs, which is asserted by the byte-determinism test in §10.3 Layer 1.

### 3.4 The lane manifest — deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`

A lane manifest is a dispatched lane's self-report. Phase 1 dispatches nothing, so the manifest, its
seven-value status enum and the `manifest_template` obligation it places on the handoff schema all
defer. **Moved verbatim to Part P2, under its original number `§3.4`**, so every `§3.4` reference
elsewhere in this document still resolves.

### 3.5 Where the lane's own bookkeeping lives — deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`

Workspace-local bookkeeping, its harvest, and §3.5a's branch-keyed workspace resolution exist only
because a lane runs in a workspace the host does not own. Phase 1's executor runs at the host root on
the integration branch (§5.14), so there is no workspace to resolve and nothing to harvest.
**Moved verbatim to Part P2, under its original numbers `§3.5` and `§3.5a`.**


---

## 4. The ledger — what survives compaction

Two tiers. Tier 1 is the only mandatory read on resume.

### 4.1 Tier 1 — the resume card

`{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/resume-card.json`. Rewritten in full at every checkpoint,
never appended to. **Hard cap 8 KB, enforced by the writer**, because an uncapped card degenerates
into a second journal.

The cap is made structurally reachable by a **rollup rule**. The first revision's arithmetic budgeted
`done[]` only and stated `8 + 6 + 8 = 22`; that ignored `open[]` and `frozen.lane_lock`, both of which
grow. Redone against the real maximum:

> `done[]` carries **one entry per completed wave** plus run-level milestones. Lane-level entries
> appear only for the **current** wave, and are collapsed into the wave's entry when the wave
> completes: `waves(8) + milestones(6) + lanes_in_current_wave(8) = 22` entries.
> `open[]` carries at most one entry per lane of the **current stage**: 8 entries, each ~180 bytes
> (two shas, a journal line; plus `workspace_ref`, `branch` and `deadline_at` in phase 2) ≈ 1.5 KB.
> `frozen.lane_lock` retains **only the current wave's** entry — a completed wave's lane lock is
> reachable from its `done[]` proof and from the journal, so retaining the map across waves buys
> nothing and grows without bound.
> Total at the design's maximum: ~22 × 90 B + 1.5 KB + a fixed ~2 KB header and `frozen` block
> ≈ **5.5 KB**, inside the 8 KB cap with margin. `resume-card.ts` enforces the cap by measurement,
> not by this arithmetic; the arithmetic exists so that hitting the cap means a bug rather than
> normal growth.

```json
{
  "schema_version": "1.0.0",
  "run_id": "2026-08-02.speckiwi.v260",
  "run_contract": "docs/research/v260-orchestrator/00.run-contract.md@sha256:9f1c…",
  "position": { "wave": 2, "stage": 2, "phase": "execute" },
  "next_action": { "verb": "execute-unit", "args": { "wave": 2, "stage": 2, "lane": "lane-3" },
                   "preconditions": ["P-DESIGN-FROZEN","P-LANE-PLAN-FROZEN",
                                     "P-HANDOFF-VERIFIED","P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED",
                                     "P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED"] },
  "frozen": {
    "engine": "kiwi-orchestrator",
    "work_root": "docs/research/v260-orchestrator/",
    "journal": "kiwi/waves.jsonl",
    "run_root": { "git_toplevel": "C:/Work/git/_Snoworca/speckiwi",
                  "mcp_workspace_root": "C:/Work/git/_Snoworca/speckiwi" },
    "isolation_profile": "none-serial",
    "proof_strength": "strong",
    "route": { "rung": "R-ORCH",
               "lock": "…/routing/route.lock.json@sha256:8c0d…",
               "probe_digest": "sha256:2e91…" },
    "base_branch": "feat/2.3.0.1",
    "integration_branch": "kiwi/orch/2026-08-02.speckiwi.v260/integration",
    "integration_base_sha": "9a01f3c…",
    "design_lock": "…/design/00.design.lock.json@sha256:4ab0…",
    "waves_lock":  "…/waves/waves.lock.json@sha256:c17e…",
    "constraints": "…/design/constraints.json@sha256:11de…",
    "convergence": "…/design/convergence-registry.json@sha256:77d2…",
    "lane_lock":   { "wave-2": "…/waves/wave-2/lanes.lock.json@sha256:5b3a…" },
    "counts": { "design_items": 41, "integration_items": 6, "constraints": 6, "waves": 4 },
    "regression_baseline": { "command": "npm test -- --no-file-parallelism",
                             "head_sha": "9a01f3c…", "failing_tests": [] }
  },
  "done": [
    { "key": "intake",  "proof": { "kind": "digest", "ref": "00.design.lock.json@sha256:4ab0…" } },
    { "key": "wave-1",  "proof": { "kind": "journal", "ref": "waves.jsonl#L18 status=complete verdict=pass" },
      "witness": { "kind": "git-trailer", "ref": "b71c904 Orch-Run=2026-08-02.speckiwi.v260 Orch-Wave=1" } },
    { "key": "wave-2/s1", "proof": { "kind": "git-trailer", "ref": "a1b2c3d,0d9e8f7 Orch-Wave=2 Orch-Stage=1" } },
    { "key": "wave-2/s2/lane-1", "proof": { "kind": "git-trailer", "ref": "51ba7de Orch-Wave=2 Orch-Stage=2 Orch-Lane=lane-1" } },
    { "key": "wave-2/s2/lane-2", "proof": { "kind": "git-trailer", "ref": "9ee01b3 Orch-Wave=2 Orch-Stage=2 Orch-Lane=lane-2" } }
  ],
  "open": [
    { "key": "wave-2/s2/lane-3", "state": "executing",
      "base_sha": "e4f5a6b…", "head_sha": "7bd41f0…", "journal_line": 44 }
  ],
  "blocked_on": null,
  "invariant_digest": "sha256:…over the frozen block…",
  "written_at": "2026-08-02T09:12:44.201Z"
}
```

**The example is internally consistent, and this is checked rather than assumed.** `e4f5a6b` is
**stage 2's** dispatch base (the 3.f′ commit of stage 2), which is why `open[]` above carries it — and
why §P2 3.4's manifest, a phase-2 artifact, carries it too — and why those and §6.3's handoff all
carry `stage: 2`. §6.3's handoff carries no `base_sha` at all — it is committed *into* `e4f5a6b`, so
no value inside it can name it (§2.1 ordering rule 2, §15.1 E44); **in phase 1 the unit runs at the
host with the integration branch already checked out (§5.14), and in phase 2 its lane receives the sha
from the spawn prompt (§P2 5.5).** **Stage 1's units committed onto the integration branch, whose head
at that point was `c88af52` — phase 1 performs no merge (§5.12, §5.14 difference 1)** — and stage 2's
dispatch base descends from it. **`frozen.regression_baseline.head_sha` is `9a01f3c`, not `e4f5a6b`**
— it is the P.4 pin, measured at the base-branch head before Phase 0.b cut the integration branch from
that same commit (§2.1, §14.1), so it is the one sha in the card that is *older* than the run; a mid-run
value there would promote an earlier unit's failures into the next unit's baseline, which is the thing
the pin exists to prevent. Revision 1 mixed `stage: 1`
and `stage: 2` across the three worked artifacts of one run id while asserting the same `base_sha`
for both, which — given ordering rule 2 — is the exact silent failure the rule exists to prevent, in
the document's own examples. §10.3's fixtures are built from these three artifacts, so a fixture
generator that reads them now produces a legal run.

Four properties carry the design:

1. **`next_action.verb` comes from a closed enum (§4.4), and `preconditions[]` is a closed enum too.**
   A resumed agent does not decide what to do; it reads a verb. An out-of-enum verb is a hard stop,
   and a closed enum is testable. The precondition vocabulary is exactly five values, each with a
   stated evaluator, because revision 1 named four and defined one:

   | value | true when |
   | --- | --- |
   | `P-DESIGN-FROZEN` | `frozen.design_lock` names a lock whose recomputed digest matches (§4.7 digest 1) |
   | `P-LANE-PLAN-FROZEN` | `frozen.lane_lock[wave-{n}]` names a lock whose recomputation matches (§4.7 digest 3) |
   | `P-HANDOFF-VERIFIED` | the lane's `lane-{k}.lock.json` exists and digest 4 matches, and the journal carries a `verify-handoff` result with verdict `pass` for that lane |
   | `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` | `orchestrate wave close --wave {n-1}` returned `ok` (§8) |
   | `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` | every lane of every stage `< s` in this wave is **settled**: it has a `git-ancestor` proof against `frozen.integration_branch`, **or** the journal carries a terminal `lane_disposition` for it (§4.2) |

   **Its phase-1 evaluator is different and weaker, and saying so is the point.** With no lane branch
   there is no `git-ancestor` proof to compute: a unit executed by §5.14 commits **onto** the
   integration branch, so its witness is the `git-trailer` kind (§4.5) — a commit on
   `frozen.integration_branch` carrying `Orch-Run`, `Orch-Wave`, `Orch-Stage` and `Orch-Lane` for that
   unit — or a terminal `lane_disposition`. That is a strictly weaker witness than an ancestry
   relation between two named refs, because a trailer is written by the same party whose work it
   attests. It is what a serial execution model can offer, and phase 2 restores the stronger one.

   **`P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED`'s second clause is a correction, not a loosening.** Revision 2
   required a `git-ancestor` proof for *every* lane of every earlier stage. A **demoted** lane is by
   definition never merged, and §7.3's demotion-legality rule explicitly permits demoting an
   independent stage-1 lane while stage 2 proceeds. So on the recovery path the design designates as
   *correct*, every subsequent resume evaluated this precondition false and §4.6's matching clause
   false → `ledger-reconciliation-divergent` (C), and the wave became **unresumable for the rest of
   its life**. The same held for the two lanes a `merge-coupling-detected` collapse resets (§7.5).
   A lane is settled when it has landed **or** when a terminal disposition says why it never will.

   All five are validated by `resume-card.ts` and asserted by E12.
2. **Every `done[]` entry carries a proof, and every entry's evidence is recomputable without the
   journal** (§4.5). An entry may additionally carry `witness`, a second `{kind, ref}` object drawn
   from the same §4.5 table. `witness` is **mandatory, and its `kind` may not be `journal`, on any
   `done[]` entry whose `proof.kind` is `journal`** — a wave-completion entry proves its verdict by
   journal line, and the witness is what keeps that entry recomputable when the journal is truncated,
   which is the event the card exists to survive. On any other entry `witness` is optional and
   advisory.
3. **`invariant_digest`** over the `frozen` block makes silent design drift loud: recompute from
   the lock files on disk; a mismatch is `run-invariant-drift`.
4. **`isolation_profile`, `engine`, `work_root`, `run_root`, `base_branch`, `integration_branch`
   and `route` are *inside* `frozen`, so `invariant_digest` covers them.** In the first revision the
   first six were sibling fields outside the digest, and §5.4's claim that the profile is *"immutable
   for the run"* was an assertion with no enforcement anywhere: `resume-card.ts`'s validation list did
   not include it, and `isolation-profile-change-requested` fires only on an **explicit** request.
   §5.4 explains why undetectability makes enforcement mandatory rather than optional.

   **`route` joins `frozen` for exactly that repaired reason** (`09` §9.3). It carries
   `{rung, lock, probe_digest}`. On resume the rung is **read from the lock, never recomputed** — a
   resumed session has no conversation and no investigators, and several probe fields are
   subagent-derived and not reproducible across a compaction, so a recomputation could legally
   produce a different rung. A probe digest that no longer matches `probe.json` on disk raises
   `run-invariant-drift` (C) rather than silently switching ladders mid-run.

   **`isolation_profile` is the constant `"none-serial"` in phase 1.** The field is kept rather than
   dropped so that the digest's field set, `resume-card.ts`'s validation list and the journal's
   `isolation` object are the same shape in both phases; `probe-isolation` never runs, so the
   profile-immutability rule compares against a value no probe produced and passes trivially. Phase 2
   restores `worktree` / `branch-serial-lane` and the rule regains its subject (§P2 5.4).

**Naming the integration branch in `frozen` is what makes the ledger's premise true.** In the first
revision *"integration branch"* and *"integration head"* appeared only incidentally, §5.6 wrote
`git switch {integration}` with `{integration}` undefined, and the card carried `open[].branch` and
`run_root` but **no integration-branch field at all** — so after compaction a resumed session could
not name the branch it must merge into, and §4.6's `git_lanes` ancestry check had no second operand.
A ledger that cannot name its own merge target does not survive compaction, whatever else it records.

The card is **derived**. If it disagrees with the journal and the journal is a superset, the card is
regenerated and the run continues (`card-stale`). Every other disagreement halts (§4.6).

### 4.2 Tier 2 — `waves.jsonl` v1.4.0

Additive fields only. Nothing changes the reading of an already-recorded event.

| Field | Type | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `engine` | `kiwi-wave-master` \| `kiwi-orchestrator` | **Absent ⇒ `kiwi-wave-master`.** The resume predicate filters by engine. Without it, `kiwi-wave-master --resume` selects an orchestrator run as "the most recent incomplete run" — `waves-event.md:156` has no producer discriminator today, and this is a live defect the extension closes for both skills. |
| `writer` | string | `speckiwi-orchestrate/{pkgVersion}`. Stamped by the tool on every write. Required on `schema_version ≥ 1.4.0` lines only; older lines report `unstamped`, never fail. |
| `event` | `intent` \| `result` | write-ahead / write-behind pairing (§4.3) |
| `verb` | string, closed enum §4.4 | the program-counter value this line concerns |
| `inputs_digest` | string | sha256 over the verb's declared inputs at intent time |
| `lane` | string | `lane-{k}` when the line concerns a lane |
| `stage` | int | the intra-wave layer this line concerns (§2.0); absent on wave-scoped lines |
| `lane_plan` | object | `{ lock_path, digest, lane_count, stage_count }` on the `freeze-lane-plan` result |
| `partition_review` | object | `{ doc_path, digest, lane_plan_digest, reviewer, verdict }` on the `review-partition` **result** line. `lane_plan_digest` is the value `freeze-lane-plan` recorded, which is what ties the verdict to the exact frozen plan; `verdict ∈ {pass, revise, abort}`; `reviewer` is `"user"` — phase 1 has no other reviewer (§2.1 3.e′) |
| `isolation` | object | `{ profile, reason, rejected[], workspace_ref, base_sha, head_sha, merge_sha, probe_evidence }` — `reason` and `rejected[]` carry §5.8's selection record, which revision 1 required in prose and gave no field |
| `lane_layer` | object | verifier-1's fifth denominator (§7.6) |
| `wave_issues` | object | `{ doc_path, digest, open, planned, resolved, deferred }` — **named `wave_issues`, not `stage_issues`**: §2.0 makes the artifact wave-scoped (`waves/wave-{n}/issues.md`, verb `orchestrate wave close`), and revision 1 shipped the old name into a closed versioned schema that FR-FLOW-061 parity tests then pin across four copies of `waves-event.md`. Renaming it *after* v1.4.0 ships is not an additive minor; renaming it now is free |
| `convergence` | object | `{ registry_digest, recipes_applied[], validate_exit, sync_index_changed }` |
| `allocation` | object | `{ target, pre_snapshot_digest, requirement_ids[], design_item_map: {req_id: string[]} }` on the `register-wave-srs` **result** line — the 3.b allocation set §5.11's `unallocated-req-id` check compares against, plus the design-item map that gives `lanes[].design_items` a producer (§5.3, §5.11) |
| `lane_disposition` | object | `{ kind, reason, at }` with `kind` a closed four-value enum — `demoted` \| `quarantined` \| `coupling-reset` \| `refuted` — written, **in phase 2**, on a `verify-lane` or `collect-lane` **result** line when a lane leaves the run without merging; **in phase 1** the carrier is the **`execute-unit` result line** (§4.4, §5.14), which carries the one kind phase 1 can produce — `kind: refuted`, from §8.1's refutation raised at 3.g. The reader is phase-agnostic: §4.6's `D(k)` reads the disposition on **any** result line. It is what makes `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` and §4.6's `lane-quarantined` class evaluable after a resume; without it a demoted or refuted lane reads as `lane-integrable` and a resumed session **merges work the run had discarded** |
| `decision` | object | one per committee-decided intake row (§7.2) and per lane decision replayed at 3.j: `{ question, options, decision, rule, committee_size, confidence, origin }` per `07:169-170` |
| `deadline_at` | string | ISO deadline on a `dispatch-lane` **intent** line (§5.5.1) |
| `postmortem` | object | `{ doc_path, digest }` on the wave's postmortem write (§5.3) |
| `coverage_residual` | array | `{req_id, reason, owner}` rows on the `R-PLAN` `dispatch-route` **result** line — the home for the plan-coverage reason, kept off the digest-pinned `route.lock.json` (`09` §9.2) |
| `card_digest` | string | sha256 of the resume card written immediately after this line |

Five of those rows are additions in revision 2 and one (`lane_disposition`) in revision 3. Each
carries a payload an earlier revision required in prose and gave no field: the 3.b allocation set and
its design-item map, the per-decision audit record `07:169-170` makes mandatory, `deadline_at`, the
postmortem pointer, `{profile, reason, rejected[]}`, and a lane's terminal disposition. `S1` (§15.3)
enumerates the v1.4.0 field set and `E28` asserts set-equality against it, so a field required by a
gate and absent from S1 produces a schema that drops or rejects it.

`phase` gains `intake`, `design`, `wave-design`, `schedule`, `handoff`, `lane`, `integrate`,
`stage-close`. Everything else — `verification`, `design_baseline`, `diff_window`,
`frozen_denominator`, `residual`, `plan_run_id`, `pipeline_run_ids` — is reused verbatim from
v1.3.0.

**Downgrade guard.** Within a run that contains any `1.4.0` line, a later line with a lower
`schema_version` is a `journal-version-downgrade` diagnostic. This mirrors the run-scoped
downgrade-bypass closure already written at `waves-event.md:135`, for the same reason: the version
is written by the producer being policed.

### 4.3 Write discipline

Per verb, three writes — write-ahead intent, write-behind fact:

```
1. orchestrate journal append  {event:"intent", verb, run_id, engine, wave, lane?, inputs_digest}
2. …perform the verb…
3. orchestrate journal append  {event:"result", verb, …, outputs, proof, card_digest}
4. orchestrate card write      (full rewrite, capped, validated)
```

The invariant a resumed session evaluates first:

> **For the current run, the last line for each `(verb, wave, lane)` key must be a `result`. An
> `intent` with no matching `result` means that verb was interrupted.**

Cost on a 4-wave, 12-lane run: roughly 90 journal lines and 45 card rewrites — trivial against the
subagent spend, and both go through a tool, so the agent never hand-rolls a JSONL append.

### 4.4 The verb enum and its three recovery classes

Closed enum. Each verb is one skill section `§V.<verb>`, so a resumed agent reads one section rather
than a 600-line document. Each declares a **recovery class**, which is what makes "interrupted"
distinguishable from "not started" — a distinction `kiwi-wave-master`'s three-stage resume
(`:473`) cannot express.

| verb | class | interrupted-recovery rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `probe-isolation` | **externally-visible** | **Phase 2 (§P2 5.5).** Preflight P.6. It commits a sentinel on `kiwi/orch/probe/{run_id}` and may edit the consumer's `.gitignore` (§5.5.2), so it is an externally-visible action and needs a line. A run whose journal already carries a `probe-isolation` **result** must not re-probe (§5.4); an `intent` with no `result` means the sentinel branch or the `.gitignore` edit may exist — inspect `git branch --list 'kiwi/orch/probe/*'` and `git status --porcelain -- .gitignore` before re-entering |
| `create-integration-branch` | **externally-visible** | Phase 0.b. `git rev-parse --verify {frozen.integration_branch}`: present ⇒ adopt, absent ⇒ create at `--base-branch` |
| `commit-run-artifacts` | **externally-visible** | inspect `git log` for a commit carrying trailers `Orch-Run: {run_id}` + `Orch-Verb: commit-run-artifacts` + the artifact set's digest in `Orch-Artifacts`. §4.8 policy 5 |
| `intake-qna` | pure-reauthor | redo; answers already in `01.intake.md` are inputs, not repeats |
| `intake-document` | pure-reauthor | redo |
| `intake-issue` | **externally-visible** | `gh issue view` is read-only, but `/kiwi-srs-research` may have persisted notes — inspect `docs/research/` and the skill's own event first |
| `intake-investigate` | pure-reauthor | redo |
| `probe-route` | **idempotent-by-key** | Phase 1.c′, keyed on `routing/probe.json`. **Not `pure-reauthor`**: S5 and S6 are subagent-derived and are not reproducible across a compaction, so a redo could produce a different rung. Recovery **reads the persisted probe and does not re-judge**; an `intent` with no `result` means the probe file may be partial — validate it against the schema and re-run only the fields marked `unreadable[]` (`09` §9.4) |
| `freeze-route` | idempotent-by-key | content-addressed; a redo is a no-op when the digest matches, mirroring `freeze-design`. **The redo reproduces the override, not the classifier's proposal**, because the gate's outcome is persisted in `routing/route-gate.json` (`09` §8.3, §9.1) and is re-read as `freezeRoute`'s third argument; re-running `computeRoute` alone would rebuild the proposed rung and write a different lock, i.e. `run-invariant-drift` on the path this class declares safe |
| `dispatch-route` | **externally-visible** | the child mutates. Inspect the child's own `pipeline.jsonl` event, then MCP `list_steps` (`R-STEP`) or `workflow_plan_status` (`R-PLAN`), before re-entering. **Never re-dispatch a possibly-running child** |
| `escalate-route` | **externally-visible** | rewrites the lock, may seal work as `out_of_scope`, may re-enter `/kiwi-srs`. Inspect `list_requirements --target …` and the latest `freeze-route` result first (`09` §7) |
| `downgrade-route` | **externally-visible** | the 2.e `downgrade-to-step` resolution only (`09` §7.4). Writes a **new** `route.lock.json` per §4.7's append-new-artifact rule rather than editing the frozen one, moves `frozen.route` to the new lock and recomputes `invariant_digest`, then hands off through `dispatch-route`. Recovery: read the latest journalled route lock — the rung is read, never recomputed — and inspect the child's `pipeline.jsonl` before re-entering |
| `author-design` / `verify-design` | pure-reauthor / idempotent-by-key | redo; round counter accumulates (`waves-event.md:164`) |
| `freeze-design` | idempotent-by-key | content-addressed; a redo is a no-op if the digest matches |
| `decompose-waves` | pure-reauthor | redo |
| `author-convergence-registry` / `verify-convergence-registry` | pure-reauthor / idempotent-by-key | redo |
| `author-wave-design` / `verify-wave-design` | pure-reauthor / idempotent-by-key | redo |
| `register-wave-srs` | **externally-visible** | `/kiwi-srs` may have authored requirements. Inspect `list_requirements --target wave-{n}` and the `srs_authored` marker (`kiwi-wave-master:227`) before re-entering |
| `plan-wave` | **externally-visible** | `/kiwi-planner` may have written `docs/plans/{plan_run_id}.*` and called `add_trace_link`. Inspect `workflow_plan_status` |
| `derive-readiness` | idempotent-by-key | pure recomputation over a fresh snapshot (§5.11) |
| `commit-wave-inputs` | **externally-visible** | inspect `git log` for a commit carrying trailers `Orch-Run: {run_id}` + `Orch-Verb: commit-wave-inputs` + `Orch-Wave: {n}` |
| `freeze-lane-plan` | idempotent-by-key | recompute; must be byte-identical or `lane-plan-drift` |
| `review-partition` | pure-reauthor | redo; a prior recorded verdict is an input, not a repeat — the user is re-asked against the current `lane_plan.digest`, and a verdict recorded against a digest superseded **by a 3.e′ re-plan** never satisfies `partition-review-unrecorded` — a 3.f″ coupling re-freeze is the one supersession that does not re-open it (§7.9 a) (§2.1 3.e′, §13). Phase 1 |
| `author-handoff` / `verify-handoff` | pure-reauthor / idempotent-by-key | redo |
| `commit-dispatch-base` | **externally-visible** | inspect `git log` for a commit carrying trailers `Orch-Run` + `Orch-Verb: commit-dispatch-base` + `Orch-Wave` + `Orch-Stage` |
| `execute-unit` | **externally-visible** | **Phase 1's execution verb (§5.14), and it replaces `dispatch-lane` / `collect-lane` / `verify-lane` / `remediate-lane` / `release-lane` / `integrate-lane` in the phase-1 enum.** One `/kiwi-pm` run over one lane's handoff, at the host, on the integration branch. Recovery: inspect `git log` on the integration branch for commits carrying `Orch-Run` + `Orch-Wave` + `Orch-Stage` + `Orch-Lane` + `Orch-Task` trailers, and `workflow_plan_status` for the plan's checkbox state, before re-entering. **Never re-run a unit whose commits are already present** — re-running is what produces the duplicate-implementation class the duplication audit then has to find |
| `dispatch-lane` | **externally-visible** | **Phase 2 (§P2 5.5).** §5.5.1's two-source reconciliation: `git branch --list 'kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w*'` **and** `git worktree list --porcelain`, keyed on the **branch** each worktree reports (§3.5a) — which also exposes an *unattributed* workspace in the pre-rename `worktree-agent-<id>` window a branch glob cannot see. The glob is `w*`, **not** `*`: the bare form also matches `frozen.integration_branch` (`kiwi/orch/{run_id}/integration`) and the probe branch (`kiwi/orch/probe/{run_id}` is outside the run prefix but `integration` is not), so a lane reconstruction with zero journal would invent a lane named `integration`. A branch with commits means the lane **ran**; it does not mean the lane **finished** — liveness is decided by the workspace lock state and the heartbeat file, never by commit presence |
| `collect-lane` | idempotent-by-key | recompute from git and the workspace-local manifest, **then harvest both to the host run directory** (§2.1 ordering rule 5). Idempotent because the copy is content-addressed; if the workspace is already gone, the host copy is authoritative and the verb is a no-op |
| `verify-lane` | idempotent-by-key | redo round — **the round's *verification* only.** Verification under both profiles is pure reading (§7.3), so redoing it is free |
| `remediate-lane` | **externally-visible** | **New in revision 3, and it must be its own verb.** Loop L's remediation is an **agent dispatch** that creates a worktree, a `-fix{r}` branch and commits (§7.3) — an externally-visible action inside a verb revision 2 typed `idempotent-by-key` with the rule *"redo round"*. A session interrupted mid-remediation would redo the round and dispatch a **second** agent onto a workspace that may still be live, which is exactly what R29 and §5.5.1 forbid and what this repository has reproduced. Recovery: resolve the `-fix{r}` ref family per §3.5a and apply §4.6's liveness rule — **never re-dispatch a possibly-live remediation**; ask the user |
| `release-lane` | **externally-visible** | `git worktree list --porcelain`: absent ⇒ already released. Lifecycle-aware — `git worktree remove` **fails on a locked (running) worktree** (`02:356-358`), and the runtime **auto-removes** a worktree whose agent changed nothing (`02:209-211`), so "absent" is not proof of a successful release |
| `integrate-lane` | **externally-visible** | `git merge-base --is-ancestor <lane_head> <integration_head>`; exit 0 ⇒ already merged, skip |
| `run-serial-epilogue` | **externally-visible** | **Phase 2 (§P2 5.13).** 3.k, all five activities **of §P2 5.13** — phase 1's 3.k runs the same task set at activity (0) (§2.1) through the one `execute-unit` executor, so what is deferred is the *distinct* executor, not the execution, and its host-execution mechanism generalises into `execute-unit` (§5.14). `/kiwi-pm` may have committed epilogue Tasks on the integration branch — inspect `git log` for `Orch-Verb: run-serial-epilogue` trailers, `workflow_plan_status`, the convergence journal line and `speckiwi validate --json` before re-entering |
| `replay-deferred-mutations` | idempotent-by-key | **Phase 2 (§P2 5.9).** replay is deduped by `args_hash`; safe to redo |
| `post-merge-verify` | idempotent-by-key | redo round |
| `wave-issue-triage` | pure-reauthor | redo; the issues doc is regenerated |
| `resolve-wave-issues` | **externally-visible** | routes to `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` or pipeline re-entry; inspect their journals first |
| `amend-design` | **externally-visible** | §8.1's sanctioned mid-wave amendment: inspect for a **new** `00.design.lock.json@sha256` and its journal line before re-authoring |
| `promote-requirements` | **externally-visible** | `update_status` / `add_verification_evidence`; inspect `get_requirement` first |
| `final-verify` | idempotent-by-key | redo round |
| `emit-and-finish` | idempotent-by-key | `pipeline.jsonl` idempotent by key; `{run_id}#r{n}` on resume (`pipeline-event.md:149`) |
| `abort-run` | **externally-visible** | §5.12. Terminal, but **not** a synonym for `halt`: it names the repository state the user is left in and writes it to the run report |
| `halt` | — | terminal |

**The three recovery classes are the closed enum `recovery_class`**, and its members are exactly
`pure-reauthor`, `idempotent-by-key` and `externally-visible` — stated here rather than only implied
by the table, because `journal-schema.ts` (§10.1) lists `recovery_class` among its closed enums and
revision 1 never declared the members anywhere a schema author could copy them.

An interrupted `pure-reauthor` or `idempotent-by-key` verb is simply redone, with no gate. An
interrupted **externally-visible** verb runs the inspection its own row names **before**
re-entering, and raises `interrupted-external-action` only when that inspection leaves the external
effect unresolved — for a lane, §4.6's `lane-possibly-live`; for `execute-unit`, a disagreement
between the `Orch-Task` trailers and `workflow_plan_status` (§5.14 *Resume*, §11.4). When the
inspection settles the state, the verb re-enters for the unfinished part with no gate. When the gate
does fire it halts even under `--auto`, because a committee cannot know what a half-finished merge
did.

**Commit identification is by git trailer, not by subject text (X-02).** Five verbs above inspect
`git log` — `commit-run-artifacts`, `commit-wave-inputs`, `commit-dispatch-base`, `execute-unit`
and (phase 2) `run-serial-epilogue`. Every one of them filters on the `Orch-*` trailer tuple, not on
subject text, because `00.charter.md:302` forbids phase and step markers
in a commit **title** and this design must not buy its recovery mechanism by violating a standing
constraint. The inspection command is:

```
git log --format='%H%x00%(trailers:key=Orch-Verb,valueonly)%x00%(trailers:key=Orch-Run,valueonly)%x00%(trailers:key=Orch-Wave,valueonly)%x00%(trailers:key=Orch-Stage,valueonly)'
```

filtered on the tuple. This is strictly stronger than a subject substring — trailers are structured,
and a subject is free text that a rebase or a human amend may reword.

**`dispatch-lane`'s recovery rule is deliberately weaker than it was.** The first revision stated
*"a branch with commits means the lane ran"* and re-dispatched on an `intent` with no `result`. That
cannot distinguish *running* from *finished*, and this repository has a recorded, reproduced failure
exactly there: background teammate names are **runtime-session-scoped**, so after a resume every
previously spawned agent is unreachable while still holding its worktree. Re-dispatching then puts
**two** agents on one branch. The rule is therefore: **a resumed session never re-dispatches a lane
whose workspace may still be alive; it raises `interrupted-external-action` and asks the user.**
§5.5.1 defines "may still be alive".

### 4.5 Proof kinds

Ordered by trust. The design deliberately maximises the share of proofs that are recomputable
**without** the journal, because the journal is the artifact most likely to be truncated.

| kind | recomputation | used for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `git-ancestor` | `git merge-base --is-ancestor <laneHeadRef(k)> <integration_head>` | lane merged. Strongest; reconstructible from `git branch --list 'kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w*'` even with zero journal |
| `git-ref` | `git rev-parse <branch>` | lane dispatched and produced commits |
| `git-trailer` | `git log --format='%H%x00%(trailers:key=Orch-Verb,valueonly)…'` | a dispatch-base or wave-inputs commit exists for a `(run, wave, stage)` (X-02) |
| `digest` | sha256 of the lock file vs the card | design / waves / lanes / handoff / registry / postmortem frozen |
| `mcp-state` | `get_requirement(id).status`, `workflow_plan_status` | REQ promoted, plan authored |
| `fs-exists` | file present at the recorded path | reports, issue docs |
| `journal` | line number + predicate in `waves.jsonl` | verification verdicts — the only claim with no external witness, which is precisely why `waves-event.md:135` exists |

**`journal` is never a *sole* proof on a verdict-bearing line.** Every line that records a verdict —
a loop's pass, a lane's integration, a wave's completion — must additionally carry at least one
externally recomputable kind (`git-ancestor`, `git-ref`, `git-trailer`, `digest` or `mcp-state`).
This is the honest boundary of §10.2's `writer`-stamp compensation: the stamp authenticates the
*path* a line was written by, not the *payload* it carries, so a `journal`-only verdict is a claim
with no witness at all.

### 4.6 Reconciliation

> **Phase 1 evaluates three of the four sources.** The predicate below reconciles `journal_lanes`,
> `lock_lanes`, `card_lanes` and `git_lanes`, and its liveness rule reads
> `git worktree list --porcelain`. Phase 1 creates no worktree and no lane branch, so `git_lanes` is
> derived from **`Orch-Lane` commit trailers on the integration branch** and the `LIVENESS(k)`
> question does not arise: a unit is running, finished or not started, and the resumed session knows
> which from its own `execute-unit` intent/result pair. **The whole liveness apparatus — `LIVENESS`,
> the unattributed-workspace clause, `lane-possibly-live`, and the never-re-dispatch rule — is phase-2
> content that is retained here in place** rather than moved, because it is inseparably interleaved
> with the classification logic phase 1 does use. `lane_disposition` and the `lane-quarantined` class
> apply in both phases: a unit can still be refuted (§8.1) in phase 1.
>
> **The outcome verbs and the rank order below are likewise phase-2 vocabulary retained in place.**
> The six lane verbs they name are phase 2 exactly as `dispatch-lane` is marked at §4.4, and in
> phase 1 the classification's only emittable outcome is **`execute-unit`** (§4.4, §5.14): a lane
> classified `not-dispatched`, and a unit with an unmatched `execute-unit` intent, both resolve to
> `execute-unit`, which §5.14's *Resume* re-enters only for Tasks carrying neither an `Orch-Task`
> trailer nor a checked box. `lane-collectable` and `lane-integrable` are **unreachable** in phase 1
> — their guards require a lane branch `B(k)` and a `collect-lane` result, and phase 1 creates no
> lane branch and never runs `collect-lane`. The rank order therefore reads `execute-unit` (1) <
> *no action* (`lane-landed`, `lane-quarantined`), tie-broken by ascending lane id. Without this
> marking §4.1 property 1 — *"an out-of-enum verb is a hard stop"* — halts a correct phase-1 run on
> the first not-yet-started unit.

The resume tool's second job, and what turns silent drift into a gate. **The comparison is scoped to
one `(wave, stage)` pair**, because the first revision compared whole-wave sets and therefore had no
classification at all for the normal mid-wave state: `lock_lanes` contains every lane of the wave
including the stages not yet dispatched, so a partially dispatched multi-stage wave fell through to
*"anything else → divergent"* on a run that was proceeding correctly.

**The predicate is per lane, not per set, and liveness is the first thing it asks.** Revision 1
compared four *sets* and classified the whole `(w,s)` pair from their agreement. That is why the
ordinary mid-stage state — dispatched, running, no result line yet — fell to `divergent`: such a lane
is in `git_lanes`, `lock_lanes` and `card_lanes` but not in `journal_lanes`, matches no named outcome,
and lands on *"anything else"*. §11.4's own worked drill asserted `consistent` for exactly that shape.
Both cannot be right. The rewrite classifies **each lane of `(w,s)` independently** and then reduces:

```
scope        = the current (wave w, stage s) from the resume card's `position`
lanes        = the lanes of lanes.lock.json whose `stage` == s          (the denominator)

per lane k, FIVE observations, none of them a set:
  J(k)  = the last journal line for
          (dispatch-lane|remediate-lane|collect-lane|verify-lane|integrate-lane, w, s, k)
  B(k)  = git rev-parse --verify laneHeadRef(k)                        (absent | sha)
          laneHeadRef(k) per §3.5a step 2: the highest -fix{r} ref, else the lane branch
  A(k)  = git merge-base --is-ancestor B(k) frozen.integration_branch  (merged?)
  D(k)  = the lane_disposition recorded on any result line for (w,s,k)  (absent | kind)
  L(k)  = LIVENESS(k) per §3.5a + §5.5.3.  THREE VALUES, AND THE THIRD IS "OTHERWISE",
          so the function is TOTAL:
            live     no terminal manifest for k, AND a worktree on some ref of k's family
                     exists, AND (it is `locked` OR its heartbeat is newer than
                     3 × --poll-interval)
            unknown  no terminal manifest for k, AND an UNATTRIBUTED workspace exists under
                     the isolation root while some lane of (w,s) has an unmatched
                     dispatch-lane or remediate-lane intent
                     (the pre-rename `worktree-agent-<id>` window, 02:173)
            dead     OTHERWISE — which covers, explicitly:
                       * a terminal manifest exists (host-harvested or workspace-local),
                         per §5.5.3's terminality predicate;  and/or
                       * no workspace on k's ref family and no unattributed workspace;  and/or
                       * a workspace exists but is UNLOCKED with a heartbeat older than
                         3 poll intervals and no terminal manifest — the agent died before
                         finishing.  The directory may still be on disk; collect() handles
                         both cases and release-lane reaps it.

per-lane classification, first match wins:
  lane-possibly-live  L(k) ∈ {live, unknown}                        → interrupted-external-action (C)
                      REGARDLESS of whether J(k) is an intent, a result, or absent
  lane-quarantined    D(k) present and A(k) false                   → no action for this lane;
                      (demoted | quarantined | coupling-reset          it is SETTLED and MUST NOT
                       | refuted)                                      be integrated
  not-dispatched      J(k) absent,  B(k) absent,  L(k)=dead         → dispatch-lane
  lane-collectable    L(k)=dead, B(k) present, A(k) false,
                      no collect-lane result                        → collect-lane  (§5.4)
  lane-integrable     L(k)=dead, collect-lane result present,
                      A(k) false                                    → verify-lane / integrate-lane
  lane-landed         A(k) true and an integrate-lane result exists → done
  journal-behind-git  A(k) true and NO integrate-lane result        → ledger-reconciliation-divergent (C)
  divergent           anything else                                 → ledger-reconciliation-divergent (C)

reduction over the stage:
  any lane-possibly-live                     → interrupted-external-action (C), stage halts
  any journal-behind-git or divergent        → ledger-reconciliation-divergent (C)
  card disagrees with the classification      → card-stale: regenerate the card, continue
  otherwise                                  → consistent; next_action per the RANK below
and, independently of the lanes: every stage < s must be SETTLED — every lane of it either has a
git-ancestor proof or a terminal lane_disposition — else divergent (the same predicate as
P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED, §4.1).
```

**The two reduction operations revision 2 left undefined.**

- **"card disagrees"** replaces revision 2's `card ⊂ (the per-lane classification)`, which compared a
  card to a classification with no stated projection — the operand shapes are not comparable after
  the per-lane rewrite. The predicate is: for each lane `k`, project the classification to the card's
  own vocabulary (`open[].state` ∈ `dispatched | collected | verified`, or membership in `done[]`;
  **phase 1 projects `open[].state` as the single value `executing`**, because `dispatch-lane` and
  `collect-lane` are not in its verb enum — §4.4, §5.14)
  and compare. Any lane where the card's projection differs from the computed class, or a lane
  present in one and absent from the other, is a disagreement. The card is derived, so a
  disagreement regenerates it.
- **"lowest-ranked"** is now a defined total order. Rank a lane by its class's `next_action` verb in
  the §2.1 phase order — `dispatch-lane` (1) < `collect-lane` (2) < `verify-lane` (3) <
  `remediate-lane` (3) < `integrate-lane` (4) < *no action* (`lane-landed`, `lane-quarantined`) — and
  break ties by ascending lane id. `next_action` is the lowest-ranked lane's own next verb.

**Five things this fixes, each of which was reachable.**

1. **`lane-possibly-live` is keyed on liveness, not on an unmatched intent.** Revision 1 defined it as
   *"a workspace exists for a lane with an **unmatched `dispatch-lane` intent**"* while §5.5 said the
   dispatch **result** line is written at spawn, when the runtime returns the workspace. A lane whose
   agent is still executing therefore had a *matched* pair, so the class could not fire for the one
   state it exists to catch. §11.4's prose already stated the correct, liveness-keyed predicate; the
   normative §4.6 stated an incompatible one, and the normative one was the one that could not fire.
2. **A finished-but-reaped lane has a class.** `lane-collectable` is the §11.4 drill's case — branch
   with commits, workspace gone. Revision 1 had no outcome for it and fell through to `divergent`; and
   even reaching `collect-lane` stranded the work, because `collect()`'s only stated route for an
   absent workspace was `no-response` → `lane-timeout` (C). §5.4's `collect()` now has a
   **branch-derived** path that needs no workspace at all.
3. **An unattributed workspace blocks the stage.** The `unknown` liveness value is the honest answer
   to the pre-rename window: a worktree exists under the isolation root, it belongs to no lane the
   host can name, and some lane was dispatched and never answered. Revision 1's second git source was
   keyed on `workspace_ref` **from the intent line**, which §5.5 makes `null` under Profile A — so it
   matched nothing and the window was uncovered despite being the stated reason the source existed.
4. **`L(k)` is total, and revision 2's was not.** Revision 2 defined `live` on a freshness conjunct
   and defined **both** `gone` and `unknown` on *"no such worktree"*, leaving the case *worktree
   exists, unlocked, heartbeat stale* with **no value at all**. That case is the modal interruption
   shape here: a stage's lanes all finish at 3.g′, the orchestrator enters loop L at 3.i (the run's
   most expensive step), the session is compacted twenty minutes in, the workspaces are still on disk
   because they are reaped only at 3.j′, the agents have terminated so the worktrees are unlocked, and
   the heartbeats are three minutes past their 180-second threshold. Every per-lane row requiring
   `gone` failed, every row requiring `live|unknown` failed, and first-match-wins reached
   *"divergent — anything else"* → `ledger-reconciliation-divergent` (C) **on a run that was
   proceeding correctly**, with the un-reaped worktrees §5.5.1 calls the documented norm here. The
   third value is now *"otherwise"* and consults §5.5.3's terminality predicate, which is the fact
   that actually distinguishes a finished agent from a running one.
5. **A lane that left the run without merging has a class, and it is not `lane-integrable`.**
   `lane-quarantined` keys on the new `lane_disposition` journal field (§4.2). Revision 2's seven
   outcomes keyed on `J/B/A/L` only and **never read the manifest `status`**, so a lane that returned
   `design-refuted` — which halts the run at 3.h *after* `collect()` completed — resumed as
   `L=dead`, branch present with commits, `A` false, `collect-lane` result present: exactly
   `lane-integrable` → *"verify-lane / integrate-lane"*. **A resumed session integrated a lane whose
   design item had been refuted**, and the card could not save it because `blocked_on` is regenerated
   by the same predicate under `card-stale`. Identically for a demoted lane (§7.3: *"its work is
   discarded"*) and for the two lanes a coupling collapse resets (§7.5).

`card-stale` self-heals only because the card is derived. Nothing else does.

**Why the second git source is not redundant.** The runtime creates the branch
`worktree-agent-<id>` (`02:173`) and the lane renames it to `kiwi/orch/…` as its first act. A lane
that dies **before** its first act leaves a workspace and a branch that the `kiwi/orch/…` glob cannot
see, so a glob-only reconciliation reads "not dispatched" while the workspace may be alive — and then
re-dispatches into it. `git worktree list --porcelain` covers exactly that window — as an
**unattributed** workspace, per §3.5a, because before the rename there is no key that attributes it to
a lane — and it is also the only source that reports the `locked` state a live agent holds.

### 4.7 Four independent drift digests

Checked at every resume and before every `freeze-*`:

1. `invariant_digest` vs recomputation from the lock files → `run-invariant-drift`.
2. `inputs_digest` on each intent line vs the current digest of those inputs. If an input changed
   between intent and result, the result is derived from something that no longer exists — the verb
   is re-run, not trusted.
3. `lanes.lock.json` vs `computeLanePlan` recomputed now → `lane-plan-drift`. **The recomputation
   uses the nine inputs the lock itself records, not the nine inputs available today**, and this is
   what makes the check meaningful rather than noisy. §3.3a's `lanes.lock.json` carries
   `sidecar_digest`, `registry_digest`, `existing_paths_digest`, `design_item_map_digest` and
   `prior_postmortem_digests[]` beside the plan; the resume tool re-reads each of those five recorded
   inputs (`existing_modules` is derived from the sidecar and is covered by `sidecar_digest`),
   re-digests them, and:
   - if all still match, it recomputes the plan, reads each `merge-into-one-lane` coupling row this
     wave's journal already carries from 3.f″ (§7.9 (a)) as the **lane-keyed** triple
     `{path, fromLane, toLane}` — `planStageCoupling`'s pure output, fixed by AC-08 and E36a — and
     applies it as a **lane merge** of the recomputed plan's `fromLane` and `toLane` components before
     comparing byte-for-byte; a difference is a **partitioner** defect and an internal error, not
     drift. The comparand is keyed on the lane pair and **not** on task ids, because the postmortem's
     task-keyed projection of the same row records `to_task: null` at 3.f″ (§7.9 (a)) and §5.3's
     `files[]` fallback can only reach tasks that *write* `path` — i.e. the writing lane, since a
     reading-lane task carrying `path` in `files[]` would have put `path` in that lane's `write_set`
     and `write-set-overlap` would already have merged the two at plan time — so no task-keyed
     `learned-coupling` edge can name the reading lane inside the same wave. The comparand is
     **composed**, not bare, because 3.f″ re-freezes the lock mid-wave over inputs none of which
     change; it stays pure and externally recomputable, since this digest already reads the journal
     for the newer-lock clause below and 3.f″'s rows are its only other input;
   - if `sidecar_digest` differs, the sidecar changed under the run: `lane-plan-drift` (C), unless a
     newer lock has been journalled under the append-new-artifact rule below;
   - if `existing_paths_digest` or `prior_postmortem_digests[]` differ, the plan is **stale but not
     wrong** — a lane created a file, or a wave wrote a postmortem, which is normal progress. This is
     recorded as a `W` and the lock is **not** recomputed mid-wave. Revision 1 recomputed
     `computeLanePlan(sidecar, registry)` with two arguments against a function §5.3
     then declared as taking five, so an implementer either fired `lane-plan-drift` on every file a lane created or
     silently excluded two real inputs from the check.
   Because the function is deterministic over its nine declared inputs (§5.3), and because the lock
   pins all nine through those five recorded digests and the recorded `lane_cap`, `code_roots` and
   `test_roots`, the comparison has a defined meaning in every case.
4. `lane-{k}.lock.json` vs the handoff prose → re-verify. A hand-edited handoff after verification
   silently changes what a lane is told to do.

**Legitimate amendment is distinguished from drift by the append-new-artifact rule**
(`kiwi-wave-master:189`): a late constraint, a re-plan after remediation, a folded-in wave issue **or
a mid-wave design amendment under §8.1** is written as a **new artifact plus a new journal line**,
never edited in place, and resolution always reads the latest pointer. `freeze-lane-plan` therefore
accepts a *newly journalled* lock as the current one, and `lane-plan-drift` fires only when the lock
on disk disagrees with a recomputation over the sidecar the lock itself names. This is the fix for
the deadlock where the normal remediation path (pipeline re-entry rewrites the sidecar) would
otherwise trip the drift detector.

**The design lock is inside that machinery, not outside it.** The first revision applied
append-new-artifact to the lane plan but left `invariant_digest` over the frozen block as an
unconditional halt on any design change, which made a mid-wave design amendment a
`run-invariant-drift` **by construction** — so the mid-wave "design found wrong" case of §8.1
had no legal route at all. `amend-design` (§4.4, §8.1) writes a new `00.design.lock.json`, journals
it, and the card's `frozen.design_lock` pointer moves to it; `run-invariant-drift` then fires only
when the digest disagrees with the lock the card **currently names**.

### 4.8 The journal and the card are untracked, and this is a decision, not an accident

`kiwi/waves.jsonl` and `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/` are neither tracked nor ignored today.
Measured: `git ls-files kiwi/` returns `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` only; `git check-ignore
kiwi/waves.jsonl` exits 1; `.gitignore:7-10` ignores only `kiwi/.pipeline-path`,
`kiwi/.status.json`, `kiwi/.srs.lock` and `kiwi/.srs.lock.guard`. Left unstated, three reachable
failures follow, and one of them turns a loud failure into a silent one:

- `git clean -fd` — this repository's own documented recovery after a hermeticity leak — deletes the
  journal and every resume card. §4.5 already names `journal` as *"the only claim with no external
  witness"*, so §4.6 then reads `journal-behind-git` → `ledger-reconciliation-divergent` (C) on a run
  that was correct.
- A `git add -A` at a dispatch-base commit stages the **live** journal and card. From then on
  `integrate(lane)`'s restore of `kiwi/waves.jsonl` truncates the journal back to the commit
  snapshot, destroying every line written since — including the `integrate-lane` intent line written
  moments earlier.
- `git status --porcelain` at the host root is permanently non-empty with `?? kiwi/orchestrator/`,
  which `dispatch-base-dirty` and the claim audit must tolerate.

**The policy, stated:**

1. `kiwi/waves.jsonl` and `kiwi/orchestrator/` are added to the **tracked** `.gitignore`, alongside
   the other `kiwi/` runtime files. The journal is machine state, it is tool-written by contract
   (§10.2), and a per-run resume card has no business in history.
2. **No phase of this design ever runs `git add -A` or `git commit -a`.** Both dispatch commits
   (3.d, 3.f′) stage an **explicit pathspec** computed from the artifacts they are committing. This
   is stated as a forbidden action in `00.run-contract.md` (§11.2).
3. `kiwi/waves.jsonl` is consequently **removed from CP-07's restore globs** — it can never match a
   tree, so including it aborted the whole restore (§5.9, RV-15).
4. `dispatch-base-dirty` is path-scoped for the same reason (§2.1 ordering rule 1).
5. **Every artifact the run authors under `docs/research/{work}/` *is* committed, on a stated
   schedule, under the verb `commit-run-artifacts` (§4.4).** Policy 1 makes the *journal and card*
   untracked because they are machine state; it says nothing about the run's prose, and revision 2
   then gave the prose no commit step at all. The schedule, all with explicit pathspecs:

   | Point | What is committed |
   | --- | --- |
   | Phase 0.b, after the integration branch exists | `00.run-contract.md`, and the consumer `.gitignore` edit if §5.5.2 closure 3 made one |
   | Phase 1.c, after the QnA closes | `00.charter.md` (when it lives in the work directory), `01.intake.md`, and every `0N.*.md`. It sits at 1.c rather than folding into the 1.c′ row because `00.charter.md`, `01.intake.md` and the `0N.*.md` set exist **before** the route gate and must be committed on all three rungs (§2.1a) — the same argument the 1.c′ row makes for the routing artifacts — and because §7.6's intent layer and §7.2's open-question set read them after `git clean -fd` |
   | Phase 1.c′, after `freeze-route` | `routing/probe.json`, `routing/00.routing.md`, `routing/route-gate.json`, `routing/route.lock.json`, and, on `R-STEP` / `R-PLAN` only, `00.run-contract.md` (the delegated-rung end-of-Phase-1 `intake_autonomy` amendment, §11.2 — with no loop D to follow N is final at 1.c, and `freeze-route` has just resolved the rung this condition reads; on those two rungs 1.c′ **is** the end of Phase 1, §2.1a). **It sits at 1.c′ and is not folded into the 1.e row**, because 1.e is `R-ORCH`-only (§2.1a) while the routing artifacts and the resume-time `probe_digest` check exist on all three rungs (§11, `09` §9.4) |
   | On each escalation, inside `escalate-route` (§4.4) | `routing/misroute-{n}.json` |
   | Phase 1.e, after `freeze-design` | `design/00.design.md`, `design/00.design.lock.json`, every `verify/design.round-{r}.md`, and `00.run-contract.md` (the `R-ORCH` end-of-Phase-1 `intake_autonomy` amendment, §11.2 — the final N exists only after loop D at 1.d) |
   | After `amend-design` (§8.1), before the affected lanes are re-dispatched | `design/00.design.{seq}.md`, `design/00.design.lock.json` |
   | Phase 2.d, after the coverage gate | `design/constraints.json`, `design/convergence-registry.json`, `waves/waves.lock.json`, every `waves/wave-{n}/excerpt.md`, and `00.run-contract.md` (the wave-order amendment, §11.2) |
   | 3.d | the wave inputs (unchanged) |
   | 3.e′, after the partition review is recorded | `waves/wave-{n}/partition.md` |
   | 3.f′ | the stage's lane artifacts (unchanged), **plus a re-authored `waves/wave-{n}/partition.md` when 3.f″ fired** (§7.9 a) |
   | 3.k, activity (4) (phase 1, §2.1; activity (5) in the §P2 5.13 sequence) | everything the wave produced after 3.f′ **and before this step**: `duplication-audit.md`, `design-item-map.json`, `waves/wave-{n}/epilogue.md` + `waves/wave-{n}/epilogue.lock.json`, every `verify/wave-{n}.round-{r}.md` and every `verify/wave-{n}-lane-{k}-handoff.round-{r}.md` (loop L's rounds are deferred with the lanes), and the **accumulated host-side `docs/plans/**` checkbox edits** §5.4.2 consequence 4 leaves in the tree |
   | 3.l, after loop P's verdict is recorded | every `verify/wave-{n}-post.round-{r}.md` |
   | 3.m, after `wave-issue-triage` writes the ledger and `freeze issues` regenerates the lock, before 3.n | `waves/wave-{n}/issues.md`, `waves/wave-{n}/issues.lock.json`. Two rows rather than one with 3.l's, because 3.m can halt on `wave-issues-open` or `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` and loop P's rounds must be durable before that halt |
   | 3.o, after the wave's `complete` line | `partition-postmortem.wave-{n}.md`, `partition-postmortem.lock.json` — its two phase-1 row sources are 3.f″'s coupling check and loop P at 3.l (§5.14), so it does not exist at 3.k |
   | Phase 4, after the final-verify verdict is recorded | every `verify/run.round-{r}.md`, including the rounds of each re-run after an appended wave |
   | Phase 6 | `00.run-report.md` |

   Without this the reachable failure is loud and total: a hermeticity leak at wave 3 prompts
   `git clean -fd` — this repository's own documented recovery, named in the first bullet above — and
   it deletes the frozen design, every lock the card's `invariant_digest` points at, and every
   verification round. The run then halts on `run-invariant-drift` with the design gone and nothing
   to recompute from. The commits also give the plan-checkbox edits a terminus, so the host tree is
   clean at each wave boundary rather than accumulating modifications forever.

---

## 5. The parallelism model

> **Phase split, applied.** §5.1, §5.2 and §5.3 are the **analysis**, and `08:96-97` ships them in
> `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` in full — *"the pure `computeLanePlan` with its declared inputs,
> the closed conflict-reason enum, stage layering, coupling analysis, `files[]` grounding, and the
> partition postmortem fed forward"*, as a frozen, **published, reviewed** artifact per wave. §5.10,
> §5.11, §5.12 and §5.14 also ship. §5.4–§5.9 and §5.13 are the **execution**, and they are in
> Part P2.
>
> The section keeps its title. It is still the parallelism model; what phase 1 does with the model is
> compute it, publish it, review it, and then walk it serially.

### 5.1 The DAG already exists; the scheduler does not

`kiwi-planner` already emits everything a scheduler needs:
`tasks[].depends_on_task?: string[]` (`skills/claude/kiwi-planner/SKILL.md:757`),
`tasks[].files[]` grounded in real files with an `[INFERRED:level]` label when estimated (`:32`),
`tasks[].test_files[]` (`:756`), `covers_ac[]` (`:758`), `req_ids[]`, phase `depends_on[]`, and the
TDD red/green pairing rule (`:145`, `:362`). `src/core/workflow/validate.ts` already parses the
sidecar into a catalog (`normalizeTasks`, `:149`), already detects cycles and missing dependencies
(`dependencyIssues`, `:173`), and already exposes `blockedBy`.

What is missing is a **ready-set projection** and a **conflict model**. `selectNextTask`
(`validate.ts:198-211`) is a serial cursor by construction: it walks tasks in declaration order and,
if the first not-done task is blocked, returns `{ nextTask: null, blockedBy }` (`:206`) —
head-of-line blocking. That is correct for `kiwi-pm` and useless for a scheduler.

**Two planner guarantees are weaker than they look, and the scheduler must not assume them.**
`C17` (`files[]` paths exist) is optional — `(선택, --check-files 시)` at `kiwi-planner:516`. `C24`
(`depends_on_task` acyclicity + referential integrity) is conditional on `--tdd-policy ≠ disabled`
at `:523`. Both must therefore be re-derived. **How they are re-derived is not symmetric, and the
first revision got the second one wrong.**

- **Cycle detection is re-run inside `computeLanePlan`.** It is pure graph work over the catalogue
  and costs nothing.
- **Path existence is *not*.** It is an impure precondition performed by `orchestrate schedule plan`
  **before** it calls the planner, and its result is passed **into** the pure function as an injected
  `existing_paths: string[]`. Its own gate is `files-not-grounded` (C).

**What `files-not-grounded` actually tests, because "declared to-be-created" has no mechanism.**
Revision 1's predicate was *"a sidecar `files[]` path does not exist **and is not declared to-be-created**"*.
Verified against the sidecar schema: `files: Array<{path, line_range?}>`
(`skills/claude/kiwi-planner/SKILL.md:744`) — **there is no to-be-created marker**, and the only
estimation signal, `[INFERRED:level]`, is routed to a different rule (`unknown-write-set`). Taken
literally the gate halts every greenfield lane, including **this document's own worked example**,
whose whole purpose is to create `src/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.ts` and its test. A gate that
refuses correct plans is not a compensation for §17.7's named weakness; it is a second defect.

The gate is therefore defined over what the data actually supports — a **near-miss detector**, which
is exactly the failure §5.1 describes (`task-catalogue.ts` written for `task-catalog.ts`) and nothing
wider:

> A `files[]` or `test_files[]` entry is **not grounded** when (a) it does not exist at the dispatch
> base **and** some existing repository path lies within Levenshtein distance 2 of it after POSIX
> normalisation — i.e. it is a probable typo for a real file — or (b) it exists and its `line_range`
> is outside the file's line count. A non-existent path with no near neighbour is a **legitimate new
> file** and passes.

Both halves are pure over `(declared_entries, existing_paths, line_counts)` — half (b) needs the
entry's `line_range` and the existing file's length, so the same impure step that collects
`existing_paths` also collects a `line_counts` map for those paths — so the detector lives in `src/`
and is table-testable; that *collection* is the impure half and stays in the command.
`--strict-grounding` tightens (a) to plain existence for repositories that never create files inside
a wave; it is off by default and journalled when used. The honest limit is stated in §17.7: this
catches a path that is a typo for a real file, not a path that exists and is simply the wrong one.

The first revision instead said *"`lane-plan.ts` re-runs its own … path-existence check, and
`orchestrate schedule plan` passes `--check-files` when it invokes the planner validator."* Both
halves fail:

- It contradicts four other statements in this document — §10.1 ("pure, deterministic"), §6.3's
  Constraints ("No `fs`, no `child_process`"), E6, and §4.7 drift digest 3, which requires
  byte-identical recomputation. **Purity wins**, and not merely because it is stated more often:
  determinism is what makes drift digest 3 possible at all, and a filesystem-dependent planner makes
  `lane-plan-drift` fire on any unrelated file creation — a worse and far more frequent failure than
  the one being prevented.
- `--check-files` is **not reachable from `src/`**. It exists only in the skill-bundled
  `validator.mjs`, whose path differs by variant (`skills/claude/kiwi-planner/validator.mjs` flat vs
  `skills/codex/kiwi-planner/scripts/validator.mjs` and `.agents/skills/kiwi-planner/scripts/validator.mjs`
  nested), and `grep -rn "check-files" src/` returns nothing. A CLI verb cannot pass a flag to a
  validator it does not invoke.

**What that mis-statement was protecting against is real and still needs a detector.** A sidecar typo
(`task-catalogue.ts` for `task-catalog.ts`) produces no `write-set-overlap` edge, so two tasks that
actually collide are scheduled into different lanes in the same stage; the real edit is never made,
and it surfaces at loop P, after promotion is queued. `files-not-grounded` is that detector, placed
where an impure check is legal. §17.7 is restated accordingly — the first revision claimed a
compensation for its own named weakness that structurally could not run.

**`normalizeTasks` must be extended.** `WorkflowTaskCatalogEntry` (`validate.ts:16-25`) and
`SidecarTask` (`:41-50`) carry no `files`, `test_files`, `covers_ac`, `type`, `action` or `tdd` —
exactly the inputs the classifier needs. The extension is additive parse only; existing consumers
read the same fields they read today. This is a change to a module already covered by
`test/core/workflow/validator.test.ts`, and that regression surface is declared, not hidden.

### 5.2 Conflict edges — a closed enum

`analyzeConflicts(catalog, registry, existingModules, priorPostmortems, constraints) → ConflictEdge[]`,
each `{ a, b, reason }` or `{ a, reason }` for unary barriers. **Five arguments, matching §10.1** —
revision 2 declared three here and four there, `learned-coupling` (§5.3) needs the fourth, and
`non-code-write-set`'s second clause needs the fifth (`constraints` = `{code_roots, test_roots}`).

| `conflict_reason` | Rule | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `task-dependency` | `b ∈ transitiveDeps(a)` from `depends_on_task` | orders lanes across stages |
| `phase-dependency` | phase `depends_on[]` lifted to task edges | orders lanes across stages |
| `write-set-overlap` | `(files ∪ test_files)(a) ∩ (files ∪ test_files)(b) ≠ ∅` after POSIX normalisation, repo-relative, case-folded on Windows, with one path being a prefix directory of the other counting as intersection | **same lane** |
| `tdd-pair` | same `covers_ac` with `tdd.phase` red / green (`kiwi-planner:145`, `:362`) | **same lane**, ordered inside it |
| `req-shared` | `req_ids` intersect | **same lane**, so a REQ's promotion after merge is atomic |
| `convergence-point` | any path in `files ∪ test_files` matches a `convergence-registry.json` entry (§5.9) | **consults `recipe.kind`** — see the rule below |
| `module-barrier` | the task's `action` declares a move / delete / signature change of an `existing_modules` entry (`kiwi-planner:51` §0.22) — the lexical predicate is stated after this table |  **stage barrier** — it invalidates every other lane's file coordinates |
| `unknown-write-set` | `files[]` empty, or any entry carrying `[INFERRED:` (`kiwi-planner:32`) | **stage barrier**, unless `--allow-inferred-write-set`, which is journalled |
| `srs-write` | the task's `type` or `action` implies an SRS mutation beyond `kiwi-coder §0.12`'s four — the lexical predicate is stated after this table | **serial epilogue** — charter C1 as a plan property, not a runtime error |
| `non-code-write-set` | the task's `type ∉ {code, perf_test}`, **or** any path in `files ∪ test_files` falls outside the run contract's declared code and test roots | **serial epilogue** — charter C1's *first* clause as a plan property (see below) |

**`shared-substrate` is deliberately absent from this enum, and revision 3 withdraws its
replacement too.** Revision 1 listed it here as *"a symbol appears in ≥2 **lanes'** `read_set`…"*.
That rule is circular (this function's output *computes* lanes, §5.3), quantifies over a handoff
field authored two phases later, and quantifies over symbols no input carries. Revision 2 moved it to
Phase 3.f″ and re-expressed it over **paths**; revision 3 finds that form **unsatisfiable as well**
— §6.2 layer 4 rejects the handoff that would trigger it one step earlier, and any file two lanes
each declare creating is already forced same-lane by `write-set-overlap` above. What survives at
3.f″ is the **cross-lane coupling** check (a path in one lane's `write_set` and another's
`read_set`), which is computable and is covered by no edge here. See §7.9 (a), gate
`stage-coupling-unresolved`, and escalation **X-04** (§0.7) for the full argument and what it costs.

**`non-code-write-set` is charter C1's first sentence, which nothing enforced.**
