`00.charter.md:135-136` has two clauses: *"A parallel task's write set may contain only **code and
tests**. No parallel task may perform an SRS mutation."* §5.10's C1 row discharged the second clause
four times over and never addressed the first, and no gate anywhere keyed on "this task is not code
or tests" — not the edge enum, not §6.2 layer 2 (which compares the handoff's `write_set` against the
lane's *computed* write set, whatever that contains), not §7.4 (whose rows are `written ⊆ declared`,
no orchestrator-only path, no SRS mutation — all of which a declared non-code path satisfies), and
not `forbidden[]`, which is a **closed list** of six path families and permits everything else.

The violation is not hypothetical: `kiwi-planner` enumerates `doc`, `file_op`, `issue`, `pr`,
`review`, `perf_test` and `infra` as first-class task types beside `code`
(`TaskType`, `skills/claude/kiwi-planner/SKILL.md:739`), and this design's **own** `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` work order
contains such a task — §10.5 step 10 edits `vitest.config.ts` and `.gitignore`. A task
`{type: "infra", files: ["vitest.config.ts"], test_files: []}` grounds cleanly, hits no registry
entry, raises no barrier, becomes lane-eligible, merges — and the *next* lane's merge gate then runs
the full suite against the thresholds that lane just changed, with every gate green.

The predicate needs a definition of "code and tests" that a consumer repository can supply, so
`design/constraints.json` gains two required arrays, authored at Phase 2.b and defaulted to
`code_roots: ["src/**"]`, `test_roots: ["test/**"]`:

> A task is lane-eligible under C1 only when its `type ∈ {code, perf_test}` **and**
> `(files ∪ test_files) ⊆ (code_roots ∪ test_roots)`. Anything else goes to the serial epilogue.
> `perf_test` is included because `kiwi-planner:521` treats it as TDD-bearing alongside `code`; `infra`
> is not, because its whole purpose is editing build and harness configuration.

Its runtime counterpart is the claim-audit row `no non-code path` and the gate `lane-non-code-write`
(C) — a lane that declares a legal write set and then writes `package.json` is caught by the same
comparison that catches any other lease breach, but the *class* is reported separately because it is
a charter-C1 breach rather than a scope error. §5.10's C1 row is restated accordingly.

**The `convergence-point` rule, which the first revision omitted.** A registry hit alone does not
decide anything; the matched point's `recipe.kind` does:

| `recipe.kind` of the matched point | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `exclusive-lane` | **lane-eligible**, under a whole-wave uniqueness constraint: at most one lane in the entire wave may own the unit, and every task touching it is forced into that one lane |
| `orchestrator-only` | **not lane-eligible** → serial epilogue |
| `regenerate` | **not lane-eligible** → serial epilogue (the generator runs there) |
| `replay` | **not lane-eligible** → serial epilogue |

Without this rule `exclusive-lane` is **unreachable**: the first revision sent *any* registry match
to the serial epilogue, so CP-03, CP-04 and CP-06 could never be assigned to a lane, and E8's test —
*"the conflict model **is** the closed `conflict_reason` enum"* — passed against a partitioner that
never honoured `exclusive-lane`, because the enum member existed and was dead. E8 is strengthened to
assert every `conflict_reason` **and** every `recipe.kind` is **reachable**, not merely present
(§15.1).

**Glob matching and point precedence, stated once and used by three callers.** Three sites match a
path against the registry with different inputs — the plan-time classifier over *declared* paths, the
claim audit over *actual* paths, and `integrate()`'s restore over *merged-index* paths — and revision
1 gave none of them a matcher.

- **Syntax.** `*` matches within one path segment and never crosses `/`; `**` matches zero or more
  whole segments; there is no brace or character-class syntax. `docs/spec/**` therefore matches
  `docs/spec/00.index.md` and `docs/spec/a/b.md`, and `src/cli/commands/*.ts` matches
  `src/cli/commands/read.ts` but not `src/cli/commands/sub/read.ts`.
- **Normalisation.** Repo-relative, POSIX separators, no leading `./`, case-folded on Windows — the
  same rule `write-set-overlap` uses, so a path cannot match one and miss the other.
- **Templating.** `{work}` is substituted at Phase 2.c from `--work`, and the substituted registry is
  what `registry_digest` covers. No other placeholder exists.
- **Precedence, because points legitimately overlap.** `docs/spec/00.index.md` matches **both** CP-01
  (`regenerate`) and CP-02 (`docs/spec/**`, `orchestrator-only`). §5.2's rule says *"the matched
  point's `recipe.kind`"*, singular, with no tie-break — so plan-time eligibility and merge-time
  restore could legitimately disagree about the same file. The total order is
  **`orchestrator-only` > `replay` > `regenerate` > `exclusive-lane`**, most-restrictive first, and it
  is applied identically at all three sites. `00.index.md` is therefore `orchestrator-only` for lane
  eligibility and for the merge restore, and CP-01's `regenerate` recipe is what the **serial
  epilogue** then runs over it — which is the behaviour both points were written to produce, now
  reachable by a stated rule instead of by luck.

**`srs-write`'s justification was wrong, and the rule it justified is right.** The first revision
argued: *"`kiwi/.srs.lock` is repo-root-relative (`src/core/mutation/srs-lock.ts:9`) with a 60 s TTL
(`:12`), so two concurrent lanes mutating SRS do not corrupt anything — they fail with `SRS-E065`."*
Report `02:152-160` measured the **opposite**: *"Root-relative. Two worktrees therefore hold two
independent locks that cannot see each other. Concurrent SRS mutation across roots is
**unguarded**."* `06:161` reaches the identical conclusion about `lease-lock.ts` — every lock path
derives from a `ProjectRoot`, so N worktrees over one `.git` get N locks and no mutual exclusion at
all.

So the correct statement is: **under Profile A the scheduling constraint is the only protection there
is.** `srs-write` → serial epilogue is not a convenience over a lock that would have caught the
violation anyway; it is the sole mechanism, and charter C1 and C2 hold **because** of it. An
implementer who trusted the old justification would be free to relax the rule, which is precisely
why the wrong reason was dangerous rather than merely inaccurate.

**The one lock this design does need, and where it comes from.** Nothing above protects against *two
orchestrator runs over one repository* — a different hazard from two lanes, and one the first
revision had no component for. `06:232` supplies it: harvest `lease-lock.ts` (H2) and **key it on the
git common dir**, not on a `ProjectRoot`, so N worktrees over one `.git` share one lock. Acquired at
Preflight P.5 — **before the probe, which writes** (§2.1) — and released at run end or
`abort-run`; gate `orchestrator-run-lock-held` (C). Its
harvest carries the §0.5 verification obligations, including a real cross-process test — the branch's
64-way race is 64 promises in one process (`06:357-359`).

`unknown-write-set` is the conservative default that makes the whole classifier safe: a task whose
write set cannot be grounded is a barrier, not a gamble.

**What `module-barrier` and `srs-write` actually compute, since `action` is a free-text `string`.**
Eight of the ten rows carry a mechanical predicate; these two were stated over what an `action`
*"declares"* or *"implies"*, which is not decidable by a pure function in `src/`. The predicate is
therefore stated here as a lexical test over the sidecar `action` string only, so `analyzeConflicts`
stays pure and E6-deterministic:

- `module-barrier` fires when `action`, case-folded, contains an `existing_modules` entry — full
  repo-relative path or basename — together with a member of the closed marker vocabulary
  `{move, moved, rename, renamed, relocate, delete, deleted, remove, removed, signature, extract,
  split}`.
- `srs-write` fires when `action`, case-folded, contains a member of the closed marker vocabulary of
  SpecKiwi mutation verbs — `{add_requirement, edit_requirement_fields, replace_acceptance_criteria,
  edit_requirement_table_rows, supersede_requirement, update_stability, register_scopes,
  scaffold_scope, append_section_note, promote_step_requirement, synthesize_step_srs}` — minus the
  four calls `kiwi-coder §0.12` already makes inline. `TaskType` (`kiwi-planner/SKILL.md:739`) has no
  `srs` member, so the `type` disjunct is dead and only this one is live.

The predicate is deliberately **conservative and its false negatives are declared, not denied**:
`kiwi-planner §0.22` makes stating the fact in `action` an *authoring* obligation, so an author who
omits it produces no edge. Two backstops already exist and are named rather than duplicated here —
gate `lane-srs-mutation-detected` with §P2 7.4's claim-audit row R6 and its host-root conjunct, and
`kiwi-coder §0.20.4`'s HALT on a structural change absent from `action` — and in phase 1 both edges
are ordering-only (§5.14 difference 6), so a miss moves a task's position, not its executor.

### 5.3 `computeLanePlan` — deterministic, pure, in `src/`

```
input  = { catalog, registry, existing_modules, existing_paths, prior_postmortems,
           design_item_map, lane_cap, code_roots, test_roots }
         existing_paths     is INJECTED, never read from disk (§5.1)
         code_roots,
         test_roots         are INJECTED from design/constraints.json by the command
                            (§5.1), never read from disk
         prior_postmortems  is the `couplings[]` projection out of
                            partition-postmortem.lock.json (§3.3a) — never Markdown
         design_item_map    is { req_id -> [D-nnn] }, recorded on the 3.b register-wave-srs
                            RESULT line (§4.2) and materialised at
                            waves/wave-{n}/design-item-map.json (§3.1)

lanes   = connected components of the union of same-lane edges
          (write-set-overlap ∪ tdd-pair ∪ req-shared ∪ exclusive-lane-unit ∪ learned-coupling),
          tasks sorted by id
stages  = topological layering of lanes under the induced lane-level dependency order
          (task-dependency ∪ phase-dependency), split by every barrier task
serial_epilogue = { convergence-point tasks whose matched recipe.kind ∈
                      {orchestrator-only, regenerate, replay} }  ∪  srs-write tasks
                  ∪  non-code-write-set tasks,
                  in declaration order
unassigned      = ∅ — the rules above are total: every task not routed to serial_epilogue is a
                  node of the same-lane edge union and so joins at least a singleton component.
                  The field is retained so the completeness invariant below stays a checkable
                  partition and so the phase-2 executor consumes an identical shape.
serialized      = the exact sequence selectNextTask would produce over the same catalog
```

**What each of the nine inputs does, because two of them did nothing.** Revision 1 declared
`existing_paths` and `prior_postmortems` as inputs and gave neither a rule, so E6's assertion that the
function *"takes path existence as an injected input"* had no observable consequence to test, and
§5.3's claim that the postmortem is *"the only mechanism by which partition quality improves"* had no
mechanism at all.

| input | effect, stated |
| --- | --- |
| `catalog` | the task graph; source of every edge |
| `registry` | the substituted convergence registry; source of `convergence-point` and its `recipe.kind` |
| `existing_modules` | source of `module-barrier` (`kiwi-planner:51` §0.22) |
| `existing_paths` | a declared path **not** in this set is a to-be-created file. **One effect, not two:** `write-set-overlap`'s prefix-directory rule is **not** applied to a non-existent path, because a directory that does not exist yet cannot be a shared parent. Revision 2 gave it a second effect — the denominator of §7.9 (a)'s shared-substrate check — and that check is withdrawn (X-04), so `planStageCoupling` takes no `existing_paths` argument. Grounding itself is the **command's** job (§5.1), so a `files-not-grounded` path never reaches this function |
| `prior_postmortems` | each `{from_task, to_task, path, detected_at, resolution}` row whose `resolution` is `merge-into-one-lane` emits a **`learned-coupling`** same-lane edge between `from_task` and `to_task` (or, when either task id no longer exists in this wave's sidecar, between the tasks whose `files[]` contain `path`). Rows with any other `resolution` — `re-planned`, `accepted` — are ignored by the partitioner and survive only as evidence. This is the whole of "partition quality improves": a coupling the merge gate caught in wave 2 forces same-lane in wave 3 |
| `design_item_map` | `lanes[k].design_items := ⋃ { design_item_map[r] : r ∈ lanes[k].req_ids }`, sorted, deduplicated. **This is the field's only producer, and revision 2 had none.** `lanes.lock.json` carried `design_items: string[]` per lane, the handoff required it, and loop H verifier 1's frozen denominator is `13 × \|task_ids\| + \|design_items\|` — while `computeLanePlan`'s inputs carried no design items and `kiwi-planner`'s `Task` interface has no design-item field (verified, `skills/claude/kiwi-planner/SKILL.md:741-759`). All three ways out were refused by the document: the function could not emit it, filling it afterwards breaks E6/E38 byte-determinism and §4.7 digest 3, and letting the 3.f handoff author assign it by judgment violates the engine rule that denominators are *"externally fixed … never computed by the verifier"* (`kiwi-wave-master:293-325`). Deriving it from an injected map fixed at 3.b satisfies all three |
| `lane_cap` | `--lanes N` (default 4, hard max 8). Overflow components are packed longest-first at component formation; after layering, any stage holding more than `N` lanes is split into consecutive stages, deterministically, longest-first (below). It is an input, not configuration: two calls differing only in `lane_cap` produce different bytes, which is why §3.3a records it and §4.7 digest 3 recomputes against the recorded value |
| `code_roots` / `test_roots` | the denominator of `non-code-write-set`'s second clause (§5.2) — `(files ∪ test_files) ⊆ (code_roots ∪ test_roots)`, and a task failing it lands in `serial_epilogue`. Injected from `design/constraints.json` by the command, never read from disk, exactly as `existing_paths` is. Like `lane_cap` they are inputs rather than configuration: two calls differing only in them produce different bytes, which is why §3.3a records them literally and §4.7 digest 3 recomputes against the recorded arrays |

`learned-coupling` is a member of the closed `conflict_reason` enum alongside the rows in §5.2, and
E8's reachability assertion covers it.

A `convergence-point` task whose matched recipe is **`exclusive-lane`** is **not** in the serial
epilogue: it joins the single wave-unique lane that owns that unit (`exclusive-lane-unit` above).
Without that term the `exclusive-lane` recipe is unreachable — see §5.2.

Rules and their reasons:

- **File overlap forces same-lane, not different-lane.** `files[]` overlap is the actual merge
  conflict predictor, and it is the same field `kiwi-coder §0.7`'s ZERO-TOLERANCE gate polices
  (`skills/claude/kiwi-coder/SKILL.md:24`).
- **Determinism is a contract.** The same sidecar plus the same registry yields a byte-identical
  `lanes.lock.json`. Without it, §4.7's drift detection is impossible.
- **`serialized` is cross-checked against `selectNextTask`.** A unit test asserts they are
  identical, so "degrading to serial is behaviour-preserving" is a measured claim, not an argued
  one.
- **Completeness invariant, checked not assumed.** Every sidecar `task_id` appears **exactly once**
  across `lanes ∪ serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned`. This is the partition-layer analogue of the
  decomposition coverage gate; a silently dropped task is the parallel-execution equivalent of an
  unassigned design item.
- **Post-conditions asserted as internal errors**, not warnings: within a stage, no cross-lane
  dependency edge and no cross-lane write-set overlap can exist. If either is found, the partitioner
  is wrong.
- **Lane cap `--lanes N`, default 4, hard max 8, and it means *per stage*.** It bounds **concurrent
  workspaces**, which is the quantity §17.2's 950 MB and §4.1's 8-entry `open[]` budget are computed
  against. Overflow components are packed longest-first at component formation; then, **after
  layering, any stage holding more than `N` lanes is split into consecutive stages**,
  deterministically, longest-first. Revision 2 packed per wave (components exist before stages) while
  §2.1 rule 4, §4.1's card budget and §17.2 all read it per stage — and a per-wave cap of 4 can still
  place 4 lanes in one stage while a per-stage cap cannot be applied before layering exists. Splitting
  after layering is the only order in which both statements are true, and it keeps the function pure
  and byte-deterministic.

  **In phase 1 the cap bounds nothing concurrent — there are no concurrent workspaces — and it is
  retained for two reasons that survive the scope cut.** It keeps `computeLanePlan` byte-identical
  across the two phases, so the phase-2 executor consumes exactly the artifact a phase-1 run
  published; and the per-stage split is what makes the published partition legible at a granularity a
  reviewer can check at 3.e′. A phase-1 run with `--lanes 8` gets up to eight units per stage and
  executes all eight one after another.
- A lane with a single task and no dependents is folded into the serial epilogue — per-lane overhead
  is fixed and per-lane gain is proportional to the work inside.
- **A stage with one lane is executed exactly like any other stage.** Revision 2's rule — *"a stage
  with fewer than 2 lanes runs serially in-tree, journalled, with no isolation cost"* — is
  **deleted**. It contradicted three things at once: profile immutability (§5.4) and the handoff's
  `isolation: worktree` field, which have no in-tree value; §5.6's `--serial` guarantee, which
  invokes this very rule for *all* stages while promising the claim audit is kept; and §5.6's two
  dirty-tree assertions, which are declared Profile-B-only, so an in-tree stage under a `worktree`
  profile had **no isolation enforcement of any kind**. Under Profile A a mixed wave — stage 1 with
  three lanes, stage 2 with one — would send stage 2 down a path with no claim audit, which is the
  only mechanical detector of `lane-srs-mutation-detected` and `lane-non-code-write`. The saving was
  one worktree; the cost was two incompatible execution paths and a hole in charter C1 enforcement.
  Under Profile B a single-lane stage is already in-tree by construction, so nothing is lost there
  either. See §16 R41.

Blocking outcomes of `orchestrate schedule plan`: an ungrounded `files[]` path (`files-not-grounded`,
raised by the **command** before the pure function is called, §5.1); dependency cycle
(`schedule-cycle`); a `tdd-pair` split across lanes (`tdd-pair-split`); a barrier stage that is
non-empty of other lanes; any `unknown-write-set` task without the flag
(`unknown-write-set-refused`); a `non-code-write-set` task that some other same-lane edge has already
pulled into a lane, which the epilogue assignment cannot then undo (`non-code-write-set-refused`);
**two lanes in different stages** each claiming the same `exclusive-lane` unit; any post-condition
failure.

*(The `exclusive-lane` guard is stated across stages deliberately. Within one stage it is unreachable
by construction — `exclusive-lane-unit` is a same-lane edge in the connected-component union, so every
task touching the unit lands in one lane. Across stages it is reachable and real: the constraint is
whole-**wave** uniqueness while lanes are formed per component and layered per stage, so a unit
touched by two tasks in different topological layers produces two owning lanes. Revision 1 stated the
guard in the unreachable form.)*

**`existing_paths` is a per-wave snapshot, and its staleness at stage ≥ 2 is accepted rather than
fixed.** It is collected once, by `orchestrate schedule plan` at 3.e (§5.1), so a file that stage 1
creates and merges still reads as absent when the lock is consulted for stage 2. Its only surviving
consumer inside the function is the prefix-directory suppression in `write-set-overlap`, and the
staleness makes that rule *less* likely to force same-lane — never more. The residual exposure is
narrow and self-closing: a directory that first appears mid-wave is created by a lane whose write set
already declares the file inside it, so the overlap is caught on the **file**, which is the stronger
signal anyway. §4.7 digest 3 already records an `existing_paths_digest` change as a `W` and refuses
to recompute the lock mid-wave, so this is consistent rather than a second opinion. (Revision 2's
other consumer, the 3.f″ shared-substrate hoist, is withdrawn — §7.9 (a) — which removes the case
where staleness could have produced a wrong *action* rather than a weaker check.)

**Cross-wave learning.** After each wave, `partition-postmortem.wave-{n}.md` records every coupling
that the plan-time classifier missed but the merge gate caught (§7.5), as
`{from_task, to_task, path, detected_at, resolution}`. It is **wave-qualified, digested into
`partition-postmortem.lock.json`, and journalled**, exactly like every other verification input — in
the first revision it was the only artifact in §3.1 with none of the three, and it was edited in
place, against §4.7's own append-new-artifact rule. The **next** wave's lane planner reads the union
of every prior wave's postmortem, resolved through the latest journalled pointer. This is the only
mechanism in the design by which partition quality improves rather than repeating its mistakes, which
is why a silently overwritten or misattributed one is worse than none at all.

### 5.4–5.9 The isolation interface, the three profiles, the selection rule and the convergence-registry integrator — deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`

`08` §3 defers *"worktree isolation entirely — the profile, the isolation probe, acquire/release/reap,
the heartbeat, lane timeouts, the join loop, liveness, the remediation branch family, per-worktree
`npm ci` with its `NODE_ENV` pin and concurrency controls, the Windows path budget, and the consumer
`.gitignore` remediation"*, and *"the lane execution apparatus — per-lane dispatch, the lane commit
obligation, … the pre-merge per-lane loop, per-lane merge with the compare-and-swap publish, per-path
restore on integrate, the deferred SRS-mutation queue and its replay"* (`08:116-130`). That is the
whole of §5.4 through §5.9.

**Moved verbatim to Part P2, under their original numbers `§5.4`, `§5.4.1`, `§5.4.2`, `§5.5`–`§5.5.3`,
`§5.6`, `§5.7`, `§5.8` and `§5.9`.** Nothing in them is withdrawn or weakened; they are the input
phase 2 is built from, and the harvest rulings they carry (H4's CAS technique in §5.9) travel with
them.

Three consequences inside phase 1, stated here because §5.10, §5.11 and §5.12 below still cite these
sections:

- **Preflight P.6, the isolation probe, does not run.** `isolation-profile-unavailable` and
  `isolation-profile-change-requested` are not phase-1 gates, and `frozen.isolation_profile` is
  recorded as the constant `"none-serial"` so the resume card's `invariant_digest` keeps a total
  field set (§4.1).
- **`integrate()` does not run.** Phase 1 has no lane branch to merge; the executor writes on the
  integration branch directly (§5.14).
- **Charter C4 survives the deferral.** `validate` then `sync-index` is attached to the end of each
  wave's serial execution in §5.14 instead of to each lane merge, and `post-merge-index-drift` keeps
  its `critical_gates[]` row there.

### 5.10 How every binding constraint is discharged

**The table below is the phase-2 discharge, and it is retained unchanged because that is what phase 2
is built from. Phase 1's discharge is different for all seven: C1, C2, C3, C5, C6 and C7 because
they are all constraints on *parallel* execution, and phase 1 has none; C4 because its attachment
point moves from each lane merge to 3.k (§5.14, RS-08).**

| Constraint | Phase-1 discharge |
| --- | --- |
| **C1a / C1b** | **Vacuous as a runtime constraint, live as a scheduling one.** No task is parallel, so neither clause binds any execution. The `non-code-write-set` and `srs-write` edges still fire and still order those tasks last within the wave (§5.14 difference 6), and the SRS mutations they gate now execute at the host in the normal way. `lane-non-code-write` and `lane-srs-mutation-detected` are phase-2 gates |
| **C2** | **Live, and enforced earlier than it was.** Ids are still allocated at 3.b before any execution, and `unallocated-req-id` at 3.c′ (§5.11) is the check. What is gone is the *runtime* backstop — a claim audit catching an `add_requirement` in a manifest — which mattered because two worktrees once allocated `FR-ARCH-001` independently. Serial execution cannot produce that race |
| **C3** | **Binds nothing.** No worktree is created. The lane apparatus travels to phase 2; ruling X-01 is closed by charter C3 (§0.7) |
| **C4** | **Live, re-attached.** `validate` → `sync-index` at 3.k rather than after each lane merge, `post-merge-index-drift` (C) unchanged (§5.14) |
| **C5** | **Binds nothing.** No worktree agent, so no split-brain. The orchestrator's own MCP `workspaceRoot` is still compared against the git toplevel at P.1 |
| **C6** | **Live as classification, dormant as protection.** The registry and the `recipe.kind` precedence order still classify paths and still route `orchestrator-only` / `regenerate` / `replay` work to 3.k. The "loud" and "silent" merge classes describe concurrent writers, of which phase 1 has none |
| **C7** | **Mostly dormant.** No per-worktree `npm ci`, no path-budget pressure from `.claude/worktrees/`, no N-concurrent-install question. What survives is CRLF: `core.autocrlf=true` with no `.gitattributes` still makes `git status --porcelain` unreliable. The phase-1 duplication audit is unaffected — it reads the wave's **commit range** (§7.9 (b)), not a working-tree status. The one phase-1 mechanism that does read `git status --porcelain` is **`dispatch-base-dirty`** (§2.1 ordering rule 1; critical, §13), evaluated once per stage per wave over the handoff front-matter path union. It is knowingly exposed to CRLF churn: a checkout whose blobs are LF under `core.autocrlf=true` can report those paths dirty with no edit, and the run halts. The operator remedy is a `.gitattributes` `text=auto` entry, or a checkout whose blobs are already LF. Hardening the gate's own mechanism is a separate designed change, not part of this discharge. *(The CRLF paragraph below is the **phase-2 claim-audit** rule; §7.3–7.5 are Part P2, so it has no phase-1 subject.)* |

| Constraint | Where it is handled — **phase 2** |
| --- | --- |
| **C1a** a parallel task's write set contains only **code and tests** | `non-code-write-set` as a scheduling edge §5.2, defined against `constraints.json`'s `code_roots` / `test_roots`; claim-audit row *no non-code path*; gate `lane-non-code-write` (C). **This row is new in revision 2.** Revision 1's C1 row addressed only the second clause and discharged it four times over, so a reader auditing C1 compliance stopped at a row that did not cover half of C1 — while `kiwi-planner` emits seven non-code task types by contract (`:739`) and this design's own work order contains one |
| **C1b** no SRS mutation in a parallel task | Lane contract §5.4; `srs-write` as a scheduling edge §5.2; `--defer-srs-mutation` **with its `kiwi-pm` pass-through** (§5.4); gate `lane-srs-mutation-detected` with a **host-side** conjunct, because a lane's MCP writes land at the host root (`02:203`) and a lane-delta check cannot see them. **Note §5.2**: the scheduling constraint is the *only* protection — `kiwi/.srs.lock` gives no cross-worktree exclusion (`02:152-160`) |
| **C2** ids allocated on the host before fan-out | Phase 3.b precedes 3.g; §5.11's derived readiness plus the `unallocated-req-id` gate at 3.c′ makes it checkable rather than assumed; `req_ids[]` and `acceptance[]` are **required, fixed inputs** of the handoff schema §6; gate `lane-srs-mutation-detected` catches an `add_requirement` in a manifest |
| **C3** worktrees cut at session-start HEAD | **Granted by charter C3 (`00.charter.md:145-149`) — X-01 closed (§0.7).** On the narrowed reading: the spawn prompt moves the lane to `base_sha` (§5.5); Phase 3.f′ commits the per-stage dispatch base; probe assertion 2, rewritten to exercise a post-worktree sha. **Profile B is immune and is the fallback for the *whole* of Profile A if the ruling goes the other way** — not merely for stages ≥ 2, because the lane's own handoff is also committed mid-session, so a stage-1 lane cannot read it either. §0.7 X-01 and §5.5's *"what assertion 2 decides"* are restated to match this row, which was right all along |
| **C4** `validate` then `sync-index` after every merge | §5.9, unconditional, gated by `post-merge-index-drift` |
| **C5** worktree agents born in split-brain | Made irrelevant by the lane contract; probe assertion 6 confirms it is present and expected. The branch's per-root MCP answer is rejected (`06:166-195`, `02:402`) |
| **C6** merge classes | For **this repository's** run, the convergence registry §5.9 covers every surface `00.charter.md:170-173` names, because CP-03..CP-06 are authored at Phase 2.c. For a **consumer**, CP-03..CP-06 are examples and the registry ships only CP-01/CP-02/CP-07 (§5.9, RV-17) — so consumer coverage of C6's "loud" class rests on `write-set-overlap` forcing same-lane plus `non-code-write-set` (§5.2) plus `forbidden[]`'s ban on `package.json`. That holds, and it is **not** the same statement as *"the registry covers every named surface"*, which is what revision 1's row said after RV-17 had moved three of the four cited points out of the shipped default. `speckiwi init` / skill mirrors are `orchestrator-only` and never parallel |
| **C7** Windows costs | Pinned `NODE_ENV=development npm ci` + `npx vitest --version`, ordered **after** the checkout; probe assertions 4 and 5 (5 corrected to the 188-char `node_modules` extremum and reordered after 4); `--install-concurrency` default 2 for the untested N-concurrent case; cost printed in the run header; CRLF handled by §7.4's normalisation rule |

**CRLF.** `core.autocrlf=true` with no `.gitattributes` (`02` §7.2) means a checkout can report
whole-file modifications on files nobody touched. Every path comparison in the claim audit is made
against `git diff --name-only <base>..<head>` — a **committed-content** diff, which is
line-ending-normalised by git — never against `git status --porcelain` of a fresh checkout. Content
digests in the manifest are advisory; the merge-base diff is authoritative.

### 5.11 Requirement readiness is derived, never trusted — harvest H3

`06:233` adds a ruling the first revision of this design had no place for: *"Wherever the design reads
requirement readiness before dispatch, it derives rather than trusts."* The gap it closes was real:
`/kiwi-srs` (3.b) and `/kiwi-planner` (3.c) carried only `child-srs-needs-user-or-failed`, and
nothing asserted the invariant charter C2 actually needs — *every task in the sidecar carries a
`req_id` from the 3.b allocation set*. Handoff resolvability catches an unresolvable id but not a
task whose `req_ids` is **empty**, and neither catches an id allocated somewhere other than 3.b.

Phase 3.c′ therefore runs two checks over a fresh snapshot:

1. **Allocation check.** `⋃ sidecar.tasks[].req_ids` ⊆ the 3.b allocation set recorded on the
   `register-wave-srs` result line; no task has an empty `req_ids`; **and every allocated `req_id`
   carries at least one `[D-nnn]` in the wave's `design_item_map`, with every `design_items[]` entry
   of the wave appearing against at least one `req_id`.** Violation: `unallocated-req-id` (C). This
   is charter C2 as a plan property rather than a runtime hope, and the second conjunct is what makes
   `computeLanePlan`'s sixth input (§5.3) a checked operand rather than an assumption.

   **How the allocation set and the map are obtained, because revision 2 named neither producer.**
   `unallocated-req-id` compared against *"the 3.b allocation set recorded on the `register-wave-srs`
   result line"* and nothing said how the orchestrator obtains it from `/kiwi-srs`, while E34 tests
   the comparison. The derivation, all mechanical and recomputable:
   - **Before** the `/kiwi-srs` hop, capture `list_requirements --target wave-{n}` and record its
     sha256 as `allocation.pre_snapshot_digest` on the `register-wave-srs` **intent** line.
   - **After** it returns, capture the same query again. `allocation.requirement_ids[]` is the
     **set difference**, sorted. A snapshot whose digest no longer matches
     `pre_snapshot_digest` on a resume means the set cannot be re-derived, and the verb's
     `externally-visible` recovery rule (§4.4) sends the session to `list_requirements` and the
     `srs_authored` marker (`kiwi-wave-master:227`) rather than to a guess.
   - **`allocation.design_item_map`** is authored by the **orchestrator**, not by `/kiwi-srs`: it
     knows which `[D-nnn]` items it handed to the wave (they are `waves.lock.json`'s per-wave
     `design_items[]` slice) and which requirement each was registered as. It is written to
     `waves/wave-{n}/design-item-map.json` (§3.1), journalled, and digested into `lanes.lock.json`
     as `design_item_map_digest`. This is §1.1's boundary rule applied once more: the agent marks the
     mapping, the tool checks that the mapping is total in both directions.
2. **Derived readiness.** `deriveCanonicalRequirementReadiness(snapshot, target, requirementIds)` and
   `scopeRequirementDiagnostics` — harvested verbatim from `wave-spec-client.ts:456-557` with their
   15 behavioural tests — compute `hardDependenciesSatisfied` / `evidenceDrift` / `ownershipVerified`
   from raw records, fail-closed on cycles, duplicate ids and summary contradiction. Violation:
   `requirement-not-ready` (C).

The point of harvesting these two functions rather than writing an equivalent is stated by the audit:
they are pure over `(snapshot, target, requirementIds)`, so **a caller cannot self-attest readiness**
(`06:162`). They work over a snapshot from any transport; the §0.5 obligation is to re-run all 15
cases against a `speckiwi list --json` snapshot to prove that. The 780 lines of MCP client around
them are **not** taken (`06:166-195`).

### 5.12 Where a run ends, and what the user is left holding

Four gates — `wave-verify-fail-residual`, `post-merge-index-drift`, `srs-mutation-replay-failed`
(**phase 2 only**, §13) and `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` — are terminal halts that occur
**after work has landed**: in phase 1, as commits on the integration branch (phase 1 performs no
merge, §5.14); in phase 2, after real merges. The first revision's verb enum had only `halt`, marked *"terminal"*, and the document never
stated what repository state the user was left in. That is not a survivable ledger property; it is
the difference between a halt and an abandonment.

**`abort-run`** is a first-class verb (§4.4) with its own recovery class and journal line. **In phase 1
step 1 is a no-op and step 3 loses two of its rows** — there is no workspace to reap, so the report
names commits and branches only, and the *"could not be reaped, possibly still running"* row that
revision 3 called the state a halted run is most likely to be in does not exist. Both return with the
lanes (§P2 5.5.1). It:

1. reaps every live workspace of the current stage (§5.5.1), lifecycle-aware — **phase 2 only**;
2. leaves `frozen.integration_branch` **exactly where it is** — merges are never unwound, because
   unwinding a merge whose lanes passed their claim audit destroys verified work to tidy a ledger;
3. writes `docs/research/{work}/00.run-report.md` naming: the integration branch and its sha, which
   waves are `complete`, which lanes merged, which lanes were reaped un-merged and where their
   branches are, **which lanes could not be reaped and are therefore possibly still running, with
   their workspace paths** (§5.5.1's *"reap that fails"* row — revision 2's report had no row for
   this state, which is the one it is most likely to be in), the gate that ended the run, and the
   exact command to resume if the cause is fixable;
4. releases the §5.2 run lock.

**Step 1 never forces a removal.** `git worktree remove` fails on a locked worktree (`02:356-358`),
and a lock means an agent may be alive. `abort-run` records the failure and reports the path; it does
not pass `--force`, because forcing it destroys work an operator may want. This is the same rule 3.j′
follows, and it is why the report has the row above.

**End-of-run disposition, for a run that completes normally.** The integration branch is left in
place and named in the run report. **The orchestrator never merges it to the base branch and never
opens a PR** — that is a deliberate boundary, restated in §14 item 2, and the reason `--auto` does
not auto-chain commit/push. `--base-branch` (§14.1) exists so the integration branch has a
defined fork point, not so the orchestrator can write to it. **The run report states one obligation the
orchestrator cannot discharge for the user:** whoever later merges `frozen.integration_branch` into
`base_branch` must run `validate` then `sync-index` on the base branch immediately after that merge —
charter C4 makes it mandatory rather than advisory, because that merge is exactly the two-concurrent-branch
case whose C1 silent class (`00.charter.md:135-139`) the merge itself gives no signal for.

### 5.13 The serial epilogue as a distinct executor — deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`

`08:126-127` defers *"the serial epilogue as a distinct executor"*. It is distinct only by contrast with
lanes: it exists so that work charter C1 bars from a **parallel** task is still executed somewhere.
With no parallel task in phase 1 there is one executor, not two.

**Moved verbatim to Part P2, under its original number `§5.13`.** Its host-execution *mechanism* —
one handoff, `/kiwi-pm` run at the host root on the integration branch, an explicit pathspec, `Orch-*`
trailers, one commit per Task — is **not** deferred: §5.14 is that mechanism, generalised from *the
epilogue's task set* to *the wave's task set*. What defers with §5.13 is the split — its
`--lane epilogue` claim-audit row set, the deferred-mutation replay it
drives, and the gate `epilogue-task-failed`, whose phase-1 counterpart is `serial-unit-failed`
(§5.14, §13).

**Loop H does not run on the phase-1 epilogue handoff either, and the phase-1 reason is positional
rather than executor-based.** The phase-2 reason at §P2 5.13 — *"the epilogue's executor is the host
with the whole run's context, so the cold-agent probe measures nothing"* — does not discriminate in
phase 1, where §5.14 gives **every** unit that same host executor (§2.1 3.k: *"Same executor as 3.g,
not a distinct one"*). What discriminates is where the document is authored: loop H is scoped to
**3.f**, over the stage's `lanes/lane-{k}.md` set, and the epilogue handoff does not exist until
**3.k**, after every stage of the wave has already executed. There is no round for it to enter and no
re-authoring window before its own execution. It still gets all five `validateHandoff` layers and a
freeze at 3.k (§6.2, §2.1 3.k activity (0)), so `handoff-not-english`, `handoff-unresolvable-reference`
and `handoff-untested-ac-over-cap` all fire on it; what it does not get is loop H's two verifiers and
`handoff-verify-failed`. **This is a divergence from `00.charter.md:60-62`**, which states the
verify-and-improve loop as a property of handoff documents as a class, and it is registered as such at
§17.17.

### 5.14 Serial execution at the host — phase 1's whole executor

**New in revision 4.** `08:118-119`: *"Implementation runs serially over the wave plan on the
integration branch, as `kiwi-wave-master` does today."* This section is that sentence made
operational. It is §5.13's host-execution mechanism generalised from *the epilogue's task set* to
*the wave's task set*, and it is the only executor in `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`.

**The walk.** For each stage of the wave in the frozen plan's topological order, and within a stage
for each lane in lane-id order:

```
/kiwi-pm --handoff docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md
         --session-suffix w{n}s{s}l{k} --no-final --no-pipeline-emit --commit-lane-work
         [--resume] [--auto] [--max] [--model <name>] [--mini|--loops N]
         [--regression-baseline <the P.4 pin>]
```

run at the **host root**, on `frozen.integration_branch`, one at a time, each waited for before the
next begins.

**Six differences from a lane dispatch, each with a reason.**

1. **No worktree and no lane branch.** Nothing to isolate from: exactly one unit is executing.
2. **No `--defer-srs-mutation`.** Charter C1 forbids SRS mutation *in a parallel task*; there is no
   parallel task, so `kiwi-coder`'s four mandatory MCP mutations execute where they were always meant
   to, at the host root, against the run's own target. This is the largest simplification the scope
   cut buys: the deferred-mutation queue, its replay, its dedupe index and
   `srs-mutation-replay-failed` all disappear with it (§P2 5.9).
3. **No claim audit.** §P2 7.4 exists because a lane's self-report is unverifiable when the lane ran
   where the host could not watch. Here the host *is* the watcher: the commit range on the
   integration branch **is** the record, and it is derived, not reported.
4. **`--commit-lane-work` is still required**, and the reason is unchanged from §P2 5.4: without it
   `kiwi-pm` commits nothing, and *"the work is written where it will live"* would leave the wave's
   output as an uncommitted working tree that the next unit's run then trips over.
5. **Commits keep the `Orch-*` trailers** — `Orch-Run`, `Orch-Wave`, `Orch-Stage`, `Orch-Lane`,
   `Orch-Task` — and no subject markers (X-02). They are the phase-1
   witness for `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` (§4.1), the input to the duplication audit's commit range,
   and the plan-checkbox replay's key.
6. **The `non-code-write-set` and `srs-write` scheduling edges still fire, and still mean something
   weaker.** They classify a task as *not lane-eligible*, and in phase 1 that classification changes
   the unit's **position** — such tasks land in `serial_epilogue` and are executed at 3.k order-last —
   rather than its **executor**, which is the same one. The edges are kept rather than disabled
   because they are part of the published analysis phase 2 consumes, and because ordering non-code
   and SRS work after the code of the same wave is independently correct.

**Gate `serial-unit-failed` (C).** A unit whose `verification_cmd` exits non-zero after one retry
against the same handoff; or that produced no commit and declared no `intentionally_empty` reason in
its `/kiwi-pm` run's own `docs/analysis/` bundle — **that bundle is phase 1's carrier for the
declaration, not §P2 3.4's lane manifest, which phase 1 does not produce (§3.1)**; or
whose `/kiwi-pm` returned `NEEDS_USER` or `FAILED`. It is `epilogue-task-failed` generalised: one
executor, therefore one gate. `epilogue-task-failed` returns in phase 2, when a second executor does
(§P2 5.13). A child's own always-halt gate still halts the parent unconditionally, through the
inherited `child-pipeline-needs-user-or-failed`.

**The intentionally-empty disposition, stated once here and referenced from §7.6 and §7.10.** The
escape above makes a commitless unit a *legal* outcome, and revision 4 left that outcome with no
downstream carrier: §7.6's fifth denominator and §7.10's *landed* both key on a trailered commit, so
a unit that took the escape produced a task that could never be `checked`, `ALL_MATCH` was forbidden,
and loop P failed with `wave-verify-fail-residual` — a **terminal** halt (§5.12) reached *after* work
landed. The sanctioned escape was strictly worse than not taking it. Three things are decided:

1. **Granularity and carrier — per task, in the unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle.** The gate's
   own predicate is per unit because a unit that commits nothing at all is the only case it can see
   from the tree; the disposition is per **task**, because a five-task unit in which four tasks commit
   never fires the gate and yet still has one task §7.6 counts in `expected`. Phase 1's carrier is the
   same bundle the gate already names (C-111): the unit declares, per task id, an
   `intentionally_empty` entry carrying the task id, a `reason` of ≥ 20 characters (§6.5 closure 2's
   bar, reused rather than reinvented) and the witness below. §P2 3.4's manifest field stays the
   **phase-2** carrier and is per lane; it is not back-ported.
2. **The witness — a passing `verification_cmd` over an unchanged write set, not a self-report.**
   §7.6 refuses the lane's own worklog `TASK_DONE` as a conjunct because it *"adds no independence"*,
   and that refusal is kept: an `intentionally_empty` entry is admissible **only** when the task's
   `verification_cmd` exits zero **and** no path in the task's `write_set` differs between the unit's
   base and head on the integration branch. The command is the same externally re-runnable artifact
   §7.6 already trusts, and the unchanged-write-set conjunct is recomputed from the tree by the
   orchestrator, not asserted by the unit — so the declaration selects *which* rule applies and the
   two checkable facts decide whether it holds. An entry failing either conjunct is not a legal
   declaration: the task is unlanded and `serial-unit-failed` / the §7.6 denominator apply unchanged.
3. **The disposition — `checked`, not removed from `expected`.** Removing it would shrink the
   denominator on a unit's own say-so, which is the vacuous-denominator failure AC-18 already had to
   repair once. The task stays in `expected` and enters `checked` on the two conjuncts above, so the
   count is unchanged and the evidence is the passing command. For §7.10 the task counts as *landed*
   with `type="test"` evidence only — its `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle entry — and no
   `type="commit"` ref, since there is no commit; `00.charter.md:297-298` is satisfied by the passing
   `verification_cmd` and the recomputed empty diff, neither of which is the unit's own assertion.

**Charter C4 attaches to the wave, not to a merge.** `validate` then `sync-index` runs at 3.k, after
the last unit of the last stage and before loop P, with `post-merge-index-drift` (C) unchanged. C4's
own wording is *"after every merge"*; phase 1 performs no merge, and the honest reading is that the
obligation attaches to *the point at which SRS-affecting work has landed*, which is 3.k. C1's silent
class — two branches adding requirements to different scope documents, merging cleanly and producing
wrong rollup counts — cannot occur without concurrent branches, so C4's *detector* role is not what
is being relied on here; its *repair* role still is, because §5.14 difference 2 puts real SRS
mutations back in the wave.

**What feeds the partition postmortem now.** §5.3 calls it *"the only mechanism by which partition
quality improves"*, and revision 3's source for its rows was the merge gate (§P2 7.5) — both lanes
green in isolation, red merged. Phase 1 has no merge gate. Its two surviving sources: 3.f″'s
cross-lane coupling check, which records every `write_set ∩ read_set` hit it forced into one lane;
and loop P, whose findings are attributed to a unit by the `Orch-Lane` trailer on the commit that
introduced the defect. **This is a real reduction and it is stated as one**: a coupling that only
manifests when two units' work is combined is exactly what a serial run cannot observe, because the
work is never separated in the first place. Phase 1's postmortem is therefore a weaker signal than
phase 2's, and the rows it does record carry `detected_at: "coupling-check"` or `"loop-P"` so a
phase-2 session can tell them apart from `"merge-gate"` rows.

**Resume.** The verb is `execute-unit` and its recovery class is `externally-visible` (§4.4): a unit
interrupted mid-run has committed some of its Tasks and not others, and re-running it duplicates
work. Recovery reads the `Orch-Task` trailers on the integration branch and `workflow_plan_status`,
and re-enters `/kiwi-pm` only for Tasks with neither a trailer nor a checked box. `--resume` is passed when `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/w{n}s{s}l{k}/pm-state.json` exists — `--session-suffix`'s relocation (§5.4) of the path `09` §5.2 names — because that file, not the plan checkboxes, is what makes `kiwi-pm` skip `status="done"` Tasks; without it the re-entry is a **new session** and re-executes the unit's completed Tasks (§22.1 AA-22), which is the duplicate-implementation class §4.4 forbids by name.

---

## 6. The handoff document

> **This section ships in full.** `08:92-94` keeps *"per-stage English handoff documents for coding
> agents — the full schema, the five-layer validator, the verification loop, the English-only gate,
> and the untested-acceptance-criterion cap with a required reason and owner"*, and names that cap as
> what closes the one critical defect a serial cut does **not** remove: critic 2's `test_id: null`
> hole, where a handoff declaring every acceptance criterion untested lets a unit pass every audit and
> promote a requirement with no executable evidence (`08:30-33`). §6.5 is therefore the single most
> load-bearing subsection in phase 1.
>
> **Seven things inside it are phase 2 and are marked where they occur:** the `manifest_template` field
> and the `## Manifest` heading (their subject, §3.4's lane manifest, is deferred — phase 1's executor
> reports through commits and its child's own bundle); `heartbeat_path` and `decisions_path`;
> `deferred_mutations_path` together with `forbidden[]`'s deferral line (§5.14 difference 2 leaves
> phase 1 with no queue, no replay and no `srs-mutation-replay-failed`; the field is written and
> unread and §6.3 carries the phase-1 wording); the
> `isolation` field; the `branch` field (`null` in phase 1 for every `handoff_kind` — §5.14's executor
> creates no lane branch; the `kiwi/orch/…` forms return in phase 2); **`bootstrap`'s `install` and `record` entries** (phase 1's unit runs at the host root in the
> operator's existing, already-installed checkout — §5.14, §14.1's *no per-unit `npm ci` in phase 1* — and
> `08:120-123` defers the heartbeat in full, so its writer goes with it; the `assert` entries stay); and the `remediation` value of `handoff_kind`, leaving
> `lane` and `epilogue` as the phase-1 kinds. **`epilogue` is phase 1's carrier for the `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned` task set
> (§5.14 difference 6), run by the same §5.14 executor; what defers with §P2 5.13 is its
> `--lane epilogue` claim-audit row set and the gate `epilogue-task-failed`, not the document.** In
> phase 1 `demoted` is empty, so §6.2 layer 2's equality is against `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned`.
> The schema is **not** re-cut to remove them: `validateHandoff` is one function
> across both phases, and a field set that changes between targets would fork the validator, the
> `lane-{k}.lock.json` schema and the loop-H denominator. In phase 1 those fields are present and
> unread.

One per lane, at `docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md`. **Written in English.**
It is the executing agent's *complete* input: it sees this document, the plan, the sidecar and the
repository, and nothing else — plus, in phase 2, the spawn prompt at §P2 5.5, which adds no
information and only names which document to read. It is committed at Phase 3.f′ before the unit
runs.

`kiwi-pm` already assembles this material — but as an ephemeral spawn prompt
(`skills/claude/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md:400-409`). It is written to a path here for the reason wave-master
gives when it refuses to pass wave bodies inline (`:219`): the verify-and-improve loop needs prose
at a path as its denominator, and a path survives compaction while a prompt does not.

### 6.1 Schema

YAML front matter (machine) + English prose body (agent). Both halves are mandatory.

**Front matter — every field required; `null` is permitted only where marked, and only with a
stated reason in the body.**

| Field | Type | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `handoff_contract` | `"1.0.0"` | fixed |
| `handoff_kind` | `lane` \| `remediation` \| `epilogue` | **New in revision 3.** One schema serves three producers — the lane handoff (3.f), loop L's remediation handoff (3.i, §7.3) and the wave's epilogue handoff (3.k, §5.13). Revision 2 invented the latter two in prose and gave neither a path, a lock, nor a validation rule, while §6.2 layer 1 counts `13 × \|task_ids\|` and layer 2 demands `task_ids` **equality** with `lanes.lock.json` — neither of which a finding list satisfies. The field is what lets §6.2 state which layers apply per kind |
| `run_id`, `lane` | string | run. For `remediation` the value is the originating lane id; for `epilogue` it is the literal `"epilogue"` |
| `wave`, `stage` | **int** | run. Both are integers wherever present (§3.4); `wave-{n}` is a path fragment only. **`stage` is `null` for an `epilogue` handoff** — a marked `null` under the §6.1 preamble, with its reason stated in the body: the epilogue runs at 3.k after the last stage (§5.13), so no stage coordinate applies |
| `isolation` | `worktree` \| `branch-serial-lane` \| `none-serial` | frozen profile — i.e. `frozen.isolation_profile` (§4.1) verbatim. **In phase 1 the value is always the constant `"none-serial"`**, because Preflight P.6 does not run and there is no profile to probe (§4.1, §5.4–5.9); the field is written and unread, which is why it is required rather than nullable (§6 preamble). `worktree` and `branch-serial-lane` return in phase 2 (§P2 5.4–5.8). **There is no profile `none`** — `--serial` is Profile B with `--lanes 1` (§P2 5.6), and `"none-serial"` is the phase-1 constant, not that deleted value. An `epilogue` handoff carries the frozen value for the record; it executes at the host regardless (§5.13) |
| `branch` | string | `kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}`, or `…-l{k}-fix{r}` for a `remediation` handoff. `null` for `epilogue`, which commits directly on `frozen.integration_branch`. **In phase 1 the value is `null` for every `handoff_kind`**, because §5.14's executor creates no lane branch and commits directly on `frozen.integration_branch` — the same reason this row already states for `epilogue`, and the premise §4.1's phase-1 `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` evaluator rests on. This is the one place `null` is marked for a `lane` handoff (§6.1 preamble); the `kiwi/orch/…` forms return in phase 2 (§P2 5.4–5.5) |
| `bootstrap` | `{kind, posix, windows}[]` where `kind ∈ {install, assert, record}` | **mandatory, ordered**; the install, its one assertion (`npx vitest --version`) and the heartbeat record, each rendered as a command the agent executes verbatim **in the host's shell**. **Phase 1: the ordered set is the `assert` entries only — the `install` and `record` entries are absent**, because §5.14's executor runs at the host root in the operator's existing checkout, which is already installed (§14.1, *"no per-unit `npm ci` in phase 1"*), and because the `record` entry writes the heartbeat, which `08:120-123` defers entirely — phase 1 has no poller, no `LIVENESS(k)` and no join loop (§P2 5.5.3, §14.1's `--poll-interval` row), and §3.1 keeps the phase-1 run directory to the resume card alone. The `install` kind with its `NODE_ENV` pin and the `record` entry both return in phase 2 (§P2 5.5), where each lane gets a fresh worktree. The `{posix, windows}` pair, the `record` typing and the checkout-in-spawn-prompt exclusion are unchanged in both phases. Three corrections in revision 3: (a) **each entry carries a `{posix, windows}` pair**, because `NODE_ENV=development npm ci` is a parse error in PowerShell and this repository's primary shell is PowerShell — `verification_cmd` is two-variant for exactly this reason (`kiwi-planner:512`) and `bootstrap` was the only mandated command field that was not; (b) the third entry's `kind`, **when present**, is **`record`**, not `assert` — it *writes* the heartbeat rather than asserting anything, and E14 requires installs to precede their assertions, which an action mistyped as an assertion silently violates; (c) **the `checkout` step is deliberately not here** — it is in the spawn prompt (§5.5), because this document is unreadable until it has run |
| `plan_run_id`, `plan_path`, `sidecar_path` | string | `/kiwi-planner` |
| `task_ids[]` | string[] | `lane`: **equals** the lane's assignment in `lanes.lock.json`. `remediation`: **equals the originating lane's**, for traceability — the remediation's work is described in `## Tasks` as loop L's finding list, not as new plan Tasks. `epilogue`: equals `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned ∪ demoted` (§5.13) |
| `req_ids[]` | string[] | **fixed, pre-allocated at Phase 3.b** (charter C2), verified at 3.c′ (§5.11) |
| `design_items[]` | string[] | the `[D-nnn]` ids assigned to this lane |
| `write_set[]` | string[] | **equals** the lane's computed write set; for a `remediation` handoff the originating lane's, unchanged; for an `epilogue` handoff the union of `files ∪ test_files` over `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned ∪ demoted`, computed from the catalog (§6.2 layer 2) |
| `read_set[]` | string[] | modules the lane may read but must not modify |
| `acceptance[]` | `{ac_id, req_id, test_id\|null, untested_reason?, untested_owner?}[]` | `get_requirement` per `req_ids`; the last two are **required whenever `test_id` is `null`** (§6.5) |
| `verification_cmd` | `{posix, windows}` | **the lane-level command, and it is the only command `collect()` runs — for both the lane verification and §7.4 row R11.** Shape rule: `kiwi-planner:512` (C13, *"`null` 또는 `{posix: non-empty, windows: non-empty}`"*); schema: `kiwi-planner:747`. **Precedence, because the field exists at two levels:** when the lane's tasks carry differing per-task `verification_cmd`s, the handoff's value is the **union command** the handoff author writes — a single invocation naming every task's test files — and the per-task values remain in the sidecar for `kiwi-coder`'s own use. `validateHandoff` layer 4 asserts that every per-task `verification_cmd`'s test files appear in the handoff-level command's arguments, **and that every non-null `acceptance[].test_id`'s file appears there too**, which is what makes one run serve R11. **Both variants must end in a machine-readable reporter flag (`--reporter=json` for vitest);** the orchestrator appends it if absent |
| `regression_baseline_ref` | string | the run-wide pinned baseline (`kiwi-wave-master:150`) |
| `depends_on_lanes[]` | string[] | may be empty; **never non-empty within a stage** — cross-stage dependencies are carried by `base_sha`, not by this field |
| `commit_policy` | `{granularity: "per-task", pathspec: "write_set", trailers: [...]}` | §5.4's commit obligation and §5.4.2's pathspec rule, rendered. **`pathspec_file` is deleted** — `--commit-lane-work` takes no argument and stages the handoff's own `write_set`, which pm already has via `--handoff` (§5.4). The literal `"write_set"` names the source so an implementer cannot re-invent a file |
| `deferred_mutations_path` | string | where `--defer-srs-mutation` writes (workspace-local, §3.5). **In phase 1 the field is written and unread**, exactly like `heartbeat_path` and `decisions_path`: §5.14 difference 2 passes no `--defer-srs-mutation`, so the queue, its harvest and its replay do not exist (§3.1, §10.1) and the four `kiwi-coder §0.12` calls run inline at the host (§7.10). It stays required rather than nullable for the same reason `isolation` does (§6 preamble). **Phase 2**: the `--defer-srs-mutation` writer and its `kiwi-pm` pass-through move with the lanes (§P2 5.4, §14 items 11–12, §15.5 S12/S16, `08:126`) |
| `decisions_path` | string | where the lane appends its committee decision records (§5.4.1, `07:169-170`). **New in revision 2** — the obligation existed and the field did not |
| `manifest_path` | string | **workspace-relative, with the full lane coordinate**: `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}.manifest.json` (§3.5). For a `remediation` handoff, `…/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}.remediation-{r}.manifest.json` |
| `manifest_template` | object | **§3.4's exact object with every value `null`, with three stated exceptions: `schema_version` carries the literal `"1.0.0"`, `intentionally_empty` carries `false`, and §3.4's six array fields — `written_paths`, `commits`, `commands_run`, `acceptance_results`, `red_evidence`, `external_side_effects` — carry `[]`.** Revision 2's normative sentence said *"every value `null`"* while §6.3's fixture — which §4.1 makes the source of §10.3's fixtures — did exactly the above. §6.3 establishes that `validateHandoff` is authored **from the bolded normative sentence**, so a validator built from the sentence rejects the design's own example, and one built from the example teaches the lane to emit `null` where §3.4 says *"an empty array is a claim, an absent field is silence"*. The exceptions are stated so both agree |
| `heartbeat_path` | string | **workspace-relative, with the full lane coordinate**: `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}.heartbeat` (§5.5.1). Its one-line JSON format is fixed in §5.5.1 |
| `forbidden[]` | string[] | the closed prohibition list, quoted verbatim (§5.4) |
| `escalation` | string | how to report `lease-breach-requested`, `needs-user` and `design-refuted` |
| `auto` | bool | the effective `--auto` state (§5.4.1 rule 3) |

**Body — ten required headings, in this order:** `## Setup`, `## Objective`, `## Context`,
`## Interfaces`, `## Tasks`, `## Acceptance`, `## Constraints`, `## Out of scope`, `## Manifest`,
`## Escalation`.

That is **ten** — the number in the bolded sentence, in the list, and in E14, all three checked
against each other. (Revision 0 said "seven" and enumerated eight; revision 1 said "eight",
enumerated nine, and then wrote *"That is nine"* two lines later, while E14 said nine —
`validateHandoff` is authored from the **bolded normative sentence**, so the mismatch was not
cosmetic.) `## Setup` renders `bootstrap` as prose the lane executes before anything else — it exists
because under Profile A `acquire()` is a no-op (§5.5) and the lane is the only party that can perform
the install. `## Manifest` names the always-present fields of `manifest_template` and states that the
manifest is the lane's only return channel.

**`verification_cmd`'s citation was wrong and it is not cosmetic.** The first revision cited
`kiwi-planner:513` — which is **C14**, `trace_links[].target.reference ⊆ task.req_ids`. The rules
that actually govern this field are **C13 at `:512`** (the `{posix, windows}` shape) and the sidecar
schema at **`:747`**. §6.2 layer 4 resolves handoff references against this schema, so an implementer
following the wrong pointer would build the wrong validation.

### 6.2 Validation — `validateHandoff(text, lane, catalog, root)`

**Five** mechanical layers. All are executable and all live in `src/` per the boundary rule. (Layer 5
was added by RV-20 without updating the count; the count is now five in the sentence and five in the
list.)

**Which layers apply per `handoff_kind`, stated once (§6.1).** `lane`: all five. `epilogue`: all
five, with layer 2's equality against `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned ∪ demoted`. `remediation`:
layers **2, 3, 4 and 5** — layer 1 is **skipped**, because its `13 × |task_ids|` field count was
already discharged on the originating lane's handoff and a remediation's `## Tasks` is a finding
list, not a set of new plan Tasks; its lock records `task_field_count: 0` (§3.3a). Revision 2
invented the remediation and epilogue handoffs and left every one of these questions open.

1. **Completeness.** For every task in the lane, thirteen fields must be present and non-empty (or
   explicitly `null` with a stated reason): `id`, `type`, `req_ids`, `files`, `test_files`,
   `action`, `acceptance_tests`, `verification_cmd`, `dod`, `rollback`, `covers_ac`,
   `depends_on_task`, `tdd`. `expected = 13 × |task_ids|`. A verifier reporting
   `checked ≠ expected` **invalidates the round**, reusing `waves-event.md:99` verbatim.
2. **Equality, not subset.** `task_ids` **equals** the assignment for this `handoff_kind` (above)
   and `write_set` **equals** the lane's computed write set — for a `remediation` handoff, the
   **originating lane's** write set, unchanged; for an `epilogue` handoff, the union of
   `files ∪ test_files` over the tasks in `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned ∪ demoted`, computed from the
   `catalog` argument `validateHandoff` already receives, since `lanes.lock.json` records `write_set`
   per lane row and the epilogue has none. A handoff can neither silently drop a task nor widen
   scope, and the claim audit (§7.4) then has something exact to compare against.
3. **English-only, over a *named* denominator.** Scan the handoff **body** plus exactly two
   front-matter fields — `escalation` and every `acceptance[].untested_reason` — for Hangul
   (`\p{Script=Hangul}`), CJK ideographs, Kana and Cyrillic; fenced code blocks, inline code spans,
   and **blockquote content** are excluded — the same construct §3.3 rule 6 names for the sibling
   prose scan, chosen because a scanner can decide it without judging provenance. Any hit is
   `handoff-not-english` (C). Those two fields are in
   the denominator because they are authored natural-language prose the executing agent reads, exactly
   as the body is. **Out of scope, named the way layer 4 names its own set so no implementer invents
   the boundary:** every other front-matter field — identifiers, paths, enums, commands, `bootstrap`,
   and `forbidden[]`, whose text is the closed list quoted verbatim from §5.4 rather than free
   authoring. The charter calls English a hard requirement; a regex is the only
   form of that requirement that will survive. **Stated limitation:** the scan is a script check,
   not a language check — it passes fluent Spanish and it passes English too poor to act on. The
   second failure is what loop H's verifier 2 is for.
4. **Resolvability, over a *named* set of fields and against a *named* tree.** Revision 2 wrote
   *"every referenced path"* with no scope and *"exists at `base_sha`"* with no way to evaluate it,
   and both halves were unrunnable — five mandatory front-matter fields are workspace-local or
   git-ignored and exist at no tree, and layer 4 runs at **3.f**, before the 3.f′ commit whose sha
   `base_sha` would name (§2.1 ordering rule 2). Fixed on both axes:

   > **The tree** is **HEAD at 3.f plus the set of paths 3.f′ is about to stage** — the dispatch base,
   > one step before it is committed, which is the same tree and is available when layer 4 runs.
   >
   > **The scoped field set** is exactly: `plan_path`, `sidecar_path`, every `write_set[]` entry,
   > every `read_set[]` entry, every non-null `acceptance[].test_id`'s file, and every path named in
   > the body's `## Interfaces` and `## Tasks` sections.
   >
   > **Explicitly out of scope, and named so no implementer invents the list:** `manifest_path`,
   > `heartbeat_path`, `deferred_mutations_path` and `decisions_path` (workspace-local by
   > construction, §3.5) and `regression_baseline_ref` (points into the git-ignored journal, §4.8).
   > Prose in `## Context` may name any path in the repository and is excluded, as it is for
   > `dispatch-base-dirty` (§2.1 ordering rule 1).

   Within that scope: every referenced path exists at the dispatch base or is a `write_set` member
   (which is by definition to-be-created or to-be-modified); every `T-PH\d{3}-\d{2}` exists in the
   named sidecar; every `req_ids` entry resolves through `get_requirement`; every
   `acceptance[].ac_id` exists on its REQ; `verification_cmd` names a real script and covers every
   per-task `verification_cmd`'s test files **and every acceptance `test_id`'s file** (§6.1); and
   every non-null `acceptance[].test_id` resolves to a **test file that exists at the dispatch base
   or is in `write_set`**, with **no two acceptance rows sharing a `test_id`**. Revision 1's layer 4
   left `test_id` unresolved, so three ACs could all point at one trivial `it("exists")` and satisfy
   every count-based closure in §6.5. An unresolvable pointer is `handoff-unresolvable-reference`
   (C) — a handoff is consumed by a cold agent with no conversation, so an unresolvable reference is
   a defect, not a nit.
5. **Non-vacuous acceptance (§6.5).** `acceptance[]` is non-empty, the number of rows with
   `test_id: null` is within the cap `max(--allow-untested-ac, ceil(|acceptance[]| / 4))` (§6.5 closure 1) **and**, for `handoff_kind ∈ {lane, remediation}`, at most one per REQ (§6.5 closure 1), and
   every null row carries `untested_reason` and `untested_owner`. Violation:
   `handoff-untested-ac-over-cap` (C).

### 6.3 Worked example

```markdown
---
handoff_contract: "1.0.0"
handoff_kind: "lane"
run_id: "2026-08-02.speckiwi.v260"
wave: 2
stage: 2
lane: "lane-3"
isolation: "none-serial"   # the phase-1 constant (§6.1); phase 2 restores "worktree"
auto: true
# base_sha is deliberately ABSENT — it is the sha of the commit that contains this file, which no
# value inside that commit can equal (§2.1 ordering rule 2). In phase 1 nothing supplies it: the
# unit runs at the host with the integration branch already checked out (§5.14); phase 2's lane
# receives it from the spawn prompt (§P2 5.5).
branch: null   # phase 1 creates no lane branch (§5.14, §6.1); phase 2 restores "kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}"
bootstrap:
  # the checkout to the dispatch base already ran, from the spawn prompt, before this file was readable
  # no `- kind: install` entry in phase 1 — the unit runs at the host root in an already-installed
  # checkout (§5.14, §6.1); phase 2 restores it with its NODE_ENV pin (§P2 5.5)
  - kind: assert
    posix:   "npx vitest --version"
    windows: "npx vitest --version"
  # no `- kind: record` entry in phase 1 — the heartbeat is deferred with worktree isolation

  - { ac_id: "AC-2", req_id: "FR-FLOW-071", test_id: "test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts::never splits a covers_ac red/green pair" }
  - { ac_id: "AC-3", req_id: "FR-FLOW-071", test_id: "test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts::produces byte-identical JSON on two calls" }

  - "Do not call any SpecKiwi MCP mutation except the four kiwi-coder §0.12 promotion calls, which

  - "Do not emit a pipeline event. The orchestrator emits one for you at integration."

             — then return, naming the reason."
---

## Setup

You run at the host root with `frozen.integration_branch` already checked out (§5.14). No checkout
is performed for you and no base sha is supplied to you — phase 1 has no spawn prompt (§6, §2.1's
orderings banner), and this document could not carry the sha anyway: it lives *inside* the commit
that would name it, so no value written here could equal it. Now run `bootstrap` in order, in your
shell's variant, before reading anything else. If the assertion fails, write `manifest_path` with
`status: "dead"` and stop — do not attempt to repair your own base.

## Objective

Implement `computeLanePlan`, the pure function that partitions a `kiwi-planner` sidecar into
execution lanes. It reads a task catalogue and a convergence registry and returns lanes, stages, a
serial epilogue, and a serialized fallback ordering. It performs no I/O.

## Context

`src/core/workflow/validate.ts` already parses the sidecar into `WorkflowTaskCatalogEntry` via the
private `normalizeTasks`, detects dependency cycles via `dependencyIssues`, and selects one next
task via `selectNextTask`. `selectNextTask` is head-of-line blocking by construction: when the first
not-done task is blocked it returns `nextTask: null`. Do not change that behaviour. Lane 2 of
**stage 1** of this wave extended `normalizeTasks` to carry `files`, `test_files`, `covers_ac`,
`type` and `action`; stage 1's units committed onto this integration branch (phase 1 performs no
merge — §4.1, §5.12, §5.14 difference 1), so that change is already in your working tree and those
fields are available to you.

## Interfaces

You add one module and one test file. You import from `src/core/workflow/validate.ts` and
`src/core/types.ts`; you modify neither. Export exactly:

```ts
export interface ConflictEdge { a: string; b?: string; reason: ConflictReason }
export interface LanePlan { digest: string; lanes: Lane[]; stages: Stage[];
                            serialEpilogue: string[]; unassigned: string[];
                            serialized: string[]; conflicts: ConflictEdge[] }
export function computeLanePlan(input: LanePlanInput): LanePlan
```

## Tasks

### T-PH003-04 — red

Write `test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts` first, and confirm it fails because
`src/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.ts` does not exist. Cover, one `it` each: every task appears in
exactly one of lanes / serialEpilogue / unassigned; no dependency edge crosses lanes within a stage;
no `files[]` overlap crosses lanes within a stage; a `covers_ac` red/green pair is never split; stage
layering is a topological order; two calls on the same input produce byte-identical JSON; a cyclic
`depends_on_task` input is rejected rather than partitioned; an empty `files[]` task becomes a
barrier; a task carrying `[INFERRED:` becomes a barrier; `serialized` equals the sequence
`selectNextTask` produces over the same catalogue.

`dod`: the file exists, every `it` fails, and no implementation file has been created. **Commit
here**, with the red run recorded into `red_evidence` in your manifest — command, non-zero exit code
and the captured failure text. Nothing here mechanically checks that a red preceded your green:
phase 1 has no claim audit (§5.14 difference 3), and your test-first discipline is the one
`kiwi-coder` already enforces inside your own `/kiwi-pm` run, reviewed afterwards by loop P (§7.6).
*(Phase 2 — §P2 7.4 row R9 verifies every `red_evidence` entry against `commands_run` and raises
`lane-evidence-irreproducible` (C); §6 preamble, §6.5, §15.5 E17a.)*

### T-PH003-05 — green

Implement `src/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.ts` until every test passes. Do not weaken a test to
reach green. Do not add behaviour the tests do not cover. **Commit.**

`dod`: `verification_cmd` exits 0; `git diff --name-only <the integration-branch head recorded at
this unit's start>..HEAD` lists exactly the two files in `write_set`; `git rev-parse HEAD` differs
from that recorded head. (That recorded head is the only base value a phase-1 unit can observe —
§5.14.)

## Acceptance

AC-1, AC-2 and AC-3 of `FR-FLOW-071` are covered by the three named tests, and no two of them name
the same test id (§6.2 layer 4). **AC-4 has no test yet** — it concerns the cross-wave
`partition-postmortem.wave-{n}.md` input, which lane 5 of wave 3 introduces; that is recorded in
`untested_reason` / `untested_owner` in the front matter. It is **1 null row of 4**, and the cap is
`ceil(4/4) = 1`, so it is exactly at the cap and legal (§6.5). Leave `AC-4` untested and say so in
your manifest; do not invent a placeholder test for it.

*(Revision 1's example carried 3 rows with 1 null and asserted it was "inside the §6.5 cap" while
the cap is 1 in 4. Under the `ceil` rule `ceil(3/4)` is also 1, so that example was in fact legal —
but the document never stated a rounding rule, so a `floor` reading made the design's only
demonstration of the legitimate case trip `handoff-untested-ac-over-cap` (C). The rounding rule is now
stated in §6.5 and the example is widened to 4 rows so it demonstrates the cap at its boundary rather
than under a rounding convention.)*

## Constraints

- `depends_on_task` acyclicity is enforced by planner validator C24 only when
  `--tdd-policy ≠ disabled` (`kiwi-planner:523`). Assume nothing; re-derive it inside the function.
- **`files[]` existence is not your problem.** C17 is optional (`kiwi-planner:516`) and the check is
  performed by `orchestrate schedule plan` *before* calling you; the result arrives as the injected
  `existing_paths: string[]` in your input. Do not touch the filesystem to check it (§5.1).
- Path comparison is POSIX-normalised, repo-relative, case-folded, and a path that is a prefix
  directory of another counts as intersecting.
- The function is pure. No `fs`, no `child_process`, no `Date.now()`, no `Math.random()`.

## Out of scope

The CLI verb, the MCP tool, the lock-file writer, and `normalizeTasks` itself. Other lanes own them.

## Manifest

Write `manifest_path` as your last act, filling every field of `manifest_template`. These are always
present and an empty array is a claim, not silence: `written_paths`, `commits`, `red_evidence`,
`external_side_effects`, `intentionally_empty`, `workspace_path`, `bootstrap_done_at`.
**`written_paths` lists what you COMMITTED**, not everything that changed under your workspace — a
child skill's uncommitted writes are not yours and are not reported here (§5.4.2 consequence 1).
Rewrite `heartbeat_path` at every Task boundary with the one-line JSON §5.5.1 specifies; the
orchestrator reads it to know you are alive and to know when your install finished. *(Phase 2 — the
heartbeat defers with worktree isolation, `08:120-123`, exactly as `bootstrap`'s `record` entry does; §6
preamble.)*

## Escalation

If `normalizeTasks` does not expose `files` in your working tree, stop — stage 1's commits are not
on this branch and your base is wrong. That is a **base** problem, not a lease problem: write the manifest with
`status: "dead"` and report it. (`lease-breach-requested` is for needing a path outside `write_set`;
the two route differently — §3.4 re-dispatches a `dead` lane once and then demotes it, while a lease
breach goes to the serial epilogue or a re-plan. Revision 1's example prescribed the wrong one.)
```

### 6.4 Loop H — handoff verification

Two verifiers by stance, the `verify-loop.md` engine (§7.1), with a mechanical denominator:

- **Verifier 1 — completeness and fidelity.** Denominator: `13 × |task_ids|` fields plus
  `|design_items|`. Every assigned design item must appear in the body. `checked ≠ expected`
  invalidates the round.
- **Verifier 2 — cold-agent executability.** Denominator: the lane's `write_set` **plus the set of
  `acceptance[]` rows carrying `test_id: null`**. In the **final round only** (because it costs a
  subagent), an **executability probe**: spawn a subagent given *only* the handoff document, and ask
  it to emit the file-change plan it would execute — no edits. Compare its file set against
  `write_set`. A mismatch is a **handoff** defect, not a coder defect.

  **The null rows are inside the denominator, and revision 1 left them outside it.** §6.5 closure 3
  requires verifier 2 to *"record an explicit verdict on each null row in its round"*, while the
  frozen denominator remained `write_set` alone and the engine rule reused verbatim is *"row count ≠
  frozen denominator invalidates the round for **both** verifiers"* (`kiwi-wave-master:319`). A
  handoff with a 2-entry `write_set` and one null row would make verifier 2 emit 3 rows against a
  denominator of 2 — an invalid round, every round, cap exhaustion, `handoff-verify-failed` (C), and
