  a demotion. Every wave containing one legitimately untested AC would serialise entirely. Adding the
  null-row set to the denominator is the narrowest fix and keeps the count-mismatch rule intact.
  §7.1's loop table is updated with it.

PASS: `validateHandoff().ok`, both denominators complete, merged `CRITICAL=0` and `HIGH=0`, and **no
edit applied in that round**.

**Phase 1 — on cap exhaustion the gate fires directly.** There is nothing to attempt first: every
unit already runs serially (§5.14) and the serial epilogue as a distinct executor is deferred
(§5.13, `08:126-127`), so there is nowhere to demote to. Cap exhaustion with the executability probe
still mismatched is `handoff-verify-failed` (C) per §2's severity rule, and the run halts for the
user one recovery earlier than revision 3 had it (§2, §13).

**Phase 2 — the demotion conjunct returns with the lanes (§P2 7.3).** There, cap exhaustion demotes
the lane to the serial epilogue rather than dispatching it — *unless* the lane's tasks are
predecessors of a later stage's lanes, in which case the epilogue runs **after** the stage that needs
them and demotion would produce a stage-2 dispatch base that does not contain work its handoff tells
the lane is present; only then does the gate fire. §P2 7.3's demotion-legality rule is the single
definition both loops use in that phase.

### 6.5 `test_id: null` is capped, attributed, and audited — the hole it closed

> **`08:30-33` singles this out: it is *"the single CRITICAL that is **not** parallel-caused … It
> survives a serial cut and must be fixed regardless."* The mechanisms named here ship in phase 1** —
> the cap, the per-REQ limit, `untested_reason`, `untested_owner`, layer 4's resolution of every
> non-null `test_id`, the no-duplicate-`test_id` rule, closure 3's loop H verifier-2 verdict, and
> §7.10's bar on `verified` for any requirement carrying a null row. **Closure 4 and the red-evidence
> check do not**: both are §7.4 rows evaluated at 3.h, and §7.3–7.5 and step 3.h are Part P2 (§5.14
> item 3, §15.5 E17/E17a). In phase 1 the cap is enforced by §6.2 layer 5 at 3.f and policed by
> closure 3's loop H verifier-2 verdict; there is no post-freeze re-check at 3.h.
>
> **Two of the exploit's links are cut by the scope cut rather than by this section, and one closure
> goes with them.** The trace below names loop L's *"may close as `realised-untested` without
> penalty"* clause and §P2 7.4's vacuous `acceptance[]` re-run; both are in Part P2. **Closure 4's
> evaluation point (§P2 7.4 at 3.h) is Part P2 as well**, and phase 1 carries its two jobs elsewhere:
> the assertion itself is §6.2 **layer 5 at 3.f** (`handoff-untested-ac-over-cap` (C), phase 1, §13),
> and the handoff-edited-after-verification window is covered by `execute-unit`'s
> `P-HANDOFF-VERIFIED` precondition (§4.1), which requires §4.7 digest 4 to match before the unit
> runs. Phase 1's chain is shorter — handoff author → loop H → loop P → 3.n — and the hole is the
> same hole, because the party choosing `null` is still the handoff author at 3.f and the bound is
> still this section's. A reader must not conclude that a serial scope closes it.

**The hole, traced link by link through the first revision.** §6.2 layer 1's denominator was
`13 × |task_ids|` **task** fields — acceptance rows were not counted. Layer 4 required only that
`acceptance[].ac_id` exists. Loop H's denominators were `13×tasks`, `design_items` and `write_set`.
Loop L verifier 1 said rows marked `test_id: null` *"may close as `realised-untested` **without**
penalty"*. §7.4's *"the tests really pass"* row re-runs `acceptance[]` — vacuous when every row is
null. The merge gate's `failing_tests ⊆ baseline` cannot be broken by adding no test. 3.n fired on
loop P's prose verdict. **Nothing bounded the number of null rows**, and the party choosing null was
the handoff author at Phase 3.f — the orchestrator itself.

The reachable exploit: a lane ships `auto-gate.ts` with every AC `test_id: null` and one
`it("exists", …)`; `verification_cmd`, chosen by the same handoff, names only that file and exits 0.
Every mechanical gate passes and `FR-FLOW-0xx` is promoted with **zero executable evidence** —
against `00.charter.md:297-298`, *"no requirement is marked verified without evidence."*

**Five closures. Four are mechanical; the fifth is partial, and saying so is the point.**

1. **Cap, with its arithmetic stated.** `cap := max(--allow-untested-ac, ceil(|acceptance[]| / 4))`,
   **and**, on a `lane` or `remediation` handoff, at most **1 null row per REQ**; both must hold
   there. On an `epilogue` handoff the count cap alone applies, raisable by `--allow-untested-ac N`
   as everywhere else. Over either is
   `handoff-untested-ac-over-cap` (C). `--allow-untested-ac N` is an **absolute row count**, not a
   ratio and not a numerator; it raises the cap, is journalled, and prints in the run header.
   - *Why the rounding rule is stated.* Revision 1 said *"at most 1 in 4"* and E14a said *"at most one
     row in four"* with no rounding convention, and §6.3's example asserted 1-of-3 was *"inside the
     §6.5 cap"*. Under `floor` that example is illegal and the design's only demonstration of the
     legitimate case trips a critical gate; under `ceil` it is legal. An implementer authoring E14a's
     boundary test had to guess. `ceil` is chosen because the alternative forbids **any** untested AC
     on a handoff with fewer than four rows, which is most handoffs, and closure 2's whole purpose is
     to make the legitimate case declarable.
   - *Why `cap` is a count everywhere.* §7.4's non-vacuous row subtracts `cap` from `|acceptance[]|`,
     which requires a count; §6.5 previously stated a ratio. One unit, defined here, used in both.
   - *Why the per-REQ bound is `lane`/`remediation` only.* §5.2 routes to the epilogue exactly the
     task types `kiwi-planner` marks TDD-exempt by construction (`doc` / `file_op` / `issue` / `pr` /
     `review`, `SKILL.md:363`, whose §9.3 acceptance kinds are `checklist` and `file_state` — neither
     a test file layer 4 can resolve), so a two-AC doc requirement violates the bound with no flag
     escape at 3.k, after the wave's stages have already committed. The residual is carried by §7.10,
     which bars `verified` for a requirement holding any null row regardless of kind, plus the
     journalled `--allow-untested-ac` count.
2. **Attribution.** A null row requires `untested_reason` (free text, ≥ 20 chars) **and**
   `untested_owner` (a named later lane, wave, or `deferred-to-user`). §6.3's worked example
   demonstrates the legitimate case — AC-4 depends on an artifact a later wave introduces — and the
   fields make that case declarable rather than silent.
3. **Second party.** A null row may not be authored **and** accepted by the same party without a
   recorded reason: the handoff author writes `untested_reason`, and loop H **verifier 2** must
   record an explicit verdict on each null row in its round — with the null-row set inside verifier
   2's frozen denominator (§6.4), or the obligation invalidates every round it is supposed to police.
   A null row with no verifier-2 verdict invalidates the round on the same rule as a count mismatch
   (`waves-event.md:99`).
4. **Non-vacuous denominator — Part P2 (§P2 7.4, §15.5 E17), stated in handoff terms because it is
   evaluated before loop L exists.**
   §P2 7.4 gains a row asserting `|acceptance[]| > 0` **and** that the number of rows whose handoff
   `test_id` is **non-null** is `≥ |acceptance[]| − cap`. Revision 1 wrote that row in loop-L verdict
   terms (*"`realised-with-test` rows ≥ …"*) while the claim audit is *"run by the orchestrator at 3.h
   **before** loop L"* and `realised-with-test` is a **loop L verifier 1** verdict assigned at 3.i —
   so at evaluation time no such verdict exists. An implementer building the literal row had to either
   invert the stated phase order (letting a fabricated-evidence lane reach loop L) or build a
   different check. In handoff terms the row is evaluable at 3.h against the frozen handoff.
   - *And it is not a duplicate of layer 5.* Layer 5 fires at **3.f**, against the handoff the author
     just wrote. This row fires at **3.h**, against the handoff `lane-{k}.lock.json` pinned — so it
     catches a handoff edited between verification and dispatch, which §4.7 digest 4 detects only on
     resume. An all-null handoff cannot reach loop L at all, and neither can a handoff whose null rows
     were widened after 3.f. **Phase 1 has no 3.h, so this row is Part P2 (§P2 7.4, §15.5 E17).** The
     re-evaluation is replaced by `execute-unit`'s `P-HANDOFF-VERIFIED` precondition (§4.1), which is
     §4.7 digest 4 checked before the unit runs — a precondition rather than an audit row, and that
     substitution is the residual if it is judged weaker.
5. **Distinct, resolvable `test_id`s — partial, and the residual is named.** §6.2 layer 4 now resolves
   every non-null `test_id` to a real test file and forbids two acceptance rows sharing one. That
   closes the *duplicate-pointer* form of vacuity mechanically. It does **not** close the
   *vacuous-test* form: three ACs pointing at three distinct `it()` blocks that each assert
   `expect(fn).toBeTypeOf("function")` satisfy the cap, satisfy closure 4, satisfy `red_evidence`
   (a trivial test genuinely fails before the module exists, so a real red precedes a real green) and
   satisfy the integrator's re-run (exit 0). Only loop L verifier 1's prose verdict stands between
   that and promotion.

**The honest statement, which revision 1 did not make.** The *no-test-at-all* class is now mechanical;
the *vacuous-test* class remains a judgment call routed to a subagent. That is arguably the right
architecture under §1.1's boundary rule — test substance is not checkable against bytes on disk —
but revision 1 headed this section *"the hole it closed"*, singular, and called all four closures
*"mechanical"*, which overstated the coverage of the charter line it serves (`00.charter.md:297-298`).
§17 carries the residual.

**And the red-evidence check, which is the other half — Part P2 (§P2 7.4, §15.5 E17a), because phase 1
produces no §3.4 lane manifest (§5.14, §3.1).** §3.4's manifest now carries `red_evidence`,
harvested from the mechanism `kiwi-coder` already implements
(`skills/claude/kiwi-coder/SKILL.md:220`, `:374-386`). §P2 7.4 verifies each entry against the lane's own
`commands_run`: the recorded red command must appear there, its `exit_code` must be non-zero, and it
must precede the green run of the same command in `commands_run` order. The first revision dropped
this entirely — §10.3 Layer 1's red-before-green discipline applied only to the orchestrator's **own**
modules, so nothing anywhere checked whether a *lane* worked test-first.

---

## 7. Verification topology

### 7.1 One engine, six callers

The whole of `kiwi-wave-master §5.5`/`§5.6` moves into `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` (§9) and is
reused **denominator-agnostically** by six loops. Nothing about the engine changes; only its
denominators differ.

> **Five callers in phase 1, six in phase 2.** Loop L is deferred with the lanes (§P2 7.3). The
> engine, the extraction and every property below are unaffected — the engine was already
> denominator-agnostic, which is why removing a caller costs nothing. The heading keeps its count so
> that `§7.1`'s citations elsewhere still resolve; the table marks which row is absent.

Engine properties, all reused verbatim and none re-derived: identical evidence bundle to both
verifiers, splitting forbidden (`kiwi-wave-master:259`); two verifiers separated by stance
(`:285-325`); denominators **externally fixed** and frozen at round entry, recorded in
`frozen_denominator`, never computed by the verifier (`:293-325`); row count ≠ frozen denominator
invalidates the round for **both** verifiers (`:319`); round = three steps, isolated →
**add-only** cross-refutation → mechanical union (`:327-333`), with the collusion argument (`:335`)
as the stated reason dismissal is impossible; verifiers **re-spawned from round 2** (`:337`); three
closing paths with the streak cost on reclassification (`:339`); **no fix applied in that round**
as the pass precondition (`:348`); caps 5 / `--max` 8 / `--mini` 3 with `--loops` winning on a
non-fatal WARN (`loop-option.md:18-26`); unreachable-PASS arithmetic (`waves-event.md:160`); **cap
exhaustion is not a pass** (`:356`); `pass + residual` vs `fail-residual` (`:358`); delegation-only
remediation routing with an explicit scope on every delegation and "a sub-loop's own PASS does not
close a parent finding" (`:362-385`).

| Loop | Scope | Verifier 1 stance | Verifier 2 stance | Frozen denominators |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **D** | the design (Phase 1.d) | fidelity to intent | implementability | open-question set; implementability set; constraint set (§7.2) |
| **W** | one wave's design (3.a) | coverage of the wave's `design_items` | internal consistency vs the frozen design lock | that wave's `design_items` slice |
| **H** | one handoff (3.f) | completeness | cold-agent executability | 13 × tasks; `design_items`; `write_set` **∪ the null `acceptance[]` rows** (§6.4) |
| ~~**L**~~ | ~~one lane, **pre-merge** (3.i)~~ | ~~AC realisation within the lane~~ | ~~artifact quality + lease conformance~~ | **deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — §P2 7.3 |
| **P** | one wave, **post-execution** (3.l) | intent realisation + process conformance | quality + cross-wave regression | §7.6 — the four wave-master layers plus a unit layer |
| **F** | the run (Phase 4) | union of all `design_items` + `integration_items` | run-scope quality | `kiwi-wave-master:429-461`, reused unchanged |

### 7.2 Loop D's denominator — the charter's "high enough quality to implement from"

Three sets, all computed **externally** before round 1:

1. **Open-question set.** Every unresolved item from `01.intake.md` — the QnA residuals plus every
   dissent item `/kiwi-srs-research` preserved. `expected = |items|`. Each must be `resolved` with
   an answer pointer, or `deferred` with a **named** reason.
2. **Implementability set.** One row per `[D-nnn]` design item, verdict ∈
   `implementable` / `needs-decision` / `contradicts-existing`. `contradicts-existing` **requires a
   `file:line` pointer into the existing codebase** — otherwise it is an opinion, not a finding.
3. **Constraint set.** The always-written `constraints.json` (`waves-event.md:90`).

PASS: every open question resolved-or-deferred-with-reason, zero `needs-decision`, zero
`contradicts-existing`, zero constraint violations, and **no edit applied in that round**.

**A `needs-decision` row is where the charter's "ask the user questions where the design is still
weak" actually happens.** This is a verb, not an aspiration: `verify-design` emitting any
`needs-decision` row routes back to `intake-qna` with that row as the question, and the loop
re-enters.

**Under `--auto`, this is a declared degradation, and the run says so out loud.** The first revision
routed it through the standard read-time replacement (`auto-option.md:280`) — a recommended option
adopted immediately, else a 3-member committee — and fired `design-intake-insufficient` only on
**cap exhaustion**. The consequence: an unattended run whose committee happens to answer every design
question converges without the user ever being consulted, at the **one** place `00.charter.md:54`
names the user explicitly (*"ask the user questions where the design is still weak"*). Nothing
recorded that this had happened.

Two options were available: declare intake QnA a `critical_gates[]` member (halting regardless of
`--auto`), or **record it as a divergence from `00.charter.md:54` and pay for it in evidence**.
The second is adopted, for a reason and with a cost.

**It is a *recorded divergence*, not a "user-accepted degradation", and revision 1's label was
wrong.** No user accepted anything; the design chose it. The distinction matters because §8 requires
user consent **even under `--auto`** for the strictly smaller act of deferring one wave-boundary issue
as `out-of-run` (`kiwi-wave-master:205`) — so calling this one "accepted" applied a consent rule to
deferring an issue while self-granting an exemption for skipping the charter's **only** named user
interaction.

**And in revision 3 it is escalated rather than merely recorded — see §0.7 X-05.** Recording it in
§17.17's register was still the design disposing of a charter sentence on the user's behalf, which
`00.charter.md:10` reserves to the charter, and the asymmetry against X-01…X-04 was arbitrary. X-05
puts the two options (amend the intake sentence, or make intake QnA a `critical_gates[]` member) to
the user in the same shape as the other four. **The behaviour and the evidence below are unchanged
under either ruling** — under (a) the three records are what the amendment relies on, under (b) they
cost nothing — so nothing here is blocked on the ruling.

- *Reason.* Making intake QnA critical would make `--auto` useless for the class of work the charter
  is about — a thin intent produces many `needs-decision` rows by construction, so an always-halting
  intake means `--auto` never survives Phase 1. That is a worse answer than an honest record.
- *Cost, paid explicitly.* `--auto` intake is recorded in **three** places, not zero:
  1. a **Phase 0 notice** in the run header — *"`--auto` is in effect; design questions will be
     answered by a committee, not by you"* — which can be printed before N exists, plus a **Phase 1.c
     line** naming the actual N once gap enumeration has produced it. Revision 1 put *"N design
     questions will be answered by committee"* in *"the run header printed before Phase 1"*, and N is
     produced by 1.c and loop D, **inside** Phase 1: at the moment that header printed, N did not
     exist;
  2. a dedicated `intake_autonomy` block in `00.run-contract.md` (§11.2), written at the end of
     Phase 1 with the final N;
  3. one journal line per committee-decided intake row, on the v1.4.0 `decision` field (§4.2),
     carrying `{question, options, decision, rule, committee_size, confidence, origin: "intake"}` per
     `07:169-170`'s audit schema.

  A decision that received no user attention leaves the **most** evidence, which is the inverse of the
  failure `07:161-167` names.
- `design-intake-insufficient` (C) still fires on cap exhaustion with rows open, unchanged.

**X-03 is decided and this paragraph is not re-opened.** Revision 3 wrote that if the user resolved
X-03 by dropping the rung, `07` Q2 would re-open and this paragraph would be re-decided with it.
`08` §2 dropped the rung **and re-decided Q2 in the same ruling**, 5-0, and Q2 stays
`leave-all-three` (`08:54-64`) — so the condition that would have re-opened this paragraph was
discharged rather than triggered. What did change is the failure mode this paragraph inherits: a
committee that cannot reach a majority on an intake question now escalates to `critical` and halts
for the user, which is a *stronger* guarantee for `00.charter.md:54` than the tie rung offered.
X-05 remains open and is unaffected by that.

### 7.3–7.5 Loop L, the claim audit and the per-lane merge gate — deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`

`08:124-127` defers *"the claim audit's eleven rows, the pre-merge per-lane loop, per-lane merge with
the compare-and-swap publish, per-path restore on integrate"*. Loop L verifies a lane before that lane
is merged; the claim audit exists because *a lane's own success report is worth nothing* when the lane
ran somewhere the host could not watch; the merge gate runs after each lane's merge. None of the three
has a subject in phase 1.

**Moved verbatim to Part P2, under their original numbers `§7.3`, `§7.4` and `§7.5`.**

**What phase 1 has instead, and it is less.** Work is written in the tree it will live in, under the
host's own eye, by `/kiwi-pm` under the same TDD obligations `kiwi-coder` already enforces, and it is
verified by **loop P** after execution (§7.6) against the wave design, the frozen design items, the
constraints and the original intent. Phase 1 therefore has **no per-unit pre-merge quality loop**,
which is a real reduction against `00.charter.md:64-66` — *"subagent feedback loops verify and improve
them until quality is sufficient, and only then do they merge"* — and `08` §4 records that reduction
as taken deliberately rather than overlooked.

Three mechanisms elsewhere read these sections and are restated for phase 1 where they stand: §7.6's
lane layer (§7.6), §7.10's promotion set and its `verified` transition (§7.10), and the partition
postmortem's input, which in phase 1 comes from 3.f″'s coupling check and loop P rather than from the
merge gate (§5.14).

### 7.6 Loop P — post-merge, per wave

> **Phase 1 reading, stated before the section rather than after it, because one of its three added
> bundle rows and both halves of its fifth denominator name artifacts Part P2 now owns.**
>
> - **Bundle.** `lanes.lock.json` and every handoff stay. *Every lane manifest and its claim-audit
>   result* is replaced by **the wave's commit range on the integration branch**, keyed by `Orch-Lane`
>   and `Orch-Task` trailers (§5.14), plus each unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle.
> - **Fifth denominator, one sub-denominator not two.** `expected` = every task
>   `lanes.lock.json` assigns to a lane, to `serial_epilogue` or to `unassigned` — in phase 1 the
>   split between them changes execution *order*, not executor, so it does not change what must be
>   checked. `checked` = tasks carrying an `Orch-Task` trailer on a commit reachable from the
>   integration head **and** whose `verification_cmd` passed, plus every task carrying an admissible
>   `intentionally_empty` declaration under §5.14's disposition (which is `checked`, never removed
>   from `expected`). The claim-audit conjunct has no phase-1
>   counterpart: **phase 1 has one witness where phase 2 has two**, and that is the concrete cost of
>   deferring §P2 7.4.
> - **Unchanged:** *one task scheduled but never landed forbids `ALL_MATCH`*, the intent layer, the
>   four wave-master layers, and both pass preconditions.
>
> The two-sub-denominator text below is retained because phase 2 needs it and because the argument
> for splitting it — that epilogue tasks are permanently unchecked unless they have an executor — is
> the same argument §5.14 answers by having exactly one executor.

Reuses `kiwi-wave-master §5.5`'s evidence bundle (`:257-283`) verbatim, with three rows added:
`lanes.lock.json`; every lane manifest and its claim-audit result; every handoff. Denominators are
wave-master's four — REQ/AC from `list_requirements` with an AC-bearing projection (`:293`), design
layer from the wave's `design_items` (`:295-299`), constraint layer from the latest
`constraints_path` (`:301-303`), preservation layer from the mechanical four-class diff derivation
(`:305-315`) — plus a **fifth**:

> **Lane layer, in two sub-denominators because `lanes.lock.json` is not all lanes.**
> - *Lane sub-denominator.* `expected` = the tasks `lanes.lock.json` assigns to **lanes**, minus any
>   task on a lane carrying a terminal `lane_disposition` (§4.2) — a demoted lane's tasks move to the
>   epilogue sub-denominator, and a `refuted` or `quarantined` lane's tasks are reported in the wave's
>   `issues.md` as `missing-task` (§8) rather than counted against a denominator nothing can satisfy.
>   `checked` = tasks with a landed lane (`git-ancestor` true against `laneHeadRef(k)`, an externally
>   recomputable proof) **and** a passing 3.j claim audit, read from the host-written
>   `audit.3j.json` (§3.2 — revision 2 said *"harvested `audit.json`"*, which is neither the right
>   producer nor a distinct file from the 3.h result it overwrote).
> - *Epilogue sub-denominator.* `expected` = the tasks `lanes.lock.json` assigns to
>   `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned`, **plus** any task demoted into the epilogue during the wave —
>   which is exactly the epilogue handoff's `task_ids[]` (§5.13). `checked` = tasks whose epilogue
>   commit is an ancestor of the integration head and whose `verification_cmd` passed at the host,
>   read from `audit.3j.json` for `--lane epilogue`. **This sub-denominator has a producer only
>   because §5.13 gives the epilogue an executor**; revision 2 made the tasks *checkable* and left
>   them *unexecuted*, so `expected ⊇ {epilogue tasks}` and `checked = 0` forbade `ALL_MATCH` on
>   every wave in every repository.
>
> One task scheduled but never landed, in either sub-denominator, forbids `ALL_MATCH`, on the same
> logic as one unmapped design item (`waves-event.md:94`).
>
> **Splitting the denominator is a correction, not a refinement.** Revision 1 set `expected` to *"the
> number of tasks in `lanes.lock.json`"* and `checked` to *"tasks with a landed lane **and** a passing
> claim audit"*. §5.3 puts `serial_epilogue` and `unassigned` **inside** `lanes.lock.json`, and
> §4.7's digest 3 requires the lock to be the recomputable `computeLanePlan` output, so they cannot be
> excluded from it. Epilogue tasks run at the host with no lane branch and no claim audit, so they are
> permanently unchecked — and CP-02 and CP-07 are **universal shipped defaults**, so every repository
> has epilogue tasks. Loop P's lane layer could therefore never reach `ALL_MATCH` in any wave. Under
> the pre-revision-1 text the worklog `TASK_DONE` conjunct happened to cover them; removing it —
> correctly, for independence — left no route, and nothing noticed because the layer was new.
>
> **The lane's own worklog `TASK_DONE` is deliberately not a third conjunct** — it is written by the
> same party as the manifest, so it adds no independence; §7.4 explains why that claim was withdrawn.

**Crucially, loop P's verifier 1 also carries the run's original intent.** `08:103-104` requires
post-merge verification *"against the wave design, the frozen design items, the constraints and
**the original intent**"*. So the bundle includes `00.charter.md` and `01.intake.md`, and verifier
1's design layer is extended by an **intent layer**: `expected` = the
open-question set from loop D, `checked` = rows whose resolution is still honoured by the merged
result. A design that faithfully implements a *drifted* design document fails here. Without this
layer nothing in the run ever re-reads what the user actually asked for after Phase 1.

`unapproved-damage = 0` and `failing_tests ⊆ baseline_failing_tests` are pass preconditions,
unchanged.

### 7.7 Test-suite hygiene under parallelism — deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`

It is hygiene *under parallelism*: concurrent suite runs over one working tree, per-workspace
`node_modules`, and the interaction with the charter's standing `--no-file-parallelism` rule. Phase 1
runs one suite at a time in one tree. **Moved verbatim to Part P2, under its original number `§7.7`.**

### 7.8 Oscillation — a real gap in the existing termination table

`kiwi-wave-master:341-360` has caps, streaks and unreachable-PASS arithmetic but no clause for a
genuinely contested finding. Added to `verify-loop.md`:

> If the same `finding_id` closes and re-opens across ≥ 2 rounds, or the same hunk is reverted and
> re-applied, the two stances are in genuine conflict and further rounds buy nothing. Stop
> immediately rather than burning the cap: record `verdict = fail-residual`,
> `reason_class = "oscillation"` — a new value in the closed `reason_class` vocabulary
> (`waves-event.md:92`) — and raise `verification-oscillation` (C).

Adding a value to a closed vocabulary is a v1.4.0 additive change and is registered as such.

### 7.9 The charter's no-duplication requirement, given a phase, a gate and an artifact

`00.charter.md:100`: *"Do not introduce code duplication or architectural technical debt."* In the
first revision this appeared **only** inside §17.6's self-criticism — no phase step, no gate table
row, no artifact, no CLI verb, no requirement. A charter requirement that exists only in the
weaknesses section is not covered, and the risk is *structurally elevated* here precisely because
lanes cannot see each other: two lanes that each need the same helper will each write one.

Two halves, both promoted out of §17.6 into the flow:

**(a) Pre-dispatch hoist — a stage check at 3.f″, not a plan-time conflict edge.** Revision 1 made
`shared-substrate` a `conflict_reason` quantified over *"a **symbol** in ≥2 **lanes'** `read_set`"*.
That predicate cannot be evaluated where it was placed, for three independent reasons — it is
circular (`analyzeConflicts`' output *computes* lanes), `read_set` is a handoff field authored two
phases later, and no input on any surface carries symbols (`kiwi-planner:744`: `files` is
`Array<{path, line_range?}>`). E8's strengthened *"every `conflict_reason` value is reachable"* was
therefore unsatisfiable, and T24's first named mechanism had no implementation. See **X-04** (§0.7)
for the user ruling on whether to recover the symbol-level form.

**Revision 2's path-level replacement is withdrawn too, and revision 3 says why rather than shipping
a dead gate.** Revision 2 moved the rule to a 3.f″ *shared-substrate hoist* with the predicate *"a
**path** in the `read_set` of ≥ 2 lanes of this stage, in the `write_set` of **no** lane, and absent
from `existing_paths`."* It cannot fire, for two independent reasons set out in full at §0.7 X-04:
§6.2 layer 4 rejects such a handoff at **3.f**, one step earlier, with
`handoff-unresolvable-reference` (C); and any file two lanes each declare creating is in both their
`write_set`s, so `write-set-overlap` has already forced them same-lane at **3.e**. The predicate is
therefore not weak — it is **wholly subsumed**, and the gate `shared-substrate-unhoisted` is deleted
from §13 (§16 R39).

**What runs at 3.f″ is the cross-lane coupling check**, which uses only fields that exist there and
is covered by no conflict edge:

> **`stage-coupling`** — a path in one lane's `write_set` **and** another lane's `read_set`, within
> one stage. Lane A changes it; lane B compiles against it. `write-set-overlap` cannot see this — it
> is write∩write — and this is the exact signature §7.5 calls *"both lanes green in isolation, red
> merged"*.

Every input exists at 3.f″: the handoffs are written and `read_set` is a real field. On a hit the
orchestrator does **not implement anything** — it **re-partitions**: the two coupled lanes are merged
into one by writing a **new** `lanes.lock.json` under §4.7's append-new-artifact rule (journalled,
digest recomputed, `frozen.lane_lock` repointed, **and the merge journalled as a `merge-into-one-lane`
row carrying `planStageCoupling`'s `{path, fromLane, toLane}` beside the postmortem's task-keyed
projection, so §4.7 digest 3's recomputation reproduces the re-frozen lock**), the affected handoffs are
re-authored, and loop H
re-runs on them. **The 3.e′ verdict is not re-sought.** `partition-review-unrecorded` is raised once
per wave at 3.e′ (§13), a coupling merge is same-lane-forcing and therefore strictly more conservative
than the plan the user passed, and phase 1 walks the stage serially either way — so the digest
comparison is read against the **3.e′** freeze, not the current `frozen.lane_lock` pointer.
`waves/wave-{n}/partition.md` is re-authored from the new lock and committed with the stage's lane
artifacts at 3.f′, so the published document never disagrees with the lock in force. Bounded at
**one pass per stage**; a second hit in the same stage is
`stage-coupling-unresolved` (C), because a stage that needs two rounds of re-partitioning is
mis-partitioned rather than merely coupled. The coupling row is also written into the wave's
postmortem with `resolution: "merge-into-one-lane"`, so the *next* wave's `computeLanePlan` emits a
`learned-coupling` same-lane edge for it without waiting for a merge failure (§5.3).

**The postmortem row's `from_task` / `to_task` are resolved by the caller, not by `planStageCoupling`.**
The pure function sees only `ParsedHandoff[]`, whose `write_set`/`read_set` are lane-level and whose
`task_ids[]` is flat, so it emits `{path, fromLane, toLane}` and nothing task-keyed. `orchestrate coupling
check` (§4.4) loads the sidecar catalog and resolves the rest — the same division §5.3 already states for
grounding ("Grounding itself is the **command's** job"). `from_task` is the writing lane's task whose
`files[] ∪ test_files[]` contains `path`. The reading side has no per-task surface at all — `read_set` is a
handoff field and the sidecar `Task` interface carries no read set (X-04), and R37 declined the `reads[]`
field that would have supplied one — so a 3.f″-sourced row records `to_task: null`, and §5.3's existing
path fallback (the tasks whose `files[]` contain `path`) supplies the reading task in the next wave.

**Re-partitioning replaces revision 2's "implement the shared unit serially, in-tree, at the host",
and that removes a second defect.** That sentence was the **only** code in the design that would land
on the integration branch with no lane branch, no claim audit, no `red_evidence`, no loop L and no
plan Task — invisible to loop P's lane layer, whose two sub-denominators are drawn entirely from
`lanes.lock.json`, and to §7.10's promotion set. Its first test execution would have been the merge
gate after the stage's *first lane merge*; if every lane of that stage then failed, untested
host-authored code would remain on the integration branch. It also contradicted §1.2's *"the
orchestrator introduces no fixer of its own"* and D2's assignment of implementation to `/kiwi-coder`
and `/kiwi-pm`, and its remedy language (*"move the path from `read_set`-with-intent-to-create into
plain `read_set`"*) named a state no schema field expresses. Re-partitioning needs no executor at
all — it is a lane-plan operation, in the vocabulary `computeLanePlan` already has. See §16 R40.

**What this half does and does not catch, stated because X-04 turns on it.** It catches **semantic
coupling between two lanes over a declared path**, and forces them same-lane before dispatch. It does
**not** prevent duplication: at file level `write-set-overlap` already covers everything a declared
surface can express, and at symbol level nothing on any surface this design reads carries a symbol.
Only option (a) of X-04 recovers the symbol level, and only by extending the planner sidecar.

**Half (a) ships in phase 1 unchanged, and this is worth stating because it is the only part of §7.9
that does.** The coupling check is analysis over authored handoffs; it runs before any execution and
needs no lane to be running. Under `08`'s serial scope its *action* is unchanged (re-partition) and
its *value* is lower — two units that would have collided are executed one after another anyway, so
the collision it prevents is a semantic one, not a merge one. It is retained at full strength for
two reasons: it is the phase-1 postmortem's main input (§5.14), and it is the analysis phase 2
consumes.

**(b) Post-execution audit — a named step at 3.k.** After the last unit of the wave has run, a
duplication auditor runs over the **union of the wave's diffs**. In phase 1 the input is the wave's
commit range on the integration branch, restricted to the union of every unit's `write_set`; in
phase 2 it is the union of the lane diffs, as written below. **The audit's value is unchanged by the
scope cut and its hit rate is not**: two units that each invent the same helper do so whether they
run concurrently or one after another — a serial executor gets no memory of the previous unit's
internals, because each unit is a fresh `/kiwi-pm` run over its own handoff (§5.14).

- input: `git diff base..integration_head` restricted to the union of every lane's `write_set`;
- output artifact: `docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/duplication-audit.md`, with one row per
  finding `{symbol_or_block, lanes[], paths[], verdict}`, verdict ∈
  `duplicate` / `parallel-evolution` / `acceptable`;
- gate: `cross-lane-duplication-unresolved` (C) when a `duplicate` row has no resolution — and a note
  is still not a resolution. **What a resolution is, stated once for both phases, because 3.k executes
  the epilogue task set at activity (0) *before* this audit runs (§2.1 3.k; §P2 5.13's five-activity
  order).** A `duplicate` row therefore cannot name a member of *this* wave's frozen
  `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned ∪ demoted` set that has yet to run — that set is fixed in
  `lanes.lock.json` at 3.e and its handoff is frozen before execution, and there is no second epilogue
  pass after the audit. `resolutionTaskId` accordingly takes **either** (i) the id of an epilogue task
  that **already ran** in activity (0) and whose commit consolidated the block — the case the plan
  anticipated as a convergence point — **or** (ii) `issue:{id}`, a `local-defect` row opened in this
  wave's `issues.md` (§8), whose `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` resolution proof is a commit sha and therefore
  forces the consolidation before the next wave's 3.a. Branch (ii) is the route for the case this
  audit exists to catch — two lanes inventing the same helper the plan never named;
- verdict step, stated separately because it is not the kernel's: the subagent judges each candidate
  row `planDuplicationAudit` emitted and records `verdict` and `resolutionTaskId` into
  `duplication-audit.md`; the tool checks only that a verdict from the closed enum was recorded per
  candidate — §1.1's boundary rule, and the same split §7.4 states in the other direction;
- requirement row: **T24** in §15.2.

**Its honest limit, unchanged from §17.6.** Near-duplicate detection by subagent is fuzzy and will
miss non-obvious duplication. It is still the weakest detector in the design. The change here is that
it is now a step that runs, produces an artifact and can fail a gate, rather than a sentence in a
list of things the design is bad at.

### 7.10 Requirement promotion at 3.n — the set, the status and the evidence

Revision 1 gave 3.n one clause — *"promote requirements (host root only, after 3.l passes)"* — and its
verb-enum row named two tools. Neither says **which** requirements, **to what status**, or **against
what evidence**, and `00.charter.md:297-298` (*"no requirement is marked verified without evidence"*) is a
binding rule that turns entirely on the third. All three are fixed here.

> **Phase 1 reading of the set, the transition and the evidence.** *Landed* means **a commit on the integration
> branch carrying the task's `Orch-Task` trailer, plus a passing `verification_cmd`** (§5.14), or an
> admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration under §5.14's disposition, whose evidence is
> `type="test"` only — the `git-ancestor`-plus-claim-audit form below is phase 2's. The `implemented → verified` transition
> drops its per-execution-path split for the same reason there is one executor: the condition is
> loop P's `pass` for the wave with no residual naming the requirement, **plus** a passing
> `verification_cmd` for every one of the requirement's tasks. **What phase 1 cannot supply is loop
> L's `realised-with-test` closure per acceptance row**, because loop L is deferred (§P2 7.3); the
> `test_id: null` rule below is unaffected and is what still bars an untested acceptance row from
> reaching `verified`. This is the third and last place the scope cut removes a witness rather than a
> capability, and all three are named: §4.1's `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED`, §7.6's fifth denominator,
> and here.
>
> **The evidence refs, same substitution §7.6's bundle row makes.** `type="commit"` takes the
> `Orch-Task`-trailered commit sha on `frozen.integration_branch` (§5.14 difference 5): phase 1 runs
> no `integrate-lane` (§4.4's verb enum) and produces no `merge_sha` (§P2 5.9). `type="test"`'s
> `detail` takes the unit's `docs/analysis/kiwi-pm-…` bundle, because there is no lane `audit.json`
> (§5.14 difference 3, §P2 7.4). And the four calls are **not replayed**: `kiwi-coder` executes its
> own mutations inline at the host root against the run's own target (§5.14 difference 2), so the
> deferred-mutation queue, its 3.k replay and its `(tool, args_hash)` dedupe are phase 2
> (§P2 5.9; §2.1's 3.k block already lists the replay as deferred).

**The set.** The wave's 3.b allocation set (§4.2's `allocation` field), restricted to requirements
**every** one of whose sidecar tasks has landed — a lane task with a `git-ancestor` proof and a
passing 3.j claim audit, or an epilogue task with a host commit and a passing `verification_cmd`.
`req-shared` forces every task of one REQ into one lane (§5.2), so within a stage the restriction is
usually vacuous; across stages and into the epilogue it is not. A requirement with any unlanded task
is left at its current status and reported in the wave's `issues.md` as a `missing-task` (§8); a task
carrying an admissible `intentionally_empty` declaration (§5.14) is landed, not unlanded, so it is
not a `missing-task`.

**The status transition, and it is two-step.**

| From | To | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `planned` / `in_progress` | `implemented` | every task landed, at 3.n |
| `implemented` | `verified` | **additionally** loop P's verdict for this wave is `pass` with no residual naming the requirement, **and** every one of the requirement's tasks carries the evidence its execution path produces: for a **lane** task, its `acceptance[]` rows all closed `realised-with-test` in loop L (§7.3); for an **epilogue** task, a passing `epilogue` claim audit and a passing `verification_cmd` at the host (§5.13) |

**The epilogue clause is a correction, not a relaxation.** Revision 2 conditioned `verified` solely
on loop-L verdicts, which epilogue tasks never receive — loop L is a per-lane loop and an epilogue
task has no lane. So a requirement whose tasks were routed to the epilogue **for charter C1
compliance** could reach `implemented` and never `verified`, in any wave, forever. That inverts
`00.charter.md:297-298`: the charter forbids marking verified *without* evidence, not marking verified
*with* evidence of a different kind. The epilogue's evidence is R1/R2/R3/R7–R11 plus a green
`verification_cmd` at the host, which is the same substance a lane's is, minus the isolation rows
that do not apply.

A requirement carrying **any** `test_id: null` acceptance row (§6.5) may reach `implemented` and
**may not** reach `verified` in this wave; its `untested_owner` names the wave or lane that will.
That is the mechanical form of `00.charter.md:297-298` at the orchestrator's own promotion step; the
`R-PLAN` rung, which promotes through `--close-reqs` rather than here, carries the same charter rule as
the second disjunct of `plan-coverage-unclosed` (§13, `09` §5.2 step 1).

**The evidence, per requirement, all four calls at the host root.**

```
add_verification_evidence(id, type="test",   ref=<verification_cmd>, detail=<lane audit.json path>)
add_verification_evidence(id, type="commit", ref=<the integrate-lane merge_sha for its lane>)
add_trace_link(id, type="Code", relation="implements", ref=<each write_set path>)
update_status(id, <implemented|verified>)
```

These are the same four `kiwi-coder §0.12` calls a phase-2 lane defers (§P2 5.4) — in phase 2
replayed at 3.k with `(tool, args_hash)` dedupe; in phase 1 executed inline by the unit itself
(§5.14) — plus the `update_status` transition 3.n performs itself. Nothing is
promoted on a prose verdict alone: `waves-event.md:101` says `verification` is **evidence, not
authority**, and §4.5 forbids `journal` as a sole proof kind on a verdict-bearing line, so the
promotion line carries an `mcp-state` proof recomputable by `get_requirement`.

---

## 8. Wave-boundary issue protocol

The charter: *"These must not be carried silently into the next stage. Plan how to resolve them,
write the plan to a document, and resolve it under the existing rules. Only then may the next stage
begin."*

**The charter's "stage" is this design's `wave` (§2.0).** The first revision wrote *"A stage here
means a wave"* while §5.3 used `stage` for an intra-wave topological layer and §2 dispatched "per
stage" on that second meaning — so the two gates below fired at **wave** boundaries while an issue
discovered between **intra-wave stages** had no gate at all, which is the charter's own case. §2.0
fixes the vocabulary; this section is renamed with it, and §8.1 supplies the missing intra-wave
route.

`wave-issue-triage` runs at 3.m, after loop P and before `promote-requirements`, and writes
`waves/wave-{n}/issues.md` plus a generated `issues.lock.json`. Its input is the union of:

- **every pre-merge loop's residual** — loops D, W, H and L, not only loop P. The shared engine
  supports `pass + residual` (`kiwi-wave-master:358`) and all five loops use it; loop L's PASS
  condition is `CRITICAL=0` and `HIGH=0` (§7.3), so **MEDIUM findings survive as residuals by
  design**. Revision 2's input list named loop P's `verification.residual[]` alone, so a lane that
  closed loop L with two MEDIUM residuals in stage 1 carried them past 3.m, past
  `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED`, and into wave 2 with nothing recording them — against
  `00.charter.md:91-92`, *"new issues sometimes surface. These must not be carried silently into the
  next stage."* Adding four loops to a union is the narrowest fix; leaving them out made the charter
  clause half-implemented;
- loop P's `verification.residual[]`;
- the claim-audit findings from both `audit.3h.json` and `audit.3j.json` (§3.2);
- the merge gate's coupling records and 3.f″'s `stage-coupling` records (§7.9 (a));
- every lane carrying a terminal `lane_disposition` (§4.2) — `demoted`, `quarantined`,
  `coupling-reset`, `refuted` — with its unlanded tasks recorded as `missing-task`;
- every lane that terminated `design-refuted` or `needs-user`;
- `duplication-audit.md`'s `duplicate` rows (§7.9 (b)), each **already opened as a `local-defect` row
  at 3.k** (§2.1 3.k activity (2)) and re-confirmed here unless the user rules otherwise — the audit
  runs at 3.k activity (2), after the epilogue set has already executed, so an unanticipated duplicate
  has no in-wave task to name and this is the row that closes it, which is why the row must exist at
  3.k for `cross-lane-duplication-unresolved` to be dischargeable there;
- and anything the user raises.

Every issue receives exactly one classification from a **closed** list:

| class | meaning | route |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `local-defect` | the code is wrong, the design is right | `/kiwi-review-fix-loop --base --head` over the wave window, **resolved before the next wave** |
| `missing-task` | design right, plan incomplete | pipeline re-entry with `--req-filter` **and** `--plan-run-id` together, **resolved before the next wave** |
| `design-gap` | the design is under-specified | append design items as a **new** artifact (never an in-place edit, `kiwi-wave-master:189`), incremental `/kiwi-srs`, re-enter from planning; a **new** `00.design.lock.json` is written and `invariant_digest` legitimately changes |
| `new-wave-required` | needs its own wave | append a wave under the `:22` exception, capped at 3 per run (`wave-append-cap-exhausted`) |
| `design-contradiction` | a design item is **false** — implementing it as written contradicts another item or a declared constraint | `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` (C). Halt, name the two contradicting `[D-nnn]` ids with evidence, end the run through `abort-run` (§5.12) so the user is told what state the repository is in |
| `out-of-run` | belongs to no wave | recorded with an `exclusion_class` from the closed vocabulary (`waves-event.md:120`) |

**`P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED`** — the precondition on the next wave's Phase 3.a, evaluated by
`orchestrate wave close --wave N`:

- every issue carries a terminal classification, and
- every `local-defect` and `missing-task` carries a **resolution proof** (a resolvable `file:line`,
  a test id, or a commit sha), and
- every `design-gap` names its new design lock digest, and
- every `out-of-run` and every `new-wave-required` carries a **recorded user decision**.

**Deferral is a user decision, not a free escape.** `out-of-run` requires user consent **even under
`--auto`**, on the same rule that governs `out_of_scope` at the coverage gate
(`kiwi-wave-master:205`). This is the difference between the charter's sentence and a routing table
that lets everything be "carried into wave-{m}" with no one noticing.

**Stated limitation, and its compensation.** The tool checks the *form* of the classification, not
its *correctness*. Labelling a `local-defect` as `out-of-run` satisfies the tool. The compensation is
that the issue list is loop P's `residual[]` — externally fixed — and the **next** wave's loop P
carries the previous wave's `out-of-run` items as a re-checked layer, in the same way §5.6's
`out_of_scope` layer is re-confirmed per item by both verifiers (`kiwi-wave-master:435`). This is
recorded in §17 as a known weakness, not hidden.

`design-contradiction` ends the run because amending the design means re-materialising design
baselines, which is re-decomposition, which by the immutability rule (`kiwi-wave-master:22`) is a new
run. It is expensive and it is the correct price; it is bounded at one per run by construction, and
it is not committee-decidable because the committee would be voting on which half of the design to
discard.

### 8.1 Mid-wave — a design item found wrong *inside* a wave

`design-gap` and `design-contradiction` above are wave-boundary classes, evaluated at 3.m. Mid-wave
there was no route at all, and the two mechanisms that should have supplied one worked against each
other: a lane that found `[D-031]` unimplementable had only `lease-breach-requested` (wrong scope),
death (which discards the finding), or implementing something wrong — while `invariant_digest` over
the frozen block made any mid-wave design amendment a `run-invariant-drift` halt **by construction**.
So the mid-wave "design found wrong" case resolved either as a silent wrong
implementation or as a drift halt with no sanctioned amendment route. Both are unacceptable.

> **Phase 1 keeps this route, with a different carrier, a different step and no lane branch (§13,
> §2.1, `08:118-119`).** The gate id `lane-design-refuted` (C), the `amend-design` verb and the
> amendment path below are phase-1 content (§2's recovery table, §4.4). What is **phase-2 vocabulary
> retained in place** is the intake's mechanism: phase 1 has no lane manifest (**§P2 3.4**) and no
> step 3.h (§2.1), so the **executing unit reports the refutation and stops**, and the gate is raised
> at **3.g**, and at **3.k activity (0)** when the reporting unit is the epilogue — same executor,
> same `escalation` instruction (§6.1, §6.3) — (§13). Its commits stay on `frozen.integration_branch` — §5.14 creates no lane branch —
> so *"its branch is preserved un-merged"* reads in phase 1 as *the unit's commits are left in place,
> unreverted*. In step 5, *"re-dispatched"* is one `execute-unit` over the re-authored handoff (§4.4,
> §5.14), and the re-dispatch cap belongs to **§P2 7.3**, which phase 1 does not have.

**The intake:** manifest `status: "design-refuted"` with `{design_item_id, evidence}` (§3.4), raised
at 3.h as `lane-design-refuted` (C). The lane stops; its branch is preserved un-merged; the finding
is a first-class artifact rather than a note inside a dead lane's report.

**The amendment path, which is legitimate and therefore must not read as drift.** The `amend-design`
verb (§4.4) reuses §4.7's append-new-artifact machinery, extended from the lane plan to the design
lock:

1. Author the amendment as a **new** `design/00.design.{seq}.md` and freeze it into a **new**
   `00.design.lock.json` — never an in-place edit.
2. Append a journal line naming the old lock digest, the new one, the refuted `[D-nnn]` and the
   lane's evidence.
3. The card's `frozen.design_lock` pointer moves to the new lock, and `invariant_digest` is
   recomputed over the updated `frozen` block. `run-invariant-drift` then fires only when the digest
   disagrees with the lock the card **currently names** — which is drift, and not this.
4. Every downstream denominator that was frozen on the old `design_items[]` — loops W, P and F — is
   re-frozen from the new lock, and the affected rounds restart. This is expensive and it is stated
   as such; it is cheaper than the alternative, which is shipping a design item known to be false.
5. Affected lanes are re-planned. A lane whose `design_items[]` intersects the refuted item is
   re-authored and re-dispatched from a **new** handoff; the re-dispatch cap of 1 (**§P2 7.3**) does not
   apply, because this is not a retry of the same handoff.

**Bound:** 2 mid-wave amendments per wave. The third is `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` (C) —
at that point the design, not the lane, is the problem.

---

## 9. D1 — the shared engine, and exactly what changes in `kiwi-wave-master`

D1 names three overlapping parts: **wave splitting, verification loops, merging.** All three are
extracted. Only extracting the verification loop would leave the other two duplicated, which is what
D1 exists to prevent.

### 9.1 The four new `_shared/kiwi/` modules

Created **by moving text, not writing text**, in all three variants
(`skills/{claude,codex,etc}/_shared/kiwi/`).

> **Three of the four are phase-1 modules; `isolation-and-integration.md` is phase 2.** Its entire
> content — the five-operation isolation interface, the profile table, the probe, the lane contract,
> `integrate()`'s restore-and-CAS mechanism — is Part P2 material. **Three clauses of it are not, and
> they must not be lost with it**: the convergence-registry `recipe.kind` enum with its lane-eligibility
> rule; the **normative shipped-default convergence registry** — §P2 5.9's *Normative shipped default*
> JSON, `CP-01`, `CP-02` and `CP-07` — which §2.1 ordering rule 1 reads when it subtracts every
> `orchestrator-only` path (`plan_path` is `orchestrator-only` because `CP-07` says so), which §2.1
> 3.k activity (1) reads as its recipe vocabulary, and on which §7.6's denominator rests (*"`CP-02`
> and `CP-07` are universal shipped defaults, so every repository has epilogue tasks"*); and *never
> merge to the base branch* + the mandatory post-merge `validate` → `sync-index`.
> All three ship in phase 1 (§5.14, §5.12, §2.1, §7.6), so they move into **`run-ledger.md`** instead
> — the **merge-time** `recipe` meaning table stays in §P2 5.9, because §5.2 already carries the
> phase-1 per-kind effect and the most-restrictive-wins precedence order — and
> `isolation-and-integration.md` is authored in phase 2 around what remains. D1's extraction is
> therefore a **three**-module change in `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` plus
> `wave-srs-registration.md` (§9.2), and a fourth module in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`. §15.3 S6
> moves with it.

| Module | Content | Moved from | What returns if the rule is lost |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `verify-loop.md` v1.0.0 | evidence-bundle rules; two stances; externally fixed frozen denominators and the invalid-round-on-count-mismatch rule; isolated → add-only cross-refutation → mechanical union; verifier re-spawn from round 2; three closing paths with the streak cost; termination table, caps, unreachable-PASS; `pass+residual` vs `fail-residual`; **new: oscillation detector (§7.8)**; delegation-only remediation routing table with the explicit-scope requirement and "a sub-loop's own PASS does not close a parent finding"; cross-wave carry-forward by file-set intersection | `kiwi-wave-master:249-425` | the whole anti-collusion and anti-skim apparatus; a cheap cap becomes unsafe; the orchestrator grows a fixer |
| `wave-decomposition.md` v1.0.0 | the two-branch split heuristic and the 3–8 bound; `existing_modules` + dependency direction as splitter input; design-baseline materialisation with the one-normative-sentence granularity rule; `integration_items`; the closed `exclusion_class` vocabulary; always-write-empty-constraints; append-new-over-edit-in-place for late inputs; carry-forward-wave denominator conversion; journal-only resolvability; the coverage gate with the **full `design_items` set** as comparison unit and user consent on any non-empty `out_of_scope` | `kiwi-wave-master:154-205` | one module cut across waves; `unmapped=0` becomes free; sub-section design passes a top-level check |
| `isolation-and-integration.md` v1.0.0 | the five-operation isolation interface and which two of the five are **not** profile-blind; the profile table and the probe procedure; the lane contract **including the commit obligation**; the convergence-registry recipe enum, **the `recipe.kind` lane-eligibility rule and the normative shipped default (`CP-01`, `CP-02`, `CP-07`)** — all three of these move to `run-ledger.md` per the note above, and only the **merge-time** `recipe` meaning table stays; the `integrate()` restore-and-CAS mechanism (§5.9); the **never-merge-to-the-base-branch** rule; the mandatory post-merge `validate` + `sync-index` sequence | **new**, plus `kiwi-pipeline:145-160` moved out of that skill's body | silent downgrade; C4's silent index corruption |
| `run-ledger.md` v1.0.0 | resume-card schema and cap; the closed verb enum and three recovery classes; write-ahead-intent / write-behind-result discipline; the proof-kind table; the reconciliation predicate; the four drift digests; the run-contract preamble convention; **plus the three clauses reassigned from `isolation-and-integration.md` per the note above** — the convergence-registry `recipe.kind` enum with its lane-eligibility rule, the **normative shipped default (`CP-01`, `CP-02`, `CP-07`)**, and the **never-merge-to-the-base-branch** rule with the mandatory post-merge `validate` + `sync-index` sequence | **new** | an interrupted action is indistinguishable from a not-started one |

**Artifact root becomes a parameter.** `kiwi-wave-master` hard-codes
`docs/analysis/kiwi-wave-master-{run_id}/` at `:173` and `:181`. `wave-decomposition.md` takes the
artifact root as an input: wave-master passes `docs/analysis/kiwi-wave-master-{run_id}/`, the
orchestrator passes `docs/research/{work}/`. This is what makes one baseline implementation serve
both callers under D3, and it is why there is **one** `freeze.ts`, not two.

### 9.2 What changes in `kiwi-wave-master`, precisely

| Change | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| §3, §3.1, §3.2 replaced by a `§0` reference to `wave-decomposition.md` | the sanctioned mechanism (`auto-option.md:285`: *"사본을 만들지 않는다"*) |
| §5.5, §5.6 replaced by a `§0` reference to `verify-loop.md` + its own denominator table | wave-master keeps four layers; the orchestrator adds two |
| §2.1 preflight, §4 target registration, §6 journal/resume, §7 options, §8 epic entry | **unchanged, stay in the skill** |
| `wt-delegation-refused` (`:39`, `:144`), the serial-by-contract statement (`:235`), `--skip-regression` / `--reviewer-off` refusals (`:148`), the `critical_gates[]` table | **unchanged, stay in the skill** |
| `docs/analysis/kiwi-wave-master-{run_id}/` | now passed as the artifact-root argument, not hard-coded |
| `pipeline-event.md` §4 T1 | gains the **missing** `kiwi-wave-master` row — it is in the §3 enum at `:84` but has no T1 row, so even the existing skill has no declared routing hint. Fixed while adding the orchestrator's row |
| Size | **573** → ~300 lines, behaviour unchanged (`wc -l skills/claude/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md` → 573; the first revision said "roughly 574") |

**§4 target registration stays in wave-master and is *duplicated by reference*, not by text.** The
orchestrator's Phase 3.b needs the same rules — excerpt as `--research-doc`, `constraints_path` as
`--constraints-doc`, `existing_modules` as authoring input, carried residuals collected in full, and
the `srs_authored` idempotency marker with its "only `srs-authoring` lines without the marker read as
in-progress" clause (`kiwi-wave-master:209-227`). One skill referencing another skill's body is not
a sanctioned pattern, so these 19 lines move into a fifth shared module,
**`wave-srs-registration.md` v1.0.0**, referenced by both. This is a small module and it exists only
to avoid a copy; that is the correct reason for a module to exist.

### 9.3 The extraction's red-green cycle — the only honest one available

Moving text out of `kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md` breaks the section-scoped assertions over that text.
They are the only regression net that exists. **The first revision said "247 … across five files
(19 + 21 + 57 + 73 + 77, measured)" without naming the files or the unit, and a critic measuring a
different quantity over a different file set got 870. Both numbers are right; the unit was
missing.** Measured, with the files named:

| File | `it()` blocks | `expect()` calls | lines |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `test/skills/kiwi-wave-master-content.test.ts` | 19 | 73 | 761 |
| `test/skills/kiwi-wave-verify-content.test.ts` | 21 | 112 | 742 |
| `test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-content.test.ts` | 57 | 214 | 1196 |
| `test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r2-content.test.ts` | 73 | 210 | 1437 |
| `test/skills/kiwi-wave-continuity-r3-content.test.ts` | 77 | 261 | 1612 |
| **total** | **247** | **870** | 5748 |

So: **247 `it()` blocks and 870 `expect()` calls across those five files.** Step 1's *"re-run the
existing mutation set"* **exposure surface** is scoped by the **870**, not the 247 — every one of those
assertions must be re-checked after the harness change, because each ranges over the appended module
bodies — and that is materially larger than the first revision implied. The exposure surface is not the
mutation denominator; the denominator is stated in step 1. The
extraction is done in a fixed four-step order, and it **must not share a wave with new-skill
authoring**:

1. **Harness change, must stay green.** Change the readers to
   `readResolvedSkill(variant, name)`, which reads the SKILL.md and **appends** the bodies of every
   `_shared/kiwi/` module its `§0` table references, in the order the `§0` table lists them.
   Against unextracted sources this is a no-op, so all 247 assertions stay green — and the harness
   change is verified precisely by staying green.
   - **Concatenation order is pinned to append, and this is load-bearing.** `sectionUnder` uses
     `findIndex`, and `auto-option.md:224` is `## 5. critical_gates[] 인터페이스`, which matches the
     `gateSection` regex used against wave-master, `kiwi-pipeline`, `kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-coder`. If
     concatenation prepended, every gate-table assertion would silently resolve against
     `auto-option.md`'s §5 and go red against unmodified sources.
   - **Heading-collision de-duplication.** Appended module bodies have their headings prefixed with
     the module name (`## [verify-loop.md] 5. …`) so no appended heading can win a `findIndex`
     against the skill's own.
   - **Re-run the existing mutation set after the harness change.** 37 `windowsAround` proximity
     assertions plus the body-wide `expect(body).toMatch` calls — 870 `expect()` calls in total over
     the five files above — now range over ~600 extra lines, and could be satisfied by shared-module
     prose instead of the sentence under test. **The exposed assertion set and the mutation denominator
     are two different quantities:** the 870 `expect()` calls above are what the harness change exposes
     and what must be re-checked; the **mutations** are counted over §10.3's bilingual modal set,
     **counted per variant** — measured, the `반드시` / `절대` / `금지` / `MUST` / `SHALL` carriers over
     the four extracted skills are claude **89**, codex **77**, etc **71**, so the two are not
     coextensive and the smaller number is the authoring cost.
     *"The existing mutation set"* names those assertions, not a checked-in record — the earlier rounds' mutations were never
     written down, and `test/skills/orchestrator-mutation-ledger.json` (§10.3) is scoped to the new
     skill and does not cover these five files. Each mutation is therefore **authored in this step**,
     one per existing normative sentence (§10.3's bilingual modal set, counted per variant) drawn
     from §10.3's five mutation classes, applied and observed red: authoring cost, not retrieval.
     Keyed on English `MUST` alone the obligation would be a **no-op against the files that exist**: the four skills this extraction touches
     (`kiwi-wave-master`, `kiwi-pipeline`, `kiwi-pm`, `kiwi-coder`) carry zero `MUST` tokens in all
     three variants. A harness change that loosens an assertion is a regression, and this is the only
     way to detect it.
2. **Move the text, without adding the `§0` reference.** A broad, genuine, unfaked **red** across
   all three variants, whose failure list enumerates exactly which rules the move touched. This is
   the proof that the move lost nothing.
3. **Add the `§0` reference line.** Green.
4. **Assert the `§0` reference line itself exists in all three variants** — red first (the assertion
   is new), then green.

### 9.4 Mirror inclusion

`.agents/skills/.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json` currently excludes `["kiwi-step",
"kiwi-wave-master"]` as hermeticity leak sentinels for `test/support/repo-hermeticity.ts:11-18`.

**That file's own recorded `reason` field begins *"FR-FLOW-029 intentional exclusion…"*, and this
run must not disturb it.** `06:35-45` establishes `FR-FLOW-029` as `verified` on mainline with 25
successors (`FR-FLOW-042`..`FR-FLOW-066`), two recording `| FR-FLOW-029 | extends |` explicitly — so
the citation is sound, and the exclusions file is one of the artifacts anchored to it. §9.2's
wave-master rewrite therefore touches a mirror-excluded skill whose exclusion rationale points at a
requirement `06` rules must not be retracted: **§9.2's edits must not alter the exclusion entry or
its stated reason**, and no step of this design may remove `kiwi-wave-master` from that list.

**`kiwi-orchestrator` is NOT added to the exclusion list.** It is mirrored normally.

The historical prune defect — `speckiwi skills install codex <skill>` deleting
`.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md` because "the only skill referencing it is
mirror-excluded" — is **fixed**, not worked around: `FR-NODE-090` ("Shared resource prune keeps files
a mirror-excluded skill references", target `v2.5.0`, **Status verified**) exists at
`docs/spec/50.nodejs-implementation.srs.md:6117`, with
`test/core/skills/shared-prune-exclusions.fr-node-090.test.ts`, and
`.agents/skills/_shared/kiwi/waves-event.md` is present today. This design therefore does **not**
claim that mirroring the orchestrator "closes the bug" — that framing would make a future prune
regression undetectable by design, because an accidental reference would mask it. Mirroring is
chosen because it is the default and there is no sentinel reason to deviate; the prune bug is
someone else's closed requirement.

The five new `_shared` modules are mirrored with everything else, and
`test/skills/kiwi-event-contract-content.test.ts` is extended to assert them across all four copies.

**The prune test must cover the combination this design creates, and today it does not.** The five
new `_shared` modules (§9.1's four plus `wave-srs-registration.md`) will be referenced by **both** a
mirrored skill (`kiwi-orchestrator`) **and** a mirror-excluded one (`kiwi-wave-master`). `FR-NODE-090`
is closed and covers the mirror-excluded-referrer case, but nothing states that
`test/core/skills/shared-prune-exclusions.fr-node-090.test.ts` covers the **both-referenced**
combination. Extending it with that case is part of **this** work, and it is step 6′ of §10.5 — not a
courtesy assumed of someone else's closed requirement.

### 9.5 Who is allowed to write `.agents/skills/**`

CP-05 marks `.agents/skills/**` `orchestrator-only`, and the first revision's note said regeneration
*"requires `speckiwi skills install`, forbidden against this repo by charter §6"* — so the only
permitted writer was the orchestrator, and the orchestrator was forbidden from writing it. Meanwhile
§9.4 mirrors `kiwi-orchestrator` normally, §14 registration 5 requires `waves-event.md` v1.4.0 *"in
all four copies … edited in one change"*, and E28 asserts set-equality across all four shipped
copies. Verified: `scripts/` holds only `release-check.mjs` and `version-check.mjs`; the mirror is
maintained out of band today. Four-copy parity is a shipping requirement with no sanctioned writer.

**The sanctioned mechanism, named:** a new verb `speckiwi skills mirror --check | --write`, which
regenerates `.agents/skills/**` from `skills/codex/**` honouring
`.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json`, and which is **not** `init` and **not** `skills install` — so
`00.charter.md:303-304`'s prohibition (never run `speckiwi init`, `speckiwi skills install`, or
`speckiwi upgrade --apply` against this repository as the target root) is untouched.
`--check` is the CI/gate form and is what E28 calls. This is **step 6″ of §10.5's work order**, it is
a numbered step rather than an assumption, and it is small: the copy rules already exist inside the
install path and are being given their own entry point.

---

## 10. Executable code versus skill prose

Applying §1.1's boundary rule. Everything in §10.1 is a function in `src/` with a real test;
everything in §10.2 is prose asserted by text-contract tests, and the design says so plainly rather
than implying behavioural coverage.

### 10.1 What becomes code

**Phase split over this list, stated once so the module inventory is not read twice.** Four modules
are **phase 2** and are named here rather than removed, because their consumers in Part P2 cite them:
`claim-audit.ts` (§P2 7.4), `replay.ts` (§P2 5.9's deferred-mutation queue), and the isolation and
`integrate()` halves of whatever module phase 2 gives them — neither of which revision 3 had listed
here as a module at all, which is itself worth noting. Three modules are **new in revision 4** for
routing: `route.ts`, `route-probe.ts`, `route-lock.ts`. Everything else ships in
`2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` unchanged, including all of `lane-plan.ts`, `conflict.ts`,
`substrate.ts`, `grounding.ts` and `task-catalog.ts` — the analysis is executable code in phase 1
even though nothing executes its output concurrently.

```
src/core/orchestrator/
  route.ts             computeRoute(probe, opts) -> RouteDecision            (pure, TOTAL; §2.1a, 09 §3)
                       Closed Rung and DisqualifierId enums; disqualifier-first — every predicate
                       REMOVES rungs and none selects one; fixed order R-PLAN -> R-STEP -> R-ORCH;
                       deterministic `removed[]` ordering. R-ORCH is removed by no predicate, so
                       the function is total by construction and a property test asserts it.
                       The route is COMPUTED, NEVER VOTED: a committee-decided route is not
                       reproducible and cannot be traced to an input (09 §3.1). Only the
                       PRESENTATION is a gate.
  route-probe.ts       parseRouteProbe(json) -> RouteProbe   (shape at 09 §3.6, beside RouteDecision)
                       deriveAnchoredRequirements(records, filePaths) -> string[]      (S3)
                       computeAnchorCoverage(records) -> number                        (S3c)
                       An unreadable field becomes an explicit `unreadable[]` entry and NEVER a
                       default — failing open on S3 yields the empty set, which ENABLES the step
                       rung; that is fail-open in the dangerous direction (09 §3.3 D8).
  route-lock.ts        freezeRoute(probe, decision, gate) -> RouteLock
                       `gate` = routing/route-gate.json (`09` §8.3, §9.1), the gate-resolution
                       record: it supplies proposed_rung, overridden_by and gates[].resolution,
                       which RouteDecision does not carry. It names a selection
                       ("proposed" | "alternative"), never a rung.
                       content-addressed; its digest participates in the resume card's
                       invariant_digest (§4.1), which is what makes "the rung is read on resume,
                       never recomputed" enforceable rather than asserted.
  journal-schema.ts    the v1.4.0 schema as data: required/optional field sets per event kind,
                       every closed enum (status, phase, verb, recovery_class, proof_kind,
                       conflict_reason, recipe_kind, exclusion_class, reason_class, issue_class,
                       manifest_status)
  waves-journal.ts     parseWavesJournal(root, {runId, engine}) -> WavesJournalView
                       WRITTEN FRESH against mainline waves-event.md v1.3.0, carrying only the
                       branch's transition table (06:218-222). NOT ported.
  waves-validate.ts    validateWavesJournal(view) -> Diagnostic[]                      (likewise)
  verification-gate.ts evaluateRound(round) -> {verdict, violations[], streak, capRemaining,
                       unreachable}
  resume.ts            computeResumeState(view, card, gitFacts, driftInputs) -> ResumeState
                       `driftInputs: DriftInputs` widens the former `lockDigests` argument so the
                       §4.7 operands digests 2, 3 and 4 need arrive injected; without them
                       `ResumeState.drift` had no computation rule.
  resume-card.ts       readCard(text) / validateCard(card, journal: WavesJournalView) /
                       writeCard(card, journal) — 8 KB cap; verb-enum and proof-kind validation;
                       invariant_digest recomputation; the rollup rule; PROFILE IMMUTABILITY
                       (frozen.isolation_profile may never differ from the journal's
                        probe-isolation result line — §5.4).
                       THE JOURNAL VIEW IS A PARAMETER: revision 2 typed this module as
                       "read / validate / write" of the card alone and then required it to
                       enforce a rule against the journal, which E12 tests. A function cannot
                       check an input it is not given.
  preflight.ts         normaliseRoot(a, b, probe) -> {match, normalisedA, normalisedB, rule}
                       rule ∈ {"separators","trailing-separator","windows-case","realpath"} — a
                       CLOSED enum, and `probe` is an INJECTED realpath resolver so the function
                       stays pure (§10.3 lists realpath among the four rules while §10.1 typed the
                       function as pure; injection is what makes both true)
  run-lock.ts          HARVEST H2 from lease-lock.ts, re-keyed on the GIT COMMON DIR (06:161).
                       acquire/renew/release; O_EXCL sentinel; pid liveness; Windows named-pipe
                       fence. One orchestrator run per repository, not per worktree.
  readiness.ts         HARVEST H3: deriveCanonicalRequirementReadiness + scopeRequirementDiagnostics
                       (wave-spec-client.ts:456-557), pure over (snapshot, target, requirementIds).
                       Transport-independent; NOT the MCP client around them. (§5.11)
  task-catalog.ts      the extension of normalizeTasks with files/test_files/covers_ac/type/action
  conflict.ts          analyzeConflicts(catalog, registry, existingModules, priorPostmortems,
                                        constraints: {codeRoots, testRoots})
                         -> ConflictEdge[]
                       incl. the recipe.kind lane-eligibility rule with §5.2's PRECEDENCE ORDER,
                       the glob matcher (** crosses segments, * does not), the non-code-write-set
                       edge and the learned-coupling edge. NOT shared-substrate — that predicate
                       is withdrawn entirely (X-04, §7.9 (a)); what 3.f″ runs is
                       substrate.ts's planStageCoupling, which is not a conflict_reason
  substrate.ts         planStageCoupling(handoffs: ParsedHandoff[])
                         -> {couplings: Array<{path, fromLane, toLane}>}
                       (pure; §7.9 (a)). RENAMED from planStageHoist and its `hoist` output
                       DELETED — the shared-substrate predicate is unsatisfiable (X-04), and
                       `existingPaths` is no longer an input because the surviving predicate
                       does not consult it.
  lane-plan.ts         computeLanePlan(input) -> LanePlan                        (pure, deterministic)
                       input carries INJECTED existing_paths: string[]; the function never touches
                       the filesystem (§5.1)
  freeze.ts            freezeLock(kind, body, inputs) -> Lock
                       kind  = §3.3a's six targets: design|waves|lanes|handoff|issues|postmortem
                       Lock  = §3.3a's common envelope plus the per-kind `body`
                       inputs = the declared input set `inputs_digest` covers; for `lanes`, E38's
                       eight §4.7 digest-3 inputs — INJECTED, so §10.3's byte-determinism property
                       is a Layer-1 unit test rather than a disk-reading one.
                       design / waves / lanes / handoff / issues / postmortem lock generation + digests
                       HARVEST H1: real git_blob_oid + sha256 + size caps + assertPinned, with
                       runTrustedGit replaced by plain git cat-file/ls-tree with explicit cwd
                       (06:160, 06:330). No null placeholder.
  handoff.ts           validateHandoff(text, lane, catalog, root) -> {ok, violations[]}
  prose-gate.ts        scanProse(text: string)
                         -> {findings: Array<{rule: "script-block" | "hedge"
                                                    | "unmarked-normative-prose",
                                              lines: number[], token?: string}>}
                       `rule` is a closed enum.
                       script-block scanner (English-only) + the hedge-vocabulary detector
                       + the unmarked-normative-prose scanner (§3.3 rule 4).
                       BOTH DETECTORS HAVE A DECLARED VOCABULARY, and revision 2 had neither:
                       the hedge list is an exported module constant in prose-gate.ts itself
                       (bilingual, one array of tokens) — a .ts constant rather than a .json
                       asset, because `npm run build` is plain tsc with rootDir src / outDir
                       dist and copies no non-.ts file, so neither test/ (absent from the
                       package `files` list) nor a JSON under src/ reaches the published
                       package, and prose-gate.ts is product code behind a critical gate
                       (`orchestrate freeze design`, §3.3); §10.3's mutation classes import
                       the constant rather than re-deriving it; and the
                       normative scan counts TOKEN OCCURRENCES (MUST / MUST NOT / SHALL,
                       MUST NOT counted once) under a "lowest-level heading" defined as a
                       heading with no deeper heading before the next heading of the same or
                       shallower level — so no sentence splitter is needed (§3.3 rules 1, 3).
  claim-audit.ts       auditLane(manifest, actualDelta, lease, registry) -> Breach[]   (pure)
                       PHASE 2 (§P2 7.4) — its input is a lane manifest, which phase 1 never
                       produces. Not written in 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator.
  issue-ledger.ts      openIssue(ledger, row)            -> {ok, violations[]}
                       planIssue(ledger, id, class)      -> {ok, violations[]}
