                       resolveIssue(ledger, id, proof, resolution) -> {ok, violations[]}
                       deferIssue(ledger, id, reason)    -> {ok, violations[]}
                       closeWave(ledger, wave, resolution)         -> {ok, violations[]}
                       Five predicates, one return shape, the same {ok, violations[]}
                       validateHandoff uses. `ledger` is the parsed issues.md rows.
                       `resolution` is the injected set a `resolution_ref` is resolved
                       against — {existingPaths: string[], lineCounts: Record<string,
                       number>, testIds: string[], commitShas: string[]}, one member per
                       `resolution_kind`; the impure collection stays in the CLI, as it
                       does for `groundFiles`. Without it E19's RESOLVABLE evidence
                       pointer degrades to a shape check on the string.
  replay.ts            PHASE 2 (§P2 5.9) — the deferred SRS-mutation queue exists only because a
                       parallel task may not mutate SRS (charter C1). Phase 1's executor mutates
                       SRS at the host in the normal way (§5.14 difference 2), so there is no
                       queue to replay and no dedupe index to persist.
                       replayDeferredMutations(queue, dedupeIndex) -> ReplayPlan       (pure planner;
                       dedupe key is (tool, args_hash) where args_hash = sha1(canonicalJson(args))
                       — the tool name is NOT inside the hash, kiwi-coder SKILL.md:563.
                       dedupeIndex is PERSISTED at kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/replay-index.json,
                       because idempotency across a resumed session requires it to survive §5.9)
  grounding.ts         groundFiles(declaredEntries: Array<{path, lineRange?}>,
                                    existingPaths: string[],
                                    lineCounts: Record<string, number>,
                                    strict: boolean)
                         -> Array<{path, verdict, nearest?}>                        (pure; §5.1's
                       near-miss detector; the impure collection of existingPaths AND lineCounts
                       stays in the CLI)
  duplication-audit.ts planDuplicationAudit(laneDiffs, writeSets) -> AuditRow[]         (pure; §7.9)
                       Constructs CANDIDATE rows, not judgments: two lanes contributing an
                       added block with the same normalizedHash, or an added top-level
                       declaration sharing a normalized declName, is one candidate row;
                       nothing else is a row. `verdict` and `resolutionTaskId` are filled by
                       the subagent (§7.9 (b)) and the tool checks only that a verdict from
                       the closed enum was recorded per candidate (§1.1).
  auto-gate.ts         decideAutoGate(input) -> {action, memberCount, rule, reason}    (§12)
                       + `export type GateId` and `export const GATE_IDS` — the closed
                       union of §13's 39 phase-1 gate ids plus the four `business-decision`
                       routing gates (`route-proposal`, `route-step-requires-mode-switch`,
                       `tdd-route-unattended`, `route-downgrade-available`). `GateId` is
                       used by `ResumeState.blocking` and `Breach.gate` below and was
                       declared nowhere, against this section's own rule that a red test
                       cannot be written first against an undeclared type. It is
                       deliberately **not** added to `journal-schema.ts`'s closed-enum
                       list: E28 asserts that list set-equal to `waves-event.md`'s field
                       tables, and §4.2's v1.4.0 table carries no `gate` field.
src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts    the `speckiwi orchestrate` namespace
src/mcp/tools/…                    the `orchestrate_*` tool family
```

**The seven output shapes a Layer-1 test must construct, declared here because a red test cannot be
written first against an undeclared return type.** Revision 1 named the functions and left
`WavesJournalView`, `ResumeState` (and especially its injected `gitFacts`), `Breach`, `ReplayPlan`,
`AuditRow` and `evaluateRound`'s verdict vocabulary unshaped, so seven modules had no authorable red
test — five of the 23 phase-1 modules (`waves-journal`, `waves-validate`, `resume`,
`verification-gate`, `duplication-audit`) plus the phase-2 `claim-audit` and `replay`, whose `Breach`
and `ReplayPlan` are declared here for their Part P2 consumers.

```ts
type Verdict = "pass" | "pass-with-residual" | "fail-residual" | "fail-cap" | "invalid";
//  "invalid" IS a verdict value: a denominator mismatch invalidates the round (waves-event.md:99),
//  consuming the cap and resetting the streak, which is different from failing it.

interface WavesJournalView { runId: string; engine: Engine; lines: WavesEvent[];
                             byVerb: Map<string, WavesEvent[]>;      // key: verb|wave|stage|lane
                             latestPerWave: Map<number, WavesEvent>;
                             schemaVersions: string[]; diagnostics: Diagnostic[] }

interface GitFacts {   // INJECTED; resume.ts never shells out
  branches: Array<{ name: string; sha: string; ancestorOfIntegration: boolean }>;
  worktrees: Array<{ path: string; branch: string | null; locked: boolean }>;
  heartbeats: Array<{ lane: string; mtimeMs: number }>;
  integrationHead: string; hostStatusPaths: string[];
}
interface ResumeState { classification: LaneClass[]; nextAction: NextAction;
                        drift: DriftReport; blocking: GateId | null }
// One entry per §4.7 digest, so `drift` has a shape a Layer-1 fixture can construct and assert.
interface DriftReport { digests: Array<{ index: 1|2|3|4;
                                         outcome: "match" | "stale-not-wrong" | "drift";
                                         gate: "run-invariant-drift" | "lane-plan-drift" | null;
                                         detail: string }> }
// `stale-not-wrong` with `gate: null` is §4.7 digest 3's `W`-not-`C` case — a differing
// `existing_paths_digest` or `prior_postmortem_digests[]`, recorded and not recomputed mid-wave.

interface Breach { row: "R1"|"R2"|"R3"|"R4"|"R5"|"R6"|"R7"|"R8"|"R9"|"R10"|"R11";
                   gate: GateId; detail: string; paths?: string[] }

interface ReplayPlan { calls: Array<{ tool: string; args: unknown; argsHash: string;
                                      action: "apply" | "skip-duplicate" }>;
                       indexAfter: Record<string, string[]> }

interface AuditRow { symbolOrBlock: string; lanes: string[]; paths: string[];
                     verdict: "duplicate" | "parallel-evolution" | "acceptable";
                     resolutionTaskId: string | null }
```

**The INPUT shapes, declared for the same reason.** Revision 2 declared seven *output* shapes and
stopped, leaving six modules with an unauthorable red test — §10.5 step 1 is *"Fixtures + Layer-1
tests … All red"*, and a fixture cannot be written without the input contract. The gap was widest
exactly where the value is highest: `evaluateRound` is §10.5 step 3's "ship first even if nothing
else does", and `decideAutoGate` is the entire executable surface of the second `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` work item
(§12), with neither its input nor its `action` vocabulary enumerated anywhere — including in
`journal-schema.ts`'s closed-enum list.

```ts
interface Round {                    // evaluateRound's sole argument
  loop: "D" | "W" | "H" | "L" | "P" | "F";
  scope: string;                     // §3.1's closed {scope} vocabulary
  roundIndex: number;                // 1-based
  mode: "normal" | "max" | "mini" | "explicit";   // supplied by the caller, never inferred
  cap: number; streakBefore: number;
  frozenDenominator: number;         // externally fixed at round entry
  rows: Array<{ id: string; verdict: string; severity: "CRITICAL"|"HIGH"|"MEDIUM"|"LOW" }>;
  fixAppliedThisRound: boolean;
  regression: { failingTests: string[]; baselineFailingTests: string[] | null; exitCode: number };
  residual: Array<{ id: string; reasonClass: string }>;
}

interface TaskCatalogEntry { id: string; phaseId: string; type: string; action: string;
                             reqIds: string[]; files: Array<{ path: string; lineRange?: string }>;
                             testFiles: string[]; coversAc: string[];
                             dependsOnTask: string[]; tdd: { phase: "red" | "green" } | null }
interface ConvergencePoint { id: string; paths: string[]; class?: string;
                             recipe: { kind: RecipeKind; command?: string } }
interface LanePlanInput { catalog: TaskCatalogEntry[]; registry: ConvergencePoint[];
                          existingModules: string[]; existingPaths: string[];
                          priorPostmortems: Array<{ fromTask: string; toTask: string; path: string;
                                                    detectedAt: string; resolution: string }>;
                          designItemMap: Record<string, string[]>;   // req_id -> [D-nnn]
                          laneCap: number;
                          codeRoots: string[]; testRoots: string[] }
interface Lane  { laneId: string; stage: number; taskIds: string[]; writeSet: string[];
                  readSet: string[]; reqIds: string[]; designItems: string[] }
interface Stage { index: number; laneIds: string[] }

interface Lease { writeSet: string[]; readSet: string[];
                  codeRoots: string[]; testRoots: string[];
                  handoffKind: "lane" | "remediation" | "epilogue" }   // selects the row set, §7.4
interface LaneDiff { laneId: string; paths: string[]; base: string; head: string;
                     addedBlocks: Array<{ path: string; normalizedHash: string;
                                         declName: string | null }> }
interface ParsedHandoff { kind: "lane" | "remediation" | "epilogue"; lane: string;
                          wave: number; stage: number | null;
                          frontMatter: Record<string, unknown>;
                          headings: string[]; body: string }
interface LockDigests { design: string; waves: string; lanes: string;
                        handoff: Record<string, string>; issues: string; postmortem: string }
// `computeResumeState`'s fourth argument. `LockDigests` alone cannot produce `ResumeState.drift`:
// §4.7's digests 2, 3 and 4 read operands no journal view, card, git fact or lock digest carries.
// They arrive injected, exactly as `gitFacts` and `existing_paths` already do — the impure
// collection stays in the CLI.
interface DriftInputs {
  lockDigests: LockDigests;
  recordedLaneInputs: {                 // what `lanes.lock.json` records (§3.3a), for digest 3
    sidecarDigest: string; registryDigest: string; existingPathsDigest: string;
    designItemMapDigest: string; priorPostmortemDigests: string[];
    laneCap: number; codeRoots: string[]; testRoots: string[] };
  recomputedLaneInputDigests: {         // the same five re-read and re-digested now
    sidecarDigest: string; registryDigest: string; existingPathsDigest: string;
    designItemMapDigest: string; priorPostmortemDigests: string[] };
  freshIntentDigests: Record<string, string>;  // per intent line id, digest 2's comparand
  handoffProseDigests: Record<string, string>; // per lane, digest 4's comparand
}

interface LaneStatus {                 // `orchestrate lane status --json` returns LaneStatus[]
  lane: string; wave: number; stage: number;
  refs: string[];                      // the lane's ref family, §3.5a step 2
  headRef: string; headSha: string | null;
  workspace: { path: string; locked: boolean } | null;
  heartbeat: { ageMs: number; bootstrapDoneAt: string | null; taskId: string | null } | null;
  manifestStatus: ManifestStatus | null;      // §3.4's closed seven-value enum
  liveness: "live" | "unknown" | "dead";      // §4.6's L(k)
  terminal: boolean; deadlineAt: string; deadlineExceeded: boolean;
}

interface AutoGateInput {              // decideAutoGate's sole argument
  gateId: string; critical: boolean;
  options: Array<{ id: string; recommended: boolean; defaultIfAuto: boolean }>;
  mode: "auto" | "auto-max";
  votes: Array<{ member: string; optionId: string; confidence: number }> | null;
  quorum: { expected: number; present: number };
  tieRung: boolean;                    // §12's construction parameter, default false
}
type AutoGateAction = "adopt-recommended" | "adopt-default-if-auto" | "adopt-majority"
                    | "escalate-critical" | "add-two-and-revote";   // last only when tieRung
```

`AutoGateAction` joins `journal-schema.ts`'s closed-enum list beside `verb`, `recovery_class` and the
rest, so the parity test in §10.4 covers it.

**Coverage registration, in the same change.** Measured `vitest.config.ts:33-39`: the coverage
`include` list is `src/cli/**`, `src/core/{mutation,parser,validator,workflow}/**`, `src/mcp/**`.
`src/core/orchestrator/**` — where all 23 phase-1 modules above go (25 across both phases) — is **not** included, so the design's
largest executable surface would carry no threshold at all, while the modules it *modifies*
(`src/core/workflow/validate.ts`, `jsonl.ts`) sit inside a 90% lines/statements threshold
(`vitest.config.ts:49`). `src/core/orchestrator/**/*.ts` is added to `include` with the same
`parserWorkflowThreshold` the workflow tree carries, in the same change that creates the directory,
and it is recorded as a contract registration (§14, item 10).

**Journal I/O reuse, and the one change it forces.** `parseWorkflowJsonl`
(`src/core/workflow/jsonl.ts:192`) and `appendWorkflowJsonl` (`:269`) already give line parsing,
invalid-line diagnostics, trailing-LF handling, and an `expectedSha256` optimistic-concurrency
guard (`:282`). They are reused — **but not naively**. Measured:

- `eventKey(event)` is `` `${skill}|${run_id}` `` (`jsonl.ts:76-78`), and `waves.jsonl` events carry
  no `skill` field and legitimately carry many lines per run;
- duplicate keys raise `SRS-W053` unconditionally (`:217-220`);
- `computeCorrections` builds `byRunId` as **one entry per run_id** (`:128-131`);
- `DEFAULT_SCHEMA_VERSIONS = ["1.0.0"]` (`:70`);
- `appendWorkflowJsonl`'s default `policy` is `"halt"` and it halts on **any** diagnostic, including
  ones parsed from the pre-existing file (`:277`, `:286-288`).

So a naive reuse would deny every append after the first line of a run. The **minimal additive
change** — one option, default-preserving, covered by the existing
`test/core/workflow/validator.test.ts` and `jsonl` tests:

```ts
export interface WorkflowJsonlParseOptions {
  // …existing…
  /** "skill-run" (default) keys events as `${skill}|${run_id}` and diagnoses duplicates and
   *  resolves CORRECTION chains. "none" is for append-only journals where many lines per run are
   *  the contract: no duplicate diagnostics, no correction resolution. */
  eventKeying?: "skill-run" | "none";
}
```

`waves.jsonl` readers pass `{ eventKeying: "none", supportedSchemaVersions:
["1.0.0","1.1.0","1.2.0","1.3.0","1.4.0"] }`. Existing callers pass nothing and behave identically.
That is reuse, not a fork, and the alternative — a second JSONL implementation — is the duplication
the charter forbids.

**`WorkflowArtifactKind`** (`src/core/workflow/artifacts.ts`) gains `waves`, `resume-card` and
`handoff` — the `lane-manifest` kind is **phase 2** (§15.5 `E22 (partial)`, §P2 3.5), because §3.1
makes the lane manifest a phase-2 artifact — and its `/^\.kiwi\/sessions\/([^/]+)\//` run-id derivation and
canonical-path bonus are extended for the `lanes/{lane}/` layout (§5.4).

**`verification-gate.ts` is the highest-value extraction in the design**, independent of everything
else. `waves-event.md` states its mechanical invariants as **bullet list items**, not table rows,
which an LLM is trusted to apply, and nothing in `src/` reads `waves.jsonl` at all (verified:
`grep -rn "waves.jsonl" src/` returns nothing; `src/core/workflow/` holds nine files, none of them a
waves reader). Each invariant is a pure predicate over a JSON object.

**The count, measured rather than asserted.** `waves-event.md` **lines 86-101 hold 16 bullets**; the
sub-range **89-99 holds 11**. The first revision said *"the **nine** invariants at
`waves-event.md:89-99`"*, which is wrong on both the number and what the number counts: the **eleven**
below are **violation codes**, and **nine** of them are sourced from `waves-event.md` —
only `fix-in-clean-round` and `cap-exhausted` come from `kiwi-wave-master:348` / `:356`. E28's parity check
must therefore **enumerate bullets by measurement**, not against a hard-coded count (§10.4).

| Violation code | Rule | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `unmapped-design-item` | one `design_layer.unmapped` entry forbids `ALL_MATCH` | `waves-event.md:94` |
| `constraint-violation` | one `constraint_layer.violations` entry forbids `ALL_MATCH` | `:89` |
| `unapproved-damage` | one `preservation_layer.rows[].verdict == "unapproved-damage"` forbids `pass` | `:95` |
| `new-regression` | `failing_tests ⊄ baseline_failing_tests` forbids `pass` | `:97` |
| `no-baseline-nonzero-exit` | baseline absent ⇒ `exit_code` must be 0 | `:98` |
| `denominator-mismatch` | rows ≠ `frozen_denominator` ⇒ round **invalid**: cap consumed, streak reset to 0 | `:99` |
| `truncated-residual` | `residual` or `unmapped` truncated rather than complete | `:91`, `:96` |
| **`complete-without-verification`** | a `complete` event with **no** `verification` object reads as **unverified**, not as clean — reported, not a hard failure | **`:100`** |
| **`verification-without-status`** | `verification` is **evidence, not authority**: `status` is the only positive completion signal, so a reader inferring completion from `verification` alone is refused | **`:101`** |
| `fix-in-clean-round` | a round that applied a fix cannot be the passing round | `kiwi-wave-master:348` |
| `cap-exhausted` | cap reached ≠ pass; verdict `fail-cap` | `:356` |

The two bold rows are **additions in this revision**. Both are mechanical bullets that the first
revision's validator table omitted entirely — `:100` is a completion-gate rule the completion-gate
row below did not encode, and `:101` is the rule that stops `verification` becoming a second source
of truth. Omitting them meant E28 would have been authored against the wrong denominator, which is
exactly the drift class §10.4 exists to catch. Both are added to `§15.1 E2`.

`validateWavesJournal` adds the journal-level rules on top:

| Rule | Source |
| --- | --- |
| `complete` requires the same run's same wave's **latest** `phase=wave-verify` line to have `verification.verdict == "pass"`; a non-terminal `in-progress` round does **not** pass the gate | `waves-event.md:135` |
| the 1.1.0 exemption is **run-scoped**: one ≥1.1.0 line in a run subjects every `complete` in that run regardless of its own `schema_version` (downgrade-bypass closure) | `:135` |
| run scoping has **no** version exemption — another run's `complete` is never this run's | `:158` |
| the gate does **not** apply to `wave="all"`, and `wave="all"` is excluded from per-wave status computation | `:139` |
| run complete = all waves `complete` **and** the latest `final-verify` verdict is `pass` | `:149` |
| a non-passing `final-verify` is written `status=failed`, never `complete` | `:141` |
| `exclusion_class` and `reason_class` values are inside their closed vocabularies | `:120`, `:92` |
| `writer` present on every ≥1.4.0 line; a lower `schema_version` after a 1.4.0 line in the same run is `journal-version-downgrade` | §4.2 |
| (1.4.0) **on a `complete` or `final-verify` line only** — every lane in `lane_plan` has a terminal state, and an integrated lane has a `merge_sha`. Never evaluated per line, or no append could succeed while a lane runs | §4.2 |
| (1.4.0) an unmatched `intent` is **never** a diagnostic — it is the normal state between §4.3's two writes, and its interpretation belongs to `computeResumeState` | §4.3, §10.1 wiring |

Two of the earlier proposals wanted a `cap - rounds < streak ⇒ fail-cap` rule here. **It is
deliberately excluded from the validator.** `waves-event.md:160` states the streak requirement is
variant-dependent for `--max` and that the SSOT is the prose table at `kiwi-wave-master §5.5.4`; the
`verification` object records `rounds` and `cap` (`:73-74`) but neither mode nor variant, and
`cap=8` is ambiguous between `--max` and `--loops 8`. A fixture test would pin a guess rather than
the contract. It stays in `evaluateRound`, which **is** given the mode explicitly by its caller.

**Wiring — the validator must not be voluntary, and the first revision's wiring could not fire.** An
agent instructed to call a detector is the same agent that skipped the gate, so the wiring matters
more than the validator. The first revision named two hosts and **both are unreachable**:

- *The pre-commit hook.* `kiwi/waves.jsonl` is untracked by policy (§4.8) and CP-07 classes it
  `orchestrator-only`/`append-journal`, so the orchestrator never commits it — the "when
  `kiwi/waves.jsonl` is in the commit" branch never executes. Worse, `installGitPreCommitHook` is
  reached only from `speckiwi init`, which `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids against this repository; it
  refuses to overwrite a pre-existing non-speckiwi hook; and it skips entirely when `.git` is a
  **file** — i.e. in **every Profile A worktree**.
- *`workflow_doctor`.* It is an on-demand MCP tool, not an always-run host. Calling it is a choice,
  which is the same failure the wiring exists to prevent.

**The wiring that can actually fire**, and it follows from §4.8 making the journal tool-written by
contract:

1. **`orchestrate journal append` validates on every write.** It is the **only always-run host that
   exists** — every line in the journal passes through it, by contract, and a hand-appended line is
   already caught by the `writer` stamp. Validation is over the resulting view, and a write that
   would make the journal invalid is refused with the diagnostic rather than written.
   - **`validateWavesJournal` does not diagnose an unmatched `intent`, and this is load-bearing.**
     §4.3's discipline is write-ahead intent → act → write-behind result, so an unmatched intent is
     the **normal transient state after every intent append**. If validation reported it, the
     validate-on-every-append host would refuse the very first write of every verb and the design
     could not execute a single step. Unmatched-intent is a `computeResumeState` **classification**
     (§4.6), never a journal diagnostic.
   - **For the same reason the (1.4.0) rule *"every lane in `lane_plan` has a terminal state"* is a
     run-completion rule, not a per-line rule.** It is evaluated only when the line under validation
     is a wave `complete` or a `final-verify`, exactly like `waves-event.md:135`'s completion gate.
     Applied per line it would refuse every append made while a lane is running.
2. **`orchestrate resume` refuses to proceed on an invalid journal.** Every resumed session runs it
   (§11.1 step 3), so the second-most-common entry point is covered too.
3. `workflow_doctor` **keeps** the check as a convenience surface — it is genuinely useful for
   retro-fitting enforcement onto historical `kiwi-wave-master` runs — but it is no longer claimed as
   the enforcement path.

§10.2's compensation and §11.3's F3 row are restated on this basis: the enforcement is at the write
and at the resume, not at the commit.

### 10.2 What stays prose

Everything requiring judgment: the wave split; the design, wave-design and handoff prose; finding
severity and `severity_class`; the legitimacy of an `exclusion_class`; the urgency and
classification of a **wave-boundary** issue (§2.0's vocabulary; revision 1 left "stage-boundary"
here and in R16 after the rename); which question to ask the user; the two-verifier stance
separation itself; whether an agent really spawned two verifiers rather than one; whether it really
appended the intent line **before** acting; whether it really refused to dismiss a finding during
cross-refutation.

**These are not testable and the SRS evidence must never say "behaviour verified".** The verification
evidence formula for every prose requirement in §15 is *"authored-text contract across three
variants + executable ledger conformance"*.

The **compensations**, which are real but bounded:

- The `writer` stamp plus `orchestrate validate --strict`: a run containing **bash-appended** journal
  lines fails its own audit. **The stamp authenticates the *path*, not the *payload*, and the first
  revision overstated it.** An agent that calls `orchestrate journal append` with a fabricated
  `frozen_denominator`, row count or residual gets a **valid** `writer` stamp and passes
  `--strict`. `evaluateRound` checks internal consistency, which raises the cost of fabrication
  substantially — `frozen_denominator`, row counts, `rounds`, `residual` and the regression block
  must all agree — but consistency is not authentication. This is **detection of the wrong tool, not
  detection of a lie**, and it is the true ceiling of test-driving prose.
- The rule that follows, and it is a rule rather than a hope: **`journal` is never a sole proof kind
  on a verdict-bearing line** (§4.5). Every verdict carries at least one externally recomputable
  proof — `git-ancestor`, `git-ref`, `git-trailer`, `digest` or `mcp-state` — so the most
  consequential claims have a witness the fabricating party does not control.

### 10.3 The test story, in three layers

**Layer 1 — executable, genuinely test-first, red before green.** Fixtures under
`test/fixtures/orchestrator/`.

- `lane-plan`: the eight rules enumerated in §5.3, plus a **characterization
  test over a pinned snapshot of real sidecars, copied into `test/fixtures/orchestrator/` at
  authoring time and named file by file — never a glob over live `docs/plans/`**, which this run's
  own 3.c writes and 3.d commits (§2.1). Free, and the only thing that surfaces degenerate groupings
  a five-task synthetic fixture never would.
- `waves-journal` + `waves-validate`: one named fixture journal per rule in §10.1's two tables, each
  asserted in **both** directions (a violating payload refused, the minimal legal payload accepted),
  plus a **composite**: a payload satisfying every other rule in §10.1's two tables and violating
  exactly one is refused, enumerated over those tables by measurement rather than a fixed count. The
  composite is the specific thing a text-presence test cannot catch. Named scenarios:
  `complete-without-latest-pass`, `pass-then-inprogress-then-complete`, `cross-run-complete`,
  `downgrade-bypass`, `wave-all-phantom`, `two-runs-interleaved`, `wave-master-run-present`,
  `rows-ne-frozen-denominator`, `unmapped-with-all-match`, `unapproved-damage-with-pass`,
  `failing-not-subset`, `absent-baseline-nonzero-exit`, `reason-class-outside-vocabulary`,
  `unstamped-writer`, `version-downgrade-within-run`.
- `resume`: an `intent` with no `result` yields `interrupted` plus the verb's recovery class; the
  four reconciliation outcomes against **injected** git facts; `Object.keys(resumeState)` asserted
  against the four fields §10.1 declares for `ResumeState`, so the resume **output**'s shape cannot
  drift silently — this freezes the output shape and does **not** detect a later journal field
  arriving unread, and saying otherwise would overstate it; and a signature that structurally cannot
  accept conversation state.
- `resume-card`: 8 KB cap; the rollup rule at the design's maximum size; verb-enum membership; every
  `done[]` entry carries a valid proof kind; `invariant_digest` recomputation.
- `preflight`: the four normalisation rules (separators, trailing separator, Windows case including
  drive letter, realpath) as a table test, both match and mismatch.
- `handoff`: 13-field counting; equality (not subset) of `task_ids` and `write_set`; the
  English-only scan including the **acceptance cases of Korean inside a fenced code block and inside
  a blockquote**;
  resolvability against a fixture spec root.
- `claim-audit`: pure, table-driven, fed a synthetic delta. The **impure** half — collecting the
  real delta — gets one small integration test against a temp git repo, and the design states that
  only the pure half is covered by the table tests.
- `verification-gate`, `conflict`, `freeze`, `issue-ledger`, `replay`, `auto-gate`, `substrate`,
  `grounding`, `duplication-audit`: ordinary unit tests. (`journal-schema` is data, not a detector;
  §10.4's contract-parity class is what covers it.)
- `serialized` vs `selectNextTask`: a differential oracle against the existing implementation.
- Byte-determinism: every computed lock artifact, same input N times, identical bytes.

**Layer 2 — contract conformance.** `test/skills/orchestrator-ledger-conformance.test.ts` runs the
documented emit examples from `waves-event.md §5` and `run-ledger.md` through the real Layer-1
functions and asserts the documented sequence produces the documented next action.

**The honest limits of Layer 2, stated because the earlier proposals overstated them.**
`waves-event.md §5` is a ```` ```bash ```` fence of `echo '{…}' >> "$WAVE_DIR/waves.jsonl"` lines
whose values are placeholders (`"<ISO>"`, `"<rid>"`, `"<sha>"`, `"<prid>"`) with an identical `ts`
on all three lines. It is **not** already-literal JSON. Anything involving ordering, recency, run
partitioning or ancestry must be supplied by the harness, at which point the test checks the
substitution fixture. So Layer 2's real coverage is the literal status / phase / verdict / wave
subset — which is still worth having, because today those examples are asserted only as text — and
for the new v1.4.0 fields it is **self-consistency**, since the examples and the code are authored
by the same party in the same change. That is named here so it is not claimed as mutual checking.

**Layer 3 — text contract for the prose**, using the existing harness and its existing honesty:
`readWaveSkill`-style ENOENT-to-`""` readers so a net-new SKILL.md fails as a clean AssertionError
(`test/skills/kiwi-wave-master-content.test.ts:27`); `skillBody` frontmatter stripping so the
`description` cannot false-green a check (`:46`); `windowsAround` proximity (`:51`); `sectionUnder`
section scoping, because neighbouring sections restate the same tokens
(`test/skills/kiwi-wave-verify-content.test.ts:50`); bilingual regexes; hedge-vocabulary absence on
normative sentences.

**The normative-sentence denominator for a skill body, defined before the bar that quantifies over
it.** §3.3 rule 3's `MUST` / `MUST NOT` / `SHALL` token set is scoped to `design/00.design.md` and
`waves/wave-{n}/design.md` and does **not** reach here. Measured in this repository, all 16
`skills/claude/*/SKILL.md` and all 7 `skills/claude/_shared/kiwi/*.md` contain **zero** `MUST`
tokens, and `skills/claude/kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md` is 573 lines of which 312 carry Hangul (the
codex and etc variants are 576 / 579 lines, 312 Hangul lines each). Applying the design-document
token set to a skill body therefore yields an **empty denominator**: the ledger would be a valid
zero-row file and its test would go green, indistinguishable from full coverage — the exact inversion
of this section's own rule that an untested rule is *reported*, not counted. Layer 3 has its own
vocabulary, declared here:

- **The modal set is bilingual, because the bodies are:** `MUST`, `MUST NOT`, `SHALL`, plus
  `반드시 … -ㄴ다 / 한다`, `절대 … 않는다`, `금지`. That is the set the shipped bodies use, and the
  set the existing harness already reads bilingually
  (`test/skills/kiwi-wave-master-content.test.ts:18-20` — *"the claude/codex canonical text is largely
  Korean … plus a bilingual (English / Korean) regex for prose concepts"*). It is **not** a widening
  of §3.3 rule 3: that rule's English-only ruling and its anti-generalisation warning stand
  unchanged for the two design documents it names, and the two sets are declared separately for
  exactly that reason — a design-document item still contains exactly one English token, and no
  downstream design-document denominator moves.
- **The denominator is counted per variant, because it is measurably variant-dependent.** One rule
  can be Korean in one variant and English in another: `skills/claude/kiwi-step/SKILL.md:18` states
  it as `반드시 `claim_step` 으로 … 선점한다` while `skills/codex/kiwi-step/SKILL.md:19` and
  `skills/etc/kiwi-step/SKILL.md:19` state it as *"the skill MUST halt immediately"* — 0 / 1 / 1
  tokens for one rule. The ledger row is therefore keyed
  `{variant, rule_id, sentence, mutation_class}` with `rule_id` a stable identifier, and
  **cross-variant parity is asserted over `rule_id`, never over token counts** — a rule present in
  one variant's ledger and absent from another's fails. Stating parity over counts would fail on
  three legitimate variants of one rule. The row additionally carries a **carrier anchor** —
  `carriers[]`, rows of `{heading_path, carrier, snippet}` naming, per row, the modal carriers that
  row covers **by stable text rather than by position**, resolved by searching `snippet` under
  `heading_path`'s lowest-level heading (§3.3 rule 1) with rule 6's fence and blockquote exclusion.
  A checked-in line range is deliberately **not** the anchor: it is a hard-coded positional constant
  against a body §10.5 step 8 authors and later steps edit, which the run-time discipline below
  forbids. The row's author maintains the anchor when the body changes, and a `snippet` that no
  longer resolves in that variant's `skillBody` is **RED for that row**.

**The bar, and how it is enforced.** Every **normative sentence** in the new skill — a sentence
carrying a member of the modal set above, counted per variant — must have at least one **red
mutation** from a named class, each class given both forms because one of the five is an
English-grammar operation with no word-level Korean analogue:

| class | English form | Korean form |
|---|---|---|
| inserted hedge | `if possible`, `should`, `where practical` before the modal | `가능하면` / `권장한다` / `원칙적으로` before the modal |
| swapped ordering pair | exchange the two clauses of the ordered pair | same — clause exchange is language-independent |
| weakened quantifier | `every` → `some` | `모든` → `일부`, `전부` → `일부` |
| deleted negation | delete `not` | **morphological, not a word deletion**: delete the `-지 않는다` suffix leaving the bare `-ㄴ다`, or delete the `절대` / `금지` carrier |
| sentence moved to an adjacent section | move it under the neighbouring heading | same |

A rule with no red mutation is **reported as untested, not counted as covered** — and a checked-in
ledger, `test/skills/orchestrator-mutation-ledger.json`, maps each normative sentence to its
mutation, with a test asserting every normative sentence in **each variant's** skill body has an
entry. **A zero-row ledger against a non-empty skill body is RED, not green** — stated explicitly
because an empty denominator passing silently is the failure this whole subsection exists to prevent.
**And so is any uncovered modal carrier:** RED when an occurrence of `MUST` (with `MUST NOT` counted
once), `SHALL`, `반드시`, `절대` or `금지` in the variant's `skillBody` — scanned under §3.3 rule 1's
lowest-level-heading definition and excluding blockquote and fenced-code content per §3.3 rule 6 — is
not covered by at least one ledger row's `carriers[]` anchor. This is **coverage, not a count
comparison**, for two reasons: §3.3 rule 3 records that a sentence splitter *"is a judgment call over
abbreviations, code spans and list punctuation"* and §10.1's `prose-gate.ts` was deliberately built so
that **no sentence splitter is needed**, so no module produces a per-variant sentence count to compare
against; and a single sentence carrying two carriers (`반드시 … 금지`) would make a count comparison
over-demand. The carrier set is whitespace-delimited and mechanically enumerable, so the count is
computed by the Layer-3 test in `test/skills/` at run time, **never against a hard-coded number**,
matching §10.4's discipline. The ledger key's `sentence` field stays the human-readable authoring
label rather than the counting unit, so the bar above still quantifies over sentences.

**The honest limit of the ledger, stated because the promise it replaces was overstated once
already.** The ledger's test catches the **omission** class — a normative sentence in the skill body with
no row. It does **not** catch the **fabrication** class — a row written without the mutation ever
being applied and observed red. The ledger is therefore a coverage guard and an authoring checklist,
not evidence that the bar was met; the redness of each named class rests on the Layer-3 mechanism
paired with it above (hedge-vocabulary absence for the inserted hedge, `sectionUnder` for the moved
sentence, `windowsAround` for the swapped ordering pair), not on the ledger. The identical promise
was made for `FR-FLOW-047..066` and not kept (they sit at `implemented`, not `verified`, because the
fourth clean round was never run), which is why the limit is written down rather than assumed.

**Cross-variant parity** is asserted for the `critical_gates[]` identifiers (FR-FLOW-061) and for
the verb enum, so a verb present in one variant and absent from another fails.

### 10.4 Three parity test classes — extending an existing harness, not inventing one

`test/release/tool-signature-parity.test.ts` **already exists** and already does most of this: it
reads `DOC_AND_SKILL_ROOTS = ["docs/spec/90.appendix.md", "AGENTS.md", "CLAUDE.md", "skills/codex",
"skills/claude", "skills/etc", ".agents/skills"]` (`:14`), builds the real program with
`buildCommand` (`:23`), and walks the command tree with `outputHelp()` (`:56`). No test class
below is a new invention; all three are extensions, and saying otherwise would be false.

- **Command citation.** Extend the existing harness to extract every `speckiwi orchestrate …`
  invocation from all three SKILL.md variants, normalise documented placeholders against the
  placeholder table declared below — enumerated by measurement from §10.6's verb table, not guessed,
  and a token in a citation that is not in it is **RED** rather than silently normalised — and
  resolve each verb and
  flag against the walked command tree. **It resolves; it does not `parse()`** — commander has no
  parse-only mode and `program.parse()` would execute action handlers, which in this repository
  would trip the hermeticity guards. A skill citing a verb or flag that does not exist becomes a red
  test instead of a runtime dead end. **Zero extracted invocations against a non-empty skill body is
  RED, not green.** The extracted invocation count is asserted per variant against that variant's own
  measured count of `speckiwi ` occurrences in the same body — by measurement at authoring time, never
  against a hard-coded number — and one negative fixture, a variant body carrying a deliberately
  unresolvable verb, is asserted red, so the class is observed red at least once before step 8 authors
  the real body. Without both, this test is green at every point of the work order: before step 8 the
  skill does not exist, and step 8 authors it so that every citation already resolves.

  | Placeholder form | Stands for |
  | --- | --- |
  | `<path>` | a filesystem path argument (`route freeze --probe`, `schedule plan --plan` / `--out`, `replay plan --queue`) |
  | `<path to routing/route-gate.json>` | the same, spelled long in the `route freeze` row — the spaces and the slash are part of the token |
  | `<sha>` | a git object id (`lane audit --at`) |
  | `<id>` | an abort reason id (`run abort --reason`) |
  | `<payload>` | a JSON document argument (`journal append`, `card write`, `round record`, `auto-gate decide`) |
  | `<manifest…>` | one or more lane manifest paths (`lane audit`) |
  | `<v>` | a preflight root value (`preflight --mcp-root`, `--git-root`) |
  | `<t>` | a target name (`readiness check --target`) |
  | `N` | a wave number, or a lane count under `--lanes` |
  | `S` | a stage number |
  | `L` | a lane id; `L\|epilogue` where the row admits the epilogue (`lane audit`) |
  | `{run_id}` | the run id, in a citation that embeds it in a path |
  | `a\|b\|c` | a closed alternation of literal subcommand words — `run lock\|unlock\|status`, `freeze design\|waves\|lanes\|handoff\|issues\|postmortem`, `issue open\|plan\|resolve\|defer\|list` |
- **Contract parity.** Parse the field tables **and the bullet-form invariants** in all four copies
  of `waves-event.md` and assert set-equality against `journal-schema.ts`. The bullets matter: the
  invariants are bullet list items, not table rows, and they are exactly what
  `verification-gate.ts` implements — so a table-only parity check would miss the one drift class it
  most needs to catch. This is the direct antidote to the drift that produced `IR-CLI-080`.
  **The test enumerates bullets by measurement, never against a hard-coded number.** Measured today:
  lines **86-101 hold 16 bullets**, of which the sub-range 89-99 holds 11, and
  `verification-gate.ts`'s **eleven** violation codes draw on **ten** of those bullets — eight
  within 89-99, plus `:100` and `:101` — plus two rules from `kiwi-wave-master`. The first revision's *"the nine invariants at `waves-event.md:89-99`"* is wrong
  on the count, the range and the unit; a parity test pinned to it would have asserted the wrong
  denominator, which is precisely the drift class this test exists to catch.
- **Gate-id parity.** Extend the same harness to extract every gate id from each variant's
  three-column `critical_gates[]` table and from each variant's declared gate-severity rows, and
  assert **set-inclusion** in the exported `GateId` union (§10.1, `auto-gate.ts`) — inclusion, not
  equality, because `critical_gates[]` is a halt list rather than a gate census (§13) and because 20
  of §13's rows are phase 2 and are undeclared in the phase-1 skill. FR-FLOW-061 already asserts the
  ids agree **across the three variants**; nothing bound them to the id a kernel module emits at exit
  2 (`{ok: false, gate: <gate_id>}`), and a divergence there is silent — `auto-option.md` §4 defaults
  a gate absent from `critical_gates[]` to `business-decision`, which a committee auto-approves at
  confidence ≥ 0.7, so a mistyped **C** gate becomes committee-approvable while all three variants
  stay green. One negative fixture — a variant declaring an id absent from the union — asserted red,
  the same discipline the command-citation bullet uses.

### 10.6 The CLI and MCP surface

`src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts`, registered exactly as `src/cli/commands/read.ts` registers the
`workflow` namespace. Every mutation verb returns the standard `MutationEnvelope` and honours
`--dry-run`, so a gate refusal is inspectable before it is consequential.

**Exit codes and failure output, stated once for the whole namespace.** Revision 1 said what mutation
verbs return and nothing about what any verb *exits*, while §5.9's own pseudo-code is written as
`git merge … || abort_integration(...)` and every skill sentence that branches on a gate needs the
same answer:

| Kind | On success | On failure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| read | exit **0**, `{ok: true, …}` on stdout | exit **1** for an operational error (unreadable file, bad JSON); the payload carries `{ok: false, error}` |
| read **(gate)** — `wave close`, `handoff validate`, `lane audit`, `validate`, `resume` | exit **0**, `{ok: true, violations: []}` | exit **2** with `{ok: false, gate: <gate_id>, violations: [...]}`. **Exit 2 is the gate-refusal code and is distinct from 1**, so a skill can branch on refusal without parsing, and a CI wrapper cannot mistake a gate for a crash |
| mutation | exit **0**, `MutationEnvelope` | exit **2** with an envelope whose `applied: false` and whose `gate` names the refusal; exit **1** on an operational error |

`--json` is mandatory for machine consumption on every verb and is what the skill body cites; the
human form is a convenience. `--dry-run` on a mutation verb that *would* be refused still exits 2, so
"inspectable before it is consequential" means the same thing in both modes.

| Verb | Kind | Kernel call |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `orchestrate resume --json` | read | `computeResumeState`; **refuses on an invalid journal** (§10.1) |
| `orchestrate preflight --mcp-root <v> --git-root <v> --json` | read | `normaliseRoot` |
| `orchestrate route probe --out routing/probe.json --json` | mutation | writes the probe from the declared producers (`09` §3.2); **exits non-zero on a schema-invalid probe** rather than substituting defaults |
| `orchestrate route freeze --probe <path> --gate <path to routing/route-gate.json> --json` | mutation | `computeRoute` + `freezeRoute`; `--gate` is the gate-resolution record (`09` §8.3, §9.1) — it names a **selection**, never a rung, so `09` §4.6's *takes no rung argument* stands; a no-op when the recomputed digest matches |
| `orchestrate route show --json` | read | the lock, the removal table and the decisive predicate — what a reviewer reads to re-derive a route |
| `orchestrate run lock\|unlock\|status --json` | mutation / read | `run-lock.ts` (H2, common-dir keyed) |
| `orchestrate run abort --reason <id> --json` | mutation | §5.12: reap, report, unlock |
| `orchestrate journal append --json <payload>` | mutation | `appendWavesEvent` (stamps `writer`, **validates on every write**) |
| `orchestrate card write --json <payload>` | mutation | `writeResumeCard` (cap + enum + profile-immutability validation) |
| `orchestrate freeze design\|waves\|lanes\|handoff\|issues\|postmortem` | mutation | `freeze.ts` (H1 pinning: sha256 + real `git_blob_oid`). **Six targets** — §3.3a, E38 and this row now name the same set |
| `orchestrate readiness check --target <t> --json` | read | `readiness.ts` (H3); backs the 3.c′ gates |
| `orchestrate schedule plan --plan <path> [--lanes N] [--allow-inferred-write-set] [--strict-grounding] --out <path>` | mutation | grounds `files[]` through the pure `groundFiles` near-miss detector (impure collection only; gate `files-not-grounded`), then calls the **pure** `computeLanePlan` with injected `existing_paths` (§5.1) |
| `orchestrate coupling check --wave N --stage S` | read (gate) | `planStageCoupling` over the stage's handoffs; backs 3.f″ and `stage-coupling-unresolved` (§7.9 (a)). Renamed from `substrate check`, whose predicate is withdrawn (X-04) |
| `orchestrate schedule show` | read | lock + digest check |
| `orchestrate handoff validate --lane L` | read | `validateHandoff` incl. the §6.5 untested-AC cap |
| `orchestrate lane audit --lane L\|epilogue [--at <sha>] [--run-acceptance] --json <manifest…>` | read (gate) | `auditLane`; `--at` selects the 3.h head or the 3.j re-audit tip (§2.1 ordering rule 6); the payload is the **effective lane record** (§7.4) — the lane manifest plus every `remediation-{r}.manifest.json`; the row set follows `handoff_kind` (§7.4); `--run-acceptance` performs row R11 by running `verification_cmd --reporter=json`. Writes `audit.3h.json` or `audit.3j.json` (§3.2) |
| `orchestrate lane harvest --lane L` | mutation | `collect()` step 4 — copy manifest, every remediation manifest, queue, decisions, review-fix-loop analysis to the host run directory (§3.5); gate `lane-state-harvest-failed` |
| `orchestrate lane release --lane L [--force]` | mutation | §5.5.1 reap of the lane's **and** its remediations' workspaces; lifecycle-aware, refuses a locked workspace without `--force`, refuses a lane whose harvest has not landed, and on a refused removal records `reap-deferred` with the path rather than passing silently (§5.5.1) |
| `orchestrate lane status [--wave N] [--stage S]` | read | returns **`LaneStatus[]`** (§10.1): ref family, head, workspace + lock, heartbeat age + `bootstrap_done_at`, manifest status, `L(k)` liveness, deadline. **This is the verb the 3.g′ join loop calls** (§5.5.3), not an on-demand convenience, and it is also what writes the deferred `dispatch-lane` result line |
| `orchestrate round record --json <payload>` | mutation | `evaluateRound` then append |
| `orchestrate issue open\|plan\|resolve\|defer\|list` | mutation / read | `issue-ledger.ts`; `resolve` collects the resolution set (impure collection only), then calls the pure predicates |
| `orchestrate wave close --wave N` | read (gate) | `issue-ledger` (renamed from `stage close`, §2.0); collects the resolution set (impure collection only), then calls the pure predicates |
| `orchestrate replay plan --queue <path>` | read | `replayDeferredMutations` |
| `orchestrate duplication plan --wave N` | read | `planDuplicationAudit` (§7.9) |
| `orchestrate validate [--strict]` | read | `validateWavesJournal` + `writer` audit |
| `orchestrate auto-gate decide --json <payload>` | read | `decideAutoGate` |

The four lifecycle verb rows (six subcommands) — `run lock/unlock/status`, `run abort`, `lane release`, `lane status` — are
additions. They are the surface an interrupted user reaches for **first**, and the first revision had
verbs for every phase except lane and run lifecycle, which is the one thing a user needs when
something has gone wrong at 2 a.m. with eight worktrees on disk.

**Four `lane *` verbs above are phase 2 and are not implemented in `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`:**
`lane audit`, `lane harvest`, `lane release`, `lane status`, together with `replay plan`. Each takes a
lane workspace or a lane manifest as its subject. **`run lock/unlock/status` and `run abort` ship**:
one run per repository is a phase-1 property (§5.2), and `abort-run` is what names the repository
state a halted run leaves behind (§5.12) — in phase 1 that state is *commits on the integration
branch*, with the workspace rows of §5.12 step 3 absent. Two of the four rows that made the 2 a.m.
argument therefore do not ship yet, and the argument narrows with them: phase 1's interrupted user is
holding a branch, not eight worktrees.

MCP mirrors, registered through the existing pattern (`src/mcp/schemas.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`,
`src/mcp/tools/{read,mutation}-tools.ts`), with `orchestrate_route_probe`, `orchestrate_route_freeze`
and `orchestrate_route_show` added in revision 4 and the four lane verbs deferred:
`orchestrate_resume`, `orchestrate_preflight`,
`orchestrate_run_lock`, `orchestrate_run_abort`, `orchestrate_journal_append`,
`orchestrate_card_write`, `orchestrate_freeze`, `orchestrate_readiness_check`,
`orchestrate_schedule_plan`, `orchestrate_coupling_check`, `orchestrate_schedule_show`,
`orchestrate_handoff_validate`,
`orchestrate_lane_audit`, `orchestrate_lane_harvest`,
`orchestrate_lane_release`, `orchestrate_lane_status`, `orchestrate_round_record`,
`orchestrate_issue_*`, `orchestrate_wave_close`, `orchestrate_duplication_plan`,
`orchestrate_validate`, `orchestrate_auto_gate`.

**`preflight` takes both roots as arguments, and this is deliberate.** The run-root check cannot be
performed *inside* a tool on either surface: the CLI resolves root from the caller's cwd, which
makes the comparison vacuous — `kiwi-wave-master:131` says exactly this and forbids the CLI as a
fallback — and the MCP server fixes `workspaceRoot` to its own process cwd
(`src/mcp/adapter.ts:37`), so a `git rev-parse` issued inside the tool would compare the server root
against itself and always match. So the **agent** obtains the two values (MCP `mcp_workspace_info`
and a Bash `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`) and the **tool** performs the judgment-free four-rule
normalisation and comparison. That is the boundary rule applied to the one gate that cannot be
fully automated.

---

## 11. The compaction-survival contract

### 11.1 What a resumed session reads, in order

This is a fixed, numbered procedure. It is the first thing in the skill body, and it is the only
part of the skill a resumed session needs before it can act.

```
1. docs/research/{work}/00.run-contract.md        (or, with no {work}, go to step 2 and read the
                                                   work_root out of the resume card)
2. speckiwi orchestrate preflight --mcp-root <mcp_workspace_info.workspaceRoot>
                                  --git-root <git rev-parse --show-toplevel> --json
3. speckiwi orchestrate resume --json
4. If blocking:  perform only next_action.verb, nothing else.
   Else:         read SKILL.md §V.<next_action.verb> — that section only.
5. Read the two or three artifacts that section names.
6. Act.
```

**Never reconstruct state from conversation.** The skill states this as a MUST and the resume tool's
signature structurally cannot accept conversation state.

Step 2 precedes step 3 because the run-root check must run **before** journal path resolution — the
ordering rule at `kiwi-wave-master:129`, whose reason is that resolving the journal at a mismatched
root pins the run to the wrong repository.

**Step 3 reads the rung; it never recomputes it.** `orchestrate resume` returns `frozen.route.rung`
from the resume card, verifies `frozen.route.probe_digest` against `routing/probe.json` on disk, and
raises `run-invariant-drift` (C) on a mismatch. `computeRoute` runs **exactly once per run**. A
resumed session has no conversation and no investigators, so several probe fields are not
reproducible; if the rung were not reconstructible from the lock alone it would not be
reconstructible at all — the same discipline the design baseline is already under (`09` §9.5).

### 11.2 `00.run-contract.md`

A short English file, created at Phase 0 and amended at exactly two later points — the end of Phase 1
(`intake_autonomy`, §7.2) and Phase 2.d after the coverage gate (the immutable wave order) — each
amendment journalled and re-committed under `commit-run-artifacts` (§4.8 policy 5). Its sha256 is
carried in the resume card's `run_contract` field (§4.1), which each amendment rewrites; the re-check
at every resume compares the file on disk against the value the card currently names, and a mismatch
is `run-invariant-drift` (C). Contents, a closed list, marked by the point that writes each:

- `run_id`, `work_root`, journal path, the pinned run root, the frozen isolation profile,
  **`base_branch` and `integration_branch`**;
- the run's **fixed path conventions** for the design lock, constraints, convergence registry, waves
  lock, and the lane lock and handoffs — `design/00.design.lock.json`, `design/constraints.json`,
  `design/convergence-registry.json`, `waves/waves.lock.json`, `waves/wave-{n}/lanes.lock.json`,
  `waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md`. The **resolved current-wave pointers** live in the card's
  `frozen.lane_lock` (§4.1), so no per-wave amendment is needed here;
- the **immutable wave order** — written at the 2.d amendment; absent on `R-STEP` and `R-PLAN`,
  which never split waves;
- an **`intake_autonomy` block** (§7.2) — written at the end-of-Phase-1 amendment: whether `--auto`
  answered design questions, how many, and where the per-decision audit records are — so a resumed
  session, and the user, can see that the design was settled without them;
- the **closed list of forbidden actions**: do not re-decompose; do not allocate a Requirement ID
  outside Phase 3.b; **do not re-dispatch any lane whose workspace may still be alive — ask the
  user** (§5.5.1); do not edit a completed lane; do not weaken or delete a test; do not write outside
  a lease; do not hand-append to `kiwi/waves.jsonl`; **never run `git add -A` or `git commit -a` —
  every commit stages an explicit pathspec** (§4.8); **never merge `integration_branch` into
  `base_branch` and never open a PR** (§5.12);
- the exact resume command.

### 11.3 Why this survives what today's contract does not

`kiwi-wave-master` already asserts journal-only resolvability (`:193`, `:271`, `:477`), but pays for
it in a form compaction degrades: the resume rules are 573 lines of prose, and **nothing in `src/`
can read `waves.jsonl`**. "Resume correctly" therefore means "read a very large document correctly
while low on context", which is exactly the operation compaction impairs.

Three failure modes are made impossible or loud:

| # | Failure | Defence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | re-doing work already done | verb-granular recovery classes (§4.4) + externally recomputable proofs (§4.5) + reconciliation (§4.6). Today's finest granularity is a whole wave (`waves-event.md:147-156`), which cannot tell an interrupted action from a not-started one |
| F2 | drifting from the frozen design | four independent digests (§4.7), with the append-new-artifact rule keeping legitimate amendment distinguishable from drift |
| F3 | reporting completion that never happened | `validateWavesJournal` makes `waves-event.md:135` executable for the first time, enforced **at every `orchestrate journal append` and at every `orchestrate resume`** (§10.1) — not at a pre-commit hook that cannot fire on an untracked file inside a worktree whose `.git` is a file. `orchestrate validate --strict` plus the `writer` stamp catches a **bash-appended** journal; it does not catch a fabricated payload written through the tool, which is why §4.5 forbids `journal` as a sole proof kind on a verdict-bearing line |

### 11.4 Worked drill

> **This drill is written against a dispatched lane and is therefore a phase-2 drill.** It is
> retained unedited because it is the only end-to-end exercise of the reconciliation predicate the
> document contains. **Phase 1's equivalent is shorter and is stated here rather than re-worked
> below**: the last line for `(execute-unit, wave-2, stage-1, lane-3)` is an `intent` with no
> `result`; the verb is externally-visible, so the resumed session inspects the integration branch for
> commits carrying that unit's `Orch-Lane` trailer and `workflow_plan_status` for its Tasks' checkbox
> state, re-enters `/kiwi-pm` for the Tasks with neither, and raises `interrupted-external-action` (C)
> only if the two sources disagree. There is no branch to resolve, no worktree to find and no liveness
> question — which is the whole of what the scope cut simplifies about resume, and §17.19 states what
> it costs.

Session dies during wave 2, lane 3. New session, empty context.

1. `orchestrate preflight` → match.
2. `orchestrate resume --json` → most recent incomplete run with `engine=kiwi-orchestrator`; parse
   `waves.jsonl` filtered by `run_id`; the last line for `(dispatch-lane, wave-2, stage-1, lane-3)`
   is `intent` with no `result`; the verb's class is **externally-visible**, so §4.6's **per-lane**
   classification runs first over the lanes of `(wave 2, stage 1)`. For lane-3:
   - `B(3)`: `git rev-parse --verify kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w2-s1-l3` resolves; the branch has 2 commits;
   - `A(3)`: not an ancestor of `frozen.integration_branch`;
   - `L(3)`: `git worktree list --porcelain` reports **no** worktree on that branch, and **no
     unattributed** worktree under `.claude/worktrees/` → `gone`. (Note the key is the *branch*, not a
     recorded `workspace_ref` — under Profile A the intent line's `workspace_ref` is `null` by
     construction, §3.5a, so a `workspace_ref`-keyed match would find nothing here and would have
     found nothing in the live case either.)
   - no `collect-lane` result exists;
   → per-lane class **`lane-collectable`**, not `divergent`; `next_action = {verb: "collect-lane",
   args: {wave: 2, stage: 1, lane: "lane-3"}}` with a note that the lane produced commits and must
   **not** be re-dispatched. Lanes 1 and 2 classify `lane-landed`, so the stage reduces to
   `consistent`.
3. `collect-lane` takes §5.4's **workspace-gone path**: the branch exists with commits, no host-side
   manifest was harvested, so a manifest is synthesised with `status: "complete-unreported"` and
   `written_paths` derived from `git diff --name-only base..head`. It carries no `red_evidence` and no
   `acceptance_results`, so §7.4 rows R8/R9/R11 fail and the lane routes to loop L's demotion path —
   **with its two commits preserved on the branch and reported**, rather than being reaped as a
   timeout. Revision 1's only stated route for an absent workspace was `no-response` → `lane-timeout`
   (C), which stranded exactly this work.
4. Recompute `invariant_digest` from the lock files the card **currently names** → matches.
5. Read SKILL.md `§V.collect-lane` (~40 lines) plus `waves/wave-2/lanes/lane-3.md`.
6. Proceed.

Context consumed before productive work: one JSON payload and two short documents.

**The three other endings, because they are the ones that matter.** If the branch had **no** commits
*and* no workspace exists, the class is `not-dispatched` and `next_action` is `dispatch-lane` again —
still externally-visible, but with an empty external effect, and the **tool says so** rather than the
agent guessing. If the workspace **does** still exist — locked, or with a heartbeat file newer than
three poll intervals — or if any **unattributed** workspace sits under the isolation root while some
lane of this stage has an unmatched dispatch intent, the class is `lane-possibly-live` →
`interrupted-external-action` (C): **never re-dispatch, ask the user** (§5.5.1). And if the branch
were already an ancestor of the integration head with no `integrate-lane` result line, the class is
`journal-behind-git` → `ledger-reconciliation-divergent` (C): the work is neither silently redone nor
silently accepted.

**This drill and §4.6's predicate are now the same machinery, and in revision 1 they were not.**
Revision 1's §4.6 compared four *sets* and had no outcome for a dispatched-but-unfinished or a
finished-but-reaped lane, so this drill — the design's single demonstration of compaction survival —
was refuted by the predicate it demonstrates: it concluded `consistent` where §4.6 computed
`divergent`. Two incompatible statements of one gate, with the normative one being the one that broke.

**One thing the verb-indexed SKILL.md does not do**, stated because it is easy to over-claim: the
`Skill` tool loads a skill's full instructions into the turn. Verb indexing reduces what the agent
must **read and reason over**, not what enters context. The context saving is real but it is a
reading-comprehension saving, not a token saving.

---

## 12. The `--auto` change (the second `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` work item)

Charter §2 and D4 put this in the same target. `07.auto-contract-decisions.md` is binding, **as
amended by `08` §2, which re-decides `07` Q3 and re-confirms `07` Q2**; this section states what the
orchestrator needs from both and what must be edited. **Revision 4 removes the quarantine: the
contract is settled in full and nothing in it waits on a user ruling.**

**The contract in force, stated once:**

> A gate offering a **recommended** option adopts it immediately, with no committee. Otherwise a
> committee of 3 — **5 under `--max`** — decides by **simple majority**. **If no majority exists, the
> gate escalates to `critical` and halts for the user.** Gates in `critical_gates[]` halt for the user
> regardless of `--auto`.

The escalation trigger is no longer "not unanimous". A 2-1 decides immediately, which is what the
user asked for, and `00.charter.md:35`'s removal of the unanimity requirement and the 3→5 ladder
is honoured exactly — now **literally**, because there is no rung left anywhere. The `--max` 5→7
rung goes by `07` §1 Q1 (`max-5-majority`, 5-0), not by the charter: `07:52-55` records that
removing the 3→5 rung did not settle `--max`, because `auto-option.md:32` seeds `--auto --max`
with 5 members directly.

**What `08` §2 changed against revision 3, and why the change is not merely a default made
permanent.** Revision 3 wrote the halt-on-tie behaviour as *"the conservative behaviour with no
ruling"* and kept a rung-shaped hole for the user to fill. `08` §2 rules that the hole stays empty,
5-0, and supplies a reason revision 3 did not have: the rung's only strong practical argument was
that halting mid-run strands live background lanes, **and under `08`'s serial scope there are no live
lanes at a gate** (`08:132-141`). The two `08` decisions were taken together and the second is what
retires the first's strongest counter-argument.

**`07` Q2 is re-decided, not carried forward, and this matters for what gets authored.** `07` §3b
made Q2's unanimous `leave-all-three` conditional on the rung existing, and one member said their
vote flipped without it. `08` re-ran Q2 anyway: it **stays `leave-all-three`** (`08:54-64`). Halting
for the user removes §3b's named unacceptable outcome — settlement by one member alone — more
completely than the rung did, while promoting the three irreversible gates to `critical_gates[]`
would halt on **every** invocation, including a unanimous 3-0. So no gate moves into
`critical_gates[]` on account of the `--auto` change.

**Two conditions travel with Q2 and must be carried into the requirement text verbatim**
(`08:66-73`, which cites `07:88-92` for the reason: these, not `critical_gates[]` membership, are the
real boundary on the affected population):

1. **`07` §3a** — under a split vote, *"the confidence"* is the **minimum within the winning bloc**,
   after the §4.1.1 adjustments.
2. **`07` §4.1** — §4.1.2's confidence-spread rung folds into §4.1.1's threshold mechanism, with
   **no re-vote on spread alone**.

They are edits 12.7 and 12.1 below, and they are now *conditions of a ruling* rather than
independently-motivated edits, which is why neither may be dropped as an optimisation.

**`decideAutoGate` keeps its `tieRung: boolean` parameter** (`08:76-77`). The parameter is
constructed `false` and no call site sets it `true`. It is retained so that a later reversal is
**additive rather than structural** — the same reason revision 3 gave, now serving a decided
contract rather than an undecided one. A test asserts the shipped construction is `false`.

**E20's tie clause is the shipped criterion and there is no second one.** `AC-E20-4` reads *"with no
tie rung configured, an unresolved tie and a degraded quorum escalate to `critical`"*. Revision 3
planned `AC-A7-1` alongside it for the granted case; **A7 is DECLINED** (§15.4), so `AC-A7-1` is not
authored. If the user ever reverses `08` §2, `AC-A7-1` is added and `AC-E20-4` is *replaced*, and
that replacement — not an addition — is what the `tieRung` parameter exists to make cheap.

**Explicitly out of scope, and it must not leak back in:** the gate that judges *why* an option
carries a recommendation. `decideAutoGate` is written so that it **accepts no parameter describing
why an option is recommended**, and a test asserts that a speed-motivated recommendation is adopted
identically to a completeness-motivated one. An architecture that cannot express the excluded
feature cannot leak it back in.

Mandatory edits — 12.1–12.7 to `auto-option.md` (all four copies) and 12.8 to
`skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/local-llm-profile.md` (the `etc` rendering only) — each of which makes the
contract self-contradictory if skipped:

| # | Edit | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 12.1 | §4.1.2's confidence-spread rung (`:209-212`) is **folded into §4.1.1's threshold mechanism**: a wide spread adjusts the adopted decision's confidence downward, and only a sub-threshold result escalates to critical. No re-vote on spread alone | with no rung left, "escalate one rung" becomes an immediate critical HALT on a ≥0.3 spread, which is routine — strictly *more* halting, in the mode the user asked to lighten |
| 12.2 | §3's `:159` prohibition is reworded | as written it forbids adopting a decision that is neither unanimous nor a plurality — which is exactly what a fast-pathed gate and a lead-member tie-break are. Unedited, a compliant agent must HALT on every fast-pathed gate |
| 12.3 | precedence stated: **`recommended` > `default_if_auto` > committee** | two bypasses with different scopes and no ranking is the likeliest next contradiction; this follows the suite's own precedent (`loop-option.md:24`, explicit `--loops` beats preset `--mini`) |
| 12.4 | the marker is a **structured field** `recommended: true` on `kiwi-pm`'s NEEDS_USER option schema; an unparsed prose `(권장)` confers nothing | three informal `(권장)` labels exist and two of them read *"(A) HALT … (권장)"* — a prose scan would auto-adopt a recommended **HALT** |
| 12.5 | `auto-option.md:157`'s `merge_method.rule` vocabulary is **restated in full** as `majority` / `recommended-fastpath` / `default-if-auto`: the §10 audit schema gains **both** zero-vote shapes, `{"rule": "recommended-fastpath", "committee_size": 0, "marked_by": "<gate declaration site>"}` and `{"rule": "default-if-auto", "committee_size": 0, "marked_by": "<gate declaration site>"}`, because 12.3 ranks **both** bypasses above the committee and `adopt-default-if-auto` is already a member of §10.1's closed `AutoGateAction`, so with only the first literal that branch would have no recordable outcome. `unanimous` becomes an *outcome* rather than a *rule*, `plurality` is **renamed `majority`** — A1's simple-majority rule, `AutoGateAction`'s `adopt-majority` (§10.1), and the value `09` §9.2's two worked lock records already write — and **`tie-break-lead` is deleted**, since 12.6 removes the row that produces it | otherwise the decisions that received **no** deliberation are the ones that leave **no** evidence — and the enumeration keeps naming a rule `08` §2 abolished 5-0 while omitting the one value the shipped kernel and `09`'s two worked lock records actually write, so the committee branch and the fast-path branch of one `resolution.rule` field would be specified from two documents that disagree on the literal |
| 12.6 | §3.1's degraded-quorum row (`auto-option.md:165`) escalates **to `critical`**, unconditionally, **and §3.1's lead-member row (`:168`) is deleted** | with 3 members and one dropped, a 1-1 among the remaining two silently becomes single-agent decision-making. `:168`'s *선임 위원 1회 재spawn* is deleted because on its re-spawn-succeeds branch it settles the tie by one member's ranking — the mechanism `08` §2 abolished 5-0 (`07:128`, *"no lead-member tie-break ships"*): a committee that produces no majority, **including through member failure**, escalates to `critical` and halts for the user. Closing only the quorum row would leave that mechanism live one row below it in the same table. **The conditional half of this row — *"and, if and only if the user grants X-03, under the same rule as a tie"* — is deleted**: `08` §2 declines X-03, so `critical` is the only form this edit has |
| 12.7 | under a split vote, "the confidence" is **the minimum within the winning bloc**, after §4.1.1's adjustments | undefined under a 2-1; the conservative reading keeps §4.1.1's ×0.7 penalty for an empty `side_effects[]` biting on exactly the gates left committee-decided. **A condition of `08`'s Q2 re-decision** (`08:70-71`), carried verbatim, not an independent improvement |
| 12.8 | **`skills/etc/_shared/kiwi/local-llm-profile.md:20-26, :54`**'s carve-out survives **verbatim** | without it every `etc` host silently jumps to a 5-member sequential committee on every gate. **The path qualifier is required:** this file exists **only** in the `etc` rendering — it is absent from `skills/claude/`, `skills/codex/` and `.agents/skills/` (verified). The first revision cited it unqualified while §12's preamble instructed "all four copies" for every row, which would have sent an implementer looking for three files that do not exist |

**SRS shape: two requirements, not one.** Amend `FR-FLOW-025` for the majority rule — its title
names the removed mechanism, so the rename needs a Change Notes row; AC-3 and AC-4 are *replaced*,
never deleted to reach green; AC-1 and AC-2 survive; `VE-1` is evidence for the ladder having been
*built* and cannot be reused. Author the recommended fast path as a **new** requirement under
`2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`, so the existing `FR-FLOW-025` citations stay valid and the zero-deliberation path gets its
own acceptance criteria and its own evidence. `supersede_requirement` is the wrong tool.
`FR-FLOW-025` is `Status=verified`, so it must be demoted to `in_progress` before any granular edit
(`src/core/mutation/edit-requirement.ts`).

Note for whoever authors from `04.auto-recommended-fastpath-impact.md`: its §3.2 and §4.3 repeat a
premise the committee found false — `auto-option.md:32` seeds `--auto --max` with a 5-member
committee **directly**, so `--max` never depended on the 3→5 rung. Do not carry that error into the
requirement text.

**Consequence to state plainly:** because the marker starts unused **in the existing suite**, the
behavioural changes users feel at first are the 3-member simple majority and the halt on a gate with
no majority.

**Revision 4 adds the marker's first consumer, and it is worth naming because `07:157-159` records
that the fast path would otherwise ship dead.** `09`'s routing gates declare it: `route-proposal`
carries `recommended: true` under five stated conditions (`09` §8.2), `route-step-requires-mode-switch`
marks `stay-and-orchestrate`, `tdd-route-unattended` marks `orchestrate-instead`, and
`route-downgrade-available` marks `continue-orchestrated`. Three of the four markers sit on the option
that **does not** mutate state or take the cheap path unattended, which is the conservative placement
and is deliberate. This is the concrete reason D4 ships the `--auto` change alongside the skill rather
than after it.
