## 13. `critical_gates[]`

Declared as a `gate_id | reason | location` three-column table, which is what
`auto-option.md:252` requires; **an undeclared `critical_gates[]` disables `--auto`**
(`:237`). Cross-variant identifier parity is asserted per FR-FLOW-061. Child gates are inherited by
the **general rule** of FR-FLOW-066, not by per-gate transcription — transcription is missed every
time a child adds a gate.

**Phase split over this table.** `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` declares the rows marked **1**;
the rows marked **2** are Part P2's and are **not** declared in the phase-1 skill — an undeclared gate
falls to `business-decision` and a committee approves it under `--auto` (`auto-option.md:190`), so
declaring a gate whose machinery does not exist is worse than omitting it: it puts a live
`critical_gates[]` identifier in the FR-FLOW-061 parity assertions with no reachable predicate behind
it. They are listed here, marked, so phase 2 declares them rather than re-deriving them.

**Four routing gates are `business-decision` and are deliberately *not* in `critical_gates[]`** —
`route-proposal`, `route-step-requires-mode-switch`, `tdd-route-unattended`,
`route-downgrade-available`. `route-proposal` fires on **every** run, so promoting it would stall
100% of unattended runs at their first decision — the same objection `08:60-64` sustained against
promoting the three `--auto` gates (`09` §8.1). They are named in §2.1 and in `09` §8.1 and are
declared in the skill body with severities, not in this table.

**Orchestrator-owned:**

| gate_id | reason | location | phase |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `run-root-preflight-mismatch` | MCP `workspaceRoot` ≠ git toplevel, or lookup failed | Preflight P.1 | 1 |
| `invalid-run-scope-option` | an explicit `--run-id` fails `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,48}$` or `git check-ref-format --allow-onelevel`, or an explicit `--work` fails `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9.-]{2,39}$` (§14.1) | Preflight **P.2** | 1 |
| `orchestrator-run-lock-held` | another orchestrator run holds the common-dir-keyed lease | Preflight **P.5** | 1 |
| `route-probe-unreadable` | MCP is unavailable such that neither `list_requirements` nor `get_active_target` can be read, so D8 removes both cheap rungs and no rung can be reached with the tools a run needs (`09` §8.1) | Phase 1.c′ | 1 |
| `route-escalation-after-landed-state` | an escalation that follows a commit on the integration branch **or** an SRS mutation that moved a requirement's `status` **or** its `stability`. The `stability` clause is load-bearing: `/kiwi-srs-feasibility` moves `stability` target-wide and not `status` (`09` §7.5) | any, on escalation | 1 |
| `route-deescalation-refused` | any attempt to move down a rung after Phase 3.b | after 3.b | 1 |
| `partition-review-unrecorded` | no `review-partition` result line for this wave whose `partition_review.lane_plan_digest` equals the `lane_plan.digest` frozen **at 3.e′** and whose `verdict` is `pass` — a later 3.f″ coupling re-freeze does not re-open it (§7.9 a) — `08:96` requires *"frozen, published, reviewed"*, and freezing alone satisfies one of the three. The reviewer is the user at the gate; the gate is `critical_gates[]`, so it halts under `--auto` (§2.1 3.e′, §2's recovery table, §17.20) | Phase 3.e′ | 1 |
| `serial-unit-failed` | a unit's `verification_cmd` exited non-zero after one retry, or it produced no commit and declared no `intentionally_empty` reason in its `/kiwi-pm` run's `docs/analysis/` bundle (phase 1's carrier — the lane manifest is phase 2, §3.1), or its `/kiwi-pm` returned `NEEDS_USER` / `FAILED` (§5.14) | Phase 3.g and 3.k activity (0) | 1 |
| `isolation-profile-unavailable` | probe failed and no permitted fallback | Preflight **P.6** | **2** |
| `isolation-profile-change-requested` | a request to change the profile mid-run, **or a re-probe in a run that already has a `probe-isolation` result line** | Preflight P.6 / Phase 3.g | **2** |
| `resume-card-missing-or-invalid` | the journal has events for the run but the card is unreadable, over cap, or its `frozen.isolation_profile` disagrees with the journal | Phase 0 | 1 |
| `ledger-reconciliation-divergent` | journal, git refs, worktrees and locks disagree for the current `(wave, stage)` | Phase 0 | 1 |
| `run-invariant-drift` | recomputed `invariant_digest` ≠ the card's, against the locks the card currently names | Phase 0 | 1 |
| `interrupted-external-action` | an `intent` with no `result` on an externally-visible verb **whose §4.4 inspection leaves the external effect unresolved** — a lane whose workspace may still be alive (§5.5.1), or an `execute-unit` unit whose `Orch-Task` trailers and `workflow_plan_status` disagree (§5.14) | Phase 0 / 3.g | 1 |
| `integration-branch-unavailable` | the run's integration branch cannot be created or adopted at `--base-branch` | Phase 0.b | 1 |
| `design-intake-insufficient` | loop D hit its cap with `needs-decision` or `contradicts-existing` rows open | Phase 1.d | 1 |
| `unmarked-normative-prose` | a normative sentence outside a `[D-nnn]` / `[I-nnn]` row (§3.3 rule 4) | Phase 1.e / 3.a | 1 |
| `design-not-frozen` | an implementation verb attempted before `P-DESIGN-FROZEN` | any | 1 |
| `wave-design-insufficient` | loop W cap exhausted | Phase 3.a | 1 |
| `convergence-without-recipe` | a declared convergence point has no recipe from the closed enum | Phase 2.c | 1 |
| `unallocated-req-id` | a sidecar task carries a `req_id` outside the 3.b allocation set, or an empty `req_ids` | Phase 3.c′ | 1 |
| `requirement-not-ready` | derived readiness reports unsatisfied hard dependencies, evidence drift or unverified ownership | Phase 3.c′ | 1 |
| `schedule-cycle` | a dependency cycle survived into the lane plan | Phase 3.e | 1 |
| `tdd-pair-split` | a `covers_ac` red/green pair landed in different lanes | Phase 3.e | 1 |
| `unknown-write-set-refused` | a task with empty or `[INFERRED:` `files[]` without the flag | Phase 3.e | 1 |
| `files-not-grounded` | a sidecar `files[]` path is a probable typo for an existing path, or its `line_range` is out of bounds (§5.1) | Phase 3.e | 1 |
| `non-code-write-set-refused` | a task outside `code_roots ∪ test_roots`, or of a non-code `type`, that another same-lane edge has already pulled into a lane (§5.2, charter C1) | Phase 3.e | 1 |
| `lane-plan-drift` | recomputed lane plan ≠ `lanes.lock.json` | Phase 3.e / resume | 1 |
| `handoff-not-english` | non-Latin script in a handoff body, its `escalation` field or any `acceptance[].untested_reason`, outside code fences | Phase 3.f; **3.k** for the epilogue handoff (§6.2, §2.1 3.k activity (0)) | 1 |
| `handoff-unresolvable-reference` | a path, task id, REQ id, AC id or command in a handoff does not resolve | Phase 3.f; **3.k** for the epilogue handoff (§6.2, §2.1 3.k activity (0)) | 1 |
| `handoff-untested-ac-over-cap` | `acceptance[]` empty, over the `test_id: null` cap, or a null row missing `untested_reason` / `untested_owner` (§6.5) | Phase 3.f; **3.k** for the epilogue handoff (§6.2, §2.1 3.k activity (0)) | 1 |
| `handoff-verify-failed` | loop H cap exhausted or the executability probe mismatched. **Phase 1 carries no demotion conjunct**: §P2 7.3's demotion-legality rule and the serial epilogue as a distinct executor are both deferred (`08:126-127`, §5.13), so the gate fires directly on cap exhaustion (§2, §6.4). The conjunct returns in phase 2, where `lane-verify-failed` carries it | Phase 3.f | 1 |
| `stage-coupling-unresolved` | a second cross-lane `write_set ∩ read_set` coupling hit in one stage after a re-partition pass (§7.9 (a)). **Replaces `shared-substrate-unhoisted`**, whose predicate is withdrawn as unsatisfiable (X-04) | Phase 3.f″ | 1 |
| `dispatch-base-dirty` | uncommitted content on a path in this stage's handoff **front matter** — `plan_path`, `sidecar_path`, `write_set`, `read_set`, the wave design, the handoffs — **minus every path matching an `orchestrator-only` glob**, evaluated immediately after the 3.f′ commit (§2.1 ordering rule 1) | Phase 3.f′ | 1 |
| `lane-workspace-dirty` | Profile B: a non-empty tree at `acquire` or at `collect` (§5.6) | Phase 3.g / 3.h | **2** |
| `lane-lease-breach` | a lane wrote outside its declared write set | Phase 3.h / 3.j re-audit | **2** |
| `lane-non-code-write` | a lane committed a path outside `code_roots ∪ test_roots` — charter C1's first clause (§5.2) | Phase 3.h / 3.j re-audit | **2** |
| `lane-srs-mutation-detected` | a lane performed an SRS mutation or allocated a Requirement ID | Phase 3.h | **2** |
| `lane-external-side-effect` | `external_side_effects` non-empty | Phase 3.h | **2** |
| `lane-evidence-irreproducible` | the integrator cannot reproduce a claimed test result, acceptance is vacuous, or a green run has no preceding red | Phase 3.h | **2** |
| `lane-timeout` | the 3.g′ join loop observed `now > deadline_at` on a non-terminal lane (§5.5.3) | Phase 3.g′ | **2** |
| `lane-child-gate-halted` | manifest `status: "needs-user"` — a child skill hit a gate that halts regardless of `--auto` (§5.4.1) | Phase 3.h | **2** |
| `lane-design-refuted` | a `[D-nnn]` item cannot be implemented as written (§8.1). **Phase 1 predicate**: the executing unit reports the refutation and stops; **phase 2 predicate**: manifest `status: "design-refuted"`. The gate id and the §8.1 route are the same in both; only the carrier differs | Phase 3.g and 3.k activity (0) (phase 1) / 3.h (phase 2) | 1 |
| `lane-repeatedly-unrecoverable` | a lane died twice and failed in the serial epilogue — the second conjunct is only evaluable **after** the epilogue runs | Phase 3.k | **2** |
| `lane-verify-failed` | loop L cap exhausted with CRITICAL/HIGH open, **and demotion is illegal** (§7.3) | Phase 3.i | **2** |
| `lane-merge-conflict` | a textual conflict inside a supposedly disjoint stage | Phase 3.j | **2** |
| `merge-coupling-detected` | both lanes green in isolation, red merged | Phase 3.j | **2** |
| `partition-quality-insufficient` | ≥2 coupling collapses in one stage | Phase 3.j | **2** |
| `integration-cas-rejected` | a concurrent writer moved the integration branch between build and publish (§5.9) | Phase 3.j | **2** |
| `integration-restore-failed` | an orchestrator-only path could not be restored or removed from the merged index (§5.9) | Phase 3.j | **2** |
| `post-merge-index-drift` | `validate --fail-on-warning` still reports drift after `sync_index` | Phase **3.k** (phase 1, §5.14) / 3.j (phase 2); **the `R-PLAN` close-out** (`09` §5.2 step 2); **the `R-STEP` close-out** (`09` §5.1) | 1 |
| `plan-coverage-unclosed` | on the `R-PLAN` rung, a member of `S2.req_ids` that is neither `verified` nor carries a reason row in the `R-PLAN` `dispatch-route` result line's `coverage_residual[]` in `kiwi/waves.jsonl` after the `--close-reqs` hop returns — **or** one that is `verified` while an acceptance criterion of it is uncovered by evidence (`get_requirement` / `check_acceptance_criteria` over `S2.req_ids`, §6.1's source) and no `coverage_residual[]` row names it with a reason **and** an owner: the `R-PLAN` form of §7.10's `test_id: null` bar (`09` §5.2 step 1, §9.2). **Capped:** the gate also fires, regardless of every row's reason and owner, when that result line carries more than `max(--allow-plan-residual, ceil(\|S2.req_ids\| / 4))` residual rows — the form §6.5 closure 1 already states for `--allow-untested-ac N` (`09` §9.2, `09` §13 A9) | the `R-PLAN` close-out | 1 |
| `epilogue-task-failed` | an epilogue task's `verification_cmd` exited non-zero, or it produced no commit and declared no `intentionally_empty` reason, or its `--lane epilogue` claim audit raised a breach (§5.13) | Phase 3.k | **2** |
| `srs-mutation-replay-failed` | the deferred queue replayed partially; SRS and code disagree | Phase 3.k | **2** |
| `lane-state-harvest-failed` | `collect()` could not copy a lane's manifest, queue or decisions log to the host run directory, so 3.j′ must not reap (§2.1 ordering rule 5) | Phase 3.j′ | **2** |
| `cross-lane-duplication-unresolved` | a `duplicate` row in the wave's duplication audit carries neither an executed epilogue consolidation task id nor an `issue:{id}` ledger reference (§7.9, §8) | Phase 3.k | 1 |
| `verification-oscillation` | a finding opened and closed across ≥2 rounds | any loop | 1 |
| `wave-issues-open` | the next wave attempted with unclosed wave-boundary issues | Phase 3.m | 1 |
| `design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary` | a design item is refuted by implementation | Phase 3.m | 1 |

**Two renames and one deletion, propagated everywhere** (§2.0, §0.6):
`stage-issues-open` → `wave-issues-open`; `design-contradiction-at-stage-boundary` →
`design-contradiction-at-wave-boundary`; `deferred-branch-audit-pending` **deleted** — it was a
build-time precondition on *this repository's* implementation order shipped as a runtime gate in the
*product's* contract, and in any consumer repo it is either permanently unsatisfiable or permanently
vacuous. FR-FLOW-061 parity assertions over gate ids must use the new names in all three variants.

**Inherited verbatim from `kiwi-wave-master`:** `unsafe-option-refused` (`--skip-regression`,
`--reviewer-off`), `wt-delegation-refused` (against a delegated `kiwi-pipeline --wt`; see §1.3 for
why the *wave-level* rationale is not inherited),
`decomposition-input-missing`, **`wave-decomposition-coverage-gap`** (used at Phase 2.d and, in revision 1,
declared in neither table — §2's *"every gate named"* is only true if §13 names it back),
`out-of-scope-user-consent`, `wave-append-cap-exhausted`,
`wave-verify-residual-critical`, `wave-verify-fail-residual`,
`wave-verify-cross-wave-fix-required`, `final-verify-residual-critical`,
`child-pipeline-needs-user-or-failed`, `child-srs-needs-user-or-failed`, `invalid-loop-option`.
**Never auto-granted by `--auto` alone** — each needs its own explicit pass-through option:
`integration-test-user-consent`, `cost-warning-large-task` (`kiwi-wave-master:499`, settled at
`01` §7.5).

Adopted as-is from `auto-option.md:254-274`: `external-module-impact`, `mcp-cli-both-unavailable`,
`self-recursive-spawn`.

**Most of these are unreachable in a correct run.** `lane-lease-breach`,
`lane-srs-mutation-detected`, `lane-external-side-effect`, `dispatch-base-dirty`,
`run-invariant-drift`, `lane-plan-drift` and `post-merge-index-drift` each cost one boolean check
when nothing is wrong. Gate *count* is not gate *cost*.

**Count after the scope cut, stated because §17.9's ratio argument turns on it.** Of the
orchestrator-owned rows above, **20 carry phase 2** and are not declared in
`2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`. Revision 4 **adds five** phase-1 rows: the three critical routing
gates (`route-probe-unreadable`, `route-escalation-after-landed-state`,
`route-deescalation-refused` — `route-override-contradicted` was withdrawn with `09` §4.6) plus
`partition-review-unrecorded` and `serial-unit-failed`; §22 AA-08 adds a **sixth**,
`plan-coverage-unclosed`, on the `R-PLAN` close-out, and §22.12 AG-13 a **seventh**,
`invalid-run-scope-option`, at Preflight P.2, which is why the table's 39 phase-1 rows are
revision 3's 32 plus five plus two and not plus five alone. Net, the phase-1 `critical_gates[]` table is
materially smaller than revision 3's, which is the mechanical form of `08`'s finding that *"roughly
31–34 of the 52–55 orchestrator-owned gates are lane, worktree, merge or epilogue specific"*
(`08:26-28`). The four routing gates that are **not** in the table — the `business-decision` ones —
are counted nowhere here on purpose; `critical_gates[]` is a halt list, not a gate census.

---

## 14. Contract registrations — easy to forget, break silently

1. **`pipeline-event.md §3` closed skill enum** (`:68-87`) gains `kiwi-orchestrator`, or
   `kiwi-pipeline` WARN-skips every event it emits.
2. **`pipeline-event.md §4` T1** gains **two** rows: `kiwi-orchestrator | TASK_DONE | null` (the run
   terminates verified on its **own integration branch**; commit/push to the base
   branch and PR creation are deliberately not auto-chained, `kiwi-pipeline:127`, §5.12) and the
   **missing** `kiwi-wave-master | TASK_DONE | null` row.
3. **`loop-option.md §6`** (`:44-54`) gains
   `kiwi-orchestrator | the ROUTED child (kiwi-tdd | kiwi-pm) or, on R-ORCH, per-wave kiwi-srs + kiwi-planner + kiwi-pm + kiwi-review-fix-loop | --mini/--loops N → the routed child, per wave, and its own D/W/H/P/F loop caps`.
   Two changes against revision 3: the routed children are propagation targets too (`09` §5.1,
   §5.2), and **loop L is absent from the cap list** because it is deferred (§P2 7.3).
3′. **`kiwi-tdd` needs a `critical_gates[]` table and a place in the closed `skill` enum**, as a
   **separate additive requirement** (`09` §11's separate-requirements block). Without it,
   `--auto` is inactive for `kiwi-tdd` by the safe default and its events are WARN-skipped, so an
   `R-STEP` run leaves exactly one journal line — the orchestrator's own. This is `09`'s A7 weakness
   and it is **not** worked around by having the parent record on the child's behalf, which
   `kiwi-pipeline:336` forbids.
4. **`src/doctor/package-doctor.ts` `EXPECTED_KIWI_SKILLS`** (**`:58-75`**, measured) gains the
   skill, which mechanically extends `EXPECTED_SKILL_ENTRYPOINTS` (**`:77`**) to require all three
   variants. *(The first revision cited `:58-73` and `:76` here and in §10.5 step 8; both were off by
   the array's real extent.)*
5. **`waves-event.md` → v1.4.0** in all four copies (three variants + the `.agents` mirror), with
   `test/skills/kiwi-event-contract-content.test.ts` extended to cover the new fields across all
   four. All four copies must be edited in one change — the test asserts across all of them, and
   `speckiwi skills mirror --write` (§9.5) is the sanctioned way the fourth copy is produced.
6. **Mirror**: `kiwi-orchestrator` is **not** added to
   `.agents/skills/.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json`, and neither of that file's two existing
   entries nor its `FR-FLOW-029`-citing `reason` may be altered by any step of this work (§9.4).
7. **Emit through MCP `workflow_pipeline_emit`** (`src/mcp/tools/mutation-tools.ts:369`), not the
   hand-rolled bash append block that `pipeline-event.md §5.1` prescribes to every other skill. This
   is the one place the charter's no-duplication instruction is discharged by *removing* duplication
   rather than avoiding it, and it is safe because emit is best-effort with a documented bash
   fallback (`pipeline-event.md:157-159`).
   **Per-lane events (phase 2 — §15.5, §P2 5.9) use the emit key `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}`, and this is not optional there.** The tool
   dedupes on `${skill}|${run_id}` (`src/core/workflow/mutation.ts:466`, `jsonl.ts:76-78`) and
   returns a **success** envelope with `written: false` on a repeat (`:467-471`), so N lanes sharing
   a bare `run_id` produce **one** event and N−1 silent no-ops. `pipeline-event.md` §5.4 already
   sanctions suffixed emit keys and states that they are *"a different id space"* with the sidecar
   regex not applied, so this uses the existing contract rather than adding one. The run-level event
   at Phase 5 keeps the bare `{run_id}` (`{run_id}#r{n}` on resume), which no lane key collides with.
8. **`workmode-policy.md`**: the orchestrator's **own wave flow (`R-ORCH`)** is body-scope, so `tdd`
   mode's step-scoped routing to `kiwi-tdd` never re-routes it — the same registration
   `kiwi-wave-master/SKILL.md:146` already carries for the wave cycle, and for that registration's own
   reason: a wave runs over a body SRS target and writes no step directory. **Per-run routing to
   `kiwi-tdd` is governed by `09` §4 and §5.1, not by this registration**: `R-STEP` dispatches
   `kiwi-tdd` (§2.1a, §2.1 Phase 1.c′ `:719`), and a mode disagreement raises
   `route-step-requires-mode-switch` rather than removing the rung (§2.1a `:1026-1031`). Read with
   S18 (§15.3 `:5042`), the `kiwi-pipeline` §2.8 half of the same boundary.
   *(Revision 2 registered the flat "the orchestrator is body-scope, so `tdd` mode's step-scoped
   routing to `kiwi-tdd` does not apply", carrying `kiwi-pipeline:196`'s two `--cycle`-entry reasons.
   **Superseded by `09`, normative on routing**: `09` §11 alternative 6 (`:1073-1076`) refuses to turn
   `kiwi-tdd`'s missing `critical_gates[]` into "this child may never be selected" and replaces it with
   §4.4's `tdd-route-unattended` gate — and registration 3′ above schedules that `critical_gates[]`
   table anyway, so the imported reason expires with this work.)*
9. **Tracked `.gitignore`** gains **two entries in phase 1**, each for a stated reason:
   `kiwi/waves.jsonl` and `kiwi/orchestrator/` (§4.8 — machine state that `git clean -fd` must be
   allowed to remove and that `git add -A` must never stage). **A third entry, `.claude/worktrees/`,
   is `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` work (§P2 5.5.2; §10.5 step 10, §15 S15, §15.5)** — today the
   exclusion lives only in `.git/info/exclude:11`, a local unshared file, so Profile A is broken on
   any fresh clone or CI runner, and phase 1 has no Profile A to break (Preflight P.6 is deferred and
   `frozen.isolation_profile` is the constant `"none-serial"`, §2.1).
   **This registration fixes *this* repository and not a single consumer**, which is why §5.5.2 closure
   3 makes the orchestrator write the entry into a consumer's own tracked `.gitignore` at Preflight
   P.6, inside the isolation probe, and re-evaluate probe assertion 7 — **also phase 2**, since `08:122-123` defers *"the consumer
   `.gitignore` remediation"* with the rest of worktree isolation. Revision 1 listed the registration
   as one of three closures for a defect that, in every consumer repository, it does not touch.
10. **`vitest.config.ts` coverage `include`** gains `src/core/orchestrator/**/*.ts` with the
   `parserWorkflowThreshold` the workflow tree already carries (§10.1). Without it the design's 23 new phase-1 modules — its largest executable surface — carry no coverage threshold at all, while the two
   files it *modifies* sit inside a 90% one.
11. **`kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop`** gain `--no-pipeline-emit` **and `--commit-lane-work`**
   — those two, and **not** `kiwi-pipeline`, which no lane invokes. (Revision 1 named `kiwi-pipeline`
   here while §5.4 and S13 both named `kiwi-pm`; §14 is the *"easy to forget, break silently"* list,
   so its version naming a skill outside the lane was the worst place for the disagreement to sit.)
   `kiwi-pm` additionally gains `--handoff <path>` with the semantics §5.4 states and
   `--defer-srs-mutation <path>` as a **pass-through** into its `kiwi-coder` spawn prompt.
   Without `--no-pipeline-emit` a lane writes a false record into `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` inside its
   workspace; without the `--defer-srs-mutation` pass-through the lane's four mandatory MCP mutations
   execute against the **host** root (§7.4 R6); without `--commit-lane-work` **on `kiwi-pm`** nothing
   commits the lane's work at all (§5.4), and without it **on `kiwi-review-fix-loop`** nothing commits
   loop L's fixes — `kiwi-review-fix-loop:199` and `:700` both make `git commit` a user decision, and
   inside a lane there is no user (§7.3). **`--commit-lane-work` takes no argument**: it stages the
   `write_set` of the handoff already passed via `--handoff`. Revision 2 typed it
   `--commit-lane-work <pathspec-file>` and pointed the file at `kiwi/orchestrator/…`, which is
   git-ignored and therefore absent from every Profile A worktree (§5.4).
12. **`kiwi-coder`** gains `--defer-srs-mutation <queue_path>` (§5.4). It is reached only through
   `kiwi-pm`'s spawn prompt, so registration 11's pass-through and this one must land together —
   either alone is inert.

### 14.1 Options

Inherited from `kiwi-wave-master:489-528`, unchanged: `--auto`, `--max`, `--mini` / `--loops N`,
`--model <name>`, `--resume`, `--run-id`, `--constraint` (repeatable), and the pure pass-throughs.
**Three of those — `--auto-integration`, `--auto-cost-warning`, `--force` — flow only when the user
specified them**, and their omission stalls an unattended run at the last hop.
**`--regression-baseline` is the exception** (`kiwi-wave-master:519-528`, whose table row `:528`
annotates it *"(§2.1 이 pin 한 값)"*, and `09` §5.2): it carries the orchestrator's own run-wide
**P.4 pin** whether or not the user supplied one, so that an earlier unit's failures are never
promoted into the next unit's baseline (`kiwi-wave-master:150`). This matches §5.14's invocation
(`[--regression-baseline <the P.4 pin>]`) and the frozen `regression_baseline_ref` (§4.1).

New. **Six of the options below are phase 2** — `--isolation`, `--install-concurrency`,
`--install-stagger`, `--lane-deadline`, `--poll-interval` and `--serial` — and are marked in the
table. `--allow-inferred-write-set` itself ships in phase 1 — `files[]` grounding and the
`unknown-write-set` barrier are phase 1 scope — and only its worktree interaction defers. **`--lanes N` ships** and means what §5.3 says it means for
the *partition*, not for concurrency. **`--serial` is not implemented in phase 1 because it would be
a no-op**: serial is the only mode there is, and an option that requests the default is a contract
surface with nothing behind it.

| Option | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `--work <name>` | the `docs/research/{work}/` directory. **Derivation, in order, because revision 2 said only *"defaults to the target name"* while §2.1 states *"Targets are not registered up front"* and no phase registers one before 3.b — Phase 1.c′'s probe field S9 *reads* an active target (§13 `route-probe-unreadable`; `09` §3.2 S9, consumed by D6 and D7) but neither creates one nor supplies `{work}`, which Preflight has already resolved:** (1) the `--work` value; (2) the slug of the intake source — the `--design-doc` basename, or `issue-{n}` for `--issue`, or the first `--constraint`-free QnA title, lower-cased, non-alphanumerics collapsed to `-`; (3) an already-active target name, **only** when `get_active_target` returns one; (4) `orchestrator-{YYYY-MM-DD}`. Validated against `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9.-]{2,39}$` at Preflight |
| `--design-doc <path>` / `--issue <n>` | intake source (§ Phase 1.a) |
| `--base-branch <name>` | where the run's integration branch forks from; defaults to the current branch. **The orchestrator never writes to it** (§5.12) |
| `--isolation worktree\|branch-serial-lane` | force a profile; refused loudly if its probe fails **Phase 2** (§P2 5.4–5.8) — phase 1 has no profile to force. |
| `--lanes N` | **per-stage** lane cap, default 4, hard max 8 — it bounds **concurrent workspaces**, which is what §17.2's 950 MB and §4.1's 8-entry `open[]` budget are computed against. Components are packed longest-first, then any stage exceeding `N` after layering is split into consecutive stages (§5.3). Revision 2 packed per wave while three other sections read it per stage **Phase 1 reading**: it bounds units per stage in the published partition, not concurrent workspaces (§5.3). |
| `--install-concurrency N` | how many lanes may run `npm ci` at once, default **2**. The N-concurrent-install-on-one-Windows-cache case is untested (`02:356-358`); this is the one-line mitigation **Phase 2** — no per-unit `npm ci` in phase 1. |
| `--lane-deadline <mins>` | per-lane wall clock, default **90**. Exceeded ⇒ `lane-timeout` (§5.5.1). **Composition with `--run-budget`, stated:** a lane's effective deadline is `min(--lane-deadline, remaining --run-budget at dispatch time)`, so a lane dispatched with 20 minutes of run budget left carries a 20-minute deadline rather than a 90-minute one it can never be given. A remediation dispatch (§7.3) carries its **own** deadline under the same formula, not a share of the lane's **Phase 2** — a serially executed unit is waited for, not polled. |
| `--run-budget <mins>` | whole-run wall clock; exceeded ⇒ the run stops at the next stage boundary through `abort-run`, never mid-integration |
| `--subagent-budget N` | ceiling on total verifier/fixer/lane spawns across the run. Six loops × up to 8 lanes × up to 8 waves is bounded only by per-loop round caps otherwise. **Exhaustion behaviour, which revision 2 omitted while `--run-budget` had one:** the orchestrator refuses to spawn past the ceiling, closes the current loop with `verdict = fail-cap` and `reason_class = "budget-exhausted"` (a value in `waves-event.md:92`'s closed vocabulary, registered in v1.4.0 alongside `oscillation`), and stops at the next **stage** boundary through `abort-run` — the same terminus `--run-budget` uses, so an operator sees one shape of ending rather than two. The remaining budget is printed in the run header and at every stage boundary |
| `--allow-inferred-write-set` | permit `[INFERRED:` `files[]` to be lane-eligible; journalled |
| `--allow-untested-ac N` | raise §6.5's `test_id: null` cap; journalled and printed in the run header |
| `--allow-plan-residual N` | raise the `R-PLAN` `coverage_residual[]` row cap (`09` §9.2). An **absolute row count**, not a fraction; journalled on the `dispatch-route` result line and printed in the run header. `R-PLAN` only — there is no residual on the other two rungs |
| `--serial` | **Profile B with `--lanes 1`** (§5.6). One lane per stage, executed in-tree, sequentially. Lane branches, the claim audit, loop L and the per-lane merge gate **all still run** — what is removed is concurrency, and only that. Intake, design authoring, handoffs, merge and post-merge verification also still run. (Revision 1 called this *"profile `none` … no lane isolation, no claim audit, no per-lane merge gate"* while §1.4 degraded to `--serial` citing §5.6, which is Profile B. The claim audit is the only mechanical detector of `lane-srs-mutation-detected`, `lane-non-code-write` and `lane-lease-breach`, so the two readings differed on whether charter C1 and C2 are enforced at all on the design's own recommended small-run path.) **Not implemented in phase 1: it requests the only mode there is.** Returns in phase 2 with its §P2 5.6 meaning. |
| `--poll-interval <secs>` | how often the 3.g′ join loop reads lane status, default **60**. Also sets the heartbeat staleness threshold at 3× (§5.5.3) **Phase 2** — there is no join loop in phase 1. |
| `--install-stagger <secs>` | hard floor between two lane spawns, default **30**, on top of the `--install-concurrency` signal (§5.5.3) **Phase 2** — nothing is spawned concurrently. |
| `--strict-grounding` | require every declared `files[]` path to exist at the dispatch base, rather than only refusing probable typos (§5.1); journalled |

The four budget options are additions. The first revision had **no** ceiling of any kind beyond the
lane count: no wall clock, no spend ceiling, no free-disk check, and no install-concurrency policy —
which for an unattended `--auto` run over eight waves is an open-ended commitment. Free disk is
checked by probe assertion 8 rather than by an option, because it is a precondition rather than a
preference.

**`run_id` is generated as well as validated, and the generation happens before anything uses it.**
Revision 2 validated `--run-id` and never said where a value comes from when the flag is absent,
while Preflight P.6's probe names a branch `kiwi/orch/probe/{run_id}` and journals a
`probe-isolation` line *"for this `run_id`"* before Phase 0 creates the run. The default is
`{YYYY-MM-DD}.{git-toplevel-basename}.{work}`, computed at **Preflight P.0** — before P.1, so every
later step including the run lock's journal line has it. `--run-id` overrides it.

`--run-id` is **validated at Preflight** against `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,48}$` and
`git check-ref-format --allow-onelevel`, because it is embedded in `frozen.integration_branch` —
`kiwi/orch/{run_id}/integration`, created at Phase 0.b (§2.1) — so an unvalidated value is a mid-run
`integration-branch-unavailable` halt taken after P.4 has pinned the baseline and P.5 has taken the
repository-wide run lock, rather than a refusal before any external effect. **Phase 2 adds** the lane
refs `kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}`, where the same bad value instead makes `git checkout -B`
fail inside a lane, after dispatch, with no manifest (§P2 5.4–5.5). A **path-length** rule is deliberately not added: the runtime
chooses the worktree prefix (§5.5), so a static budget would be a guess, and probe assertion 5
measures the real 188-character extremum on the actual host instead.

**Phase 2 (§P2 5.6) — superseded by the scope cut.** `--serial` exists so the machinery is provably optional: a user who does not want it gets one flag,
and the run costs approximately what a `kiwi-wave-master` run costs today plus the handoff and
post-merge steps — with lane branches, the claim audit, loop L and the per-lane merge gate all still
running (§5.6).

---

## 15. Requirement outline

**Nothing here has been written into `docs/spec/`.** This is the outline for a later authoring step,
which §10.5 step 0 sequences: register `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` and record its goal **before** any of this is
authored, and demote `FR-FLOW-025` to `in_progress` in the same step. Grouping follows the
**verifiability seam**, not the topic: a requirement whose acceptance criteria are executable never
shares a block with one whose criteria are text-contract, because their verification evidence differs
in kind and a mixed block cannot be gated per-AC.

Scope prefixes follow the existing documents: `FR-FLOW` → `60.workflow-release.srs.md`,
`IR-CLI` → `30.cli-interface.srs.md`, `FR-MCP` → `40.mcp-stdio-interface.srs.md`,
`FR-NODE` → `50.nodejs-implementation.srs.md`.

**Target split, applied in revision 4.** §15.1–§15.4 are the `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`
authoring set. **§15.5 collects the rows that move to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** and they are
**not authored** in phase 1 — authoring a requirement whose behaviour does not ship is the mechanism
by which a target accumulates rows that can never reach `verified`, which `00.charter.md:297-298` is
about. Rows that survive in a **weakened** form are marked in place rather than moved, because the
weakening is the requirement's phase-1 content.

**Routing rows are new in revision 4** and follow `09` §11's outline: E47–E50e (executable), E24/E26
extended for the surface, T33–T38 and T40 (authored text), and S17 (a contract registration). `09` §11's
placeholder ids (`FR-FLOW-0a`…) are not used here — this document's local `E*`/`T*`/`S*` numbering is
what §10.5 authors from.

**Deliberately absent: the `safe-path.ts` TOCTOU fix (`06` H5).** `06:164` and `06:479-480` rule it a
standalone security fix on `feat/2.3.0.1`, under its own requirement, independent of the orchestrator
and *"should not wait for it"*. It has no row here on purpose (§0.6). A later session must not fold
it into a `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` wave, and must not lose it — §10.5 step 11 gates branch deletion on it landing.

**Harvest verification requirements — per harvest, not per module (§0.5, `06:373-388`).** These are
separate rows because the obligation is on the *port*, not on the ported code's behaviour, and
because `06:386-388` is explicit that a ported passing test does not satisfy TDD when the port
changes the code's dependencies.

**They are named `HV-1`…`HV-5`, not `H-1`…`H-5`.** Revision 1 used `H5` for `06`'s **safe-path TOCTOU
fix** (§0.6, out of scope) and `H-5` for the **gitconfig / three-cwd** requirement row here — two
different things one hyphen apart — while §10.5 step 11 gated an *irreversible branch deletion* on
"H1–H5". `HV-*` removes the collision: `06`'s harvest items keep the names `H1`…`H5` throughout §0.4–
§0.6, and the requirement rows that verify four of them are `HV-*`.

### 15.2 Authored-text contract — evidence is text tests across three variants, plus ledger conformance

*Evidence kind: authored-text contract across three variants + executable ledger conformance.* **Plus `07` §5a's mandatory runtime-lag note (`07:191-196`).** These rows edit **bundled** skill text under `skills/{claude,codex,etc}/`; the running agent reads the **installed** copy under `~/.claude/skills/`. `00.charter.md:303-304` forbids `speckiwi init` / `skills install` / `upgrade --apply` against this repository, so a reinstall here is unavailable and `07` pre-authorised the alternative: **every requirement in §15.2, §15.3 and §15.4 records, as part of its verification evidence, an explicit note that runtime lag is expected, naming the installed path that still holds the old text.** Without it, `verified` means *"verified in a file nobody executes"* — `07:195`'s words — and `00.charter.md:297-298` is breached by the exact mechanism `07` §5 exists to name. §10.5 step 10′ sequences it.

| # | Scope | One-line statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| T1 | FR-FLOW | `kiwi-orchestrator` exists in three variants, is verb-indexed with one `§V.<verb>` section per enum member, and declares a three-column `critical_gates[]` table with cross-variant identifier parity. |
| T2 | FR-FLOW | A resumed session reads the run contract, runs preflight, runs resume, and performs only `next_action.verb`, never reconstructing state from conversation. |
| T3 | FR-FLOW | Every verb writes a write-ahead `intent` line and a write-behind `result` line through the tool, and an `intent` with no `result` on an externally-visible verb halts for inspection. |
| T4 | FR-FLOW | Intake accepts thin intent, a research or design document, or a GitHub issue, investigates existing code with parallel analysts, and questions the user where the design is weak. |
| T5 | FR-FLOW | The design document is authored at body scope with marked `[D-nnn]` items and is frozen before any implementation verb may run. |
| T6 | FR-FLOW | Loop D's frozen denominator is the open-question set, the implementability set and the constraint set, and a `needs-decision` row routes back to a user question. |
| T7 | FR-FLOW | Each wave has an authored English wave design document, verified by its own loop before SRS registration. |
| T8 | FR-FLOW | Each lane has an English handoff document conforming to the handoff schema, verified by its own loop including a final-round executability probe. |
| T9 | FR-FLOW | Isolation is a named profile chosen by probe, immutable for the run, journalled, and never silently downgraded. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states the isolation profile is chosen by probe, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T10 | FR-FLOW | The worktree probe asserts that a lane can move its worktree to the required base sha, and that a pinned `NODE_ENV=development npm ci` yields a working vitest. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states the worktree probe, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T11 | FR-FLOW | A lane produces code and tests only — **its declared write set lies inside the run's code and test roots** — **and commits them to its own branch, one commit per Task, staging an explicit pathspec drawn from `write_set` and never `git add -A`, with the run coordinates in git trailers and never in a commit subject**: no SRS mutation, no Requirement-ID allocation, no pipeline event, no push, no PR, no test weakening, no write outside its declared set. Orchestrator-only content a child skill writes inside the workspace is left uncommitted and harvested by the orchestrator. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states a lane's write set, its own branch and its commit obligation, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T12 | FR-FLOW | Every artifact a lane needs — including its own handoff — is committed to **that stage's** dispatch base before dispatch, and a lane's `base_sha` is that commit. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states the per-stage dispatch base and `base_sha`, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T13 | FR-FLOW | Each lane is verified and improved by a two-verifier loop **before** it is merged, with its acceptance rows as the frozen denominator; the loop's remediation runs against the lane's work — under the worktree profile as a dispatch onto a **fresh `-fix{r}` branch**, because git refuses to check out a branch another worktree holds; in-tree on the lane branch under the serial profile — and commits under the same obligation as the lane, through a `--commit-lane-work` flag on the remediating skill, so the tip that is merged is the tip the loop improved. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states loop L and its remediation branch, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T14 | FR-FLOW | Lanes are merged one at a time in lane-id order, each **re-audited at the tip about to be merged**, with a build, full-suite and validate/sync-index gate after each merge, and each merge concluded so the next can begin. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states per-lane merge, re-audit and the merge gate, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T15 | FR-FLOW | `validate` followed by `sync-index` is mandatory after every merge, and residual index drift halts the run. **Phase 1**: phase 1 performs no merge, so the obligation attaches to the end of each wave's serial execution at **3.k** — after the last unit of the last stage, before loop P — with `post-merge-index-drift` (C) unchanged; per-merge attachment returns in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` (§5.14, §13). |
| T16 | FR-FLOW | Convergence points are declared with a recipe from a closed four-value enum; the recipe decides lane eligibility; orchestrator-only paths are restored or removed per path at merge and replayed at the host root. **Phase 1**: the recipe enum and its lane-eligibility rule ship and decide execution *order*; the per-path restore at merge and the host replay are phase 2 (§P2 5.9). |
| T17 | FR-FLOW | Post-merge wave verification runs against the wave design, the frozen design items, the constraints, the preservation layer, the lane layer **and the original intent**. **Phase 1**: unchanged except that the lane layer becomes a unit layer with one witness instead of two (§7.6). |
| T18 | FR-FLOW | Wave-boundary issues are classified from a closed list, planned in a document, and the next wave cannot start until each carries a terminal classification and a resolution proof; deferral requires user consent even under `--auto`. |
| T19 | FR-FLOW | A verification loop that oscillates terminates immediately with `reason_class="oscillation"` rather than consuming its cap. |
| T20 | FR-FLOW | Lane verification budgets are separate from the run budget; an unconverged lane is demoted to the serial epilogue **when no task of a later stage depends on it**, and halts the run for the user when that condition fails; re-dispatch is capped at one. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states lane verification budgets, demotion and re-dispatch, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T21 | FR-FLOW | Requirement promotion happens once, at the host root, after the wave's post-merge verification passes: the wave's allocation set restricted to requirements all of whose tasks landed, transitioned `→ implemented` and, only where loop P passed and no acceptance row is untested, `→ verified`, each carrying a test-kind and a commit-kind verification evidence row. **Phase 1**: *landed* is an `Orch-Task` trailer on the integration branch plus a passing `verification_cmd`; the loop-L acceptance-closure conjunct is phase 2 (§7.10). |
| T22 | FR-FLOW | The orchestrator's task-level isolation is distinguished in its own §0 from `kiwi-wave-master`'s wave-level `--wt` refusal, and it never passes `--wt` to a delegated pipeline. **Phase 1**: the §0 distinction rests on §1.3's phase-1 ground — no per-wave worktree to break accumulation, every unit executed at the host root on the integration branch, `frozen.isolation_profile` the constant `"none-serial"` (§1.3, §2.1 P.6, §4.1, §5.14). The task-granularity isolation half re-enters in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` (§P2 5.5/5.9). The `--wt` non-propagation conjunct is unchanged and inherited verbatim (§13, §2.1 P.2). |
| T23 | FR-FLOW | Below the refusal floor the orchestrator degrades to `--serial` — one lane per stage under the branch-serial profile, **retaining lane branches, the claim audit, loop L and the per-lane merge gate** — and reports that concurrency, not isolation, is what was disabled. **A single-lane stage carries no exemption under any profile**: there is no in-tree, audit-free path for a stage that happens to hold one lane. **Superseded by the scope cut.** There is no concurrency to disable below the refusal floor. What ships in its place is `route-downgrade-available` (§1.4, `09` §7.4): at the end of Phase 2, a one-wave run whose step rung was never disqualified is offered the cheap rung, with `continue-orchestrated` recommended. |
| T24 | FR-FLOW | The charter's no-duplication requirement is discharged by **one** shipped mechanism and one recorded absence: a duplication audit over the union of the wave's lane diffs runs in the serial epilogue and produces an artifact that can fail a gate; and, separately, a path in one lane's `write_set` and another lane's `read_set` within a stage forces the two lanes into one before dispatch, which prevents *coupling* rather than *duplication*. The **preventive** half of no-duplication is **not** discharged at any level — at file level `write-set-overlap` already covers every declared surface, and at symbol level no planner output carries a symbol — and is escalated as X-04. **Phase 1**: the coupling half ships unchanged; the duplication audit runs over the wave's commit range instead of the union of lane diffs (§7.9 (b)). The recorded absence is unchanged. |
| T25 | FR-FLOW | Each lane carries a deadline and writes a heartbeat file the orchestrator polls; a named join step evaluates the deadline and the heartbeat while lanes run; a lane's state is harvested to the host **before** its workspace is reaped at the stage boundary or at any halt; a lane's state and every remediation workspace's state are harvested before any reap; a reap that a lock refuses is **recorded and reported**, never forced and never silently passed; and a resumed session facing a lane whose workspace is live, or facing any unattributed workspace under the isolation root, **never re-dispatches** and asks the user. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states lane deadlines, heartbeats, harvest-before-reap and never-re-dispatch, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T26 | FR-FLOW | A lane that finds a design item unimplementable terminates as `design-refuted`, and a mid-wave design amendment is written as a new lock plus a new journal line rather than read as drift. |
| T27 | FR-FLOW | A child skill's always-halt gate inside a lane surfaces at the host as `needs-user` with its gate id and payload, and every committee decision made inside a lane is written to the host root. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states a child skill's always-halt gate inside a lane, which phase 1 does not have. |
| T28 | FR-FLOW | Under `--auto`, intake questions answered by committee rather than by the user are recorded in the run header, in the run contract, and one journal line per decision. |
| T29 | FR-FLOW | The run has a named integration branch recorded in the frozen block, the orchestrator never writes to the base branch and never opens a PR, **every artifact the run authors under its work root is committed on a stated schedule so `git clean -fd` cannot destroy the frozen design or its locks**, and an aborted run reports the repository state it leaves behind — including any workspace it could not reclaim. **Phase 1**: unchanged, except that an aborted run reports commits on the integration branch rather than workspaces it could not reclaim (§5.12). |
| **T30** | FR-FLOW | The serial epilogue has a named executor, a lease, a commit rule, a claim audit with a stated row set, and a gate: its task set is executed at the host on the integration branch through one epilogue handoff before the convergence recipes, the deferred-mutation replay and the duplication audit run. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`** — it states the serial epilogue as a distinct executor, which phase 1 does not have. |
| **T31** | FR-FLOW | A handoff never carries the sha of the commit that contains it; the dispatch base sha reaches the lane through the spawn prompt, and the run's ledger records it in the journal and the resume card. **Phase 1**: the *reaches the lane through the spawn prompt* clause moves to §15.5 as `T31 (partial)` — phase 1 has no spawn prompt; the unit runs at the host with the integration branch already checked out (§5.14). The handoff-rejection clause and the ledger-record clause are phase-1 content (§15.1 E44, §4.1 `open[].base_sha`, §4.2 `isolation.base_sha`). |
| **T32** | FR-FLOW | Every pre-merge loop's residual — D, W, H and L, not only P — enters the wave-boundary issue ledger, so a finding that closed as `pass-with-residual` cannot cross a wave boundary unrecorded. **Phase 1**: D, W and H; L rejoins with loop L. |
| **T33** | FR-FLOW | `kiwi-orchestrator` computes a route at Phase 1.c′ — after the intake investigators and the QnA, before design authoring, and before any SRS mutation, target registration or plan authoring — from a probe frozen to `routing/probe.json` before the gate is raised, with every probe field carrying a named producer and a named tool call. |
| **T34** | FR-FLOW | Routing is **disqualifier-first**: each predicate removes rungs and none selects one; the first survivor in the fixed order `R-PLAN → R-STEP → R-ORCH` is selected; `R-ORCH` is total; and a wrong route is traceable either to one named predicate and one recorded value, or — when the gate selected the alternative — to the recorded `proposed_rung`, `overridden_by` and ballot resolution, in `route.lock.json`. |
| **T35** | FR-FLOW | The persisted work-mode **never removes** the step rung; a classifier/mode disagreement raises `route-step-requires-mode-switch` with `stay-and-orchestrate` recommended; the orchestrator never calls `set_work_mode` on its own authority; and under `--auto` an `R-STEP` selection raises `tdd-route-unattended` with `orchestrate-instead` recommended rather than disqualifying the rung. |
| **T36** | FR-FLOW | The route table: per rung, the artifacts that must exist before invocation, the exact invocation form, the flag propagation set, and the orchestrator's obligation after the child returns — including `R-PLAN`'s plan-coverage close-out, which verifies the denominator it has rather than claiming a design-layer verdict it has none for. |
| **T37** | FR-FLOW | `R-ORCH` drives the extracted shared wave engine directly; `kiwi-orchestrator` never invokes `kiwi-wave-master`; an explicit user request for `kiwi-wave-master` is not intercepted; and the wave-semantic critical gates are **declared** in the orchestrator's own `critical_gates[]` rather than inherited, because `_shared` modules are not children and carry no gate declarations. |
| **T38** | FR-FLOW | Escalation is one-way with the named triggers and carry manifests; completed work is sealed `out_of_scope` with `exclusion_class = "already-implemented"`; `route-escalation-after-landed-state` is critical and fires on a landed commit **or** an SRS `status` **or** `stability` mutation, while an escalation detected before the first red test is free and ungated; de-escalation after Phase 3.b is refused; and on resume the rung is read from the lock and never recomputed. |
| **T39** | FR-FLOW | Implementation in `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` runs **serially** at the host root on the run's integration branch, one unit of the frozen partition at a time, in stage order and lane-id order, through one `/kiwi-pm` run per handoff; the run delivers no wall-clock speedup; and the per-task parallelisability analysis is nonetheless produced in full, frozen, published and reviewed per wave. |
| **T40** | FR-FLOW | The four `business-decision` routing gates — `route-proposal`, `route-step-requires-mode-switch`, `tdd-route-unattended`, `route-downgrade-available` — are declared in the skill body with their severities across all three variants and are deliberately **absent** from `critical_gates[]`, with the recorded reason that `route-proposal` fires on every run (§13, `09` §8.1); the declaration is what pins the ≥ 0.7 threshold rather than relying on `auto-option.md:190`'s undeclared-gate default. |

### 15.3 Shared-contract and migration requirements

*Evidence kind: as §15.2, including the mandatory runtime-lag note — for the `FR-FLOW` rows. **S14 and S15 are the exception**: they are executable rows and take §15.1's kind (unit/integration tests; `07` §5a's runtime-lag obligation is not applicable). S14 is a `src/` CLI verb (§9.5, §10.5 step 6″, red first) and S15 is repository configuration (§10.5 step 10); neither edits bundled skill text and neither has an installed copy in between, so there is no installed path for a runtime-lag note to name.*

| # | Scope | One-line statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | FR-FLOW | `waves-event.md` v1.4.0 adds `engine`, `writer`, `event`, `verb`, `inputs_digest`, `lane`, `stage`, `lane_plan`, **`partition_review`** (§4.2 — the 3.e′ verdict object the `partition-review-unrecorded` gate reads), `isolation` (carrying `profile`, `reason`, `rejected[]`), `lane_layer`, **`wave_issues`** (not `stage_issues` — §2.0's vocabulary, fixed before the schema ships), `convergence`, `allocation`, `decision`, `deadline_at`, `postmortem`, **`coverage_residual`** (`{req_id, reason, owner}` rows on the `R-PLAN` `dispatch-route` result line, the home the `plan-coverage-unclosed` gate reads — `09` §9.2), **`lane_disposition`** and `card_digest` — with `allocation` carrying `pre_snapshot_digest` and `design_item_map` — plus the extended `phase` enum and the `reason_class` additions `oscillation` and `budget-exhausted`, additively. |
| S2 | FR-FLOW | The resume predicate filters by `engine`, and an absent `engine` reads as `kiwi-wave-master`. |
| S3 | FR-FLOW | `_shared/kiwi/verify-loop.md` carries the cross-verification engine, denominator-agnostically, and `kiwi-wave-master` references it rather than inlining it. |
| S4 | FR-FLOW | `_shared/kiwi/wave-decomposition.md` carries wave splitting, design-baseline materialisation and the coverage gate, with the artifact root as a parameter. |
| S5 | FR-FLOW | `_shared/kiwi/wave-srs-registration.md` carries the per-wave `/kiwi-srs` registration contract, referenced by both orchestrators. |
| S6 | FR-FLOW | `_shared/kiwi/isolation-and-integration.md` carries the five-operation isolation interface, the profiles, the probe, the lane contract including the commit obligation, and the restore-and-CAS integration mechanism. **→ §15.5, `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`.** Three of its clauses do **not** move and are authored into `run-ledger.md` in phase 1 instead: the `recipe.kind` lane-eligibility rule; the **normative shipped-default convergence registry** — §P2 5.9's *Normative shipped default* JSON, `CP-01`, `CP-02` and `CP-07`; and the **never-merge-to-the-base-branch** rule together with the mandatory post-merge `validate` → `sync-index` (§9.1). |
| S7 | FR-FLOW | `_shared/kiwi/run-ledger.md` carries the resume-card schema, the verb enum, the three recovery classes, the write discipline, the proof kinds and the reconciliation predicate. |
| S8 | FR-FLOW | The extraction preserves behaviour: every pre-existing `kiwi-wave-master` text assertion resolves after the move, and each **normative sentence** — §10.3's bilingual modal set, counted per variant, because the four extracted bodies carry zero `MUST` tokens in all three variants — still has a red mutation. |
| S9 | FR-FLOW | `pipeline-event.md` registers `kiwi-orchestrator` in the closed skill enum and gives T1 rows to both `kiwi-orchestrator` and `kiwi-wave-master`. |
| S10 | FR-FLOW | `loop-option.md §6` records the orchestrator's propagation targets. |
| S11 | FR-FLOW | `kiwi-pm` accepts `--session-suffix <lane>`, moving its whole session directory under `lanes/{lane}/`; `--no-final`, skipping T-final promotion; and `--handoff <path>`, which supplies the **execution set and the lease** and nothing else — pm executes **exactly** the front matter's `task_ids[]`, in sidecar declaration order, taking each Task's body from `sidecar_path`; `write_set` is the commit pathspec; `req_ids[]` and `acceptance[]` are read-only context passed into the coder spawn prompt; a `depends_on_task` predecessor outside `task_ids[]` is treated as **satisfied**; and `--from-task` is refused together with `--handoff`. *(Revision 2's S11 still stated the superseded "input SSOT" phrasing — the one §5.4 calls "not a definition an implementer can build" — so the requirement authored from §15 would have re-introduced the defect RW-12 closed in §5.4 alone: two lanes on one plan each executing every task and each tripping `lane-lease-breach`.)* |
| S12 | FR-FLOW | `kiwi-coder` accepts `--defer-srs-mutation <path>`, recording its four mandatory MCP mutations to a queue instead of calling them. |
| S13 | FR-FLOW | `kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop` — and **not** `kiwi-pipeline`, which no lane invokes — accept `--no-pipeline-emit`, suppressing the `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` append so a lane writes no false pipeline record; the orchestrator emits one host-root event per lane at integration instead, **under the per-lane emit key `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}`**, without which the tool's `skill|run_id` dedupe silently discards every lane after the first. |
| S16 | FR-FLOW | **`kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop`** accept `--commit-lane-work` — **no argument** — committing each completed Task (pm) or each applied fix round (review-fix-loop) with only the handoff's `write_set` staged and the `commit_policy` trailers attached; and `kiwi-pm` accepts `--defer-srs-mutation <path>` as a pass-through into its `kiwi-coder` spawn prompt. Without the flag on pm, nothing in a lane commits; without it on review-fix-loop, nothing commits loop L's fixes and the merged tip is the pre-fix commit; without the pass-through, `kiwi-coder`'s four mandatory MCP mutations execute against the host root. A **pathspec file** is deliberately not used: any path under `kiwi/orchestrator/` is git-ignored and therefore absent from a Profile A worktree. |
| S14 | FR-NODE | `speckiwi skills mirror --check \| --write` regenerates and verifies `.agents/skills/**` from the `codex` variant honouring the exclusions file, without invoking `init` or `skills install`. |
| S15 | FR-NODE | `.gitignore` covers `kiwi/waves.jsonl` and `kiwi/orchestrator/`, and coverage `include` covers `src/core/orchestrator/**/*.ts`. *(`.claude/worktrees/` moves to §15.5 with the worktree profile.)* |
| **S17** | FR-FLOW | **`kiwi-tdd` declares a `critical_gates[]` table and joins `pipeline-event.md`'s closed `skill` enum with a T1 row** — authored as a **separate additive requirement**, not folded into the routing rows. Without it `--auto` is inactive for `kiwi-tdd` by the safe default and its events are WARN-skipped, so an `R-STEP` run's only journal record is the orchestrator's own (`09` §5.1, §9, A7). |
| **S18** | FR-FLOW | **`kiwi-pipeline` §2.8 gains one sentence**: when a `kiwi-orchestrator` run has frozen a route, §2.8's step-scoped conjunct is **satisfied by** `route.lock.json` and is not re-judged. Today §2.8 never runs inside an orchestrator run, because `--cycle`/`--from=` entries are excluded from its routing and the orchestrator calls the stages individually; the amendment exists so that a later change to that exclusion does not silently create two live routers (`09` §4.1, §11). |

### 15.4 The `--auto` change

*Evidence kind: authored-text contract across the four `auto-option.md` copies + `decideAutoGate` unit tests, **plus the `07` §5a runtime-lag note**. Revision 1's §15.4 was the only requirement block in §15 with no evidence-kind header at all, which is precisely where `07:191-196` bites: A1–A6 could go green over the bundled files while every `--auto` gate on the machine still ran the old unanimity ladder.*

| # | Scope | One-line statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A1 | FR-FLOW | **Amend `FR-FLOW-025`**: `--auto` convenes 3 members (5 under `--max`) and decides by **simple majority**; the unanimity requirement and the 3→5 / 5→7 escalation ladder are removed. |
| A2 | FR-FLOW | **New**: a gate option carrying the structured marker `recommended: true` is adopted immediately with no committee; an unparsed prose `(권장)` confers nothing; precedence is `recommended` > `default_if_auto` > committee; and §3's ladder-violation prohibition (`:159`) is reworded so that it admits a zero-vote fast-pathed adoption and still forbids every other arbitrary adoption (§12 edit 12.2). |
| A3 | FR-FLOW | The confidence-spread rule folds into the confidence-threshold mechanism and never triggers a re-vote on spread alone. |
| A4 | FR-FLOW | Under a split vote the governing confidence is the minimum within the winning bloc after adjustment. |
| A5 | FR-FLOW | The decision audit log records **each zero-vote adoption** with `committee_size: 0` and a `marked_by`: a `recommended`-marked adoption as `{rule: "recommended-fastpath", …}` and a `default_if_auto` adoption as `{rule: "default-if-auto", …}` — the two branches 12.3 ranks above the committee. |
| A6 | FR-FLOW | The `etc` local-LLM profile's committee carve-out survives the rewrite, in the one rendering where the file exists. |
| **A7** | FR-FLOW | **DECLINED — `08` §2, 5-0.** The tie rung is **not** authored, in either target. It is kept as a row rather than deleted so that a later session reads a decision rather than an absence. |
| **A8** | FR-FLOW | **New**: an absence of majority, **and** a degraded quorum, escalate the gate to `critical` and **halt for the user**. `decideAutoGate` takes a `tieRung: boolean` construction parameter that ships `false`, and a test asserts the shipped construction. |
| **A9** | FR-FLOW | **New, and it is a condition of A2's population rather than a separate improvement** (`08:66-73`): the two `07` conditions travel verbatim — under a split vote the governing confidence is the **minimum within the winning bloc** after the §4.1.1 adjustments (which is A4), and §4.1.2's confidence-spread rung folds into §4.1.1's threshold mechanism with **no re-vote on spread alone** (which is A3). A9 exists so the SRS records *why* A3 and A4 may not be dropped: they, not `critical_gates[]` membership, are the boundary on the population Q2 leaves committee-decided. |

**A1 was one requirement stating two contradictory things and is now split.** The first revision's A1
read *"the 3→5 / 5→7 ladder are removed; a tie escalates once by two members"* — a sentence that
denies and asserts a two-member escalation in the same clause. A requirement authored from it is
self-contradictory on its face and cannot be gated per-AC. A1 carries only the charter-conformant
half.

**Revision 4 closes the quarantine rather than filling it.** Revision 3 parked the disputed half in
A7 behind X-03 and moved A4's degraded-quorum clause there with it. `08` §2 declines the rung, so A7
is DECLINED and the degraded-quorum clause needed a home that ships: it is **A8**, alongside the
no-majority halt, because both are now the same rule. Nothing in §15.4 is pending.

**No gate is promoted to `critical_gates[]` by the `--auto` change.** `08` re-decided `07` Q2 and it
stays `leave-all-three`, so the three irreversible / externally visible gates remain
committee-decided under a 2-1 — bounded by A4's minimum-within-the-winning-bloc rule and §4.1.1's
×0.7 penalty for an empty `side_effects[]`, which is what A9 records.

### 15.5 `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` — the deferred requirement set, **not authored in phase 1**

`08` §3 defers the behaviour these rows state. **They are listed, not authored.** Authoring a
requirement under `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` whose behaviour does not ship produces a row that
can never reach `verified` on evidence, which is the failure `00.charter.md:297-298` exists to prevent
and which `06` found three instances of on the deferred branch.

| Row | Statement, in one line | Where the design lives |
| --- | --- | --- |
| E17, E17a | the lane claim audit's eleven rows, the effective lane record, `--at <sha>`, and the `red_evidence` check | §P2 7.4 |
| E30, E31 | `integrate()`'s per-path restore and its expected-parent CAS publish | §P2 5.9 |
| HV-4 | the CAS-rejection harvest test on `integrate()`'s expected-parent publish (`06` H4) | §P2 5.9 |
| E40 | `collect()`'s `complete-unreported` synthesis and the harvest precondition on reaping | §P2 5.4 |
| E41 | `LIVENESS(k)`'s totality | §P2 5.5.1 |
| E42 (partial) | the *never offered `integrate-lane`* clause only — `execute-unit` replaces `integrate-lane` in the phase-1 verb enum (§4.4). The `lane-quarantined` classification and the `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` disposition clause stay in §15.1 as phase-1 content (§4.6, §4.1) | §P2 5.9 |
| E45 | the per-lane pipeline event key `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}` | §P2 5.9 |
| E46 | `orchestrate lane audit`'s effective-record input and its `audit.3h/3j` split | §P2 7.4 |
| E43 (partial) | `orchestrate lane status --json`'s `LaneStatus[]` output shape, and `auditLane`'s declared input type | §P2 5.5.3, §P2 7.4 |
| E24 (partial) | the deferred half of the `orchestrate` CLI surface — the four `lane *` verbs (`audit`, `harvest`, `release`, `status`) and `replay plan`; the phase-1 verbs stay in §15.1 E24, and `lane audit` / `lane status`'s output shape have their own rows above (E46, E43 (partial)) | §P2 3.5, §P2 5.5.1, §P2 5.5.3, §P2 5.9, §P2 7.4 |
| E18 | deferred-mutation replay planning and its `(tool, args_hash)` dedupe | §P2 5.9 |
| E22 (partial) | the `lane-manifest` artifact kind only — the `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/` layout stays in §15.1 as phase-1 content (§3.2, §5.14, §10.5 step 9) | §P2 3.5 |
| T9, T10 | the isolation profile, its immutability, and the worktree probe's assertions | §P2 5.4, §P2 5.5 |
| T11, T12 | the lane contract, its commit obligation, and the per-stage dispatch base | §P2 5.4, §P2 5.5 |
| T13, T14 | loop L with its `-fix{r}` remediation branch, and per-lane merge with re-audit | §P2 7.3, §P2 7.5 |
| T20, T25 | lane budgets, demotion, re-dispatch cap, deadlines, heartbeats, harvest-before-reap | §P2 7.3, §P2 5.5.1 |
| T27 | a child's always-halt gate surfacing from inside a lane, and lane-local decision records | §P2 5.4.1 |
| T30 | the serial epilogue as a distinct executor with its own audit row set and gate | §P2 5.13 |
| T31 (partial) | the *dispatch base sha reaches the lane through the spawn prompt* clause only — phase 1's ledger still records it in the journal and the card (§4.1, §4.2), and the handoff still carries none (§15.1 E44) | §P2 5.5 |
| S6 | `_shared/kiwi/isolation-and-integration.md`, minus the three clauses §9.1 reassigns | §P2 5.4–5.9 |
| S12, S16 (partial) | `kiwi-coder --defer-srs-mutation` and its `kiwi-pm` pass-through | §P2 5.4 |
| S13 (partial) | the orchestrator's per-lane host-root pipeline event at integration, under the emit key `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}` — the `--no-pipeline-emit` flag itself stays in phase 1 | §P2 5.9 |
| S15 (partial) | `.gitignore` coverage of `.claude/worktrees/` | §P2 5.5.2 |
| 20 `critical_gates[]` rows | every gate marked **2** in §13 | §13 |
| X-01 | the charter C3 amendment — **granted**; C3 now reads in the narrowed form (`00.charter.md:145-160`). Phase 2 must still run probe assertion 2 | §0.7 |

**Two flags stay in phase 1 although their stated motivation was a lane.** `kiwi-pm --handoff`,
`--session-suffix`, `--no-final`, `--no-pipeline-emit` and `--commit-lane-work` (S11, the surviving
halves of S13 and S16) are what §5.14's executor calls; without them phase 1 has no executor at all.
`--commit-lane-work` on `kiwi-review-fix-loop` moves to phase 2 with loop L.

## 16. Rejected alternatives

Recorded so a later session does not re-propose them.

| # | Alternative | Why rejected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| R1 | **Adopt `work/kiwi-wave-master-multiroot-g0` as the foundation.** | Its execution host is under an un-liftable security HOLD (`MANIFEST_CHILD_TRUST_SAFETY`, fail-closed, not `--auto`-bypassable); it forked at `2.3.1` and lacks all of `v2.4.0`–`v2.5.1`, so adopting it reverts verified requirements; it collides with four `v2.4.0`-verified Requirement IDs; and its central thesis (per-root MCP servers) solves a problem C1 removes. **Harvest, don't adopt** (§0.4). |
| R2 | **Discard the branch.** | Still rejected, but **the stated rationale was half wrong and `06` corrected it.** `event-schema.ts` and `waves.ts` are *not* "exactly the validator this design needs": `event-schema.ts:199` hard-rejects any `schema_version != "1.0.0"` against mainline's shipped v1.3.0 and requires `manifest_hash` on every event, so porting it is a contract regression (`06:197-216`); `waves.ts` is inseparable from the manifest model (`06:113`). What survives the filter and justifies keeping the branch is H1 (pinning), H2 (`lease-lock.ts`, re-keyed), H3 (the readiness functions) and H4 (the CAS technique) — under 1,000 lines total. |
| R3 | **Per-root MCP server spawn to fix the split-brain.** | `REL-MCP-004` / `IR-CLI-045` prohibit per-call root by design and the CLI refuses an explicit root (`src/cli/commands/mcp.ts:25-28`). C1 removes the need entirely: hoist SRS out of lanes and there is nothing to route. |
| R4 | **Have `kiwi-orchestrator` simply call `kiwi-wave-master`.** | Wave-master's execution hop is `/kiwi-pipeline --cycle --from=feasibility --run`, whose implementation stage is `kiwi-pm`, which is strictly sequential by declaration order. Task-level parallelism cannot be injected from outside without an executor-substitution option on wave-master, which is not cheaper than the D1 extraction already settled. |
| R5 | **A new journal file (`orchestrator.jsonl`).** | It would fork the run-scoped resume predicate and duplicate the completion gate, and double the exposure to the mirror-prune class. `waves-event.md:3` sanctions an additive minor, which is strictly cheaper. |
| R6 | **Spawn `kiwi-coder` per task directly, bypassing `kiwi-pm`.** | Duplicates pm's loop, its three-state protocol and its lifecycle gate. Two additive flags on `kiwi-pm` cost far less. |
| R7 | **Implement `kiwi-pm`'s deferred v0.2 "parallel Task spawn".** | Its own text (`:1154`) conditions it on race-safety pre-verification. `--session-suffix` achieves the same effect **structurally** — parallelism between isolation units, serialism untouched inside each one — and *discharges* the sequential-spawn precondition rather than weakening it. |
| R8 | **`freeze` extracts `design_items` from unmarked prose.** | Distinguishing normative from illustrative sentences is a semantic judgment (`kiwi-wave-master:177` says so). An unstable extractor silently rewrites every verification denominator while the digest reports "no drift". The agent marks; the tool validates (§3.3). |
| R9 | **The skill hand-appends `waves.jsonl` in bash, as every other skill does.** | The charter forbids extending that duplication, and it would defeat the `writer` stamp, which is the only after-the-fact evidence that the sanctioned path was used. |
| R10 | **`orchestrate resume` performs the run-root preflight itself.** | Impossible on both surfaces: the CLI resolves root from the caller's cwd (making the check vacuous, and `kiwi-wave-master:131` forbids it as a fallback), and the MCP server fixes `workspaceRoot` to its own cwd (`src/mcp/adapter.ts:37`), so it would compare its root against itself. The agent supplies both values; the tool normalises and compares (§10.6). |
| R11 | **Reuse `appendWorkflowJsonl` unchanged for the waves journal.** | `eventKey` is `skill\|run_id` and waves events carry no `skill` and many lines per run, so every line after the first raises `SRS-W053` and the default `policy: "halt"` denies the append. One additive `eventKeying` option fixes it without a second JSONL implementation. |
| R12 | **Include the `cap - rounds < streak ⇒ fail-cap` rule in the journal validator.** | The streak requirement is variant-dependent and the journal records neither mode nor variant, so the test would pin a guess rather than the contract. It stays in `evaluateRound`, whose caller supplies the mode. |
| R13 | **Make mirroring `kiwi-orchestrator` the fix for the `_shared` prune bug.** | `FR-NODE-090` (v2.5.0, verified) already fixes it. Relying on an accidental reference would make a future prune regression undetectable by design. |
| R14 | **Refuse the run below the lane-count floor.** | Small-but-unclear work is exactly where intake QnA, design authoring and post-merge verification pay for themselves, and lane count is only known after they have run. Degrade to `--serial` instead (§1.4). **The verdict stands; its remedy is superseded by the scope cut.** Phase 1 has no lane-count floor and does not implement `--serial` (§14.1). What fires in its place is `route-downgrade-available` at the end of Phase 2, with `continue-orchestrated` recommended (§1.4, RS-15, `09` §7.4); `--serial` returns in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`. |
| R15 | **Ship Profile C (`patch-lane`) in 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator.** | Its completion proof is strictly weaker than `git-ancestor`, and Profile B already covers the negative-worktree case with full-strength proofs. Specified, not built. |
| R16 | **Let a wave-boundary issue close by deferral without a user decision.** | The charter says resolve, then proceed. Two of three routes being deferral would let the next wave begin with the issue unresolved, which is the exact failure the charter names. *(Renamed from "stage-boundary" per §2.0.)* |
| R17 | **A `handoff-lint` CLI verb separate from `handoff validate`.** | A ~15-line regex does not need its own command surface; it is one violation class inside `validateHandoff`. |
| R18 | **The `--auto` judgment gate that asks *why* an option is recommended.** | Explicitly excluded by the charter for `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`. `decideAutoGate` is written so it cannot express it (§12). |
| R19 | **`--wt` delegation to a child `kiwi-pipeline`.** | A cycle-scoped worktree nested inside a lane-scoped one is not a supported topology, and the child's merge gate would land work the orchestrator's claim audit never saw. Inherited as a refusal. |
| R20 | **Lane agents call SRS mutations because `kiwi-coder §0.12` requires them.** | The mutations are recorded, not skipped: `--defer-srs-mutation` writes them to the existing `mcp_call_log[]` queue with the existing `args_hash` dedupe, and the orchestrator replays at the host root. Skipping them would break traceability; calling them would break C1. |

**Rejected in revision 1.** Each of these was a live option; each is recorded so it is not re-proposed.

| # | Alternative | Why rejected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| R21 | **Port `event-schema.ts` and extend it to v1.4.0** (the first revision's own A2 default). | `06:200` overrides it explicitly: *"That default is wrong and is hereby overridden."* `event-schema.ts:199` rejects any `schema_version != "1.0.0"` while mainline shipped v1.3.0, and `manifest_hash` is required on every event against a manifest model this design does not have. Write fresh; carry the ~20-line transition table only. |
| R22 | **Keep `deferred-branch-audit-pending` as a runtime `critical_gates[]` entry.** | A build-time precondition on *this repository's* implementation order, placed in the *product's* runtime contract. In any consumer repo it is permanently unsatisfiable (halting every run) or permanently vacuous — dead prose inside a closed enum that FR-FLOW-061 parity tests then assert across three variants. Build order belongs in §10.5. |
| R23 | **Keep the always-PR-under-`--auto` rule.** | It appeared exactly once, as the *contents* of a shared module, and contradicted five other places: §5.5 / §5.6 / §5.9 all merge directly, the lane contract forbids PRs outright, and §14 item 2 states commit/push is deliberately not auto-chained. No phase step, no CLI verb, no gate, no requirement. The direct-merge steps have all four. What survives is the **never-direct-merge-to-the-base-branch** half, which now has a mechanism: `--base-branch` plus a named `integration_branch` (§5.12). A PR, if wanted, is a post-run `kiwi-commit-auto-pr` hop. |
| R24 | **Make `computeLanePlan` impure so it can check path existence**, per the first revision's §5.1. | Determinism is load-bearing for drift digest 3 and E6, and a filesystem-dependent planner makes `lane-plan-drift` fire on any unrelated file creation — a worse and more frequent failure than the one prevented. The check moves to `orchestrate schedule plan` with its own gate, and existence arrives as injected input. Separately, `--check-files` is unreachable from `src/` (`grep -rn "check-files" src/` → nothing). |
| R25 | **Make `exclusive-lane` reachable for CP-04 (`skills/**`) and CP-06 (`package.json`)** to fix its unreachability. | It fixes the enum at the cost of violating charter C1 (*"only code and tests"*) and this design's own `forbidden[]`, both of which ban a lane from touching those paths. Reclassify them `orchestrator-only`; keep `exclusive-lane` for CP-03 (`src/cli/**`, `src/mcp/**`), which is code and is what C6's "at most one concurrent writer" describes; and add the missing `recipe.kind` rule so the enum member is reachable **legitimately**. |
| R26 | **Widen the claim audit so a lane may write `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl`**, keeping the first revision's drop-at-merge-and-replay story. | The drop mechanism could not execute (§5.9), so widening the audit leaves the line permanently on the integration branch *and* replayed at the host root — duplicate events forever. The replay story existed only to undo a write that need not happen. The lane emits nothing; the orchestrator emits one event per lane at integration. |
| R27 | **Unwind merges on `abort-run`.** | Every merged lane passed its claim audit and its loop L. Unwinding destroys verified work to tidy a ledger. `abort-run` leaves the integration branch where it is and **reports** the state instead (§5.12). |
| R28 | **Make intake QnA a `critical_gates[]` member so `--auto` cannot answer design questions.** | A thin intent produces many `needs-decision` rows by construction, so an always-halting intake means `--auto` never survives Phase 1 — which makes the mode useless for the class of work the charter is about. Recorded as a declared degradation with three independent audit records instead (§7.2). Re-decided if X-03 re-opens `07` Q2. |
| R29 | **Re-dispatch a lane whose `dispatch-lane` intent has no result.** | Branch commits cannot distinguish *running* from *finished*, and background teammate names are runtime-session-scoped in this repository — after a resume, previously spawned agents are unreachable **while still holding their worktrees**. Re-dispatching puts two agents on one branch. Never re-dispatch a possibly-live lane; ask the user (§5.5.1). |
| R30 | **Author the tie rung into `FR-FLOW-025`'s amendment alongside the majority rule.** | The two halves contradict each other in one sentence and cannot be gated per-AC. The rung also needs a charter amendment that has not been granted. Split: A1 carries the settled half, A7 quarantines the disputed one (X-03). *(That last clause is revision 1's disposition and is **superseded**: `08` §2 decided X-03 5-0, so A7 is **DECLINED** and not pending — §15.4 closes the quarantine rather than filling it. The two reasons above are unaffected and the alternative stays rejected.)* |

**Rejected in revision 2.** Same purpose: each was a live option this round, and each is recorded so it is not re-proposed.

| # | Alternative | Why rejected |
| --- | --- | --- |
