| R31 | **Add three suppression flags — `--no-plan-checkbox`, an analysis-dir suppressor, and a third for the next child-skill write — so a lane never produces an orchestrator-only path.** | Three flags on two skills this design does not own, each threaded through `kiwi-pm`'s spawn prompt into `kiwi-coder`, and every future child-skill write reopens the problem. §5.4.2's pathspec rule is one sentence, is enforced by the party that must already be told what to stage, and is closed under child skills this design has not seen. `kiwi-pm`'s plan-checkbox edit is *allowed to happen* — it is simply never committed, and the orchestrator replays its semantic effect through `workflow_task_check`. |
| R32 | **Keep loop L's remediation "inside the lane workspace" and have the orchestrator commit the fixer's output itself.** | Under the worktree profile there is no mechanism to put an agent in an existing workspace (§5.5's opening rule), so the premise fails before the commit question arises. And an orchestrator-authored commit on a lane branch has no lane attribution, no `red_evidence` and no claim-audit trail — it would be exactly the unaudited-work class §7.4 exists to prevent. The remediation is a **dispatch**, reusing the only validated mechanism, and it inherits the lane's commit obligation whole. |
| R33 | **Keep `--serial` as a third profile `none` with no claim audit, on the grounds that a single in-tree lane needs no isolation.** | The claim audit is the *only* mechanical detector of `lane-srs-mutation-detected`, `lane-non-code-write` and `lane-lease-breach` — i.e. of charter C1 and C2 at execution time. §1.4 routes small runs into `--serial` **by default**, so a `none` profile removes C1 enforcement from the most-travelled path in the design. `--serial` is Profile B with `--lanes 1`; the cost is one branch and one audit per stage, which is trivial. **Superseded by the scope cut as to phase 1.** §1.4 no longer routes small runs into `--serial` — the rewritten floor fires `route-downgrade-available` instead (RS-15) — and phase 1 has neither `--serial` (§14.1) nor the claim audit, which returns with the lanes (§P2 7.4). The rejection stands for `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`, where both return. |
| R34 | **Move the claim audit after loop L so the non-vacuous-acceptance row can be stated in loop-L verdict terms.** | It inverts §7.4's stated order and lets a lane with fabricated evidence reach loop L and spend a subagent budget before being caught. The row is instead restated in **handoff** terms — non-null `test_id` count — which is evaluable at 3.h against the frozen handoff, and the audit additionally **re-runs** at 3.j so the post-loop-L tip is covered too. |
| R35 | **Have the host `git checkout` the lane branch to run §7.4's acceptance re-run.** | Mid-stage that destroys the integration state the next merge depends on, and under Profile A it also discards the `node_modules` the lane's own `npm ci` produced. The re-run executes with **cwd = the lane workspace** under Profile A and on the lane branch inside `collect()` under Profile B, neither of which moves the host's HEAD. |
| R36 | **Have the probe make its throwaway commit on `--base-branch` after the probe workspace exists.** | Two defects at once: it writes to the branch §5.12 and §14.1 forbid the orchestrator from writing to, and it needs a mid-flight host→agent channel the dispatch model does not have. `02:183-187` makes the coordination unnecessary — a commit made after **session start** and before spawn is already outside the worktree's default HEAD while reachable in the shared object DB — so the sentinel goes on a throwaway branch before the spawn. |
| R37 | **Extend the `kiwi-planner` sidecar with `reads[]` so `shared-substrate` can stay a plan-time conflict edge.** | Not rejected on merit — it is the better mechanism, and it is **option (a) of X-04**, put to the user. It is not adopted unilaterally because it is a schema change to a skill this design does not own, imposed on **every** plan rather than only orchestrator runs, on top of the flag changes already imposed. Meanwhile the path-level 3.f″ check ships. *(Revision 2's count of six predates the scope cut; §10.5 step 9 carries the current arithmetic — seven flag changes across both targets, five of them in phase 1. The rejection is unaffected.)* |
| R38 | **Make `files-not-grounded` require plain existence, per revision 1's literal wording.** | It halts every greenfield lane, including §6.3's own worked example, whose purpose is creating two new files. The sidecar has no to-be-created marker (`kiwi-planner:744`), so the literal rule cannot distinguish a new file from a typo. The near-miss detector catches the failure §5.1 actually names (`task-catalogue.ts` for `task-catalog.ts`) and passes legitimate new files; `--strict-grounding` remains available for repositories that never create files inside a wave. |

**Rejected in revision 3.** Same purpose: each was a live option this round, and each is recorded so it is not re-proposed.

| # | Alternative | Why rejected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| R39 | **Keep the 3.f″ shared-substrate hoist and fix it by exempting `read_set`-with-intent-to-create from §6.2 layer 4.** | The exemption removes the one gate that makes a handoff's references resolvable, in order to admit a class that `write-set-overlap` has *already* forced same-lane at 3.e — a lane that intends to create a file carries it in `write_set`, and §6.2 layer 2 requires `write_set` to **equal** the computed write set. The predicate would still fire on nothing. Withdrawing it and saying so in X-04 is the honest answer; the coupling half, which is genuinely uncovered, is what 3.f″ keeps. |
| R40 | **Let the orchestrator implement the hoisted shared unit itself, serially and in-tree, as revision 2 specified.** | It is the only code in the design that would land on the integration branch with no lane branch, no plan Task, no claim audit, no `red_evidence`, no loop L and no promotion-set membership — invisible to loop P's two lane sub-denominators, which are drawn entirely from `lanes.lock.json`. Its first test execution would be the merge gate after the stage's *first lane merge*, so a stage whose lanes all fail leaves untested host-authored code on the branch. It also contradicts §1.2 (*"no fixer of its own"*) and D2. Re-partitioning the two lanes achieves the intent inside `computeLanePlan`'s existing vocabulary, with no executor at all. |
| R41 | **Keep §5.3's "a stage with fewer than 2 lanes runs serially in-tree, with no isolation cost".** | It gives a single-lane stage a *second* execution path with no claim audit — the only mechanical detector of `lane-srs-mutation-detected`, `lane-non-code-write` and `lane-lease-breach` — while §5.6's dirty-tree assertions are Profile-B-only, so under Profile A a mixed wave's one-lane stage had **no** isolation enforcement at all. It also contradicts profile immutability, the handoff's `isolation` field, and §5.6's `--serial` guarantee, which cites this very rule while promising the opposite. The saving is one worktree; the cost is a hole in charter C1 on a shape that occurs in most multi-stage waves. |
| R42 | **Keep `--commit-lane-work <pathspec-file>` and place the file inside 3.f′'s commit.** | It works, and it is still worse: it adds a fourth committed artifact per lane, a file format the document would have to specify (one path per line? git pathspec syntax? relative to what?), and a second source of truth for a set the handoff already carries and §6.2 layer 2 already pins by equality. pm receives `--handoff`; `write_set` is right there. Deleting the field removes a whole class of divergence between what a lane was told to write and what it was told to stage. |
| R43 | **Let the 3.j re-audit read only the remediation's manifest, since it describes the tip being merged.** | It fails R9: the remediation carries no `red_evidence` for the original acceptance criteria, so every remediated lane would report a green with no preceding red. Reading only the lane's manifest fails R2 symmetrically, because the fixer's paths are in the diff and not in `written_paths`. The **effective lane record** — union in round order — is the only reading under which both rows mean what they say. |
| R44 | **Reap the lane's workspace before 3.i so loop L's remediation can reuse the lane branch.** | It destroys the `node_modules` that workspace's own `npm ci` produced, which R11 needs at 3.h and 3.j (R35), and it discards the workspace before `collect()`'s harvest has landed under Profile A (§2.1 ordering rule 5). A distinct `-fix{r}` branch costs one ref and no state. |
| R45 | **Escalate `--auto` intake to `critical_gates[]` unilaterally rather than putting X-05 to the user.** | That is option (b) of X-05 and it may well be the right answer — but it is the user's to make: it removes the mode for the class of work the charter is about (R28), and `00.charter.md:10` makes the charter, not this document, the authority on its own sentence. Deciding it here would be the same silent redefinition §0.7 exists to prevent. |
| R46 | **Give the epilogue its own isolation profile value (`none`) so its handoff can declare it.** | `--serial` already taught this lesson: a third profile value is a second execution contract, and R33 records what that cost the claim audit. The epilogue is distinguished by `handoff_kind: "epilogue"`, which selects its executor, its audit row set and its layer set explicitly — one field, three consumers, no new profile. |

---

## 17. What this design is bad at

Stated plainly, because a proposal with no named weakness is a proposal that has not been examined.

> **Read this section against the phase split.** Rows 1, 2 and several below describe Part P2's
> machinery, and in `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` they are not weaknesses because the machinery is
> not there. They are retained unchanged — a phase-2 session needs them, and deleting a design's own
> criticism when the criticised part is postponed is how a weakness becomes a surprise. **The
> phase-1-specific weaknesses `08`'s cut introduces are new rows 19–21 at the end of this section.**
>
> Row 1's weakness in particular becomes the *whole* of phase 1: `08` says so directly —
> `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` *"delivers no wall-clock speedup"* (`08:118`).

1. **Under Profile B there is no wall-clock parallelism at all.** If the worktree probe fails, the
   user gets merge hygiene, lane-granular verification and lane-granular resume — and no speed. The
   charter asked for parallel worktrees; Profile B is a degraded answer, and the design says so at
   dispatch time rather than letting it be discovered at wave 2.

2. **Profile A's cost is real and grows linearly.** ~119 MB and ~4 s of `npm ci` per lane; at the
   cap of 8 that is ~950 MB, checked by probe assertion 8 — which §5.8 rule 3 now actually consults,
   having counted six assertions in revision 1 while eight were defined — rather than assumed. N concurrent
   `npm ci` runs sharing one Windows npm cache was **not tested** (`02:356-358`); the mitigation is
   `--install-concurrency`, default 2, which reduces the exposure without eliminating it. Likewise
   the identical empty `speckiwi-cli-init-*` directories in every worktree (`02` §5.6) have an
   unknown mechanism, so a worktree is not *provably* a pristine checkout of its commit.

3. **The design-item marking rule pushes work onto the design author.** Every normative sentence
   must be written as a `[D-nnn]` list row, in **English**. That is a real authoring burden, and the
   `unmarked-normative-prose` gate — a gate now, not a warning (§3.3) — will fire often on a first
   draft. The trade is deliberate: a warning's miss is silent and shrinks every downstream
   denominator, which is worse than an annoying gate.

4. **`P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` checks form, not correctness.** Mislabelling a `local-defect` as
   `out-of-run` satisfies the tool. The compensation — the next wave's loop P re-checks the previous
   wave's `out-of-run` items — is one wave late, and a run's last wave has no successor to catch it.

5. **`unknown-write-set` will fire more than anyone expects.** `kiwi-planner`'s `files[]` grounding
   is optional by default (`C17` runs only under `--check-files`), and `[INFERRED:` labels are
   normal output for greenfield tasks. Many first-attempt plans will serialise almost entirely, and
   the honest response is to improve the plan, not to pass `--allow-inferred-write-set`.

6. **Cross-lane duplicated implementation is detected weakly, and the *preventive* half is now
   provably absent rather than path-level.** Two lanes that each need the same helper cannot see each other. §7.9
   gives both halves a step, an artifact and a gate — the 3.f″ shared-substrate hoist and the
   serial-epilogue duplication audit — but: (a) near-duplicate detection by subagent is fuzzy and
   will miss non-obvious duplication; and (b) revision 1's *symbol*-level preventive rule was
   **uncomputable** — it quantified over lanes inside the function that computes lanes, over a
   handoff field authored two phases later, and over symbols no planner output carries
   (`kiwi-planner:744`). Revision 2's path-level replacement is **also** withdrawn in revision 3, and for a
   sharper reason than weakness: it could not fire. §6.2 layer 4 rejects the handoff that would
   trigger it one phase earlier, and any file two lanes each declare creating is already forced
   same-lane by `write-set-overlap` at 3.e — so the path-level preventive rule was **wholly
   subsumed**. What 3.f″ now runs is the cross-lane **coupling** check, which is real and uncovered
   elsewhere but prevents coupling, not duplication. So: at file level prevention is `write-set-overlap`
   and always was; at symbol level there is none and no surface carries a symbol; detection is the
   post-merge audit, which is fuzzy. Recovering the symbol level needs a planner schema change and is
   escalated as **X-04**. Each revision's statement here has been narrower than the last, which is the
   correct direction and is not a comfortable one.

7. **`files[]` is a prediction, and the whole lane model rests on it.** The claim audit turns a
   mis-predicted write set into a detectable halt rather than a wrong merge, which is the right
   failure mode, but it does not make the prediction better. A repository whose plans routinely
   mis-predict write sets will halt often. **Revision 0 claimed a compensation here that could not
   run** — it assigned the C17 path-existence check to `computeLanePlan` (which must be pure) and to
   a `--check-files` flag on a validator the CLI does not invoke. **Revision 1's replacement was
   narrower than it read**: `files-not-grounded` as *"does not exist and is not declared
   to-be-created"* had no mechanism for the second clause, because the sidecar has no such marker —
   read literally it refused every greenfield lane, including this document's own example. What
   actually ships (§5.1) is a **near-miss** detector: it catches a path that is a typo for a real
   file, and it does **not** catch a path that exists and is simply the wrong one, nor a genuinely
   missing path with no near neighbour. That is a real reduction in coverage against revision 1's
   claim, and it is the honest one.

8. **Semantic coupling with disjoint write sets is caught at the merge gate, not before.** The
   `read_set ∩ write_set` check is a heuristic over declared reads. The real detector is N−1 extra
   full-suite runs, which is the design's largest recurring cost.

9. **The executable surface is small relative to the prose surface.** Roughly 25 modules against
   **59** own gates — 39 of them declared in phase 1 (§13; `~52` was revision 3's table, before
   revision 4's six additions — **five** of those survive, `route-override-contradicted` having been
   withdrawn, §22 AA-08's `plan-coverage-unclosed` restores the sixth, and §22.12 AG-13's
   `invalid-run-scope-option` adds a seventh) — six loops, a closed
   classification enum and a phase flow. On this repository's
   own precedent that is a few hundred text assertions against a few dozen tested functions.
   Nothing tests that an agent obeys the resume card, appends the intent line **before** acting,
   spawns two verifiers rather than one, or refuses dismissal during cross-refutation. Revisions 1
   and 2 both made the ratio slightly worse, not better: each added more gates than modules.

10. **Layer 2 conformance is self-consistency for everything new.** The v1.4.0 examples and the code
    that reads them are authored by the same party in the same change. Only the pre-existing
    `waves-event.md §5` subset is genuine mutual checking, and even that is placeholder-laden.

11. **`--session-suffix` is a contract reversal in `kiwi-pm`, not an added option.** `pm.lock`'s
    stated purpose is preventing concurrent multi-plan execution (`:1146`). Making the session
    directory lane-scoped changes what that lock protects, and the change reaches §0.11, §2.3, §7.2,
    the artifact table, the spawn prompt, and `artifacts.ts`. It is the largest change this design
    imposes on a skill it does not own.

11a. **The *vacuous-test* class of untested acceptance is not mechanically closed.** §6.5 bounds
    `test_id: null` rows by count, requires attribution and a second-party verdict, forbids two
    acceptance rows sharing one `test_id`, and requires every non-null `test_id` to resolve — all
    mechanical. None of that distinguishes a real test from three distinct
    `expect(fn).toBeTypeOf("function")` blocks: the cap holds, the non-vacuous row holds,
    `red_evidence` holds (a trivial test genuinely fails before the module exists), and the
    integrator's re-run exits 0. Only loop L verifier 1's prose verdict stands between that and
    promotion. That is arguably correct under §1.1's boundary rule — test *substance* is not checkable
    against bytes on disk — but it means `00.charter.md:297-298` is discharged mechanically for the
    *no-test* class and by judgment for the *weak-test* class, and revision 1's §6.5 claimed all four
    closures were mechanical.

12. **A5 is a real bureaucracy load.** A 4-wave, 12-lane run writes 2 run-level prose documents,
    3 run-level locks, 12 per-wave prose documents, 8 per-wave locks, 12 handoffs, 12 manifests,
    ~90 journal lines and ~45 card rewrites. Eleven of the artifacts are generated and none of the
    journal writing is by hand, but a reader who counts files will find 37 of them, and for a
    two-lane wave that overhead is not repaid. That is what the refusal floor and `--serial` exist
    for, and they are a mitigation, not a cure.

13. **The `06` audit is resolved, and it moved this design more than expected.** It is no longer a
    blocker: `deferred-branch-audit-pending` is deleted and A1–A3 are closed (§0.4). But the honest
    record is that `06` **reversed** one of §0.4's own rulings (A2: "port" → "write fresh"),
    **excluded** a module §0.4 had listed as a harvest candidate (`trusted-git.ts`, pending Defect
    B), and **added two components** the design did not have (the run lock, readiness derivation).
    Roughly a fifth of the executable surface changed shape as a result. The residual weakness is
    that the harvest's §0.5 pre-trust verifications are real work — a cross-process lock test, 15
    attack cases re-run without `runTrustedGit`, 15 readiness cases re-run over a CLI snapshot, all
    of it under a non-trivial gitconfig from three cwds — and none of it is optional.

14. **This design's own `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` run will barely parallelise, and the design says so before it is
    discovered.** §10.5's work order puts step 6 (`skills/**`) and step 8 (three SKILL.md variants)
    inside CP-04 and step 7 (CLI + MCP) inside CP-03; CP-04 is now `orchestrator-only` (§5.9) and
    CP-03 is `exclusive-lane` with whole-wave uniqueness. Combined with `unknown-write-set` firing
    more than expected (§17.5), the first run of this skill on this repository degrades to near-total
    serialisation while `lanes.lock.json` still faithfully reports lanes and stages. That is not a
    defect in the partitioner; it is what the repository's own convergence surface costs, and it
    means the first real measurement of the parallelism benefit will come from a *different*
    repository than this one.

15. **Six things are user rulings, not design decisions, and the design cannot proceed past them
    on its own** (§0.7). **Two of the six are no longer open, and this row is retained, marked, for
    the other four:** X-01 is **closed by charter C3** (`00.charter.md:145-160`), which adopted the
    narrowed form this design proposed, while `08:128-129` retires the lane apparatus it governed
    into `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`; and `08` §2 **decided X-03 5-0** — the tie rung is dropped, a
    no-majority gate escalates to `critical` and halts, and `07` Q2 was re-decided and stays
    `leave-all-three`. X-01 (**granted**) decided whether multi-stage Profile A is legal at all; X-02 decides
    whether machine-generated run markers are exempt from `00.charter.md:302` (the design assumes
    **not**, and pays for it with git trailers); X-03 (**DECIDED by `08` §2**) decided whether the tie
    rung stands, and dragged
    `07` Q2 with it; X-04 decides whether the charter's no-duplication requirement gets a
    symbol-level preventive half at the cost of a planner schema change; **X-05 decides whether
    `--auto` may answer the charter's only named user interaction, which revision 2 recorded as a
    divergence rather than escalating**; **X-06 decides whether the charter's front-loaded-SRS
    compaction defence is amended or the SRS is front-loaded**. Each has a stated default
    and a stated cost, but a design whose frame depends on four open rulings (X-02, X-04, X-05, X-06)
    is less settled than its
    length suggests.

16. **`.agents/skills/**` had no sanctioned writer at all until §9.5 invented one.** Four-copy parity
    is asserted by E28 and required by §14 registration 5, while `speckiwi skills install` is
    forbidden against this repository and the mirror was maintained out of band. The fix —
    `speckiwi skills mirror --check | --write` — is new tooling this design must build in order to
    satisfy a test this design also writes, which is a circularity worth naming even though the
    alternative (hand-maintained four-copy parity) is worse.

17. **Five divergences from binding documents are recorded rather than resolved.**
    - *The charter's front-loaded-SRS defence is not implemented as written.* `00.charter.md:96-98`
      says *"The defence is front-loaded: **author the SRS carefully at the start**"*; this design
      defers SRS to per-wave 3.b and states *"Targets are not registered up front"*, substituting a
      front-loaded **design** document plus a ledger. The substitution is probably better — a design
      document survives compaction and a `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` SRS authored before decomposition would be
      re-authored anyway — but it **is** a divergence from a binding document, and the first revision
      did not record it as one. **This revision promotes it out of this register to §0.7 as X-06**,
      for the same reason X-05 was promoted: `00.charter.md:10` makes the charter the authority on its
      own sentence, and a register entry is a record rather than a disposition. It stays listed here
      so the register is complete; the disposition now lives in X-06.
    - *D3's "one numbered directory" is nested, not flat.* `00.charter.md:110` says artifacts share
      *"one numbered directory"*; this design nests unnumbered `design/`, `waves/` and `verify/`
      subtrees under it, departing from the settled decision's wording and from `docs/research/`'s
      existing flat-numbered convention. The reason is that a 4-wave 12-lane run produces ~37
      artifacts and a flat namespace makes `wave-2/lane-3`'s handoff indistinguishable from
      `wave-3/lane-2`'s by sort order. Harmless, deliberate, and now recorded.
    - *Under `--auto`, the charter's one named user interaction is answered by a committee.*
      `00.charter.md:54` is the single place the charter names the user in the intake section
      (*"ask the user questions where the design is still weak"*), and §7.2 lets `--auto` route those
      questions to a 3-member committee. Revision 1 labelled this *"a user-accepted degradation"*,
      which asserts a consent nobody gave — and did so in a design that requires user consent **even
      under `--auto`** for the strictly smaller act of deferring one wave-boundary issue (§8).
      **Revision 3 promotes it out of this register to §0.7 as X-05**, because
      `00.charter.md:10` makes the charter the authority on its own sentence and a register entry is
      a record rather than a disposition — four conflicts of equal or lesser weight already became
      user rulings, and this one did not. It stays listed here so the register is complete, but the
      disposition now lives in X-05, with the same shape as the other four: narrowest change, what
      ships meanwhile, consequence of a refusal. The three audit records (§7.2) are mandatory under
      either ruling.
    - *A fourth item, whose discharge `08`'s scope cut removed — superseded, retained for the record.*
      `00.charter.md:50` asks for *"all the way to merged, verified code"*, while §5.12 leaves the
      integration branch unmerged and forbids a PR. Revision 3 discharged this by noting that the
      charter's own detailed §3 requires **lanes** to merge after verification (`00.charter.md:64-67`),
      which §5.9 does. **That discharge does not hold in phase 1**: §5.9 is deferred (§P2 5.9) and
      **phase 1 performs no merge** (§5.14). The charter's *"merged"* is therefore satisfied in phase 1
      only by the run's commits landing on its own integration branch, and per-lane merge returns in
      `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`. The reduction is a real divergence in phase 1 and is recorded as
      one where the cut states it — the scope banner above and `08:145-150` — not softened here. The
      run's **terminus** is unaffected by the cut and remains a deliberate boundary consistent with
      `kiwi-pipeline:127` and §14 registration 2.
    - *A fifth item, new in this revision: one handoff per wave sits outside the charter's
      verify-and-improve loop.* `00.charter.md:60-62` states the loop as a property of handoff
      documents as a class — *"The handoff documents themselves go through a feedback loop of
      verification and improvement, like any other artifact."* The **epilogue handoff** is exempt from
      loop H (§5.13, §P2 5.13) and is the one document §5.2 routes every `srs-write` and
      `non-code-write-set` task into, i.e. charter C1's routed set, in every repository. It still gets
      all five `validateHandoff` layers and a freeze at 3.k, so the English gate, the reference gate
      and the untested-AC cap all fire on it; what it does not get is loop H's two verifiers —
      including verifier 2's per-null-row verdict, which §6.5 closure 3 makes load-bearing. The
      phase-1 reason is positional (loop H is scoped to 3.f; the document does not exist until 3.k),
      not the phase-2 executor argument, which does not discriminate in phase 1. Recorded, not
      discharged.

18. **`06`'s branch-disposition items are sequenced but not owned.** §10.5 steps 11 and 12 — tag and
    delete the branch after `06`'s **H1–H5** land (verified here by `HV-1`…`HV-5`, except `06`'s H5
    which is the out-of-scope safe-path fix; revision 1 spelled two different things `H5` and `H-5`
    and then gated an irreversible branch deletion on the ambiguity) — and write the missing
    `docs/next/` entry, are marked in the
    audit as user decisions (`06:487`). Step 11 depends on **H5**, which is deliberately outside this
    design's requirement set (§0.6), so the branch cannot be archived by completing `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` alone.
    That cross-dependency is real and is the kind of thing that gets lost. **Revision 4 makes it
    worse**: H4 now lands in `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` with `integrate()` (§10.5 step 4′), so
    branch deletion waits on a *second target* as well as on a fix outside this design.

*The three rows below are new in revision 4. They are the weaknesses `08`'s scope cut **introduces**,
not the ones it postpones.*

19. **Phase 1 has one witness where the design was built for two.** Three mechanisms lose their
    independent check, and each is marked where it stands: `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` falls back
    from a `git-ancestor` relation between two refs to a commit trailer written by the same party
    whose work it attests (§4.1); loop P's fifth denominator loses its claim-audit conjunct (§7.6);
    and §7.10's `verified` transition loses loop L's per-acceptance-row closure. Revision 1's headline
    claim — *"a lane's own success report is worth nothing … it re-derives every claim from
    artifacts"* — was this design's strongest verification property, and **phase 1 does not have it.**
    What phase 1 has instead is that the work is written in the tree it will live in, by a child under
    the host's own eye, which is the footing `kiwi-wave-master` runs on today. It is a real reduction
    and nothing else compensates for it.

20. **The partition artifact ships without its consumer, so its accuracy has no feedback loop.**
    `computeLanePlan`'s output is frozen, published, reviewed — and then walked serially. The
    mechanism by which partition quality was supposed to improve (the merge gate catching a coupling
    the classifier missed, recorded in the postmortem, fed forward as a `learned-coupling` edge) is in
    Part P2. Phase 1's postmortem is fed by the 3.f″ coupling check and by loop P (§5.14), neither of
    which can observe *"both units green in isolation, red combined"*, because nothing is ever
    combined. So the analysis `08:112-115` calls the user's most important step ships **with its
    accuracy unmeasured**, and the first real measurement arrives in phase 2. The 3.e′ human review
    stands in for it — and a human judging a partition is exactly the kind of judgment §1.1's boundary
    rule says a tool cannot check.

21. **Two gate ids were minted rather than reused, and both are debt if phase 2 lands.**
    `serial-unit-failed` is `epilogue-task-failed` with a wider subject, and `partition-review-unrecorded`
    exists because `08:96`'s word *"reviewed"* had no mechanism behind it. When phase 2 restores the
    lane executor there will be two gates for one predicate unless one is retired, and retiring a
    shipped `critical_gates[]` identifier costs a rename across three variants plus the FR-FLOW-061
    parity assertions, plus the `GateId` union and its parity assertion (§10.4). The alternative — reusing `epilogue-task-failed` for phase 1's only executor —
    would name the gate after a thing phase 1 does not have. Named, not solved.

---
## P2. Deferred to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` — concurrent lane execution

`08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md` §3 cuts **concurrent execution of any kind** out of
`2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator`, 5-0, on a measured finding distribution: of the 36–39 CRITICAL rows
raised across three revision rounds, 33–37 sit in the lane / worktree / merge / epilogue machinery,
rounds 2 and 3 produced defects **exclusively** there, 11 of 11 defects created by a revision's own
fixes were in that region, and nothing outside it regressed at all (`08:12-42`). The cut is a decision
about where the defects live, not a refutation of the design below.

**What this part is.** Every section `08` §3 lists under *what defers*, moved here **verbatim and
under its original section number**, so that

- every `§3.4`, `§3.5`, `§3.5a`, `§5.4`–`§5.9`, `§5.13`, `§7.3`–`§7.5` and `§7.7` cross-reference
  elsewhere in this document still resolves — now to a section this part marks as phase 2; and
- `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes` is built from this text rather than re-derived.

The moved sections keep the vocabulary they were written in (*"the lane's workspace"*, *"before
merge"*, *"at dispatch"*). That is deliberate: rewriting 1,600 lines of unshipped machinery into a
conditional voice would be a fourth revision of exactly the region `08` measured as the source of
92–95% of this design's critical defects.

**What is *not* here, and must not be moved here later.** The per-task parallelisability **analysis**
ships in `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` **in full**, as a frozen, published, reviewed artifact per
wave (`08:96-97`, `08:112-115`): §5.1's DAG read and the `normalizeTasks` extension, §5.2's closed
`conflict_reason` enum, §5.3's pure `computeLanePlan` with its nine declared inputs, stage layering,
`files[]` grounding, `learned-coupling` and the completeness invariant, §7.9 (a)'s cross-lane coupling
check, and the partition postmortem fed forward. Only the concurrent **execution** of that analysis's
output defers. The user's stated priority — *"dividing the tasks correctly at the start is the most
important step"* — is the half that ships.

**Status of everything below.** Not implemented, not authored into `docs/spec/`, and not in the
`2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` requirement set; its requirement rows are collected in §15.5, outside
the phase-1 authoring set. **The lane apparatus X-01 governed travels with it in its entirety**
(`08:128-129`), and X-01's own ruling was closed by charter C3 (`00.charter.md:145-160`) — which is
why §0.7 carries X-01 and X-03 as closed. §0.7's open set is X-02, X-04, X-05 and X-06.

**One thing the reader must carry in.** The sections below refer to phase-flow steps — 3.g dispatch,
3.g′ join, 3.h collect + claim audit, 3.i loop L, 3.j integrate, 3.j′ harvest-then-reap, and 3.k as a
lane epilogue — that §2.1 no longer contains. §2.1's *"What Phase 3 looked like before the scope cut"*
block names each of them and states where each re-enters when phase 2 is built.

---

### 3.4 The lane manifest

Written by the lane agent as its last act, **into its own workspace** (§3.5). It is the lane's claim,
and the claim is never trusted — §7.4 re-derives every field from git and from the integrator's own
test run.

```json
{
  "schema_version": "1.0.0",
  "run_id": "2026-08-02.speckiwi.v260",
  "wave": 2, "stage": 2, "lane": "lane-3",
  "workspace_path": ".claude/worktrees/agent-a76b7da46dc5cc54e",
  "base_sha": "e4f5a6b…", "head_sha": "7bd41f0…",
  "status": "complete",
  "intentionally_empty": false,
  "bootstrap_done_at": "2026-08-02T09:04:11Z",
  "heartbeat_at": "2026-08-02T09:41:02Z",
  "written_paths": [
    { "path": "src/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.ts", "before_sha256": null, "after_sha256": "…" },
    { "path": "test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts", "before_sha256": null, "after_sha256": "…" }
  ],
  "commits": ["3c1f0aa…", "7bd41f0…"],
  "commands_run": [{ "cmd": "npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts",
                     "exit_code": 0, "at": "2026-08-02T…Z" }],
  "acceptance_results": [{ "test": "test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts", "exit_code": 0 }],
  "red_evidence": [
    { "ac_id": "AC-1", "test_id": "test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts::assigns every task to exactly one lane",
      "command": "npx vitest run --no-file-parallelism test/core/orchestrator/lane-plan.test.ts",
      "exit_code": 1, "captured_failure": "Cannot find module '…/lane-plan.js'", "at": "2026-08-02T…Z" }
  ],
  "deferred_mutations_path": ".kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/lane-3/deferred-mutations.jsonl",
  "external_side_effects": [],
  "needs_user": null,
  "design_refuted": null,
  "finished_at": "2026-08-02T…Z"
}
```

`external_side_effects`, `written_paths`, `commits`, `red_evidence`, `intentionally_empty`,
`workspace_path` and `bootstrap_done_at` are **always present**; the empty array is a falsifiable
claim and an absent field is silence (`waves-event.md:90`). `intentionally_empty` was required by
three places in revision 1 — the `collect()` precondition (§5.4), claim-audit row **R1** (§7.4) and E17 —
and defined by none; when it is `true`, `## Escalation` requires a stated reason in the body, and the
audit reads that reason as part of row R1. **This field is the phase-2 carrier for that declaration.**
Phase 1 has no manifest (§3.1), and `serial-unit-failed` reads the same declaration from the unit's own
`docs/analysis/` bundle instead (§5.14, §13, §15.1 E51).

**`wave` is an integer everywhere.** Revision 1 wrote `"wave": "wave-2"` here and in §6.3 while §4.1's
card and §4.6's `w{w}-s{s}` glob used the integer, and §4.6 joins all four artifacts on that key. The
type is fixed as **int** in the manifest, the card, the lock files, the handoff front matter and every
journal payload; `wave-{n}` remains a **path** fragment only.

**Two other fields, and why they exist.** `workspace_path` is the lane's own record of where it ran —
the only self-reported copy of a value the host otherwise derives from `git worktree list --porcelain`
(§4.6), so a disagreement between them is itself evidence. `bootstrap_done_at` is written immediately
after the `npx vitest --version` assertion and is what makes `--install-concurrency` observable
(§5.5.3); without it the orchestrator could stagger dispatch by a clock but never by a signal.

**The schema is delivered to the lane, not assumed of it.** §6.1's front matter carries
`manifest_template` — this exact object with every value replaced by `null` — and §6.3's body carries
a `## Manifest` heading that names the always-present fields. Revision 1 required a cold agent to
produce the 22-field JSON above, with `before_sha256`/`after_sha256`, a `commands_run[]` log and a
`red_evidence[]` array, from a one-line spawn instruction, and then raised `lane-evidence-irreproducible`
(C, *"not committee-decidable even under `--auto`"*) on an honest lane that omitted a field it was
never shown.

**`red_evidence` is not new machinery — it is the one existing mechanical TDD proof, which the first
revision dropped.** `kiwi-coder` already captures `sidecar.tdd.red_evidence` with
`command` / `exit_code` / `captured_failure` / `timestamp` (`skills/claude/kiwi-coder/SKILL.md:220`,
`:374-386`). Nothing in the first revision's §7.4 checked it and the manifest had no field for it, so
the design threw away the only artifact that distinguishes test-first from test-after inside a lane.
§7.4 now verifies it against the lane's own `commands_run` (RV-20).

**The status enum, closed and complete — seven values:**

| `status` | Meaning | Route |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `complete` | the lane finished and claims success | §7.4 claim audit, then loop L |
| **`complete-unreported`** | **synthesised by `collect()`**, never written by a lane: the branch carries commits but no manifest survives (§5.4's workspace-gone path) | loop L's demotion path with the commits preserved |
| `dead` | the lane aborted itself (base mismatch, unfixable finding) | re-dispatch once (§7.3), then demote |
| `lease-breach-requested` | the lane needs to write outside its lease and stopped | serial epilogue or re-plan |
| **`needs-user`** | the lane's **child skill** hit a gate that halts regardless of `--auto` | 3.h gate `lane-child-gate-halted` (C); carries `{gate_id, payload}` (§5.4.1) |
| **`design-refuted`** | the lane found a `[D-nnn]` item unimplementable **as written** | 3.h gate `lane-design-refuted` (C); carries `{design_item_id, evidence}` (§8.1) |
| **`no-response`** | the lane produced no manifest before its deadline **and** its branch carries no commits | 3.h gate `lane-timeout` (C); §5.5.1 |

Four are additions since revision 1. `needs-user` and `no-response` were the two states a lane could
actually reach and could not express; `design-refuted` is the mid-wave intake for the "design found wrong" case (§8.1), which previously had no representable outcome at all; and
`complete-unreported` is `collect()`'s workspace-gone synthesis. **`complete-unreported` is listed
here, in the closed table `journal-schema.ts` encodes, rather than only in §5.4's prose** — revision 2
declared the enum *"closed and complete"* with six rows and then called `complete-unreported` *"a
sixth `status` value"* in §5.4, where it is the seventh and is absent from the table a schema author
copies from.

### 3.5 Where the lane's own bookkeeping lives — workspace-local, harvested, never committed

Four lane-written artifacts sit outside the committed tree, and the first revision left them
ambiguous. Stating them exactly:

| Artifact | Location | Committed by the lane? | How the host reads it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `w{n}-s{s}-l{k}.manifest.json` | **workspace-local**, at `{workspace}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}.manifest.json` | **No.** Never staged, never merged. | Resolved through §3.5a, then **copied** to the host run directory by `collect()`. Everything downstream reads the host copy. |
| `w{n}-s{s}-l{k}.heartbeat` | **workspace-local**, beside the manifest | **No.** | Same resolution; read live by the 3.g′ poller (§5.5.3). Not harvested — it is a liveness signal, not evidence. |
| `deferred-mutations.jsonl` | `{workspace}/.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/` — ignored at `.gitignore:20` | **No**, and it cannot be: `.kiwi/` is git-ignored. | Same resolution, then copied by `collect()`. Replay at 3.k reads the **host** copy (§2.1 ordering rule 5). |
| `decisions.jsonl` | beside `deferred-mutations.jsonl` | **No.** | Same resolution, then copied by `collect()`; replayed into `docs/analysis/kiwi-orchestrator-{run_id}/decisions/{wave}-{stage}-{lane}.jsonl` at 3.j (§5.4.1). Its path is named to the lane by the handoff's `decisions_path` front-matter field (§6.1) — revision 1 made the record mandatory under `07:169-170` without ever telling the lane where to write it. |
| a `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` line | **not written at all** | — | The **orchestrator** emits one host-root pipeline event per lane at integrate time, derived from the manifest, under the per-lane emit key `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}` (§5.9). |

**The workspace-local paths carry the full `w{n}-s{s}-l{k}` coordinate, not a bare `lane-{k}`.**
Revision 2 wrote `lanes/lane-{k}.manifest.json` and `lanes/lane-{k}.heartbeat` while the host harvest
path already carried the full coordinate. Under Profile B every stage shares one tree and lane ids
restart per stage (§4.1's card shows `wave-2/s2/lane-1`), so stage 2's `lane-3` would overwrite stage
1's leftover — and a stage-2 lane that dies before writing its manifest would make `collect()` step 2
read **stage 1's** manifest and report the wrong `head_sha` with a passing shape. The coordinate
costs nothing and removes the collision in both profiles.

#### 3.5a How the host resolves a lane's workspace — the key is the branch, not a recorded path

Revision 1 said the host reads the manifest *"using the `workspace_ref` recorded on the `dispatch-lane`
**intent** line"* while §5.5 declared that line's `workspace_ref` to be `null` under Profile A. Both
statements were true and together they resolved to nothing. The producer question — *how does the host
learn the runtime-assigned directory?* — was never answered, which also made `lane-possibly-live`
unreachable under the preferred profile.

**It is answered by `git worktree list --porcelain`, whose output pairs a path with a branch.** The
lane's first act (now in the spawn prompt itself, §5.5) is
`git checkout -B kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k} {base_sha}`, so from that moment the runtime's
worktree is the **only** worktree whose branch is that lane's branch. Resolution, in order:

1. `git worktree list --porcelain` at the host root; parse `worktree <path>` / `branch <ref>` pairs.
2. **Lane `k` owns a *family* of refs, not one ref**, and resolution matches the family:
   `refs/heads/kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}` (the lane branch) and
   `refs/heads/kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}-fix{r}` for each loop-L remediation round `r`
   (§7.3). A worktree on any member of the family is a workspace **of lane k**. This is a **derived**
   key, not a self-reported one, and it is available from the agent's first act.
   - `laneHeadRef(k)` — used by §4.6's `B(k)`, by §5.9 step 0 and by every `git-ancestor` proof — is
     the highest-numbered existing `-fix{r}` ref, else the lane branch.
   - The **family exists because git refuses otherwise.** Reproduced (git 2.48.1.windows.1, two
     worktrees over one `.git`): `git checkout -B <branch>` on a branch another worktree holds exits
     **128** with *"fatal: '<branch>' is already used by worktree at …"*. Revision 2's loop-L
     remediation checked out the **lane's own** branch while the lane's workspace still held it and
     was reaped only at 3.j′ — so the remediation agent's first command failed, it could not locate a
     `manifest_path` to report `dead`, and loop L's PASS precondition (*"no fix applied in that
     round"*) was unreachable in every round: cap exhaustion → `lane-verify-failed` (C) or demotion,
     **on every lane that needed any fix, under the preferred profile**. §7.3's mechanism is
     inoperative without the distinct branch.
3. A pair under the isolation root whose branch matches **no** ref family of the current run and
   which is not the host root is an **unattributed workspace**. During a stage with any unmatched
   `dispatch-lane` or `remediate-lane` intent, an unattributed workspace is treated as *possibly that
   agent, pre-rename* (`worktree-agent-<id>`, `02:173`) — see §4.6.
4. `dispatch(lane)`'s **result** line records the resolved path as `workspace_ref` once step 2
   succeeds, and the manifest's `workspace_path` records the agent's own view of it. The **intent**
   line carries `workspace_ref: null` and no reader depends on it. **The party that writes the
   result line is the 3.g′ poll loop** (§5.5.3), on its first pass in which branch-keyed resolution
   succeeds — revision 2 deferred the line until resolution succeeded and then assigned nobody to
   perform it, so §4.3's invariant read every just-dispatched lane as interrupted for the first
   seconds of every stage.

Under Profile B the workspace is the host tree and the branch is `workspace_ref`; steps 1–3 are a
no-op and step 2 reduces to `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`.

The manifest path moved out of `docs/research/{work}/` in this revision. In the first revision it was
`docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.manifest.json` — a path CP-07 declares
`orchestrator-only`, which §7.4's orchestrator-only row then fails **as a critical lease breach** on
every lane that writes it. Under Profile B it was worse: the lane wrote directly into the
orchestrator-only region of the host tree and, being uncommitted, dirtied the tree for the next
stage's dispatch-base check. Moving it into the workspace's own `kiwi/orchestrator/` tree makes it
uncommitted by construction, keeps it out of every convergence glob, and makes `collect()`'s
resolution **one of the isolation interface's two** profile-aware steps, beside `acquire()` — which
is stated in §5.4 rather than claimed away.

### 5.4 The isolation interface

Chosen once at Preflight P.6 by **probe**, never by assumption; recorded **inside** the resume card's
`frozen` block (§4.1) and in a journal `result` line; **immutable for the run**. A change request is
`isolation-profile-change-requested` (C).

**Why immutability must be *enforced*, and why the first revision's reason was the wrong one.** §5.4
said the profile is immutable *"because changing it mid-run would give lanes incompatible completion
proofs"*, while §5.6 said the opposite — *"the **same** proof kinds, so `computeResumeState` cannot
tell the profiles apart and needs no branch."* **§5.6's fact is right and §5.4's reason was wrong.**
Both profiles emit `git-ancestor` and `git-ref`, so a mid-run switch produces **no** proof mismatch
at all. That is a *stronger* argument for immutability, not a weaker one: the switch is
**undetectable**, and §4.6's four-way reconciliation would return `consistent` across it.

The reachable failure, in full: a resumed session re-probes (the `probe-isolation` journal line was a
convention, not a checked precondition), assertion 4 fails because this shell carries
`NODE_ENV=production`, §5.8 rule 4 downgrades to `branch-serial-lane` and writes it to the card;
journal, git refs, locks and card all agree; verdict `consistent`. From that point the C3 staleness
rule, the `npm ci` pin and the Windows path budget silently stop applying. Three changes close it:

1. `isolation_profile` (with `engine`, `work_root`, `run_root`, `base_branch`,
   `integration_branch`) moved **inside** `frozen`, so `invariant_digest` covers it (§4.1).
2. Profile immutability added to `resume-card.ts`'s validation list (§10.1) and to E12 (§15.1).
3. `probe-isolation` made a **checked precondition**: a run whose journal already carries a
   `probe-isolation` result line for this `run_id` **must not re-probe**; re-probing is
   `isolation-profile-change-requested` (C).

Five operations. Each returns a *proof*, and the proof kinds are identical across profiles, which is
what makes everything above this line profile-blind — **with exactly two stated exceptions**, marked
below.

| op | input | output proof | profile-blind? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `acquire(lane, base_sha)` | lane id, stage base | `workspace_ref` (opaque) + `base_sha` | **No — a no-op stub under Profile A** (§5.5) |
| `dispatch(lane)` | lane id, `base_sha`, **repo-relative** handoff path | child agent record + `intent` journal line | invocation differs per profile (§5.5, §5.6) |
| `collect(lane)` | lane id (workspace resolved per §3.5a) | `head_sha`, changed-file list, manifest, acceptance results, **host-harvested state** | **No — manifest resolution differs** (§3.5) |
| `integrate(lane)` | audited `head_sha`, `frozen.integration_branch` | `merge_sha` | yes |
| `release(lane)` | resolved workspace | released bool | lifecycle differs (§5.5.1) |

The first revision claimed all five were profile-blind. Three are; two are not, and pretending
otherwise is what left `collect()` with no defined way to read a Profile-A lane's manifest out of
`.claude/worktrees/agent-*/…`.

**`dispatch`'s handoff path is repo-relative, not workspace-absolute.** Revision 1's table said
*"handoff path **inside the workspace**"* while §5.5's spawn prompt passed
`docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md`. The repo-relative form is correct and is what
survives: the lane's cwd **is** its workspace root, and the handoff is committed into `base_sha`, so
the same string resolves in both profiles and needs no knowledge of the runtime's directory name.

**`collect(lane)` has four steps and the fourth is new.**

```
collect(lane):
  1. resolve the workspace per §3.5a; if it is gone, fall through to 1′
  1′ WORKSPACE-GONE PATH (the §11.4 drill's case, and revision 1 had none):
       head_sha := git rev-parse kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}
       if that branch is absent            -> status no-response  (lane-timeout, C)
       if a HOST-harvested manifest exists  -> use it
       else                                 -> synthesise a manifest with
                                               status="complete-unreported", written_paths derived
                                               from `git diff --name-only base..head`, and NO
                                               red_evidence and NO acceptance_results
       A synthesised manifest cannot satisfy §7.4's red-before-green or acceptance rows, so the lane
       goes to loop L's demotion path with its commits PRESERVED rather than being reaped as a
       timeout. Two commits of real work are never stranded by the absence of a manifest.
  2. head_sha, `git diff --name-only base..head`, read the manifest and the heartbeat
  3. run `verification_cmd` ONCE, with `--reporter=json`, cwd = the lane's workspace under
     Profile A or the host tree still on the lane branch under Profile B. That single run is
     BOTH the lane-level verification (§6.1) and §7.4 row R11's acceptance re-run — R11 reads
     the JSON report and asserts every non-null acceptance[].test_id is reported `passed`.
     Revision 2 ran these as two steps under Profile B and as neither under Profile A.
  4. HARVEST: copy the manifest, every remediation-{r}.manifest.json, deferred-mutations.jsonl,
     decisions.jsonl and docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-*/ to
     {git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/, then clean the workspace's
     orchestrator-only residue (§5.4.2). 3.j′ refuses to reap until this has happened
     (`lane-state-harvest-failed`, C).

There are FOUR numbered steps plus 1′ — 1, 1′, 2, 3, 4. Revision 2 referred to "steps 1–5
(through the harvest)" in §5.6, which named a step that does not exist; the harvest is step 4.

Under Profile B step 4 does NOT run here: it runs after loop L, because loop L creates the
evidence step 4 is supposed to harvest (§2.1 ordering rule 5, §5.6).
```

`complete-unreported` is the **seventh** `status` value, and it is in §3.4's closed table.

**The lane contract, enforced by the handoff schema and re-derived by the claim audit:**

- A lane produces **code and tests only**.
- **A lane MUST commit its work to its own branch.** This is a positive obligation, stated because
  the first revision had none — and because **both** skills that execute a lane decline to commit by
  contract: `kiwi-coder SKILL.md:176` (*"git commit 은 사용자 결정"*) and `:836`
  (*"| git commit / push | 사용자 결정 |"*), and `kiwi-pm` routes to `kiwi-commit-auto-push` only
  *after* it returns (`pipeline-event.md:100`). Without an explicit obligation the reachable outcome
  is: `collect()` finds `head_sha == base_sha`, `git diff --name-only base..head` is empty, §7.4 row
  **R2** compares it against a non-empty `written_paths` and raises `lane-lease-breach` on **every** lane
  — or, if the lane honestly reports an empty manifest, every audit row passes vacuously,
  `integrate()` merges an empty branch, `release()` drops the worktree, and the lane's entire output
  is destroyed **with a `git-ancestor` proof recorded as success**.
  - **Granularity:** one commit per plan Task, in task order, so `red_evidence` and the green commit
    are separable. A lane MAY squash within a Task.
  - **Pathspec — see §5.4.2.** Every lane commit stages an **explicit pathspec that *is* the
    handoff's `write_set`**. Never `git add -A`, never `git add .`, never `git commit -a`. This is
    what keeps a child skill's orchestrator-only writes out of `base..head`.
  - **Message form (X-02):** subject is plain descriptive English with no run id, wave, stage, lane
    or phase marker; the coordinates go in `Orch-Run` / `Orch-Wave` / `Orch-Stage` / `Orch-Lane` /
    `Orch-Task` trailers. No signature of any kind (`00.charter.md:302`).
  - **`collect()` precondition:** `head_sha != base_sha`, **or** the manifest declares
    `"intentionally_empty": true` with a stated reason. Neither ⇒ the lane is treated as dead.
  - **The obligation has a carrier, and revision 1 gave it none.** The two other lane behaviour
    changes each got a flag and a requirement (`--defer-srs-mutation`, S12; `--no-pipeline-emit`,
    S13) while the commit change got a sentence. `kiwi-coder SKILL.md:176` and `:836` make committing
    a **user decision**, and inside a lane there is no user — so per §5.4.1 rule 1 the honest outcome
    was `status: "needs-user"` → `lane-child-gate-halted` (C) on every lane, or no commit at all and a
    death by claim-audit row R1. The carrier is **`kiwi-pm --commit-lane-work`** (§15.3 **S16**,
    §14 item 11): pm commits each completed Task itself, staging **exactly the handoff's
    `write_set`**, with the trailers `commit_policy` names. It is one flag on a skill that already
    owns Task-completion bookkeeping (`kiwi-pm:24`, `:168`, `:353`, `:817`), and it supplies the user
    decision `kiwi-coder` correctly refuses to make for itself rather than overriding it.
  - **`--commit-lane-work` takes no argument, and this is a correction.** Revision 2 typed it
    `--commit-lane-work <pathspec-file>` and then gave the file two incompatible locations — *"beside
    the handoff"* (§5.4) and `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/lane-3.pathspec` (§6.3's worked
    example, which §6.1 called *"the file `--commit-lane-work` reads"*). The normative one **cannot
    exist inside a Profile A lane**: `kiwi/orchestrator/` is untracked and git-ignored by §4.8 policy
    1, so it is in no commit and therefore absent from a worktree cut at session-start HEAD
    (`02:170-179`: ignored state absent, `git status` empty) — and 3.f′ stages handoffs and the lane
    lock, not a `.pathspec`. pm would have read `commit_policy.pathspec_file`, got ENOENT, committed
    nothing, and `collect()` would find `head_sha == base_sha` → **R1 death on every lane**, which is
    the exact outcome this carrier exists to prevent, arriving through the carrier. pm already
    receives `--handoff`, which carries `write_set` — the third statement revision 2 made
    (*"`write_set` becomes the pathspec for `--commit-lane-work`"*) is the one that ships, and
    `commit_policy.pathspec_file` is deleted from the schema. See §16 R42.
- **No SRS mutation and no Requirement-ID allocation inside a lane.** All ids are allocated at Phase
  3.b, at the host root, serially, before any lane exists (charter C2). Violation:
  `lane-srs-mutation-detected` (C).
- **A lane emits no pipeline event.** See below.
- No `git push`, no PR, no `package.json` edit, no test weakening or deletion
  (`kiwi-coder §0.20.3`'s closed four-item list, `SKILL.md:47`), no write outside the declared
  write set.
- A lane that *needs* to write outside its lease stops and reports `lease-breach-requested` — a
  defined terminal state, not a failure.

#### 5.4.2 The lane's write pathspec, and the orchestrator-only residue a lane cannot avoid producing

This subsection exists because revision 1 constrained a lane commit's **granularity, message form and
trailers** and never its **pathspec**, and because the two child skills a lane must run write
orchestrator-only paths at three places by their own contracts. Revision 1 closed one of the three and
left two.

**The three residues, each verified.**

| Residue | Written by | Contract that makes it unavoidable | CP |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` | `kiwi-pm`, `kiwi-review-fix-loop` | `pipeline-event.md:120-123` resolves the directory as `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/kiwi`, which inside a Profile-A worktree is the **worktree** root | CP-07 |
| `docs/plans/{plan}.plan.md` checkbox | `kiwi-pm`, unconditionally on every completed Task | `kiwi-pm:24` (*"`plan.md` 체크박스 갱신 … 은 PM 중앙 집중 관리 책임으로 예외"*), `:168`, `:353`, `:817` | CP-07 |
| `docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-{run-id}/` | `kiwi-review-fix-loop`, on every run | `skills/claude/kiwi-review-fix-loop/SKILL.md:200`, `:634` | CP-07 |

Revision 1 suppressed the first with `--no-pipeline-emit` and left the other two. The plan checkbox is
worse than the pipeline line, because §6.3's own `forbidden[]` says *"Do not edit `docs/spec/`,
**`docs/plans/`**…"* — the handoff forbids the lane from doing what its own mandated executor does by
contract, and §5.9 still carried a *"plan checkbox updates → replayed through MCP
`workflow_task_check`"* bullet that only makes sense if the lane committed the edit it is forbidden to
make.

**The rule, which closes all three with one mechanism rather than three flags.**

> **A lane's commits stage an explicit pathspec drawn from `write_set`, and nothing else.**
> Orchestrator-only content a child skill writes inside the workspace is **left uncommitted**. It is
> therefore never in `git diff --name-only base..head`, never in the claim audit's denominator, never
> in a merge, and never on the integration branch. `collect()` step 4 harvests what is worth keeping
> and then removes the residue; the orchestrator replays the *semantic* effect at the host root.

Consequences, each stated because each was a live failure:

1. **The claim audit is unaffected and needs no widening — and `written_paths` means *committed*.**
   `written ⊆ declared` compares a committed-content diff against `write_set`; uncommitted residue is
   invisible to it. R26's rejection of "widen the audit" stands, and so does §7.4's orchestrator-only
   row — which now catches only a lane that *deliberately* staged such a path.
   **The manifest field is therefore defined as `written_paths` = *the paths this lane
   committed***, not the paths it touched, and §3.4 says so at the field. Revision 2 split "written"
   from "committed" here and left `written_paths` on the old side: an honest lane that ran
   `/kiwi-pm` — which updates the plan checkbox by contract (`kiwi-pm:24`) — would record
   `docs/plans/…plan.md` in `written_paths`, R2 would find it missing from the committed diff, and
   the lane would die on `lane-lease-breach` (C), *"not committee-decidable"*. The lane's only
   alternative was to under-report, which is precisely the falsification the manifest exists to make
   detectable. `before_sha256`/`after_sha256` are the digests of the committed content at
   `base_sha` and at `head_sha`. `forbidden[]`'s two lines are reconciled the same way (§6.3): *do
   not **commit** these paths* and *do not **write** any file outside `write_set` **except** what a
   child skill writes for you, which you leave uncommitted* — revision 2 stated the two as a flat
   contradiction one bullet apart.
2. **The Profile B dirty-tree assertions become path-scoped.** §5.6's `acquire`/`collect` assertions
   were `git status --porcelain` **empty**, which residue makes impossible. They are re-scoped to
   `git status --porcelain -- <write_set> <read_set>`: a lane may leave orchestrator-only residue and
   may not leave uncommitted code. `collect()` step 4 then reverts the residue
   (`git checkout -- <tracked residue>` plus `git clean -fd -- <untracked residue globs>`) **before**
   switching back, so lane 2 never inherits lane 1's plan-checkbox edit.
3. **`--no-pipeline-emit` is still wanted, and is still passed everywhere the lane invokes.** Not
   because the write would merge — it no longer can — but because a lane-local `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl`
   line is a *false* pipeline record the orchestrator would then have to distinguish from its own. It
   is passed to **`/kiwi-pm`** and to **`/kiwi-review-fix-loop`**, which are the two skills a lane
   invokes. §14 item 11 previously named `kiwi-pipeline`, a skill no lane invokes; that is corrected,
   and S13's list — `kiwi-pm` and `kiwi-review-fix-loop` — is the authoritative one.
4. **The plan checkbox is replayed, not committed, and the replay has an input.** The orchestrator
   calls `workflow_task_check` at the **host** root at integrate time. **The completed `task_id` set
   is derived from the lane's own commit trailers** — every lane commit carries `Orch-Task` by
   `commit_policy` (§6.1), so the set is
   `git log --format='%(trailers:key=Orch-Task,valueonly)' base..head`, deduplicated. That is
   externally recomputable, survives the reap, and needs nothing the harvest does not already have.
   Revision 2 derived it from *"the manifest's `commands_run` and the pm session state it
   harvests"*: `commands_run` is `{cmd, exit_code, at}` and carries no task id, §3.4's manifest has
   no completed-task field, and pm session state is **not** in §3.5's harvest list and is destroyed
   with the workspace at 3.j′ under Profile A — so the replay had no source at all. No
   `--no-plan-checkbox` flag is invented, because the edit is allowed to happen; it is simply never
   committed **by the lane**. The host-side edits it replays are committed by the orchestrator at 3.k
   (§4.8 policy 5), which is also what keeps `dispatch-base-dirty` evaluable (§2.1 ordering rule 1).
5. **`docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-*/` is harvested, not discarded — and under Profile B the
   harvest runs after loop L for exactly this reason.** It is loop L's evidence bundle, created by
   `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` on every run (`:200`, `:634`). `collect()` step 4 copies it to
   `docs/analysis/kiwi-orchestrator-{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/review-fix-loop/` at the **host**
   root, where it is committed by the orchestrator in the serial epilogue like any other
   orchestrator-owned artifact. Under Profile A the bundle sits in the remediation workspace and is
   harvested at 3.j′ before the reap; under Profile B it is created *during* loop L, so step 4 is
   ordered after it (§2.1 ordering rule 5, §5.6). Revision 2 harvested before loop L and then ran
   `git clean -fd` over the untracked residue globs, deleting the bundle it claimed to preserve.

**Why this beats the alternative of new suppression flags.** Suppressing three writes needs three
flags on two skills this design does not own, each of which must then be threaded through pm's spawn
prompt to coder; and every future child-skill write reopens the problem. A pathspec rule is one
sentence, is enforced by the party that already must be told what to stage
(`kiwi-pm --commit-lane-work`, §15.3 S16), and is closed under child skills the design has not seen.

**Why the lane emits no pipeline event — a contradiction resolved, not split.** The first revision
stated *"A lane's `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` line lands inside its own workspace and is dropped at merge,
then replayed at host root."* For a line to be *dropped at merge* it must be **in the lane's commit**
— and §7.4's orchestrator-only row makes any surviving orchestrator-only path a critical
`lane-lease-breach`, CP-07 marks `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` orchestrator-only, and §6.2 layer 2 requires
`write_set` to **equal** the lane's computed write set, which never contains it. Verified:
`kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` is the **only** tracked file under `kiwi/` (`git ls-files kiwi/`), and
`pipeline-event.md:120-123` resolves the directory as `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/kiwi`, which
inside a Profile-A worktree is the worktree root.

**The audit wins and this sentence is deleted.** Three grounds: (a) the lane contract's own first
line is *"a lane produces code and tests only"* and charter C1 says the same — a pipeline event is
neither; (b) the sentence's premise depends on the drop-at-merge mechanism, which §5.9 shows could
not execute at all, so widening the audit to tolerate the write would leave the line **permanently**
on the integration branch *and* replayed at the host root — duplicate events forever; (c) the
"replay" story existed only to undo a write that need not happen.

The mechanism: `/kiwi-pm` and `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` gain a `--no-pipeline-emit` pass-through
(§15.3 S13), the pathspec rule of §5.4.2 makes the write unmergeable in any case, and the
**orchestrator** emits one host-root pipeline event per lane at integrate time, derived from the
manifest. §7.4's *"three independent signals"* row loses its third signal as a consequence, and is
restated honestly rather than left claiming an independence it no longer has (§7.4).

This is not a workaround for the MCP root pin; it is the correct layering — a lane implements tasks,
the **run** owns requirement lifecycle, which is the same reason `kiwi-pm` centralises T-final
promotion (`skills/claude/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md:867`). It happens to neutralise C5 completely.

**Making the lane contract real requires six additive flags on existing skills** — this is the one
place the design changes a delegated skill's behaviour, and it is unavoidable. All six are
**`kiwi-pm` pass-throughs or `kiwi-pm` flags**, because `/kiwi-pm` is the only skill the lane invokes
directly (§5.5) and `kiwi-coder` is reached through pm's own spawn prompt (`kiwi-pm:400-409`).
`--commit-lane-work` (§15.3 S16) and `--no-pipeline-emit` (§15.3 S13) are stated above; the other
four are:

- **`kiwi-coder --defer-srs-mutation <queue_path>`, reached through `kiwi-pm --defer-srs-mutation
  <queue_path>`.** The pass-through half is the correction: revision 1 put the flag on `kiwi-coder`
  and then stated a dispatch invocation that runs only `/kiwi-pm` (§5.5), with no route from one to
  the other — S11 listed three pm flags and not this one, S12 covered coder only, and §14 item 11
  omitted it. The consequence was not cosmetic. `kiwi-coder §0.12`'s four mutations would execute; per
  charter C5 and `02:203` a worktree agent's MCP calls land at the **host** root; so `docs/spec/`
  would be mutated mid-wave from inside a lane. §7.4's SRS row checks
  *"`deferred-mutations.jsonl` contains only the four calls … `docs/spec/` untouched **in the
  delta**"* — the queue file would be **absent** (vacuously satisfying the first clause) and the
  lane's git delta clean (the write went to a different tree), so the audit **passes** while C1 and C2
  are violated silently, by exactly the Class-C mechanism §5.9 exists to prevent. Two closures:
  `kiwi-pm` forwards the flag verbatim into its `kiwi-coder` spawn prompt (§15.3 S11), and §7.4's SRS
  row gains a **host-side** conjunct — `git status --porcelain -- docs/spec/` at the **host root**
  must be unchanged across the stage, and `list_requirements` for the wave's target must return the
  3.b allocation set unchanged. A lane-caused host mutation is then detectable where it actually
  lands.

  `kiwi-coder §0.12`
  (`SKILL.md:29`) makes four MCP mutations mandatory per Task — `add_trace_link`,
  `add_verification_evidence(type=test)`, `update_status`, `add_completed_work` — and already
  records each into `state.json mcp_call_log[]` for idempotent dedupe. The flag changes exactly one
  thing: **record but do not call.** The queue format is the existing `mcp_call_log[]` schema; the
  orchestrator replays at host root and dedupes by the same key. Nothing new is invented.
  - **The dedupe key is `args_hash = sha1(canonicalJson(args))` — the tool name is *not* hashed.**
    Verified at `kiwi-coder SKILL.md:563`:
    `{tool, args, args_hash: sha1(canonicalJson(args)), ok, response_hash, dry_run, called_at}`.
    The first revision wrote `sha1(call | canonicalJson(args))`, which would have had `replay.ts`
    compute keys with the tool name included while `deferred-mutations.jsonl` carried keys without
    it: **every dedupe lookup misses**. Since §4.4 declares `replay-deferred-mutations`
    `idempotent-by-key` on exactly that basis, a session resuming after `integrate-lane` would
    re-apply `add_verification_evidence` and `add_completed_work` for every lane, duplicating
    evidence rows in `docs/spec/` and the completed-work log. E18 would have encoded the wrong
    formula.
  - **Disambiguation is the replayer's job, because the key is not tool-unique.** Two different
    tools can produce identical `canonicalJson(args)` — `update_status` and `add_completed_work`
    share an `{id, target}` shape. `replayDeferredMutations` therefore dedupes on the pair
    `(tool, args_hash)` while **matching** against the recorded `args_hash` for idempotency; the
    queue entry already carries `tool` beside `args_hash`, so no format change is needed and the
    existing key stays byte-compatible with `kiwi-coder`'s.
- **`kiwi-pm --session-suffix <lane>` and `--no-final`.** `kiwi-pm`'s worklog race-freedom is
  explicitly conditioned on sequential spawn (`SKILL.md:194`: *"worklog.jsonl 만 양쪽이 append
  (race 없음 — sequential spawn)"*), and `.kiwi/sessions/{run_id}/` holds `pm-state.json`,
  `pm.lock`, `worklog.jsonl`, `state.json` and `reports/` (`:164-170`) keyed by the plan's own
  `run_id` (`:181`). `--session-suffix` moves the whole directory to
  `.kiwi/sessions/{plan_run_id}/lanes/{lane}/`, restoring **per-lane ownership of every one of
  those files** — so the race-freedom precondition is *discharged*, not weakened, and pm's deferred
  v0.2 "parallel Task spawn, race safety pre-verification required" (`:1145`, `:1154`) is
  deliberately **not** implemented. `--no-final` skips T-final REQ promotion, because a REQ spans
  lanes and its `all_done` denominator would be the lane's task subset, which is wrong.
- **`kiwi-pm --handoff <path>`, and what it actually means.** Revision 1 defined it only as
  *"taking the lane's handoff document as its input SSOT"*, which is not a definition an implementer
  can build: the handoff is not a plan, carries no Task bodies, no `tdd`, no `acceptance_tests`, no
  `dod` and no `rollback`. Worse, `kiwi-pm` executes **every** task in the sidecar in declaration
  order and its only selector is `--from-task`, a *start point* — so two lanes dispatched
  concurrently against the same `plan_path` would each run all of the plan's tasks, each write every
  file in it, and each trip `lane-lease-breach`. The exact semantics:

  > `--handoff <path>` supplies the **execution set and the lease**, never the Task bodies. pm reads
  > the front matter's `task_ids[]` and executes **exactly those** tasks, in sidecar declaration
  > order, taking each Task's body from `sidecar_path` as it does today. `write_set` becomes the
  > pathspec for `--commit-lane-work`. `req_ids[]` and `acceptance[]` are read-only context passed
  > into the coder spawn prompt. `--from-task` is refused together with `--handoff`.
  >
  > **`depends_on_task` is evaluated within the execution set only.** A predecessor outside
  > `task_ids[]` is treated as **satisfied**, because by construction it is either in an earlier stage
  > (already merged into `base_sha`, §2.1 ordering rule 2) or in the same lane (same connected
  > component, §5.3). pm's `depends-on-violation` gate (`kiwi-pm:92`) therefore does not fire on
  > out-of-lane predecessors; a violation *inside* the set is still a gate.

  `task_ids[]` is generally **non-contiguous** — it is a connected component of the conflict graph —
  which is precisely why a start-point selector cannot express it.

The full cost of `--session-suffix` is declared, not understated: it touches `kiwi-pm` §0.11
(`:30`), the §2.3 lock lifecycle (`:165`), §7.2 recovery, the artifact table (`:164-170`), and the
`RUN_ID=` line in the spawn prompt (`:409`) from which `kiwi-coder` derives its own `.kiwi/` paths.
It additionally requires `src/core/workflow/artifacts.ts` to recognise the suffixed layout — the
canonical-path bonus and the `/^\.kiwi\/sessions\/([^/]+)\//` run-id derivation both assume the flat
form today, so `workflow_latest_artifact` / `workflow_resolve_artifact` / `workflow_session_status`
would otherwise mis-resolve lane artifacts. That is one of the SRS items in §15.

#### 5.4.1 How `--auto` crosses into a lane, and where the lane's decisions land

A lane runs child skills that have their own gates, so `--auto` cannot simply be forwarded and
forgotten. Three rules:

1. **A child's `critical_gates[]` gate always produces `needs-user`, never a lane-local decision.**
   `kiwi-pm SKILL.md:101` inherits child critical gates by **general rule** and HALTs regardless of
   `--auto`; §13 adopts the same FR-FLOW-066 general-inheritance rule for the orchestrator. Inside a
   lane there is no user to halt for, so the lane writes `status: "needs-user"` with
   `{gate_id, payload}` and stops. 3.h raises `lane-child-gate-halted` (C) at the host, where a user
   exists.
2. **`--auto` *is* forwarded for non-critical severities, and every committee decision made inside a
   lane is written to the host root.** `07:169-170` requires a decision audit record for
   *every* autonomous decision — *"the decisions that received no deliberation are precisely the ones
   that leave no evidence"* — and a decision log stranded inside a worktree that `release()` then
   deletes is exactly no evidence. The lane appends each record to the path the handoff's
   **`decisions_path`** front-matter field names (§6.1) — a sibling of `deferred_mutations_path` —
   which `collect()` **harvests to the host run directory** (§3.5) and the orchestrator replays into
   `docs/analysis/kiwi-orchestrator-{run_id}/decisions/{wave}-{stage}-{lane}.jsonl` at integrate time,
   alongside the pipeline event. Revision 1 made the record mandatory and gave the lane no field
   naming where to write it, in a document whose §6 declares the handoff to be the lane's *complete*
   input — so the obligation was unreachable by the party bound to it.
3. **`--auto` is never forwarded to a lane silently.** The handoff's front matter carries the
   effective `--auto` state, so the lane's own record and the host's record agree, and a resumed
   session can tell whether a lane ran attended or not.

### 5.5 Profile A — `worktree` (preferred)

The **only validated mechanism** is the agent runtime's own `isolation: "worktree"` agent (report
`02` §5.1): it creates `<repo>/.claude/worktrees/agent-<id>` on branch `worktree-agent-<id>`, at
**session-start HEAD**, with `node_modules` absent, sharing the parent's MCP connection. The
orchestrator does **not** issue `git worktree add` itself; a skill cannot make a subagent work
inside a directory the skill created.

**`acquire()` is a no-op stub under Profile A, and this is not a detail.** §5.4 orders
`acquire(lane, base_sha)` → `dispatch(lane)`, and the first revision put the pinned install inside
`acquire`. But under Profile A the workspace **does not exist** until `dispatch` — the runtime
creates it at spawn. So `acquire` had nothing to act on, `workspace_ref` could not be recorded on the
write-ahead `intent` line, and the install had to be performed by the lane agent — which the handoff
schema had no field for, whose required body headings contained no setup section, and whose worked
example never ran `npm ci`. `02:342-352` measured that without the pin `npm ci` omits devDependencies
and **exits 0**, so every Profile A lane would have run its `verification_cmd` with no vitest
installed. Under Profile A:

```
acquire(lane, base_sha) : no-op. Returns {workspace_ref: null, base_sha}. The intent line records
                          workspace_ref=null; no reader depends on it. The dispatch RESULT line
                          records the path once §3.5a's branch-keyed resolution finds it.
```

and the install moves into the handoff, where the agent that must perform it can read it — as
mandatory `bootstrap` front matter plus a mandatory `## Setup` body heading (§6.1). **The `checkout`
step does not move into the handoff; it moves into the spawn prompt.** See below.

Three measured facts and how each is handled:

**C3 — the worktree is cut at session-start HEAD and sees no uncommitted work.** This is the
constraint that kills naive designs, and its exact scope is under user ruling — see **X-01 (§0.7)**,
which proposes the narrowest charter amendment and states what dies if the user declines. On the
narrowed reading the worktree shares the repository's object database, so any **committed** sha is
reachable; `02:185` measured exactly that, computing `git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD 031c4ec`
**from inside** the worktree, which requires `031c4ec` to resolve there. The lane agent's **first
acts** are therefore fixed, and they run **before** the install because `package-lock.json` differs
between session-start HEAD and `base_sha`:

```
git checkout -B kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k} {base_sha}   <- IN THE SPAWN PROMPT
git rev-parse HEAD                       # must equal {base_sha}; if not, abort with status=dead
   posix:   NODE_ENV=development npm ci --ignore-scripts    # AFTER the checkout, never before
   windows: $env:NODE_ENV="development"; npm ci --ignore-scripts
npx vitest --version                     # must succeed; otherwise abort with status=dead
```
