**Both shell forms are given, because this host runs PowerShell.** `NODE_ENV=development cmd` is a
POSIX env-var prefix and a **parse error** in PowerShell. Revision 2 rendered the bare POSIX form in
`bootstrap` — a field it defines as *"commands the lane executes verbatim"* — and in probe assertion
4, while `verification_cmd` is `{posix, windows}` precisely because a bare command is not portable
(`kiwi-planner:512`). The consequence was shell-dependent rather than certain (`02:338-342` measured
the command succeeding on this host through a bash shim), which is exactly why the field needs the
two-variant shape the design already uses elsewhere rather than a hope.

**The first two lines are in the spawn prompt, not in the handoff, and getting this wrong made the
whole profile unrunnable.** Revision 1's spawn prompt was *"Read
`docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md` and follow it exactly. Perform its `## Setup`
section first."* — while the handoff is committed at **3.f′**, i.e. mid-session, and charter C3 /
`02:183-187` put the worktree at **session-start HEAD**. So at spawn that path does not exist in the
lane's checkout. The only act that makes it readable is the `checkout` to `base_sha`, and revision 1
put that act inside the file that cannot be read until it happens. The agent's first tool call would
be an ENOENT; it could not even locate `manifest_path` to report `dead`; `collect()` would find no
manifest and no commits → `no-response` → `lane-timeout` (C) on **every** lane of **every** Profile A
run. The circular dependency is broken by putting the two lines the orchestrator already knows at
dispatch time — the branch name and `base_sha` — into the prompt, and leaving `## Setup` to cover the
install and the assertions, which is what it was for.

Phase **3.d** commits the wave inputs — wave design, excerpt, plan, sidecar, constraints, registry —
and Phase **3.f′** commits the stage's lane artifacts: the handoffs, the lane lock and any substrate
hoisted at 3.f″. `base_sha` is 3.f′'s sha, and 3.f′ descends from 3.d, so a lane at `base_sha` sees
both sets. (Revision 1 attributed all of it to 3.f′, which contradicted the split RV-04 introduced;
the effect was harmless — 3.f′ is a descendant — but the sentence was wrong.) Uncommitted host work is
invisible to lanes, full stop, and the skill says so.

**C7 — `node_modules` is absent and this shell carries `NODE_ENV=production`.** The pin above is
mandatory and the `npx vitest --version` check is what converts a silent failure into a loud one:
`02:342-352` measured 23 MB / 3,508 files / no vitest / **exit 0** under `NODE_ENV=production`,
against 119 MB / 7,507 files / 4 s with the pin. At the lane cap of 8 that is ~950 MB, stated in the
run header before dispatch. **Install concurrency is serialised**: `acquire()` being a no-op means
the installs happen inside N concurrently dispatched lanes, and N concurrent `npm ci` against one
shared Windows npm cache was **not tested** (`02:356-358`). The orchestrator therefore staggers
dispatch by a stated interval so at most `--install-concurrency` (default **2**) lanes are installing
at once; this is a one-line mitigation for an admitted-untested case and it costs a few seconds.

**Windows path budget.** Measured worst case is **188 of 260** characters with `core.longpaths`
unset, and the 188 is driven by `node_modules` at 116 characters, not by the longest **tracked** path
at 108 (`02:309-319`), against a runtime worktree prefix of 72. The design does **not** choose the
prefix — the runtime does — so the budget is unchanged from the measurement, and the probe verifies
it empirically. The first revision's assertion 5 created the longest *tracked* path, which measures
the **wrong extremum**; it is corrected below and reordered to run **after** the install.

**The probe** — this is the part every earlier proposal got wrong by testing the wrong properties.
One probe lane, created and released before any real work, whose result is journalled as
`probe-isolation`; a run whose journal already carries that line for this `run_id` **must not
re-probe** (§5.4).

| # | Probe assertion | Which constraint it tests |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | the agent's `pwd` equals its git toplevel, and both are under `.claude/worktrees/` | topology |
| 2 | **before spawning the probe, the host commits a sentinel onto a throwaway branch `kiwi/orch/probe/{run_id}` created off `--base-branch` — never onto the base branch itself.** The probe then runs `git checkout -B probe/x <that sentinel sha>`, asserts `git rev-parse HEAD == <that sha>`, and asserts the sentinel file is present in its tree | **C3 — the decisive one, now actually exercised** |
| 3 | a write inside the worktree succeeds and `git status --porcelain` shows it | file axis |
| 4 | the host's shell variant of the pinned install — posix `NODE_ENV=development npm ci --ignore-scripts`, windows `$env:NODE_ENV="development"; npm ci --ignore-scripts` — exits 0 **and** `npx vitest --version` succeeds | **C7** |
| 5 | *(after 4)* creating a path of the measured worst-case length — 188 characters, i.e. the `node_modules` extremum, not the tracked one — succeeds inside the worktree | **C7 path budget** |
| 6 | `mcp_workspace_info.workspaceRoot ≠ git toplevel` — the split-brain is **expected**, and its presence confirms the lane contract is necessary rather than optional | C5 |
| 7 | **the isolation root is git-ignored at the host**: `git check-ignore .claude/worktrees` exits 0. On failure, §5.5.2's remediation runs **once** and the assertion is re-evaluated | §5.5.2 |
| 8 | **free disk ≥ `--lanes N` × 119 MB × 1.3** at the isolation root | §17.2, `02` §7.5 |

The host deletes `kiwi/orch/probe/{run_id}` and the probe workspace when the probe ends, in
`abort-run`'s reap path as well as the success path.

**Assertion 2's procedure needed rewriting twice, and this is the executable form.** Revision 1's
version tested a sha that necessarily *predated* the probe worktree, which is not the disputed case.
Revision 2's first attempt — *"the host makes a throwaway commit **after** the probe workspace
exists"* — fixed that and created two new defects: it wrote to the branch §5.12 and §14.1 forbid the
orchestrator from writing to, and it required a **mid-flight synchronisation channel** between host
and probe agent that this design does not have (dispatch is fire-and-forget with the manifest as the
only return channel, §5.5). The form above needs neither. `02:183-187` establishes that the worktree
is cut at **session-start HEAD**, so a commit made *after session start and before spawn* is already
outside the worktree's default HEAD while being reachable in the shared object database — which is
precisely the property under dispute. Committing it to a throwaway branch keeps `--base-branch`
untouched. The probe therefore runs entirely after the sha exists, with no coordination at all.

**What assertion 2 decides, restated — the first revision's rationale was wrong in both directions.**
It said assertion 2 is *"the one that decides Profile A"* because *"a lane pinned at a stale HEAD
would still merge cleanly and still produce a valid `git-ancestor` proof, so nothing downstream can
detect it."* Three detectors in fact exist: §7.4's `git diff --name-only base..head` against the
*declared* base surfaces every intervening path as outside `write_set` → `lane-lease-breach`; the
lane's own second act aborts with `status=dead` when `git rev-parse HEAD != base_sha`; and §4.6's
ancestry check compares against `frozen.integration_branch`. And the probe as written could not test
the dangerous case anyway: it ran at Preflight P.6, *"created and released before any real work"*, so
the `{base_sha}` available to it necessarily **predated** the probe worktree — the case that matters
is a sha created **after** the worktree existed. The corrected procedure creates that sha
deliberately.

The honest statement of what assertion 2 decides: **whether lane isolation under Profile A is
possible at all — not merely whether stages ≥ 2 are.** Revision 2 wrote *"stages ≥ 2 cannot use
Profile A"*, which understates it in the same direction §0.7 X-01 did. Every artifact a lane reads is
committed mid-session: the wave inputs at 3.d and the lane's **own handoff** at 3.f′. If a worktree
cannot reach a commit made after session start, a **stage-1** lane cannot read its own handoff either
— it ENOENTs on its first tool call and aborts on its second act. So assertion 2's failure kills
Profile A entirely and every stage falls back to Profile B, which is what §5.10's C3 row has always
said. That is exactly the question X-01 puts to the user, and the probe is the empirical half of that
answer.

**Profile A's `dispatch(lane)`, stated exactly — symmetrically with Profile B.** The first revision
gave Profile B's invocation and left Profile A's, the *preferred* profile, unstated; §5.4's table
said only *"child agent record"*. Meanwhile the six flags this design invents
(`--session-suffix`, `--no-final`, `--handoff`, `--defer-srs-mutation`, `--no-pipeline-emit`,
`--commit-lane-work`) and §7.3's in-workspace `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` all presuppose that
`kiwi-pm` / `kiwi-coder` run **inside** the lane, while
§6's *"the lane sees this document, the plan, the sidecar and the repository at `base_sha`, and
nothing else"* reads as a bare agent with no skill invocation at all. An implementer would have built
one of two incompatible things.

```
dispatch(lane) : spawn ONE agent with isolation: "worktree", whose entire prompt is:

    FIRST, before reading any file, run exactly these two commands:
      git checkout -B kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k} {base_sha}
      git rev-parse HEAD          # if this is not {base_sha}, stop and report "base mismatch"

    Then read docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md and follow it exactly.
    Perform its `## Setup` section first, in order.
    Then run:
      /kiwi-pm --session-suffix {lane} --no-final --no-pipeline-emit
               --defer-srs-mutation {deferred_mutations_path}
               --commit-lane-work
               --handoff docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md
    Write the manifest named by `manifest_path` in that document as your last act, using the
    `manifest_template` in its front matter.
    Your final text response is ignored; the manifest is the contract.
```

**The checkout is in the prompt because the handoff is unreadable until it has run** — see the C3
paragraph above. `{run_id}`, `{n}`, `{s}`, `{k}` and `{base_sha}` are all known to the orchestrator at
dispatch time, so nothing is inferred. If the `rev-parse` disagrees the agent stops **without** a
manifest, and §4.6 classifies the lane by liveness and branch state, not by a manifest it could not
write.

Five properties this fixes: the handoff path is passed **by path, not inline**, so it survives
compaction inside the lane and is readable at `base_sha`; the checkout precedes the read, so the path
resolves; the child's return channel is the **manifest**, not the agent's final text, so `collect()`
has a machine-readable contract rather than prose to parse; `/kiwi-pm` is named explicitly, so §7.3's
remediation and §5.4's six flags all have a stated host and a stated route; and the manifest's own
schema arrives with the handoff (`manifest_template`, §6.1) instead of being assumed of a cold agent.
§6's *"complete input"* sentence is re-checked against this: the handoff, the plan, the sidecar and
the repository at `base_sha` **are** the complete input, and the spawn prompt above adds only the
branch name and the base sha. The branch name is also in the front matter (`branch`); **the base sha
is not, and cannot be** — §2.1 ordering rule 2 shows it is a fixed point, since the handoff lives
inside the commit whose sha it would name. Revision 2's sentence here claimed *"both of which are
also written in the handoff's front matter"*; the second half is struck. The prompt is therefore a
bootstrap **and** the sole carrier of one value, which is stated rather than glossed: the handoff
remains the complete input for everything the lane must *do*, and the prompt supplies the one
coordinate no file inside `base_sha` can hold.

`integrate()` and `release()` run at the **host root**, never in the lane. The **acceptance re-run**
of §7.4's last row is the one host-executed command whose cwd is the lane workspace — the host may
run a process in that directory; what a skill cannot do is make a *subagent* work there (§5.5's
opening sentence), and the re-run is not a subagent.

#### 5.5.1 Lane lifecycle — deadline, liveness, reap

The first revision had none of this: "timeout", "deadline", "heartbeat", "orphan" and "reap" appeared
nowhere, `release(lane)` was declared but no phase step called it, and `status` had no value for a
lane that never answers. Three concrete consequences, all reachable here: up to 8 background agents
keep running after an interrupt; `git worktree list` shows six stale worktrees on this checkout right
now; and a resumed session reading a `dispatch-lane` intent-without-result would re-dispatch and put
**two** agents on one branch.

| Mechanism | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| **Deadline** | Every lane carries `deadline_at` on its `dispatch-lane` intent line and in the card's `open[]`. Default `--lane-deadline` **90 min**, bounded by the run budget (§14.1). Exceeded ⇒ `lane-timeout` (C) and manifest status `no-response`. |
| **Liveness** | The lane rewrites a **separate one-line heartbeat file**, `{workspace}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}.heartbeat`, at each Task boundary and immediately after `bootstrap`. Liveness is `mtime(heartbeat) > now − 3 × --poll-interval` **OR** the workspace's `locked` state in `git worktree list --porcelain` — a live agent holds the lock (`02:356-358`). Commit presence is **never** a liveness signal. **The file is separate from the manifest deliberately:** revision 1 put `heartbeat_at` *in the manifest* while §3.4 and the spawn prompt both make the manifest *"your last act"*, so the during-run liveness signal first existed when the lane finished — the one moment it is not needed. `heartbeat_at` survives in the manifest as the lane's own final record; the file is what the 3.g′ poller reads. |
| **Heartbeat format** | **One line of JSON, rewritten in full — never appended to:** `{"lane":"lane-3","wave":2,"stage":2,"at":"<ISO-8601>","bootstrap_done_at":"<ISO-8601>"\|null,"task_id":"<the Task in progress>"\|null}`. Revision 2 left the format unspecified while §5.5.3 item 4 makes dispatch stagger depend on the **host parsing `bootstrap_done_at` out of it** — so a lane writing a bare ISO timestamp and a host expecting JSON means `--install-concurrency` never releases and dispatch falls back to the `--install-stagger` floor forever, silently. The file is small, atomic to write, and its `mtime` is the liveness signal; the JSON body is what makes the stagger a *signal* rather than a *guess*. |
| **Reap** | `release-lane` runs at **3.j′** for every lane of the stage — **for the lane workspace and every remediation workspace of that lane (§7.3)**, and **after `collect()`'s harvest step has landed the state at the host** (§2.1 ordering rule 5) — and at every `halt` / `abort-run`. It is lifecycle-aware in both directions: `git worktree remove` **fails on a locked (running)** worktree, and the runtime **auto-removes** a worktree whose agent changed nothing (`02:209-211`) — so "absent" is not proof of a clean release, and a lane that did nothing has **no workspace for `collect()` to read**. `collect()` treats an absent workspace **with no branch** as `no-response`; an absent workspace **with a branch carrying commits** takes the workspace-gone path of §5.4 and its commits are preserved. |
| **Reap that fails** | **Stated, because revision 2 recorded that `git worktree remove` fails on a locked worktree and then gave neither 3.j′ nor `abort-run` an outcome for it.** `release-lane` without `--force` on a locked workspace does not retry and does not silently pass. If the lane's manifest is **terminal**, the lock is stale (the runtime holds it, the agent is gone): the verb records `reap-deferred` with the path, raises a **W**, and the path is carried into the run report. If the manifest is **not** terminal, the agent may still be running and this is `interrupted-external-action` (C) — never a forced removal, because forcing it destroys a live agent's work. `abort-run`'s report gains the row *"still running, workspace not reclaimed"* (§5.12), which is the state this repository has reproduced (six stale worktrees) and the one C5 was written about. |
| **Release before re-dispatch** | §7.3 re-dispatches a `dead` lane once, from the same handoff, with `git checkout -B` on the **same** branch. Git refuses that while another worktree holds the branch (§3.5a step 2, reproduced, exit 128), so `release-lane` on the dead lane's workspace is a **precondition** of the re-dispatch, not a later cleanup. A `dead` lane has a terminal manifest, so the release takes the stale-lock path above; if it cannot be released, the lane is `lane-possibly-live` and the run asks the user rather than re-dispatching into a held branch. |
| **Resumed session facing a possibly-live lane** | **Never re-dispatch. Always require user adjudication.** §4.6 classifies it `lane-possibly-live` → `interrupted-external-action` (C), keyed on **liveness** — a live or unattributed workspace — and never on whether the journal shows a matched intent/result pair, which is exactly the mistake revision 1 made. This is the safe answer and it is deliberately not committee-decidable: the failure mode it prevents is recorded and reproduced in this repository — background teammate names are runtime-session-scoped, so after a resume every previously spawned agent is unreachable **while still holding its worktree**. |

#### 5.5.2 Profile A depends on `.claude/worktrees/` being ignored, and today that is machine-local

Verified: `git check-ignore -v .claude/worktrees` resolves to **`.git/info/exclude:11`**, not to
`.gitignore` — which contains no `.claude/` entry at all. `02:213` records that git does **not**
auto-ignore nested worktrees and that the exclusion is a *"local, unshared"* harness-maintained file.

On any fresh clone, CI runner, or second developer machine, every lane worktree is therefore
untracked content **inside** the host tree: `git status --porcelain` becomes thousands of lines,
`dispatch-base-dirty` becomes noise, and any broad `git add` at a dispatch-base commit stages another
lane's entire source tree. Neither the first revision's preflight nor its six probe assertions checked
this. Four closures, and the third is the one revision 1 was missing:

1. **Probe assertion 7** — `git check-ignore .claude/worktrees` must exit 0 — and §5.8's selection
   rule now counts **eight** assertions, so assertion 7 is actually consulted. Revision 1 added the
   assertion and left rule 3 reading *"all six assertions pass"*, which made this closure and
   assertion 8 decorative: nothing else in the design reads either result.
2. `.claude/worktrees/` is added to **this repository's** tracked `.gitignore` (§14, registration 9).
3. **A remediation for consumer repositories, because closure 2 is not one.** Registration 9 changes
   *speckiwi's own* `.gitignore`; a consumer installing `kiwi-orchestrator` never receives it, so
   assertion 7 fails on **every** consumer's first run — silently disabling Profile A (rule 4) or hard
   halting under an explicit `--isolation worktree` (rule 2), with no instruction anywhere telling the
   user what to add. The orchestrator therefore performs the remediation itself, once, at Preflight
   P.6 — inside the probe step, which the P.5 run lock already protects: it appends `.claude/worktrees/` to the consumer's **tracked** `.gitignore` with a one-line
   comment naming the run, journals the edit, prints it in the run header, and re-evaluates assertion
   7. Under `--auto` this is a `business-decision`-severity gate, not a silent write. A **second**
   failure rejects Profile A per rule 4. This is one of the few writes the orchestrator makes outside
   `docs/research/{work}/`, and it is stated rather than assumed.
   **The edit is committed, not left dangling.** It rides Phase 0.b's `commit-run-artifacts` commit
   (§4.8 policy 5) with an explicit `.gitignore` pathspec and a comment naming the run. Revision 2
   made the write and gave it no terminus: neither `abort-run` nor the normal end of run reverted or
   committed it, so a consumer was left with a modified tracked file and no record of why — and the
   next stage's `dispatch-base-dirty` would have to tolerate it. Committing it is also what makes
   the fix *persist* for the consumer's later runs, which is the point of making it at all.
4. `dispatch-base-dirty` is path-scoped (§2.1) so it cannot be poisoned by untracked worktree content
   even where the ignore is absent.

#### 5.5.3 The join model — how the orchestrator learns a stage is finished

Revision 1 never stated whether `dispatch(lane)` blocks; whether 3.h knows all lanes are done; who
evaluates `deadline_at`; or what the *"stated interval"* of the install stagger is. All four are
answered here, because an implementer choosing differently on any one of them builds a different
skill: a non-blocking read at 3.h classifies eight just-spawned lanes as `no-response` within a second
of dispatch, and a blocking dispatch makes `--install-concurrency` and `--lane-deadline`
unimplementable because a running lane cannot be observed.

1. **`dispatch(lane)` is non-blocking under Profile A** and returns as soon as the agent is spawned.
   Under Profile B it is blocking by construction — one lane at a time in one tree — and 3.g′ is a
   no-op there. **The `dispatch-lane` *result* line is written by the 3.g′ poll loop**, on its first
   pass in which §3.5a step 2 resolves the workspace by branch. Revision 2 deferred the line until
   resolution succeeded (§3.5a step 4) and assigned no party to write it afterwards, so §4.3's
   invariant — *"the last line for each `(verb, wave, lane)` key must be a `result`"* — read every
   dispatched lane as interrupted for the seconds-to-minutes between spawn and the lane's first
   `git checkout -B`. Under Profile B `dispatch` blocks, so it writes its own result line.
2. **Phase 3.g′ is a poll loop at the host**, `orchestrate lane status --wave N --stage S --json`
   every `--poll-interval` (default **60 s**). Its output schema is `LaneStatus[]` (§10.1) — revision
   2 described the payload in prose while the join loop, `lane-timeout` and reconciliation all
   consume it, so two implementers would disagree about when `lane-timeout` (C) fires. Per lane it
   reports the observations §4.6's `L(k)` needs: terminal-manifest presence and its `status`,
   workspace path and `locked` state, heartbeat age and `bootstrap_done_at`, branch tip, deadline.
   A lane is **terminal** when a manifest exists for it with a status from §3.4's closed enum, or
   when no workspace on its ref family exists (which takes §5.4's workspace-gone path), or when
   `now > deadline_at`.
3. **The deadline has an evaluator, and it is this loop.** Revision 1 gave every lane a `deadline_at`
   and gated on it at 3.h with no party assigned to read the clock — the same *"calling it is a
   choice"* defect §10.1 correctly rejects for `workflow_doctor`. `lane-timeout` (C) is raised by 3.g′
   the first time it observes `now > deadline_at` on a non-terminal lane.
4. **Dispatch is staggered by a signal, not by a guess.** The orchestrator dispatches lane *i+1* when
   fewer than `--install-concurrency` (default **2**) lanes are *installing*, and "installing" is
   observable: a lane writes `bootstrap_done_at` into its heartbeat file immediately after
   `npx vitest --version` succeeds. A lane with a workspace and no `bootstrap_done_at` is installing.
   A hard floor of `--install-stagger` (default **30 s**) applies between spawns so the first two
   lanes do not race the same cold cache. Revision 1 said the orchestrator *"staggers dispatch by a
   stated interval"* and stated no interval and no signal.
5. **The whole stage's join is bounded** by `min(max lane deadline, remaining --run-budget)`. When the
   run budget expires first, the run stops at the **stage boundary** through `abort-run` (§5.12),
   never mid-integration.

### 5.6 Profile B — `branch-serial-lane` (the fallback)

If the probe fails on any assertion, or the user passes `--isolation=branch-serial-lane`, or
`--serial` is given, lanes execute **sequentially in the host tree**, each on its own branch:

```
acquire(lane)   : assert `git status --porcelain -- <write_set> <read_set>` is EMPTY   <- mandatory
                  git switch -c kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k} {base_sha}
                                                                  → workspace_ref = branch
dispatch(lane)  : /kiwi-pm --session-suffix {lane} --no-final --no-pipeline-emit
                    --defer-srs-mutation {deferred_mutations_path}
                    --commit-lane-work
                    --handoff docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.md
                  (the handoff's task_ids[] IS the task subset — §5.4's --handoff semantics)

collect(lane) steps 1-3 :
                  git rev-parse HEAD ; assert head_sha != base_sha or intentionally_empty ;
                  read the manifest ;
                  run verification_cmd ONCE with --reporter=json, HERE, while still on the
                    lane branch — that run is both the lane verification and §7.4 row R11 ;
                  assert `git status --porcelain -- <write_set> <read_set>` is EMPTY   <- mandatory

verify(lane)    : loop L (§7.3), on the lane branch, BEFORE the harvest and BEFORE the switch
                  → its remediation creates docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-{run-id}/

  re-read head_sha ; §7.4 re-audit at that tip (§2.1 ordering rule 6)

collect(lane) step 4 :
                  harvest manifest + every remediation manifest + queue + decisions
                    + docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-*/ to the host run directory (§3.5) ;
                  revert the orchestrator-only residue (§5.4.2 consequence 2) ;
                  assert `git status --porcelain -- <write_set> <read_set>` is EMPTY   <- mandatory
                  git switch {frozen.integration_branch}

integrate(lane) : §5.9, at frozen.integration_branch              → merge_sha
release(lane)   : git branch -d (optional)
```

**The block above and the prose below now state the same order, and in revision 2 they did not.**
Revision 2's pseudo-code put the harvest inside `collect()` before loop L while its prose gave
`harvest → loop L → re-read → re-audit → revert → switch`; an implementer reads the block first. Both
are replaced by the single sequence above, whose one substantive change is that **step 4 runs after
loop L**: loop L's remediation creates `docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-{run-id}/` by contract
(`kiwi-review-fix-loop:200`, `:634`), i.e. after any earlier harvest and before the residue revert's
`git clean -fd`. Under revision 2's order loop L's entire evidence bundle was created and then
deleted, unharvested, on the `--serial` path §1.4 routes small runs into by default — while §5.4.2
consequence 5 asserted it *"is harvested, not discarded"*.

**The two dirty-tree assertions are not hygiene; they are the whole of Profile B's isolation.** All
lanes share one tree, so uncommitted work does not stay with its author: without them, lane 1's
uncommitted work rides `git switch` into lane 2's branch, lane 2 commits, and lane 2's `base..head`
now contains lane 1's files — producing either a false `lane-lease-breach` against lane 2 or, worse,
lane 1's **unaudited** output merging under lane 2's identity, having passed no claim audit and no
loop L. A non-empty result at either point is `lane-workspace-dirty` (C).

**They are path-scoped to `write_set ∪ read_set`, not tree-wide.** A tree-wide predicate cannot pass
under §5.4.2: the lane's child skills write orchestrator-only residue (`kiwi/pipeline.jsonl`,
`docs/plans/…` checkboxes, `docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-…/`) that the pathspec rule
deliberately leaves uncommitted, and `kiwi/orchestrator/` is untracked by design (§4.8). Scoping the
assertion to the lease keeps the property that matters — *no uncommitted **code** crosses a lane
boundary* — and `collect()`'s residue revert is what stops the *non-code* residue crossing one.

**Loop L runs before the switch, and this ordering is load-bearing under Profile B.** Revision 1 put
loop L *"at the lane head, in the lane's workspace, after `collect()`"* while `collect()`'s last act
was `git switch {frozen.integration_branch}`. The reachable consequence: loop L's remediation edits
and commits land on the **integration branch**, `integrate(lane)` then merges a lane branch that does
not contain the fix, the merge gate's full suite runs against a tree that does, and the fix has passed
no claim audit and carries no lane attribution. Phase 3.h/3.i ordering makes that the normal path, not
an edge case. Under Profile B the sequence is therefore the one in the block above: `collect()` steps
**1–3** → **loop L on the lane branch** → re-read `head_sha` → §7.4 re-audit → `collect()` **step 4
(harvest)** → residue revert → switch. (`collect()` has steps 1, 1′, 2, 3, 4 — revision 2 wrote
*"steps 1–5"*, naming a step that does not exist.) Under
Profile A the question does not arise: the host never switches, and §7.3's remediation is a
**re-dispatch** into a fresh workspace on a fresh `-fix{r}` branch pinned to the lane head.

Isolation becomes **temporal instead of spatial**. What is lost is wall-clock parallelism, and only
that. What is kept, unchanged: per-lane branches and independently verifiable histories; the
**same** proof kinds — which, per §5.4, is precisely why profile immutability must be *enforced*
rather than asserted; per-lane verification loops with lane-scoped denominators; real merges with
real merge shas; post-merge verification; the claim audit; lane-scoped `kiwi-pm` sessions and
therefore lane-granular resume. No `npm ci`, no path budget, no staleness — Profile B is immune to C3
and C7 by construction, and it is the profile stages ≥ 2 fall back to if the user declines X-01.

**This must be said to the user at Phase 0 and printed in the run header**, not discovered at wave
2: under Profile B the run gains merge hygiene, lane-granular verification and lane-granular resume,
but **not speed**.

**`--serial` is Profile B with one lane per stage. There is no profile `none`, and the value is
deleted.** Revision 1 described `--serial` three incompatible ways: §1.4 degraded to *"`--serial`
(**§5.6**)"* — which is Profile B; §5.8 rule 1 mapped it to *"profile = **none** (single in-tree
stage), skip probing"*; and §14.1 said profile `none` has *"no lane isolation, **no claim audit**, no
per-lane merge gate."* The three differ on the mechanism that enforces charter C1 and C2 at the lane
boundary — the claim audit is the **only** mechanical detector of `lane-srs-mutation-detected`,
`lane-non-code-write` and `lane-lease-breach` — so two implementers would build two fallbacks, one of
which silently removes C1 enforcement from the path §1.4 routes small runs into **by default**. That
is unacceptable for a value the design recommends.

Under the unified reading, `--serial`:

- sets `frozen.isolation_profile = "branch-serial-lane"` and **skips the probe**;
- still writes a `probe-isolation` **result** line recording `{profile: "branch-serial-lane", reason:
  "--serial", rejected: [], probe_evidence: null}`, so §5.4's profile-immutability check has an
  operand. Revision 1's *"skip probing"* left `resume-card.ts`'s new immutability rule (RV-24) with
  nothing to compare against on the design's own recommended degradation path — it either failed
  closed on a correct run or was silently vacuous;
- forces `--lanes 1`, so every stage has exactly one lane — which under Profile B is in-tree by
  construction, on its own branch, with the two dirty-tree assertions and the claim audit intact.
  Revision 2 justified this by invoking `computeLanePlan`'s *"a stage with fewer than 2 lanes runs
  serially in-tree, journalled, with **no isolation cost**"* rule, which promised the opposite of the
  bullet below and is why R33 recorded that the two readings differ on whether charter C1 is enforced
  at all. **That rule is deleted (§5.3)**, so the guarantee below now holds without an exception;
- **keeps** per-lane branches, the claim audit, loop L, the per-lane merge gate, handoffs, intake,
  design authoring and post-merge verification. What it removes is concurrency, and only that.

§1.4, §5.8, §14.1 and T23 are restated on this basis.

### 5.7 Profile C — `patch-lane` (named, not shipped in 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator)

For repositories where branch creation is refused. `collect()` captures
`git diff > waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.patch`; `integrate()` is `git apply --3way`. The
completion proof degrades from `git-ancestor` to `digest` over the patch plus a post-apply tree
hash, so the run is marked `proof_strength: "degraded"` and post-merge verification is forced to
`--max`. It is specified so the interface is provably not worktree-shaped; it is **not** built in
2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator.

### 5.8 Selection rule

```
1. --serial                                  → profile = branch-serial-lane with --lanes 1;
                                               skip the probe, but WRITE the probe-isolation
                                               result line with reason "--serial" (§5.6)
2. explicit --isolation <profile>            → probe it; failure is a CRITICAL HALT, never a
                                               silent downgrade
3. else probe A; ALL EIGHT assertions pass   → profile = worktree
                                               (assertion 7 after at most one remediation, §5.5.2)
4. else                                      → profile = branch-serial-lane, WARN the user that
                                               parallelism is logical only, journal the rejected
                                               profile and EVERY failing assertion
5. if branch creation is also refused        → isolation-profile-unavailable (C)
```

**"All eight", not "all six".** Revision 1 added assertions 7 and 8 to close two named defects and
left rule 3 counting six, which made both closures inoperative: §5.8 is the **only** rule that turns a
probe result into a decision, and nothing else in the design reads assertion 7 or 8. The consequence
was worse in a consumer repository than here — assertion 7 is the *only* protection a consumer has
(§5.5.2 closure 3), and at `--lanes 8` on a host with 400 MB free, assertion 8 unread means Profile A
is selected and fails mid-stage after roughly three installs, with up to eight background agents
already dispatched.

Silent downgrade is forbidden. `{profile, reason, rejected[]}` is journalled. The reason is
recorded history: a silently substituted root is what turned a tool constraint into *writing to the
wrong repository* (`01:376`).

### 5.9 The convergence registry — integration by regeneration, not textual merge

Some paths cannot be inside any lane's lease. They are enumerated at Phase 2.c with a **closed
four-value recipe enum**, so the registry is schema-checkable and a path with no recipe is not
eligible to be a convergence point.

**Which of these ship, and which are examples — stated, because the first revision did not.** §2.c
calls this step *"convergence registry **authoring**"*, i.e. per-run, while the JSON below is a
fully-populated CP-01..CP-07 over paths that exist only in this repository, one of whose notes even
reasons about *"charter §6"* — a document that ships with nothing. Read as the shipped default it
gives every consumer repository speckiwi's paths; read as an example it leaves Phase 2.c with no
default at all and `convergence-without-recipe` (C) firing on the first run in any repo. The
ambiguity is load-bearing, because §5.2's `recipe.kind` rule and §7.4 row **R4** both treat these entries
as real.

| Point | Status |
| --- | --- |
| CP-01, CP-02 | **Universal defaults, shipped with the skill.** Every SpecKiwi consumer has `docs/spec/` and `00.index.md`, and C1/C4 apply to all of them. |
| CP-07 | **Universal defaults**, minus the `docs/research/{work}/**` entry, which is templated on `--work`. |
| CP-03, CP-04, CP-05, CP-06 | **Speckiwi-specific examples.** They are moved out of the normative JSON into the labelled example below. A consumer authors its own equivalents at Phase 2.c; the skill ships the *shape*, not these paths. |

**Normative shipped default:**

```json
{
  "schema_version": "1.0.0",
  "points": [
    { "id": "CP-01", "paths": ["docs/spec/00.index.md"], "class": "generated",
      "recipe": { "kind": "regenerate", "command": "mcp:sync_index" } },
    { "id": "CP-02", "paths": ["docs/spec/**"], "class": "single-writer",
      "recipe": { "kind": "orchestrator-only" } },
    { "id": "CP-07", "paths": ["kiwi/pipeline.jsonl", ".kiwi/**",
                               "docs/plans/**", "docs/analysis/**", "docs/research/{work}/**",
                               "docs/spec/91.completed-work-log.md"],
      "class": "append-journal", "recipe": { "kind": "orchestrator-only" } }
  ]
}
```

`kiwi/waves.jsonl` was **removed** from CP-07. It is untracked by policy (§4.8), so it can never
match a tree — and including it is what aborted the whole restore step (below).

**Speckiwi-specific example, authored at Phase 2.c for this repository's own `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` run:**

```json
{ "id": "CP-03", "paths": ["src/cli/index.ts", "src/cli/commands/*.ts",
                           "src/mcp/tools/read-tools.ts", "src/mcp/tools/mutation-tools.ts",
                           "src/mcp/server.ts", "src/mcp/schemas.ts"],
  "class": "registry", "recipe": { "kind": "exclusive-lane" } },
{ "id": "CP-04", "paths": ["skills/claude/**", "skills/codex/**", "skills/etc/**"],
  "class": "parity-unit", "recipe": { "kind": "orchestrator-only" },
  "note": "FR-FLOW-061 identifier parity: the three variants are ONE indivisible unit" },
{ "id": "CP-05", "paths": [".agents/skills/**"], "class": "generated",
  "recipe": { "kind": "orchestrator-only" },
  "note": "regenerated by `speckiwi skills mirror --write`; see §9.5" },
{ "id": "CP-06", "paths": ["package.json", "package-lock.json"], "class": "single-writer",
  "recipe": { "kind": "orchestrator-only" } }
```

**CP-04 and CP-06 were reclassified from `exclusive-lane` to `orchestrator-only` in this revision,
and the reason is a hard constraint, not a preference.** Making `exclusive-lane` reachable for them
would violate two things at once: charter C1 — *"A parallel task's write set may contain only **code
and tests**"* (`00.charter.md:135`) — and this design's own `forbidden[]` line *"Do not edit
`docs/spec/`, `docs/plans/`, `kiwi/`, `.agents/`, `package.json`, or any `skills/` file"* plus §5.4's
"no `package.json` edit". The registry as first written declared a lane-eligible recipe for two path
sets the lane contract independently bans. **`exclusive-lane` survives for CP-03 only** — `src/cli/**`
and `src/mcp/**` are code, and they are exactly what charter C6's *"Loud, so at most one concurrent
writer each"* (`00.charter.md:170`) describes.

**Consequence for this design's own 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator run, stated in §17.14.** §10.5's work order puts step 6
(`skills/**`) and step 8 (three SKILL.md variants) inside CP-04, and step 7 (CLI + MCP) inside CP-03.
With `unknown-write-set` also firing more than expected (§17.5), this design's own first run degrades
to near-total serialisation while `lanes.lock.json` still reports lanes and stages. That is honest and
it is recorded, not hidden.

**`class` is documentation and `recipe.kind` is behaviour, and the schema says so.** Five `class`
values appear above (`generated`, `single-writer`, `append-journal`, `registry`, `parity-unit`) and
nothing reads any of them — `convergence-without-recipe` polices `recipe` only, §5.2 keys on
`recipe.kind`, §5.9's restore keys on `recipe.kind`. `class` is therefore declared **optional, free
text, and non-behavioural** in the registry schema, so that no implementer builds a second dispatch
on it and no reviewer mistakes it for one. Revision 1 left it looking like a closed vocabulary with a
consumer.

| recipe | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `regenerate` | discard both sides, re-run the generator |
| `replay` | re-apply each lane's recorded entries in lane-id order (used for deferred mutations) |
| `exclusive-lane` | at most one lane in the whole wave may own the unit |
| `orchestrator-only` | no lane may touch it; lane-side content is dropped at merge and replayed |

**How `orchestrator-only` is enforced at merge — the mechanism, rewritten.** The first revision
specified:

```
git merge --no-commit --no-ff <lane_branch>
git checkout HEAD -- <every orchestrator-only path glob>     # restore the integration head's copy
git commit -m "orch({run_id}): integrate wave-{n} lane-{k}"
```

**That cannot execute in this repository, and it fails in the worst possible way.**
`git checkout <tree-ish> -- <pathspecs>` is **all-or-nothing**: one pathspec matching nothing aborts
the command and restores *nothing*. One of CP-07's globs matches nothing by construction — `.kiwi/**`
is ignored at `.gitignore:20` and therefore never in any tree — and the first revision's CP-07 carried
a second, `kiwi/waves.jsonl`, which is untracked (`git ls-files kiwi/` → `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` only)
and is removed above. The restore aborts before touching
anything; the first revision specified no error handling, so `git commit` then ran anyway. The lane's
`kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` line landed on the integration branch **and** was replayed at the host root —
duplicate events, permanently — and, in the same abort, CP-02 (`docs/spec/**`) was left unrestored,
so a lane that touched a scope SRS merged its edit. That is charter C1's silent class, and this step
was the design's claimed prevention for it.

A second, independent defect sits in the same three lines: even for a glob that *does* match,
`git checkout HEAD -- <glob>` cannot **remove** a file the merge newly **added** — and §7.3 routes
lane remediation to `/kiwi-review-fix-loop` inside the lane workspace, which creates
`docs/analysis/kiwi-{skill}-{run_id}/`, a CP-07 path.

**The replacement is per-path, tolerant of non-matching and newly-added paths, and fails closed:**

```
# 0. preconditions
expectedParent := git rev-parse {frozen.integration_branch}       # HEAD is on that branch
lane_head      := THE SHA THE §7.4 RE-AUDIT AT 3.j PASSED         # not 3.h's head_sha
assert `git status --porcelain` shows no MERGE_HEAD leftover; else abort_integration("dirty-index")

# 1. merge, no commit
git merge --no-commit --no-ff <lane_head>              || abort_integration("merge-failed")

# 2. compute the merged index's path set ONCE, then act per path
merged_paths := git diff --name-only --cached HEAD
for p in merged_paths ∩ orchestrator_only_globs:          # precedence per §5.2
      if git cat-file -e HEAD:p  : git checkout HEAD -- p     # existed before -> restore
      else                       : git rm --cached --force -- p ; rm -f p   # merge ADDED it -> remove
      on any non-zero exit       : abort_integration("restore-failed", p)

# 3. re-verify: the path set must now be disjoint from the orchestrator-only globs
if (git diff --name-only --cached HEAD) ∩ orchestrator_only_globs ≠ ∅ :
      abort_integration("restore-incomplete")

# 4. materialise the index as a tree, then build the commit object; subject carries no marker (X-02)
tree := git write-tree                                 || abort_integration("write-tree-failed")
new  := git commit-tree <tree> -p <expectedParent> -p <lane_head> -F <msg-with-Orch-* trailers>
                                                       || abort_integration("commit-tree-failed")

# 5. A3 CAS publish — harvested technique H4 (06:163, 06:230)
git update-ref refs/heads/{frozen.integration_branch} <new> <expectedParent>
      || abort_integration("cas-rejected: a concurrent writer moved the branch")

# 6. CONCLUDE THE MERGE, then verify — both halves are mandatory
git reset --hard <new>          # clears MERGE_HEAD / MERGE_MSG and syncs index+worktree to <new>
assert rev-parse(branch) == new
assert first-parent(new) == expectedParent  and  second-parent(new) == lane_head
assert tree(new) == <tree>
assert (git diff --name-only expectedParent..new) ⊆ (write_set ∪ merge-resolution paths)
assert `git rev-parse --verify -q MERGE_HEAD` fails      # nothing left to conclude
```

`abort_integration` runs `git merge --abort` (falling back to `git reset --hard <expectedParent>`),
journals the failure, and raises the named gate. **It never commits.** Every step above has explicit
error handling; the first revision had none on any step, which is what let a failed restore proceed
to `git commit`.

**Steps 4 and 6 are corrections, and without them the second lane of any stage cannot merge.**
Revision 1 replaced `git commit` with `git commit-tree` + `git update-ref` and (a) never produced the
`<tree>` argument — no `git write-tree` appeared anywhere in the sequence — and (b) never concluded
the merge. `git commit` is what clears `.git/MERGE_HEAD` and `MERGE_MSG`; `commit-tree` does not touch
them. §7.5 pins **lane-id order within a stage**, i.e. several merges in sequence on one branch, so
lane 1 would move the ref and leave `MERGE_HEAD` in place, and lane 2's step 1 would fail with
*"fatal: You have not concluded your merge (MERGE_HEAD exists)"* → `abort_integration("merge-failed")`
→ `lane-merge-conflict` (C), whose declared meaning is *"a textual conflict inside a supposedly
disjoint stage"* (§13) — telling the operator the partition was wrong when it was not. Step 6's
`git reset --hard <new>` concludes the merge, and the final assertion makes the conclusion checked
rather than assumed.

**Step 0's `lane_head` is the re-audited tip, and it is `laneHeadRef(k)`, not the lane branch.**
Loop L runs at 3.i and its remediation commits on a `-fix{r}` branch (§7.3, §3.5a step 2), so 3.h's
`head_sha` is stale by the time 3.j runs **and the tip lives on a different ref**. Merging the lane
branch **name** rather than the audited sha, as revision 1 did, meant the sha that passed the audit
and the sha that landed could differ — and rows **R2–R7, R9 and R11** of §7.4 were never re-evaluated
against the difference. (Revision 2 wrote *"rows 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 8"*, pre-renumbering positions
that no longer denote those rows.) §2.1 ordering rule 6 re-runs them; this step pins the result.

**The CAS is the audit's A3 harvest (H4) and it is authored *inside* this shape, not bolted on.**
`06:230` rules that `integrate()` stays `git merge --no-ff` at the host root **plus** an
expected-parent guard on the ref update, taking `update-ref <branch> <new> <expectedParent>`
(`wave-product-committer.ts:759`) and the post-hoc verification of HEAD / parent / tree / path-set
(`:771-791`) as a **technique**, not as the 836-line module. Note the ordering consequence: the CAS
makes the *publish* atomic, which means a broken restore would publish atomically too. Steps 2–3 must
therefore be correct **before** step 5 is reached, and step 3 exists precisely to make that
structural rather than hoped-for. Per `06:163` this harvest requires its own test — a second writer
moving the branch between build and publish, asserting the CAS rejects rather than overwrites (§0.5).

**What is replayed at the host root, and exactly when.** Revision 1 stated the deferred-mutation
replay in two places at two different phases — here, at integrate time, and at 3.k *"once per wave,
after the last stage"* — and never reconciled them. The 3.k reading is the one that ships, because a
replay at integrate time cannot be made idempotent across a resumed session that dies between two
lanes of a stage, and because the epilogue is where every other host-root SRS act already happens.
The 3.j reading was also unexecutable for every stage but the last: 3.j′ reaps the workspace, and the
queue lived inside it. Both are fixed by §2.1 ordering rule 5's harvest.

| What | Where the input comes from | Phase | Failure |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| deferred MCP mutations, deduped on `(tool, args_hash)` (§5.4) | the **host** copy at `kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/deferred-mutations.jsonl`, harvested by `collect()` | **3.k**, once per wave, over every stage's lanes in `(stage, lane-id)` order | `srs-mutation-replay-failed` (C) |
| plan checkbox updates, through MCP `workflow_task_check` / `workflow_task_status_set` | the completed `task_id` set derived from the lane's **commit trailers**: `git log --format='%(trailers:key=Orch-Task,valueonly)' base..head`, deduplicated (§5.4.2 consequence 4) — **not** a committed lane edit, which §5.4.2 forbids, and **not** pm session state, which is never harvested | **3.j**, per lane, idempotent by task id | recorded, retried at 3.k |
| the lane's `decisions.jsonl` → `docs/analysis/kiwi-orchestrator-{run_id}/decisions/` | the **host** copy (§3.5) | **3.j**, per lane | recorded |
| **one** pipeline event per lane through MCP `workflow_pipeline_emit`, **under the per-lane emit key `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}`** | the **host** manifest copy | **3.j**, per lane | best-effort (`pipeline-event.md:157-159`) |

**The per-lane emit key is mandatory, and without it every lane after the first is silently
discarded.** Verified in `src/`: `workflow_pipeline_emit` computes
`incomingEventKey = ${skill}|${run_id}` (`src/core/workflow/mutation.ts:466`) and, when any existing
entry matches, returns `mutationOk` with `written: false` and `journalState: "confirmed"` — a
**success** envelope with no write (`:467-471`). `src/core/workflow/jsonl.ts:76-78` confirms the key
shape, and `pipeline-event.md` §5.2 states the same idempotency rule in prose. Revision 2 required
*"one pipeline event per lane"* and gave every lane the same `skill` and the same bare `run_id`, so
lane 1's event was written, lanes 2..N emitted and received `ok`, and nothing was appended — the
design's replacement for the lane-side pipeline line (R26) produced one event per **run**, with no
error anywhere. `pipeline-event.md` §5.4 already establishes suffixed emit keys as the sanctioned
mechanism (`{run_id}#r{n}` for re-entry) and states explicitly that emit keys are *"a different id
space"* from plan `run_id`s with the sidecar regex **not** applied, so `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}` is
legal by the existing contract rather than a new one. Phase 5's run-level emit keeps the bare
`{run_id}` (or `{run_id}#r{n}` on resume), which no lane key can collide with.

There is no lane-side pipeline line to replay — the lane never writes one (§5.4) and, under §5.4.2's
pathspec rule, could not commit one if it did.

Partial replay failure is `srs-mutation-replay-failed` (C) — it leaves SRS and code disagreeing,
which the wave denominator would otherwise report as a design gap rather than as a tooling failure.
`replay.ts`'s `dedupeIndex` is **persisted**, at
`kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/replay-index.json`, rewritten after each successful call: idempotency
across a resumed session requires the index to survive the session, and revision 1 gave it no home in
§3.1 or §3.2, so `replay-deferred-mutations`'s `idempotent-by-key` recovery class had nothing to key
against after a compaction.

**C4 is discharged here and is unconditional.** After **every** `integrate(lane)` and after the
serial epilogue:

```
speckiwi validate --json
mcp:sync_index
speckiwi validate --fail-on-warning --json
```

If the third call still reports `SRS-W019` / `SRS-W020` or any error, `post-merge-index-drift` (C)
halts. This is the only detector for the Class-C silent rollup corruption that report `02` §6.3
reproduced — two branches each adding one requirement to *different* scope documents auto-merged
`00.index.md` cleanly and produced `2` where the truth was `3`, with no conflict and no warning from
git. The result of every run is journalled in the `convergence` object (§4.2).

### 5.13 The serial epilogue — an executor, a lease, a commit rule, an audit and a gate

**Charter C1's mandated destination had none of those, and this is the largest hole revision 2
left.** Five separate rules route work here: §5.2 sends `srs-write` and `non-code-write-set` tasks,
calling it *"charter C1 as a plan property"*; §5.9 sends every `orchestrator-only` / `regenerate` /
`replay` convergence task; §7.3 sends every demoted lane's tasks; §7.5 sends a coupled pair's
re-plan; §3.4 sends a `lease-breach-requested` lane. Phase 3.k's entire content was three
activities — convergence recipes, deferred-mutation replay, duplication audit — **none of which is
"execute the epilogue task set"**. No party was named, no `orchestrate` verb existed, no gate covered
an epilogue task failing, and §13 had no `epilogue-task-*` row. Contrast §5.4–§5.5, which specify a
lane's executor, flags, commit pathspec, trailers, manifest and eleven audit rows.

The failure is reachable from this design's **own** work order: §10.5 step 10 edits `vitest.config.ts`
and `.gitignore`, so `type: "infra"` fires `non-code-write-set` and the task lands in
`serial_epilogue`. The wave completes; 3.k runs its three activities; the task is never executed. At
3.l §7.6's epilogue sub-denominator has `expected ⊇ {that task}` and `checked = 0`, and *"one task
scheduled but never landed … forbids `ALL_MATCH`"* → `wave-verify-fail-residual` (C), which §5.12
classifies as a **terminal halt after merges have landed**. CP-02 and CP-07 are universal shipped
defaults, so **every repository has epilogue tasks** — which is RW-39's own argument. Splitting the
denominator made epilogue tasks *checkable*; it did not make them *executed*.

**The executor, and it is not a new one.** D2 assigns implementation to `/kiwi-coder` and `/kiwi-pm`,
and §1.2 says the orchestrator introduces *"no fixer of its own"*. So the epilogue is executed the
same way a lane is, minus the isolation:

> Once per wave at 3.k, the orchestrator authors **one epilogue handoff** at
> `docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/epilogue.md` — the §6.1 schema with
> `handoff_kind: "epilogue"`, `lane: "epilogue"`, and `task_ids[]` = `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned`
> ∪ every task demoted into the epilogue during the wave, in `lanes.lock.json` declaration order —
> and runs it at the **host root, on the integration branch**:
>
> ```
> /kiwi-pm --handoff docs/research/{work}/waves/wave-{n}/epilogue.md
>          --session-suffix epilogue --no-final --no-pipeline-emit --commit-lane-work
> ```

Four differences from a lane, each with a reason:

1. **No `--defer-srs-mutation`.** Epilogue tasks are exactly the tasks C1 excludes from parallel
   execution, and C1 forbids SRS mutation *in a parallel task*. At the host, serially, an SRS
   mutation is legal and is the whole point of routing `srs-write` here. The deferred **lane** queues
   are still replayed separately, at activity (3).
2. **No worktree, under either profile**, because there is nothing to isolate from — 3.k runs after
   the last stage's 3.j′, so no lane workspace is live.
3. **A different `forbidden[]`.** The epilogue handoff's `forbidden[]` drops the orchestrator-only
   prohibition for the paths its own tasks and convergence recipes own; it keeps every other line,
   including no push, no PR, no test weakening, no commit-subject markers.
4. **A different audit row set.** `orchestrate lane audit --lane epilogue` runs **R1, R2, R3, R7, R8,
   R9, R10 and R11** and **not R4, R5 or R6** — those three exist to enforce the lane restrictions
   the epilogue is *defined* to be outside of (orchestrator-only paths, non-code paths, SRS
   mutation). Running them here would fail every epilogue by construction; omitting them elsewhere
   would remove C1's enforcement. The row set is per `handoff_kind`, stated once, in §7.4.

**Validation, commit and gate.**

- `validateHandoff` runs on the epilogue handoff exactly as on a lane handoff (all five layers, §6.2)
  — `task_ids` equality is against `serial_epilogue ∪ unassigned ∪ demoted` rather than a lane row.
  **Loop H does not run on it**: loop H's verifier 2 is a cold-agent executability probe, and the
  epilogue's executor is the host with the whole run's context, so the probe measures nothing. That
  is stated rather than assumed, and it is the only handoff exempt from loop H.
- Commits follow the same rule as a lane's: one per Task, explicit pathspec = the epilogue handoff's
  `write_set`, `Orch-*` trailers, no subject markers (X-02). They land directly on the integration
  branch — there is no epilogue branch and no merge, because there is nothing to merge from.
- **Gate `epilogue-task-failed` (C)**: any epilogue task whose `verification_cmd` exits non-zero,
  or that produced no commit and declared no `intentionally_empty` reason, or whose audit raises a
  breach. It halts for the user, at the host, before 3.l — so loop P's epilogue sub-denominator is
  evaluated against a task set that actually ran.

**3.k's five activities, in order**, with the epilogue tasks first because the convergence recipes
and the duplication audit both read the tree they produce:

1. execute the epilogue task set, as above → `epilogue-task-failed` (C);
2. convergence recipes from the registry (`regenerate` / `replay` / `orchestrator-only`, §5.9);
3. deferred-mutation replay from the **host** copies harvested at each stage's 3.j′ →
   `srs-mutation-replay-failed` (C);
4. the cross-lane duplication audit → `cross-lane-duplication-unresolved` (C);
5. commit the run's own artifacts for this wave (§4.8 policy 5) → `commit-run-artifacts`.

**Knock-on, closed here.** §7.10's promotion set counts *"an epilogue task with a host commit and a
passing `verification_cmd`"* — which is now a real, produced thing. And §7.10's `verified` transition
is restated there so that a requirement whose tasks were serialised **for C1 compliance** can still
reach `verified`: loop L's verdicts apply to lane tasks; an epilogue task's equivalent is its
passing audit plus loop P's wave verdict. Revision 2 conditioned `verified` on loop-L verdicts that
epilogue tasks can never receive, so a REQ serialised for charter compliance was permanently barred
from `verified` — against `00.charter.md:297-298` read in the other direction.

### 7.3 Loop L — per-lane, **before** merge

This is the charter's *"subagent feedback loops verify and improve them until quality is sufficient,
and only then do they merge"*, and it is a real loop with named machinery, not a verb name.

**Where loop L runs, per profile, because "in the lane's workspace" is not a mechanism under Profile
A.** §5.5 states flatly that *"a skill cannot make a subagent work inside a directory the skill
created"*, and the runtime cuts a **new** worktree at session-start HEAD for each
`isolation: "worktree"` agent. By 3.i the lane agent has terminated — its manifest is *"your last
act"*. So revision 1's *"executed inside the lane workspace"* had no executable path under the
preferred profile, and nothing committed the fixes it produced.

| Profile | Loop L's verifiers | Loop L's **remediation** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A (worktree)** | run at the **host**, reading `laneHeadRef(k)` and the harvested manifest. Verification is pure reading — a diff, a manifest, a test run whose cwd is the workspace that produced the tip (§5.5) — and needs no agent inside the workspace | a **remediation dispatch**, journalled under its own verb `remediate-lane` (§4.4): the orchestrator spawns a *new* `isolation: "worktree"` agent whose spawn prompt begins `git checkout -B kiwi/orch/{run_id}/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}-fix{r} {laneHeadRef(k)}` — **a fresh branch, not the lane's** — carrying a **remediation handoff** at `waves/wave-{n}/lanes/lane-{k}.fix-{r}.md` (`handoff_kind: "remediation"`, §6.1) whose `## Tasks` is loop L's finding list and whose `write_set` is the original lane's, unchanged. It runs `/kiwi-review-fix-loop --commit-lane-work --no-pipeline-emit` and commits under the lane's own obligation. Its manifest is harvested as `remediation-{r}.manifest.json` (§3.2) |
| **B (branch-serial-lane)** | run at the host **while still on the lane branch**, before `collect()`'s step 4 and its final `git switch` (§5.6) | `/kiwi-review-fix-loop --base {the dispatch base sha} --head {head_sha} --commit-lane-work --no-pipeline-emit`, in the host tree on the lane branch, then re-read `head_sha`. No fix branch is needed — nothing else holds the lane branch — but `--commit-lane-work` is passed identically |

**The remediation branch is `-fix{r}`, not the lane branch, because git refuses otherwise.**
Reproduced in a scratch repository (git 2.48.1.windows.1, two worktrees over one `.git`):
`git checkout -B <branch>` from the second worktree, on a branch the first holds, exits **128** with
*"fatal: '<branch>' is already used by worktree at …"*. Revision 2's remediation checked out the
lane's own branch while the lane's workspace still held it — and §7.4 states in as many words that
*"that directory still exists at 3.h/3.j (it is reaped at 3.j′)"*, with 3.j′ after 3.i (§2.1). So the
remediation agent's **first command failed**, it had no `manifest_path` it could locate to report
`dead`, and loop L's PASS precondition (*"no fix applied in that round"*) was unreachable in every
round → cap exhaustion → `lane-verify-failed` (C) or demotion, **on every lane that needed any fix,
under the preferred profile**. The charter's central sentence — *"verify and improve them until
quality is sufficient, and only then do they merge"* (`00.charter.md:64-66`) — had no executable path.
RW-08's closure of IM-CR3 was therefore inoperative, and so was §7.3's *"re-dispatched from the same
handoff exactly once"*, which hit the same refusal; §5.5.1's *"release before re-dispatch"* row
closes that half.

Four consequences of the fresh branch, propagated:

- `laneHeadRef(k)` (§3.5a step 2) is the highest existing `-fix{r}` ref, else the lane branch. It is
  what §4.6's `B(k)`, §5.9 step 0's `lane_head`, and every `git-ancestor` proof read.
- **R11's cwd moves with it.** §7.4's re-run executes in the workspace of the agent that produced the
  tip — the round-`r` remediation workspace when a remediation ran, the lane's otherwise. A working
  tree does not move when a branch ref does, so running the re-audit in the *original* lane workspace
  would execute the **pre-fix** files while claiming to verify the merged tip.
- 3.j′ reaps the lane workspace **and every remediation workspace** of that lane (§5.5.1).
- `remediate-lane` is its own **externally-visible** verb (§4.4). Revision 2 left the dispatch inside
  `verify-lane`, typed `idempotent-by-key` with the rule *"redo round"* — so a session interrupted
  mid-remediation would redo the round and put a **second** agent onto a possibly-live workspace,
  which is the failure R29 and §5.5.1 forbid and this repository has reproduced. The enum is declared
  closed and *"an out-of-enum verb is a hard stop"*, so the action needed a member, not a footnote.

**`--commit-lane-work` is passed to `/kiwi-review-fix-loop`, and it needed adding there.** Revision 2
wrote that the remediation *"commits under the same obligation (`--commit-lane-work`)"* while that
flag was registered on **`kiwi-pm` only** (S16, §14 item 11) and `kiwi-review-fix-loop` gained nothing
but `--no-pipeline-emit`. Verified in the source revision 2 did not check:
`skills/claude/kiwi-review-fix-loop/SKILL.md:199` — *"코드 변경: fix 대상 파일에 직접 작성.
**git commit 은 사용자 결정**"* — and `:700` — *"| git commit / push | 사용자 결정 |"*. Inside a
lane there is no user, so per §5.4.1 rule 1 the honest outcome is `needs-user`, and under Profile B
the row named no committing party at all while R32 forbids the only remaining candidate (an
orchestrator-authored commit on a lane branch has no lane attribution, no `red_evidence` and no
audit trail). The reachable consequence: round 1 finds a HIGH, the fixer edits files, nothing commits
them, round 2 re-reads the branch and finds the same HIGH, the cap exhausts — or, if a round passes,
`integrate()` merges the **pre-fix** commit while `waves.jsonl` records a passing loop L verdict
against a tree that was never merged, which is verbatim the failure RW-08/RW-09 record as closed.
The argument RW-02 used to invent the flag for `kiwi-pm` applies here word for word: the flag supplies
the user decision the skill correctly refuses to make for itself. S13 and §14 item 11 carry it, and
§10.5 step 9 counts **seven** flags, not six.

Three things this fixes, each of which revision 1 left open:

1. **There is a mechanism.** Under A the fixer is a dispatch, which is the only validated way to put
   an agent in a worktree; under B it is an in-tree run on the correct branch.
2. **The fixes are committed, by a named party, under the pathspec rule.** Revision 1's commit
   obligation bound *the lane agent*, which no longer exists at 3.i, so loop L's rounds 1..n−1 produced
   edits nobody committed while its PASS precondition is *"no fix applied in that round"* — i.e. fixes
   necessarily existed and necessarily were not merged. `integrate()` would have merged the pre-fix
   commit while `waves.jsonl` recorded a passing loop L verdict against it.
3. **`--no-pipeline-emit` is passed.** Revision 1's remediation invocation was the **one** place the
   flag was omitted, while §5.4, §14 item 11 and S13 all exist to prevent exactly that write.
   Together with §5.4.2's pathspec rule, the lane's `base..head` can no longer acquire
   `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl` or `docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-*/`, which is what made every
   remediated lane fail its own lease-conformance check and halt the run.

Loop L's denominators, evaluated at the lane head:

- **Verifier 1 denominator:** the lane's `acceptance[]` rows from the handoff front matter —
  externally fixed, one row per `{ac_id, req_id}`. Verdict per row:
  `realised-with-test` / `realised-untested` / `not-realised`, each with a resolvable pointer.
  Rows the handoff explicitly marked `test_id: null` may close as `realised-untested` **without**
  penalty; every other row must be `realised-with-test`.
- **Verifier 2 denominators:** (a) **lease conformance** — `git diff --name-only base..head` ⊆
  `write_set`; (b) **preservation** — the four-class mechanical derivation from
  `kiwi-wave-master:305-315` over the lane diff, two-value verdict enum
  `intended-improvement` / `unapproved-damage`, with `kiwi-coder §0.20.2`'s export-surface
  definition of a public symbol and `§0.20.3`'s closed four-item weakening list as the criteria;
  (c) **lane regression** — the lane's own `verification_cmd`, not the full suite (§7.7).
- **The improve half is delegated**, per the engine's delegation-only rule: quality and
  implementation findings route to
  `/kiwi-review-fix-loop --base {base_sha} --head {head_sha} --no-pipeline-emit`, hosted per the
  profile table above, so `kiwi-coder §0.9`'s cwd gate is satisfied rather than violated — the
  fixer's cwd *is* a checkout of the lane branch. `--close-reqs` is **not** passed at lane level: the
  lane's REQs are not verified until loop P runs, and review-fix-loop's own PASS does not satisfy a
  parent gate (`kiwi-wave-master:377`). (`kiwi-review-fix-loop §0.8` at `SKILL.md:30` already forbids
  MCP mutation unless `--close-reqs` is passed, so no SRS write can leak in through this hop.)
- Findings that require a plan Task that never existed cannot be fixed in-lane. They abort the lane
  with `status: "dead"` and route to the serial epilogue; a code reviewer cannot invent plan items
  (`kiwi-wave-master:369`).
- **A finding that a `[D-nnn]` design item is *false* is not a lane defect and must not be laundered
  as one.** It terminates the lane with `status: "design-refuted"` carrying `{design_item_id,
  evidence}` and routes to §8.1's mid-wave amendment path. Without this state a lane that discovers
  an unimplementable design item has three options and none is correct: `lease-breach-requested` (a
  path-scope state that says nothing about the design), death into the serial epilogue (which
  **discards the finding** — loop L's denominator is `acceptance[]` rows, not design validity), or
  implementing something wrong. The mid-wave "design found wrong" case resolved as a
  silent wrong implementation.
- **A child skill's own critical gate is not a lane death either.** A `/kiwi-pm` running inside a
  lane inherits its children's `critical_gates[]` by general rule and HALTs *"`--auto` 무관"*
  (`skills/claude/kiwi-pm/SKILL.md:101`, with further always-HALT channels at `:525`, `:670`, `:696`,
  `:713`). The lane's manifest status is `needs-user` carrying `{gate_id, payload}`, surfaced at 3.h
  by `lane-child-gate-halted` (C). Without it the agent blocks indefinitely, the orchestrator sees no
  manifest, and the run's only interpretation is "dead" — which **discards the finding**. See §5.4.1.
- **Lane budgets are separate from run budgets.** A lane that exhausts its cap is **demoted, not
  retried** *when demotion is legal*: its work is discarded, its tasks move to the serial epilogue,
  and the run's cap is untouched. One bad lane can never consume the run.
- **Demotion legality — the single definition, used by loops H and L and by §2's severity rule.**
  The serial epilogue runs at **3.k, after the last stage**. So demotion is legal only when **no task
  of a later stage depends on any task of the demoted lane** — otherwise the epilogue would run after
  the stage that needs it, and stage 2's 3.f′ would commit a dispatch base that does not contain work
  stage 2's handoffs tell their lanes is already present (as §6.3's own `## Context` does). Formally:
  demotion is legal iff `transitiveDependents(lane.task_ids) ∩ (tasks of stages > s) = ∅`, computed
  from the frozen `lanes.lock.json`. When it is **illegal**, the loop's gate fires instead —
  `handoff-verify-failed` (C) or `lane-verify-failed` (C) — and the run halts for the user. Revision 1
  stated demotion unconditionally and had no gate path at all for the dependent case, so a demoted
  stage-1 lane silently produced a false stage-2 base whose only defence was an illustrative
  `## Escalation` paragraph inside a worked example.
- **Re-dispatch cap is 1, hard-coded and journalled, and `release-lane` is its precondition.** A lane
  that dies once is re-dispatched from the same handoff exactly once — safe because the handoff is
  the complete input and the lane produced no external side effect. **The dead lane's workspace is
  released first** (§5.5.1's *"release before re-dispatch"* row), because the re-dispatch reuses the
  lane's branch name and git refuses `checkout -B` on a branch another worktree holds; if the release
  cannot be performed the lane is `lane-possibly-live` and the run asks the user instead. A second
  death demotes it (subject to demotion legality). A third failure is
  `lane-repeatedly-unrecoverable` (C), **raised at 3.k** — its second conjunct, *"failed in the
  serial epilogue"*, is only evaluable after the epilogue runs (§13; §2.1's flow lists it at 3.k for
  the same reason, which revision 2 corrected in §13 alone).
- **Every disposition that ends a lane without merging is journalled.** Demotion, quarantine after a
  `design-refuted` or `needs-user` halt, and a coupling reset all write `lane_disposition` on the
  lane's `verify-lane` or `collect-lane` **result** line (§4.2). Without it a resumed session
  reclassifies the lane `lane-integrable` and merges work the run had discarded (§4.6 item 5), and
  `P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` reads false forever (§4.1).

PASS: `CRITICAL=0`, `HIGH=0`, every `acceptance[]` row terminal, lease conformance clean,
`unapproved-damage = 0`, lane regression green, **and no fix applied in that round**.

### 7.4 The claim audit — a lane's own success report is worth nothing

Mechanical, no subagent, run by the orchestrator **twice**: at 3.h before loop L, and again at 3.j
against the tip `integrate()` is about to merge (§2.1 ordering rule 6). It re-derives every claim
from artifacts. **The rows are numbered, and every cross-reference in this document cites the number
in this table** — revision 2 inserted three rows into revision 1's list and left six references
pointing at their old positions, one of which (*"§7.4 row 3 wins"*, which was written when the orchestrator-only rule sat at position 3 and now denotes **R4**) is the disposition that resolves
the whole lane-writes-an-orchestrator-only-path contradiction and, followed by number, implements
`written ⊆ declared` instead.

| # | Check | Method | Failure | Re-run at 3.j? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **R1** | the lane committed at all | `head_sha != base_sha`, or `manifest.intentionally_empty == true` with a reason | treated as a death (§7.3) | no |
| **R2** | manifest ≡ actual delta | `git diff --name-only base..head` vs `written_paths` | `lane-lease-breach` (C) | **yes** |
| **R3** | written ⊆ declared | that same set vs `write_set` | `lane-lease-breach` (C) | **yes** |
| **R4** | no orchestrator-only path survived | that set vs the registry's `orchestrator-only` globs, resolved under §5.2's precedence order | `lane-lease-breach` (C) | **yes** |
| **R5** | **no non-code path** | that set ⊆ `constraints.json`'s `code_roots ∪ test_roots` (§5.2, charter C1's first clause) | `lane-non-code-write` (C) | **yes** |
| **R6** | no SRS mutation | (a) the harvested `deferred-mutations.jsonl` contains only the four `kiwi-coder §0.12` calls and no `add_requirement`; (b) `docs/spec/` is untouched in the lane delta; **(c) `docs/spec/` is unchanged at the HOST root across the stage, and `list_requirements` for the wave's target still returns the 3.b allocation set** | `lane-srs-mutation-detected` (C) | **yes** |
| **R7** | no external side effect | `external_side_effects == []`; no remote ref moved | `lane-external-side-effect` (C) | **yes** |
| **R8** | **acceptance is non-vacuous** | `\|acceptance[]\| > 0`, and the number of `acceptance[]` rows whose **handoff `test_id` is non-null** is `≥ \|acceptance[]\| − cap`, with `cap` the count defined in §6.5 | `lane-evidence-irreproducible` (C) | no (the handoff is frozen) |
| **R9** | **red preceded green** | every `red_evidence` entry's `command` appears in `commands_run` with a **non-zero** exit code, and precedes the same command's zero-exit run in `commands_run` order | `lane-evidence-irreproducible` (C) | **yes** |
| **R10** | the lane really finished | manifest has `finished_at` **and** the branch has commits (see *"the three independent signals"* note at the end of this section) | treated as a death (§7.3) | no |
| **R11** | the tests really pass | the **integrator** runs the handoff's `verification_cmd` once with `--reporter=json` and asserts that **every** non-null `acceptance[].test_id` appears in the report as `passed` — see below for the command and the cwd | `lane-evidence-irreproducible` (C) | **yes** |

**R11's command construction, because "re-run the `acceptance[]` tests" is not an invocation.**
`test_id` is `path::name` (§6.1). Revision 2 rested *"the whole authentication story"* on this row and
never said which runner, `-t` versus file-scoped, POSIX versus Windows, or how a partial-name
collision resolves — so two implementers would produce two different denominators for a gate declared
*"not committee-decidable"*. It is fixed to one invocation:

> R11 runs the handoff's `verification_cmd` — the host's `{posix, windows}` variant, with
> `--reporter=json` appended if absent (§6.1) — and parses the report. It **passes** iff the process
> exits 0 **and**, for every non-null `acceptance[].test_id`, the report contains a test whose file
> equals the `test_id`'s path and whose **full name matches exactly**, with status `passed`.

Three properties follow. Exact full-name matching removes partial-name collisions, which a `-t`
substring filter cannot. Layer 4 already asserts that `verification_cmd` covers every acceptance
`test_id`'s file (§6.1, §6.2), so one run is sufficient by construction. And **the lane verification
and R11 become one process**, which is what §6.1's *"the only one `collect()` runs"* always claimed
while §5.6 listed them as two steps and Profile A ran neither (§5.4 `collect()` step 3).

**What the 3.j re-audit reads: the *effective lane record*.** Loop L's remediation writes its own
manifest; the lane wrote one earlier; revision 2 said neither which the re-audit reads nor whether
they combine, and both single-manifest readings fail. The record is defined:

> **`effective(k)`** = the lane's harvested `manifest.json` unioned with every
> `remediation-{r}.manifest.json` in ascending `r`: `written_paths` is the **union** (deduplicated on
> path, `before_sha256` from the earliest entry and `after_sha256` from the latest), `commits` and
> `commands_run` are **concatenated in round order**, `red_evidence` is the union, and the scalar
> fields (`status`, `head_sha`, `finished_at`) come from the **last** manifest.

R2 then compares `git diff --name-only <dispatch base>..<laneHeadRef(k)>` against
`effective(k).written_paths`; R9 checks each `red_evidence` entry against the concatenated
`commands_run`, so a lane's red run and the fixer's later green run of the same command are correctly
ordered. **R3 is unchanged and deliberately so**: the fixer's lease *is* the lane's `write_set`
(§7.3's remediation handoff), so `written ⊆ declared` holds against the same set. Revision 2's own
justification paragraph said a fix touching a `read_set` module was *"legal for the fixer, not for
the lane"* — that sentence is **deleted**, because it contradicts the remediation handoff's own
`write_set` rule two sections earlier and would have made R3 fail on the action it called legal. A
fixer that needs a path outside `write_set` reports `lease-breach-requested`, exactly as a lane does.

**R6(c) is the host-side conjunct, and without it the SRS row is satisfiable by a lane that violated
C1.** A worktree lane's MCP calls land at the **host** root (charter C5, `02:203`). If
`--defer-srs-mutation` fails to reach `kiwi-coder` — which, before §5.4's pass-through correction, it
structurally could not — the queue file is **absent** (vacuously satisfying (a)) and the lane's git
delta is **clean** (satisfying (b)), because the write went to a different tree. Rows (a) and (b)
together are blind to the only place the violation lands.

**Where R11 executes, because "at the lane head" is a sha, not a location.** Under **Profile A** the
integrator runs `verification_cmd` as a host-launched process whose **cwd is the workspace of the
agent that produced the audited tip** — the round-`r` remediation workspace when a remediation ran,
the lane's workspace otherwise. That directory still exists at 3.h/3.j (all of them are reaped at
3.j′, §5.5.1), and it is the only checkout that has the `node_modules` that agent's own `npm ci`
produced. **The cwd must follow the tip**: a working tree does not move when a branch ref does, so
running in the original lane workspace after a remediation would execute the pre-fix files while
claiming to verify the merged tip. This is legal: the host may run a process in a directory; what a
skill cannot do is make a *subagent* work there (§5.5). Under **Profile B** it runs in the host tree
**while `collect()` is still on the lane branch** (§5.6), before the switch back. Neither reading
