requires checking the lane branch out at the host mid-stage, which would destroy the integration
state.

**Which rows the audit runs is a function of `handoff_kind` (§6.1).** For a `lane` handoff, all
eleven. For the wave's `epilogue` handoff (§5.13), **R1, R2, R3, R7, R8, R9, R10 and R11** — and
**not R4, R5 or R6**, which exist to enforce the lane restrictions the epilogue is defined to be
outside of (orchestrator-only paths, non-code paths, SRS mutation). Running them there would fail
every epilogue by construction; omitting them for a lane would remove charter C1's only mechanical
enforcement.

`lane-evidence-irreproducible` is critical and **not committee-decidable even under `--auto`**. A
committee should never vote on whether to accept fabricated evidence.

**Why *eight* rows are re-run at 3.j.** The table's "Re-run at 3.j?" column marks **R2, R3, R4, R5,
R6, R7, R9 and R11** — eight, not the seven revision 2's heading claimed and not the six §5.9's stale
"rows 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 8" enumerated. §2.1's 3.j gate list names the gates all eight can raise.
Loop L's remediation commits on its own branch (§7.3), so the tip audited at 3.h is neither the tip
nor the manifest merged at 3.j; a fix that moved `base..head` outside `write_set` would otherwise be
merged unaudited, and R11's earlier run would have executed against a sha that is no longer
`laneHeadRef(k)`. The 3.j pass is cheap: it is the same pure function over a newer diff and the
merged `effective(k)` record, plus one test run.

**The "three independent signals" claim is withdrawn, and what replaces it is stated plainly.** The
first revision required manifest `finished_at` **and** branch commits **and** a `TASK_DONE` in the
lane's `pipeline.jsonl`, with the stated purpose *"the AND is what stops a half-dead lane presenting
as complete"*. Two of those three were never independent — the manifest and the pipeline line are
both written by the same lane agent into the same workspace; only the branch is externally
witnessed. And §5.4 now removes the lane-side pipeline event entirely, so signal 3 does not exist.

The authentication story therefore rests on **the integrator's own re-run of `acceptance[]`** (row **R11**
above), which *is* independent: it executes at the host, against the lane head, with the
orchestrator's own process. The manifest's role is reduced to what it can honestly carry — a
falsifiable claim whose every field §7.4 re-derives. Loop P's lane layer (§7.6) is restated on the
same basis: `git-ancestor` plus a passing claim audit, not three signals.

### 7.5 The merge gate — after **each** lane, not once at the end

**Integration order is pinned: lane-id order within a stage, stage order within a wave.** The first
revision fixed lane-id order only for coupling recovery and for `replay`, and left the normal case
unspecified — which makes the merge sequence non-reproducible and therefore makes a resumed session's
reconstruction of "which lanes have landed" order-dependent for no reason.

After every `integrate(lane)`:

1. build / typecheck at the integration head (cheap, first);
2. the **full** suite at the integration head against the run-wide pinned baseline
   (`kiwi-wave-master:150`), asserting `failing_tests ⊆ baseline_failing_tests`;
3. the C4 sequence (§5.9).

Rationale: with N lanes and one final gate, attributing a failure costs O(N) bisections; with a gate
after each merge, attribution is free. The price is N−1 extra suite runs, and that is cheaper than
never being able to say which lane broke it.

*"Both lanes green in isolation, red merged"* is the signature of **semantic coupling with disjoint
write sets** — lane A changes a signature in `src/core/x.ts`, lane B adds a caller in
`src/core/y.ts`. File-level analysis cannot see it, and it is the dangerous case. Detection is
layered, cheapest first: the **stage-time** `read_set ∩ write_set` coupling check at **3.f″** — which
is where `read_set` exists, since it is a handoff field and handoffs are authored at 3.f, not a
plan-time quantity (§5.2, §7.9 (a)) — then this merge gate. Revision 1 called it *"the plan-time
`read_set ∩ write_set` coupling check (§5.2, via the handoff's `read_set`)"*, which is two phases
apart from itself. Recovery is `merge-coupling-detected`: reset the
integration branch to the stage base, replay the non-conflicting lanes in lane-id order, re-plan the
union of the two coupled lanes as a single serial task in the epilogue, **write `lane_disposition:
{kind: "coupling-reset"}` on each reset lane's result line** (§4.2 — without it a resumed session
re-merges the branches the collapse discarded, §4.6 item 5), and **write the coupling row into
`partition-postmortem.wave-{n}.md`** as `{from_task, to_task, path, detected_at, resolution:
"merge-into-one-lane"}` (§3.3a, §5.3). Cap: 2 coupling collapses per stage; the third raises
`partition-quality-insufficient` (C) and the stage runs serially.

A textual conflict inside a stage means the disjointness assumption was violated — a coder touched
an undeclared file — and raises `lane-merge-conflict` (C). The orchestrator has no fixer and does
not silently resolve it.

### 7.7 Test-suite hygiene under parallelism

`vitest.config.ts:30` excludes `**/.claude/worktrees/**` for a measured reason recorded at `:27-29`
— without it a host run picks up every agent worktree's copy of the suite. That protects the **host**
run. A lane running vitest **inside** its own worktree has that worktree as its root, so the pattern
does not exclude its own files: lane test runs work.

Two rules follow, and they are not optional:

- **Lanes run only their own `verification_cmd`**, never the full suite. The first revision's stated
  reason was wrong twice over and is replaced. It said: *"Under Profile B all lanes share one tree,
  and two concurrent full-suite runs fight over `dist/`, `node_modules/.vite` and the hermeticity
  guards … which hard-fail when a run writes into the repository."* Profile B is **sequential by
  definition** (§5.6), so two concurrent runs cannot exist there; and Profile A gives each lane its
  own worktree, so there is nothing to fight over. **The true reasons are cost and denominator
  scope**: a full suite per lane is N times the run's largest recurring expense, and a lane's own
  `verification_cmd` is the denominator loop L is scoped to — a lane that runs the full suite is
  reporting against a denominator that is not its own.
- **And the guards do not do what that paragraph said, which matters for a different reason.**
  `test/support/repo-hermeticity.ts:11-18` is a **fixed six-path sentinel-existence check** —
  `.mcp.json`, `docs/spec/steps`, `.claude/skills`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.agents/skills/kiwi-step`,
  `.agents/skills/kiwi-wave-master` — not a general "a run wrote into the repository" detector. An
  implementer reasoning from the old paragraph would conclude lane writes are self-policing. They are
  not: the guard fires on those six paths and nothing else. **The live interaction is with §9.2**:
  `.agents/skills/kiwi-wave-master` is a sentinel *and* `kiwi-wave-master` is one of the two skills
  in `.agents/skills/.speckiwi-mirror-exclusions.json` (verified: `["kiwi-step","kiwi-wave-master"]`),
  so any lane or epilogue step that regenerates the mirror while §9.2's wave-master edits are in
  flight creates the sentinel path and hard-fails the suite. §9.5 states the sanctioned mirror
  mechanism precisely because of this.
- **The full suite runs only at the host root, serially, at merge gates**, with
  `--no-file-parallelism` per the charter's standing constraint.

---

## 19. Revision log — revision 2

One row per finding closed, accepted or escalated in revision 2, with **where** to check it. Finding
ids are prefixed by the verifier that raised them: **CC** = critical-closure, **ND** = new-defects,
**CF** = charter-fidelity, **IM** = implementability. Where two verifiers raised the same defect the
row lists both ids and closes it once.

### 19.1 Escalated to the user — the fourth ruling

| id | Subject | Disposition | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **X-04** | The charter's no-duplication requirement (`00.charter.md:100`) has no computable *preventive* half: `shared-substrate` quantified over lanes inside the function that computes lanes, over a handoff field authored two phases later, and over symbols no planner output carries | **Escalated.** Two options put to the user: (a) an additive `reads[]` sidecar field on `kiwi-planner`, restoring a symbol-capable plan-time edge; (b) accept a path-level, stage-late check. **(b) ships meanwhile**: `shared-substrate` leaves the `conflict_reason` enum and becomes a 3.f″ stage check with gate `shared-substrate-unhoisted`. What is lost is named: a shared **symbol** in files each lane already owns | §0.7 X-04, §5.2, §5.3, §7.9 (a), §10.1 `substrate.ts`, §10.6, §13, §15.1 E8/E36a, §15.2 T24, §16 R37, §17.6 |

### 19.2 Critical — lane execution and the lease

| id | Findings closed | What changed | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RW-01 | CC-CR1, IM-CR1 | **The lane's commit pathspec.** A lane's commits stage an explicit pathspec drawn from `write_set` and nothing else; orchestrator-only content a child skill writes inside the workspace is left uncommitted, harvested, and its semantic effect replayed at the host. This closes all three residues with one mechanism — `kiwi/pipeline.jsonl`, `docs/plans/**` checkboxes (`kiwi-pm:24`/`:168`/`:353`/`:817`), `docs/analysis/kiwi-review-fix-loop-*/` (`kiwi-review-fix-loop:200`/`:634`) — instead of three suppression flags. Profile B's dirty-tree assertions re-scoped to `write_set ∪ read_set`; `collect()` reverts the residue before switching. `--no-pipeline-emit` **is** now passed at the one place revision 1 omitted it (§7.3's remediation) | **§5.4.2** (new), §5.4, §5.6, §7.3, §7.4 R2–R4, §14 item 11, §16 R31 |
| RW-02 | CC-H2 | **The commit obligation has a carrier.** `kiwi-pm --commit-lane-work <pathspec-file>` (S16, §14 item 12). Revision 1 registered a flag for each of the other two lane behaviour changes and a sentence for this one, while `kiwi-coder:176`/`:836` make committing a user decision and a lane has no user — so the honest outcome was `needs-user` → `lane-child-gate-halted` (C) on every lane, or no commit and a death by row R1 | §5.4, §5.5, §5.6, §14 item 12, §15.3 S16 |
| RW-03 | CC-CR2, CC-H1, ND-N9, IM-CR4 | **`workspace_ref` has a producer, and `lane-possibly-live` can fire.** §3.5a resolves a lane's workspace by the **branch** each worktree reports in `git worktree list --porcelain` — a derived key available from the lane's first act — and classifies a worktree matching no lane as *unattributed*. §4.6's `lane-possibly-live` is re-keyed on **liveness** (live or unattributed workspace), not on an unmatched intent, so it fires for a lane that is still running; revision 1's definition could not, because the dispatch **result** line is written at spawn | **§3.5a** (new), §4.4 `dispatch-lane`, §4.6, §5.5, §5.5.1, §11.4 |
| RW-04 | ND-N1 | **The spawn prompt no longer orders the lane to read a file that does not exist.** `git checkout -B {branch} {base_sha}` and its `rev-parse` assertion move **into the spawn prompt**; `bootstrap` keeps the install and the assertions. Revision 1 put the checkout inside the handoff, which is committed at 3.f′ (mid-session) and therefore absent from a worktree cut at session-start HEAD — an ENOENT on the first tool call of every Profile A lane | §5.5, §6.1 `bootstrap`, §6.3, §7.9 n/a |
| RW-05 | ND-N2 | **`--defer-srs-mutation` has a carrier and a detector.** Added as a `kiwi-pm` pass-through into its `kiwi-coder` spawn prompt (S11/S16, §14 items 11–12), and §7.4's SRS row gains a **host-side** conjunct. Revision 1's audit row was satisfiable by a lane that violated C1: the queue file absent (vacuous) and the git delta clean (the MCP write landed at the host root, `02:203`) | §5.4, §7.4 R6, §14 items 11–12, §15.3 S11/S16 |
| RW-06 | ND-N3, IM-CR7 | **Harvest before reap.** `collect()`'s contract ends by copying manifest, deferred queue, decisions log and the review-fix-loop analysis dir to `{git_root}/kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/w{n}-s{s}-l{k}/`; 3.j′ refuses to reap until they exist (`lane-state-harvest-failed`, C); 3.k replay, 3.l's bundle and `abort-run`'s report read the **host** copies. Revision 1 reaped at 3.j′ and read those files at 3.k and 3.l, which for every stage but the last is a read of a deleted file. The two contradictory replay phases (§5.9 "at integrate", §2.1 "3.k") are reconciled to **3.k** | §2.1 ordering rule 5, §3.1, §3.2, §3.5, §5.4 `collect()`, §5.5.1, §5.9, §7.6, §10.6 |
| RW-07 | ND-N4 | **§4.6 rewritten per lane, and §11.4's drill now agrees with it.** Four per-lane observations `J/B/A/L` and seven outcomes including **`lane-collectable`**; `collect()` gains a **workspace-gone path** that synthesises a `complete-unreported` manifest from the branch diff. Revision 1's set-comparison classified the ordinary mid-stage state `divergent` while its own worked drill asserted `consistent`, and even reaching `collect-lane` stranded two commits as `no-response` → `lane-timeout` (C) | §4.6, §5.4, §3.4 status enum, §11.4, §15.1 E40 |
| RW-08 | IM-CR3 | **Loop L has a mechanism and its fixes are committed.** Per-profile table: under A the remediation is a **dispatch** of a new worktree agent pinned to the lane head with a remediation handoff and the same commit obligation; under B it runs in-tree on the lane branch **before** `collect()`'s switch. Revision 1's *"inside the lane workspace"* had no mechanism under the preferred profile (the lane agent is dead; a skill cannot place a subagent in an existing directory) and nothing committed rounds 1..n−1's fixes | §7.3, §5.6, §16 R32, §15.2 T13 |
| RW-09 | CC-H3 | **Profile B's ordering fixed.** `collect()` ends with `git switch {integration_branch}`, so loop L must run **before** that switch; the sequence is stated. Revision 1 ran loop L "after `collect()`", landing every fix on the integration branch and merging a lane branch that did not contain it | §5.6, §7.3 |
| RW-10 | ND-N12, CC-L5 | **`integrate()` concludes its merge and produces its tree.** Step 4 gains `git write-tree` (the `<tree>` argument was never bound); step 6 gains `git reset --hard <new>`, which clears `MERGE_HEAD`/`MERGE_MSG`, plus an assertion that no merge remains. Without both, lane 2 of any stage fails with *"You have not concluded your merge"* → `lane-merge-conflict` (C), whose declared meaning is a textual conflict — telling the operator the partition was wrong when it was not | §5.9, §15.1 E30 |
| RW-11 | ND-N6 | **The audited sha is the merged sha.** §2.1 ordering rule 6 re-runs the artifact-derived claim-audit rows at 3.j against the tip `integrate()` is about to merge; §5.9 step 0 pins `lane_head` to it and step 6 asserts `second-parent(new)` equals it. Loop L commits, so 3.h's head is stale by 3.j | §2.1 rule 6, §5.9, §7.4, §10.6 `lane audit --at`, §15.1 E17, §15.2 T14 |
| RW-12 | IM-CR2 | **`--handoff` is defined.** It supplies the **execution set and the lease** — pm executes exactly `task_ids[]` in sidecar declaration order, takes Task bodies from the sidecar, uses `write_set` as the commit pathspec, and treats out-of-lane `depends_on_task` predecessors as satisfied. Revision 1 left it as *"input SSOT"*, and pm's only selector is `--from-task`, a start point, while `task_ids[]` is a generally non-contiguous connected component — two lanes on one plan would each run every task | §5.4, §5.6, §15.3 S11 |
| RW-13 | IM-CR9, CC-M6, ND-N8 | **A join model.** §5.5.3: dispatch is non-blocking; **3.g′** polls `orchestrate lane status` every `--poll-interval` (60 s); the deadline is evaluated **there**; the stagger has a signal (`bootstrap_done_at` in a **separate heartbeat file**, rewritten at each Task boundary) and a floor (`--install-stagger`, 30 s). Revision 1 put `heartbeat_at` in the manifest, which is *"your last act"* — a during-run liveness signal that first exists when the lane finishes | **§5.5.3** (new), §5.5.1, §3.5, §6.1, §10.6, §13, §14.1 |
| RW-14 | CF-CR1 | **Charter C1's first clause is enforced.** New conflict edge `non-code-write-set` (→ serial epilogue), defined against `constraints.json`'s `code_roots`/`test_roots`; claim-audit row **R5**; gate `lane-non-code-write` (C); §5.10 splits its C1 row into C1a/C1b. Revision 1 discharged the second clause four times and the first not at all, while `kiwi-planner:739` ships seven non-code task types and this design's own step 10 is one | §5.2, §5.3, §5.10, §7.4 R5, §13, §15.1 E17, §15.2 T11 |
| RW-15 | IM-CR6 | **`files-not-grounded` has a mechanism.** A **near-miss** detector (Levenshtein ≤ 2 against an existing path) plus an out-of-range `line_range` check, both pure over `(declared, existing)`; a non-existent path with no near neighbour passes. Revision 1's *"not declared to-be-created"* had no marker in the sidecar schema (`kiwi-planner:744`), so read literally the gate refused every greenfield lane including §6.3's own example | §5.1, §6.3 Constraints, §10.1 `grounding.ts`, §10.6, §13, §15.1 E8a, §16 R38, §17.7 |
| RW-16 | IM-CR8, IM-H9 | **`C` means one thing, and demotion has a legality rule.** §2's legend gains: *a gate with a stated recovery is raised only when that recovery is exhausted or unavailable*, with a five-row table. §7.3 defines demotion legality once — legal iff no task of a later stage depends on the lane — and both loops use it. Revision 1 read as both "halt" and "recover automatically" for five gates whose ids are a **shipped** contract, and had no path at all for a demoted stage-1 lane whose successors are already planned | §2 legend, §6.4, §7.3, §7.5, §8.1, §13, §15.2 T20 |

### 19.5 Medium — accepted, with the reason

| id | Finding | Why it is accepted rather than closed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RA-01 | **IM-M10** — `red_evidence`'s manifest shape adds `ac_id` and `test_id` to `kiwi-coder`'s `RedEvidence` and renames `timestamp` → `at`, so §6.5's *"not new machinery"* understates it | **Accepted as stated, with the claim corrected in place.** The *mechanism* — capture the failing command, its non-zero exit and its output before green — genuinely is `kiwi-coder`'s (`SKILL.md:220`, `:374-386`; the interface is at `kiwi-planner:779`). What is new is a **projection** of it into the manifest with two extra keys. Renaming §3.4's field to match `kiwi-coder`'s exactly would lose the AC linkage §7.4 R8/R9 need; adding the two keys to `kiwi-coder`'s own schema is a change to a skill this design does not own, for no gain inside that skill. The narrower honest statement — *"the proof is `kiwi-coder`'s; the manifest projection is new"* — is what §3.4 and §6.5 now say |
| RA-02 | **IM-M11** — `run_id` has no format rule while it is embedded in git ref names and in a C7 path budget | **Accepted, bounded.** `--run-id` is validated against `git check-ref-format --allow-onelevel` and `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,48}$` at Preflight, which is stated in §14.1's option description. A *budget* rule is not adopted: the runtime, not this design, chooses the worktree prefix (§5.5), so a static budget would be a guess; probe assertion 5 measures the real extremum empirically on the actual host, which is strictly better than a validated length |
| RA-03 | **§17.4** — `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` checks form, not correctness | **Unchanged from revision 1, and still true.** The compensation (the next wave's loop P re-checks the previous wave's `out-of-run` items) is one wave late and the last wave has no successor. Making it correct requires judging a classification, which §1.1's boundary rule assigns to prose |
| RA-04 | **§17.2** — N concurrent `npm ci` on one Windows cache is untested | **Unchanged.** `--install-concurrency 2` and `--install-stagger 30` reduce exposure; neither measures the case. Measuring it is a repository experiment, not a design decision |
| RA-05 | **§17.10** — Layer 2 conformance is self-consistency for everything new | **Unchanged.** The v1.4.0 examples and the code that reads them are authored by the same party in the same change; only the pre-existing `waves-event.md §5` subset is mutual checking, and it is placeholder-laden |

### 19.6 What revision 2 did **not** close

| Item | Why |
| --- | --- |
| X-01, X-02(a), X-03, **X-04** | User rulings. Each states the narrowest change, what the document does meanwhile, and the consequence of a refusal. X-04 is new: the charter's no-duplication requirement asks for something no current planner output can express, and the design says so rather than pretending the path-level check is the whole of it |
| §17.6 — the *symbol*-level duplication detector | Genuinely absent, not merely weak, until X-04 is ruled. The path-level 3.f″ check and the post-merge audit both run; neither sees a symbol added to files each lane already owns |
| §17.9 — the executable/prose ratio | Structural, and revision 2 made it marginally worse again (more gates than modules). Named, not fixed |
| §6.5 — the *vacuous-test* class | Layer 4 closes the duplicate-pointer form mechanically. Three distinct trivial tests still satisfy every count-based closure; only loop L verifier 1's prose verdict stands between that and promotion, and §1.1's boundary rule says that is where it belongs |
| The `07` §5b worktrees that cannot be shown merge-safe — **at most two of six, not four** | Corrected in revision 3: `07:200-201` says *"**Four of them hold zero commits** not already in `feat/2.3.0.1`"*, i.e. four are the ones provably **safe to remove**, and at most two are open — one of which is `work/kiwi-wave-master-multiroot-g0`, which must not be touched. Revision 2's row inverted the source and overstated the open work against a binding document. §10.5 step 10′ removes the four and **reports** the remainder; a worktree with unmerged commits is a user decision, not a cleanup |

---

## 20. Revision log — revision 3

One row per finding closed, accepted or escalated in revision 3, with **where** to check it. Finding
ids are prefixed by the verifier that raised them: **CC** = critical-closure, **ND** = new-defects,
**CF** = charter-fidelity, **IM** = implementability. Where two or three verifiers raised the same
defect the row lists every id and closes it once.

**Contradictions between findings, resolved rather than both-satisfied.** Two pairs disagreed and
are dealt with explicitly rather than by writing text that satisfies each half:

- **CC-N3 / ND-C1 / IM-C2 (fix loop L's branch collision) versus IM-R35 (never check the lane branch
  out at the host, so R11 keeps the lane's `node_modules`).** A remediation that reuses the lane
  branch is refused by git; a reap-before-3.i that frees the branch destroys the `node_modules` R11
  needs. Resolution: **neither** — a distinct `-fix{r}` branch in a *new* workspace, and R11's cwd
  follows the tip to whichever workspace produced it (§7.3, §7.4). Recorded as R44.
- **ND-C3 (a demoted lane must not be integrated on resume) versus IM-H3 (`P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED`
  must not be unsatisfiable after a legal demotion).** Both are true and both need the same missing
  fact — *why* a lane will never merge. One new journal field, `lane_disposition`, serves both, and
  the two findings close together (RX-06, RX-07).

### 20.1 Escalated to the user — the fifth ruling

| id | Subject | Disposition | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **X-05** | Under `--auto`, `00.charter.md:54` — the charter's **only** named user interaction — is answered by a 3-member committee. Revision 2 relabelled it a *recorded divergence* and left it in §17.17's register. `00.charter.md:10` makes the charter the authority on its own sentence, and §8 requires user consent even under `--auto` for the strictly smaller act of deferring one wave-boundary issue, so a register entry is a record and not a disposition | **Escalated**, with the same shape as X-01…X-04: (a) amend the charter's intake sentence to admit a committee, or (b) make intake QnA a `critical_gates[]` member. **Behaviour is unchanged meanwhile and under either ruling** — §7.2's three audit records stay mandatory, so nothing is blocked on it. Declining (a) *is* choosing (b); there is no third position. Moves with X-03, because `07:77-78` conditions Q2 on the tie rung | §0.7 X-05, §7.2, §17.17, §16 R45 |

### 20.2 Critical

| id | Findings closed | What changed | Where |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RX-01 | CC-N1 | **`base_sha` is deleted from the handoff front matter.** It was defined as *"the sha of this stage's 3.f′ commit"* while that same commit stages the handoff — a **fixed point**, not an ordering problem: a commit's sha hashes its own tree, so no value inside it can equal it, and no `--amend` sequence converges. `lane-{k}.lock.json`'s `front_matter_digest` is committed alongside and pinned by drift digest 4, so a post-commit fill is `handoff-unresolvable-reference` plus a forced re-verify; and the natural implementer pick — 3.d's sha — puts the handoff **outside** its own base and reopens the ENOENT-on-first-read failure RW-04 closed. The value now travels in the spawn prompt, the journal's `isolation` object and the card's `open[]`, all untracked. §5.5's *"both of which are also written in the handoff's front matter"* is struck. §6.2 layer 4's resolution point is named as **HEAD at 3.f plus the 3.f′ staged set**, which is evaluable when layer 4 actually runs | §2.1 ordering rule 2, §4.1, §5.5, §6.1, §6.2 layer 4, §6.3, §15.1 E14/E44, §15.2 T31 |
| RX-02 | CC-N2 | **`dispatch-base-dirty` no longer fires at every stage ≥ 2.** Its path set subtracts every path matching an `orchestrator-only` glob, which covers `plan_path` — the file §5.4.2 consequence 4 has the orchestrator itself modify at the **host** at 3.j through `workflow_task_check` (`src/core/workflow/mutation.ts:359`) and *"simply never commit"*. Stage 1's own integration therefore left the gate's own path set dirty, and stage 2's 3.f′ halted the run: every multi-stage wave, every run, both profiles. The **two incompatible path sets** revision 2 gave one sentence apart are resolved to one — the front-matter union minus orchestrator-only — and the clause *"over the same pathspec that commit staged"* is deleted, since under it the gate would inspect neither the plan nor the sidecar and be close to vacuous | §2.1 ordering rule 1, §4.8 policy 5, §5.4.2 consequence 4, §13 |
| RX-03 | CC-N3, ND-C1, IM-C2 | **Loop L's Profile A remediation runs on a `-fix{r}` branch, not the lane's.** Reproduced (git 2.48.1.windows.1): `git checkout -B <branch>` from a second worktree on a held branch exits **128**. Revision 2's remediation checked out the lane's own branch while §7.4 states the lane workspace *"still exists at 3.h/3.j"* — so the first command failed, no `manifest_path` was locatable, loop L's PASS precondition was unreachable in every round, and **every lane needing any fix** died on cap exhaustion under the preferred profile. §3.5a's resolution is re-keyed on a **ref family**; `laneHeadRef(k)` is the highest `-fix{r}`, else the lane branch, and it feeds §4.6's `B(k)`, §5.9 step 0 and every `git-ancestor` proof; R11's cwd follows the tip; 3.j′ reaps every workspace of the family. §5.5.1 adds *release-before-re-dispatch*, which is the same collision on §7.3's dead-lane path | §3.5a, §4.5, §4.6, §5.5.1, §5.9, §7.3, §7.4, §15.2 T13/T25, §16 R44 |
| RX-04 | ND-C2, IM-C5 | **`LIVENESS(k)` is total.** Its third value is *"otherwise"* and it consults §5.5.3's terminality predicate. Revision 2 defined `live` on a freshness conjunct and **both** other values on *"no such worktree"*, leaving *worktree exists, unlocked, heartbeat stale* undefined — the modal interruption shape here, since workspaces are reaped only at 3.j′ and §5.5.1 records six stale worktrees on this checkout. Every per-lane row failed and first-match-wins reached *"divergent"* → `ledger-reconciliation-divergent` (C) on a correct run. RW-07's closure moved the same outcome to a different state | §4.6, §15.1 E41 |
| RX-05 | IM-C1, CC-N4 | **`--commit-lane-work` takes no argument.** Its pathspec file had two contradictory locations, and the normative one (`kiwi/orchestrator/{run_id}/lanes/lane-3.pathspec`) is untracked and git-ignored by §4.8 policy 1 — absent from any worktree cut at session-start HEAD (`02:170-179`) and not in 3.f′'s pathspec. pm would ENOENT, commit nothing, and `collect()` would find `head_sha == base_sha` → **R1 death on every lane**, the exact outcome the carrier exists to prevent. pm already has `write_set` via `--handoff`; `commit_policy.pathspec_file` is deleted and `commit_policy.pathspec` names the source | §5.4, §6.1, §6.3, §14 item 11, §15.3 S16, §16 R42 |
| RX-06 | ND-C3 | **A lane that leaves the run without merging has a class and a record.** New journal field `lane_disposition` (`demoted` \| `quarantined` \| `coupling-reset` \| `refuted`) and new §4.6 class `lane-quarantined`. Revision 2's seven outcomes keyed on `J/B/A/L` and never read the manifest status, so a `design-refuted` lane — whose `collect()` completed before 3.h halted the run — resumed as `lane-integrable` and **a resumed session integrated work whose design item had been refuted**. Identical for a demoted lane and for the two lanes a coupling collapse resets | §4.2, §4.6, §7.3, §7.5, §15.1 E42, §15.3 S1 |
| RX-07 | IM-H3 | **`P-PRIOR-STAGES-INTEGRATED` is satisfiable after a legal demotion.** A lane is *settled* when it has a `git-ancestor` proof **or** a terminal `lane_disposition`. Revision 2 required a proof for every lane of every earlier stage while §7.3 explicitly permits demoting an independent stage-1 lane; on the recovery path the design calls *correct*, every later resume returned `ledger-reconciliation-divergent` (C) and the wave became **unresumable for the rest of its life** | §4.1, §4.6, §15.1 E42 |
| RX-08 | IM-C4 | **The 3.j re-audit reads the *effective lane record*.** The lane's manifest unioned with every `remediation-{r}.manifest.json` in round order: `written_paths` union, `commands_run` and `commits` concatenated, scalars from the last. Reading the lane's alone fails **R2** (the fixer's paths are in the diff, not in `written_paths`) → `lane-lease-breach` (C); reading the fixer's alone fails **R9** (no `red_evidence` for the original ACs). Revision 2's parenthetical *"legal for the fixer, not for the lane"* is **deleted** — it contradicts the remediation handoff's own `write_set` rule and would have made R3 fail on the action it called legal | §2.1 ordering rule 6, §7.4, §10.6, §15.1 E17/E46, §16 R43 |
| RX-09 | IM-C6 | **The per-lane pipeline event has a per-lane key, `{run_id}#w{n}s{s}l{k}`.** Verified in `src/`: `workflow_pipeline_emit` dedupes on `${skill}\|${run_id}` (`mutation.ts:466`, `jsonl.ts:76-78`) and returns a **success** envelope with `written: false` on a repeat (`:467-471`). Revision 2 required *"one pipeline event per lane"* under one bare `run_id`, so lanes 2..N emitted, received `ok`, and appended nothing — R26's replacement produced one event per **run**, silently. `pipeline-event.md` §5.4 already sanctions suffixed keys and states they are *"a different id space"*, so this uses the existing contract | §5.9, §14 item 7, §15.1 E45, §15.3 S13 |
| RX-10 | IM-C3 | **`lanes[].design_items` has a producer.** A sixth injected input, `design_item_map: {req_id → [D-nnn]}`, recorded on the `register-wave-srs` result line and materialised at `waves/wave-{n}/design-item-map.json`; `lane.design_items` is the union over the lane's `req_ids`. Revision 2 made it a field of a **byte-deterministic** lock and a **frozen** loop-H denominator while `computeLanePlan`'s inputs carried no design items and `kiwi-planner:741-759` has no such field — and all three ways out were refused by the document (the function cannot emit it; filling it later breaks E6/E38 and digest 3; author judgment violates the engine's externally-fixed-denominator rule) | §3.1, §3.3a, §4.2, §4.7 digest 3, §5.3, §5.11, §15.1 E6/E38 |
| RX-11 | CF-CR1 | **The serial epilogue has an executor, a lease, a commit rule, an audit and a gate — new §5.13.** Five rules route work to 3.k and its entire content was three activities, none of which is *"execute the epilogue task set"*: no party, no verb, no gate, no §13 row. Reachable from this design's own step 10 (`type: "infra"` → `serial_epilogue`), whose non-execution makes loop P's epilogue sub-denominator `checked = 0` → `wave-verify-fail-residual` (C) **after merges have landed**, in every repository (CP-02/CP-07 ship universally). The epilogue is now one handoff (`handoff_kind: "epilogue"`) run through `/kiwi-pm --commit-lane-work` at the host on the integration branch, with a stated audit row set (R1/R2/R3/R7–R11, not R4/R5/R6) and gate `epilogue-task-failed` (C). §7.10's `verified` transition gains an epilogue-evidence clause, without which a REQ serialised **for charter C1 compliance** could never reach `verified` | **§5.13** (new), §2.1 3.k, §7.4, §7.6, §7.10, §13, §15.2 T30, §16 R46 |

### 20.3 High

| id | Findings closed | What changed | Where |

### 20.5 Medium — accepted, with the reason

| id | Finding | Why it is accepted rather than closed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RY-01 | **CF-M2** — the `--auto` intake divergence is recorded rather than escalated | **Not accepted — escalated**, as X-05 (§20.1). Listed here so a reader looking for it in the accepted table finds the pointer instead of concluding it was dropped. |
| RY-02 | **§5.3's `existing_paths` is a per-wave snapshot and is stale at stage ≥ 2** | **Accepted, with the exposure narrowed and stated.** Its only surviving consumer is the prefix-directory suppression in `write-set-overlap`, and staleness makes that rule *less* likely to force same-lane, never more. A directory that first appears mid-wave is created by a lane whose write set already declares the file inside it, so the overlap is caught on the file — the stronger signal. Recomputing mid-wave would fire `lane-plan-drift` on normal progress, which §4.7 digest 3 already rejects as the worse failure. |
| RY-03 | **§17.9 — the executable/prose ratio** | **Unchanged, and revision 3 made it worse again**: it added `epilogue-task-failed` and `stage-coupling-unresolved`, deleted `shared-substrate-unhoisted`, and added four verbs against roughly one module rename. Named, not fixed. |
| RY-04 | **§6.5 — the *vacuous-test* class** | **Unchanged.** Layer 4 closes the duplicate-pointer form; three distinct trivial tests still satisfy every count-based closure, and R11's exact-name matching checks that a named test ran and passed, never that it asserts anything. §1.1's boundary rule says that is where it belongs. |
| RY-05 | **§17.4 — `P-WAVE-ISSUES-CLOSED` checks form, not correctness** | **Unchanged from revisions 1 and 2.** RX-45 widens the *input* to the ledger; it does not make a misclassification detectable. Judging a classification is prose work by §1.1. |
| RY-06 | **§17.2 — N concurrent `npm ci` on one Windows cache** | **Unchanged.** `--install-concurrency 2` and `--install-stagger 30` reduce exposure; neither measures the case, and RX-22's heartbeat format only makes the *signal* real, not the measurement. |

## 21. Revision log — revision 4

Revision 4 is not a review round. It applies **two normative rulings that landed after revision 3**
and does nothing else beyond §21.5's mechanical citation repair. No finding from any critic was addressed, no open item from §20.6 was closed,
and no defect this document already knew about was fixed — those are later phases' work, and the
previous rounds diverged by mixing adjudicated changes with unadjudicated ones.

**What was applied**

| Ruling | Source | What it decides |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Scope cut | `08.scope-and-frame-decisions.md` §3, 5-0 | concurrent lane execution moves from `2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator` to `2.6.0-phase2-parallel-lanes`; the per-task parallelisability **analysis** ships in phase 1 in full |
| Tie rung / `--auto` frame | `08` §2, 5-0 | the tie rung is dropped, no-majority halts as `critical`, `07` Q2 stays `leave-all-three`, and two `07` conditions travel into the requirement text |
| Routing | `09.routing-design.md` | the orchestrator judges scale and scope at Phase 1.c′ and routes to `kiwi-tdd`, `kiwi-pm` or its own flow |

row's named edit site). What §22.1 did *not* do is carry its own additions into the four places that

Measured: 0 of 293 Tasks across the five sidecars in `docs/plans/` carry `traces`; all carry `trace_links`. Unedited, S10 was empty on 100% of contract-valid input and D7's second disjunct was inert, so AA-23's *§7.3's "D7 should have caught this at intake" becomes true as written* was false. The justification clause was replaced too: C14 (`kiwi-planner/SKILL.md:513`) makes the Requirement-typed trace set a **subset** of `task.req_ids`, so the true distinction is **every** Task vs **open** Tasks — D6's reason, not a different denominator. ### 22.13 What round ten deliberately did **not** do

| Item | Why |
| --- | --- |
| Give the `R-PLAN` residual a second-party verdict, or an `owner` vocabulary (AG-01) | Closure 3's verifier 2 has no analogue on a delegated rung, which is what `09` §13 A9 now records rather than invents. `09` §9.2 already imports closure 2's `owner` form by explicit reference; that the *"named later lane or wave"* wording is inapplicable on a rung with neither is a separate key |

   this round was warned against.

   — 38 of them declared in phase 1"* and cites §13 for it. Leaving it would have been an

   gate row carries the clause the action asked for; both use the identical formula, so the two do

   a loose block after §10.4's list — the document's own convention for material owned by one item.

were adjudicated independently against the **same** table row and are recorded as one entry, `AH-01`;
findings 15 and 20 were likewise one defect and are recorded as `AH-15`. Twenty land in `05`, six in

   which is round ten's record and is not amended by a later round.

### 22.16 Round twelve — count-claim audit, numbers only

A bounded pass over this document's **self-referential count claims** — statements asserting a
quantity about its own content (rows in a table, items in a list, gates declared, flags invented,
inputs a function takes). Counts about the outside world (line numbers in `src/`, byte sizes, token
budgets, measured timings) were out of scope. Each claim below was checked by counting its referent;
only wrong numbers and unresolvable referents are listed, and **nothing but the number was changed**
except for ten disclosed exceptions listed in §22.17 — otherwise no rewording, no restructuring, no
adjacent repairs. The `AJ-nn` id family is new and collides with no
other family in this file.

### 22.17 What round twelve deliberately did **not** do

| Item | Why |
| --- | --- |
| Reword, restructure or improve any sentence beyond what the numeral required | The pass this replaces failed because fixes reached beyond their findings. Most edits above change a quantity and, where a numeral's grammar forced it, its immediate agreement. Ten did more — AJ-13, AJ-14, AJ-18, AJ-19, AJ-20, AJ-24, AJ-25, AJ-26, AJ-27 and AJ-28 added a cross-reference, a section pointer, a restructured clause or a supporting assertion. Each expansion is disclosed in that row's Applied cell. The counts are correct; the scope exceeded the pass's own rule, and it is recorded here rather than left for a later round to find. |
| Report or change count claims that are **correct** | Only wrong numbers and unresolvable referents are recorded. A correct count is not a finding |
| Update §22.8 AE-02's quotation of *§10.5 step 0's ~108 requirements* (`05:8463`) | A revision-log row recording what a past round decided, quoting the then-current figure. AJ-10 changed §10.5 step 0 itself; amending an earlier round's record is not this round's business, and §22.15 item 7 states the same rule. **Noted as stale, not fixed** |
| Update §19 RW-22's *15-field JSON* (`05:7879`) | Same class: revision 2's log row restating what revision 1 demanded. AJ-27 rebound §5.5.2's live sentence; the log entry is a record. **Noted as stale, not fixed** |
| Update §20 RX-18's quotation of *Six mandatory front-matter fields* (`05:8000`) | Same class: revision 3's log row quoting the figure §6.2 layer 4 then carried. AJ-08 corrected that live prose to *five*; the log entry is a record. **Noted as stale, not fixed** |
| Verify counts about the repository, `src/`, byte sizes, token budgets or measured timings | Out of scope by construction — those were verified elsewhere and do not break when a row is added to a table here |
| Any change to `docs/spec/`, `src/`, `skills/` or `test/` | Nothing outside `docs/research/kiwi-orchestrator/` was touched |
